# EHF-PHYS-ALG-01 — Coxeter/Yang-Baxter No-Fit Bridge Test

**Branch:** EHF-PHYS-ALG  
**Inputs:** EHF-HC Foundation v1.0; GROW-01 through GROW-08; PHYS-CHAN consolidated verdict  
**External comparison:** Coxeter/symmetric-group presentations, braid group, rational Yang-Baxter Baxterization, quantum-integrability framework  
**Status:** Exact Coxeter/braid algebra bridge certified; Yang-Baxterization conditionally available; integrable-physics bridge not certified  
**Certification:** \(186/186\) strict checks passed.

---

# 1. Why this branch

The partial-wave branch ended with a precise mathematical channel-incidence theorem but no microscopic physical bridge.

The alternative candidate from PHYS-CHAN-01 was the permutation/Coxeter/Yang-Baxter direction.

This route is structurally better motivated because the HC microscopic moves are already literal permutations of cyclic positions.

The no-fit question is:

> **How much Coxeter/braid/Yang-Baxter structure is already forced by the HC reversal algebra, before adding a spectral parameter, tensor-product representation, transfer matrix, or Hamiltonian?**

The answer divides sharply into three levels:

\[
\boxed{\text{native Coxeter structure}}
\]

\[
\boxed{\text{conditional Yang-Baxterization}}
\]

\[
\boxed{\text{unestablished integrable physics}}.
\]

---

# 2. LR2 is native type-A Coxeter structure

An LR2 positional move exchanges two adjacent cyclic positions:

\[
s_i=(i,i+1).
\]

Therefore:

\[
\boxed{s_i^2=e.}
\]

For neighboring generators:

\[
\boxed{
s_is_{i+1}s_i
=
s_{i+1}s_is_{i+1}.
}
\]

For nonadjacent disjoint generators:

\[
\boxed{
s_is_j=s_js_i.
}
\]

These are exactly the type-A Coxeter relations.

The symmetric group \(S_n\) is classically generated by adjacent transpositions with precisely these involution, braid and far-commutation relations.

Because HC uses a cyclic list of adjacent transpositions, there are \(n\) generators rather than the standard \(n-1\).

The wrap generator:

\[
(n-1,0)
\]

is redundant: it is conjugate to an ordinary adjacent transposition by a word in the other generators.

Thus the cyclic LR2 generator system is best described as:

\[
\boxed{
\text{a finite }S_n\text{ quotient of a cyclic/affine-type-A Coxeter presentation.}
}
\]

It is not the infinite affine Coxeter group itself.

Exact group-order and relation checks passed for:

\[
5\le n\le14.
\]

---

# 3. LR3 is also a genuine reflection system

A length-three reversal swaps only its endpoints:

\[
R_{i,3}=(i,i+2).
\]

So LR3 is a transposition and therefore a genuine reflection in \(S_n\).

Its generator geometry is distance two rather than distance one.

Two LR3 generators whose endpoint transpositions share one endpoint obey the braid relation.

Disjoint LR3 transpositions commute.

This reproduces the GROW-07 group split:

## Odd \(n\)

The step-two position graph is one cycle, and LR3 generates:

\[
\boxed{S_n.}
\]

## Even \(n=2m\)

Step two preserves positional parity and gives two cycles, so LR3 generates:

\[
\boxed{S_m\times S_m.}
\]

Thus LR3's special even-layer sector behavior already has an exact permutation/reflection-algebra counterpart.

---

# 4. Higher LR support is not a new Coxeter reflection

A length-\(\ell\) block reversal is:

\[
R_{i,\ell}.
\]

Inside its \(\ell\)-site interval it is the longest element:

\[
w_0
\]

of the parabolic subgroup:

\[
S_\ell.
\]

Its Coxeter length is:

\[
\boxed{
\operatorname{len}(R_{i,\ell})
=
\binom{\ell}{2}.
}
\]

As a permutation it is the product of:

\[
\boxed{
\left\lfloor\frac{\ell}{2}\right\rfloor
}
\]

disjoint transpositions.

Therefore:

\[
\boxed{
R_{i,\ell}
\text{ is a single }S_n\text{ reflection iff }\ell=2\text{ or }3.
}
\]

For:

\[
\ell\ge4,
\]

the LR channel move is a composite involution.

So higher reversal supports do not introduce independent primitive reflection generators.

They are canonical composite words in the LR2 Coxeter algebra.

This is a major simplification of the hierarchy.

---

# 5. Re-reading GROW-08

GROW-08 found the recursive identity:

\[
s_0s_1\cdots s_{m-1}R_mR_{m+1}=e.
\]

The Coxeter interpretation is now immediate.

The block reversal:

\[
R_m
\]

is the longest element of the \(m\)-site parabolic subgroup.

Adding:

\[
m
\]

simple adjacent transpositions increases the Coxeter length by:

\[
m.
\]

Indeed:

\[
\binom m2+m
=
\binom{m+1}{2}.
\]

Therefore the GROW-08 recursive channel polygon is exactly the standard longest-element recursion written in support colors.

In particular, the LR2/LR3/LR4 pentagon:

\[
(2,2,2,3,4)
\]

is not evidence by itself for a fusion-category or \(6j\)-symbol pentagon.

It is already explained inside the type-A permutation algebra.

This is a useful negative reinterpretation.

---

# 6. Full higher-support overlap algebra is richer than a simple braid chain

For each support \(\ell\), compare two reversals whose start positions differ by \(d\).

The product order:

\[
m_\ell(d)
=
\operatorname{ord}
\left(
R_{0,\ell}R_{d,\ell}
\right)
\]

depends strongly on overlap.

Examples from the exact census include:

\[
\ell=4:
\quad
m(1)=5,\;
m(2)=3,\;
m(3)=6,\;
m(4)=2,
\]

and:

\[
\ell=5:
\quad
m(1)=3,\;
m(2)=6,\;
m(3)=3,\;
m(4)=6,\;
m(5)=2.
\]

So the complete higher-support move family is not merely a copy of the nearest-neighbor braid presentation.

Its partial-overlap commutators from GROW-08 are genuine composite Coxeter-word effects.

---

# 7. A new exact embedded-braid theorem

There is nevertheless a uniform braid structure inside every support channel.

Define:

\[
\boxed{
d_\ell
=
\left\lceil\frac{\ell}{2}\right\rceil.
}
\]

Consider the subfamily of support-\(\ell\) reversals whose starts are spaced by \(d_\ell\):

\[
g_j
=
R_{jd_\ell,\ell}.
\]

Then neighboring generators overlap in exactly the configuration for which:

\[
\boxed{
(g_jg_{j+1})^3=e.
}
\]

Since both are involutions:

\[
\boxed{
g_jg_{j+1}g_j
=
g_{j+1}g_jg_{j+1}.
}
\]

Next-nearest generators begin \(2d_\ell\) sites apart.

Since:

\[
2d_\ell\ge\ell,
\]

their support intervals are disjoint, so:

\[
\boxed{
g_jg_{j+2}=g_{j+2}g_j.
}
\]

Therefore every LR support contains a naturally spaced type-A braid/Coxeter subchain:

\[
\boxed{
LR_\ell
\supset
\text{embedded braid chain at stride }\left\lceil\ell/2\right\rceil.
}
\]

This was certified for:

\[
2\le\ell\le14.
\]

The product can be written explicitly as disjoint 3-cycles, giving an elementary all-\(\ell\) proof.

---

# 8. Why this does not make the whole LR family a braid chain

The full start family:

\[
R_{0,\ell},
R_{1,\ell},
R_{2,\ell},\ldots
\]

contains all overlap depths.

For \(\ell\ge3\), those extra overlaps introduce product orders other than \(2\) and \(3\).

Under the literal unit-start ordering, only LR2 satisfies both:

- neighbor braid relation;
- next-nearest far commutation.

Thus the embedded braid chain for higher support requires selecting the canonical stride:

\[
d_\ell=\lceil\ell/2\rceil.
\]

This is a derived support-dependent subfamily, not the complete LR channel geometry.

---

# 9. Constant braid/Yang-Baxter structure is already native

The braid relation:

\[
g_ig_{i+1}g_i
=
g_{i+1}g_ig_{i+1}
\]

is the constant braided Yang-Baxter relation.

Therefore LR2 supplies a native constant Yang-Baxter/braid representation immediately.

The same is true on every embedded \(d_\ell\)-spaced support subchain.

No continuous spectral parameter is required for this constant relation.

So the strongest exact algebra statement is:

\[
\boxed{
\textbf{HC contains native braid-group/Coxeter representations.}
}
\]

---

# 10. Standard Baxterization

For involutive braid generators \(g_i\), define:

\[
\boxed{
\check R_i(u)
=
I+u\,g_i.
}
\]

Then:

\[
\boxed{
\check R_i(u)
\check R_{i+1}(u+v)
\check R_i(v)
=
\check R_{i+1}(v)
\check R_i(u+v)
\check R_{i+1}(u).
}
\]

The audit verified this symbolically in the regular representation of \(S_3\).

More generally, use:

\[
\check R_i(u)=I+f(u)g_i.
\]

Expansion of the braided Yang-Baxter equation gives exactly the functional requirement:

\[
\boxed{
f(u+v)=f(u)+f(v).
}
\]

Under ordinary regularity assumptions this gives:

\[
f(u)=cu.
\]

Thus once an additive spectral coordinate is introduced, the standard linear Baxterization is essentially fixed up to scale.

This is a genuine exact algebraic bridge.

---

# 11. But the spectral parameter is not HC-native

Nothing in the raw HC or GROW foundations supplies a continuous variable:

\[
u.
\]

In particular, the framework has not derived:

- rapidity;
- momentum;
- energy;
- spectral parameter;
- physical time.

Therefore:

\[
\boxed{
\check R_i(u)=I+u g_i
}
\]

is an available Baxterization of the HC braid algebra.

It is not forced by the HC comparison structure itself.

The choice ledger must retain:

\[
\boxed{
\text{spectral parameter = additional structure}.
}
\]

---

# 12. Tensor-product obstruction

The usual rational permutation \(R\)-matrix is represented locally on tensor factors.

A homogeneous quantum chain uses:

\[
V^{\otimes n},
\]

whose dimension scales by the fixed ratio:

\[
\frac{\dim V^{\otimes(n+1)}}{\dim V^{\otimes n}}
=
\dim V.
\]

HC state counts instead obey:

\[
|HC_n|
=
\frac{(n-1)!}{2}.
\]

Hence:

\[
\boxed{
\frac{|HC_{n+1}|}{|HC_n|}
=
n.
}
\]

The growth ratio changes with layer.

Therefore the native HC state spaces are not a homogeneous fixed-local-dimension tensor-power chain.

This is an exact structural obstruction to identifying HC state space directly with the standard local quantum-chain Hilbert space.

It does not forbid embedding HC into a larger tensor space or constructing a constrained model.

Such an embedding would be additional structure.

---

# 13. Transfer matrix is not yet derived

In quantum integrability, a spectral-parameter \(R\)-matrix becomes physically powerful when used to construct a monodromy/transfer matrix, leading to commuting operators:

\[
[t(u),t(v)]=0.
\]

HC currently provides none of the extra ingredients canonically:

- fixed local space \(V\);
- auxiliary space;
- tensor placement \(R_{0i}(u)\);
- ordered monodromy;
- auxiliary trace;
- boundary conditions.

Therefore the existence of a Baxterized group-algebra element does not by itself establish HC quantum integrability.

---

# 14. No Hamiltonian yet

A familiar integrable-chain Hamiltonian is then obtained from a transfer matrix by a logarithmic derivative at a regular spectral point.

HC has not derived:

- that transfer matrix;
- a physical time generator;
- an energy interpretation;
- a local Hermitian Hamiltonian.

Therefore:

\[
\boxed{
\textbf{no Heisenberg/XXX-type HC Hamiltonian is certified.}
}
\]

One can construct such models from permutation \(R\)-matrices after choosing a tensor representation.

That is a standard external construction, not an HC consequence.

---

# 15. What is genuinely stronger than the partial-wave branch

The Legendre bridge arose only after:

- discarding self propagation;
- forgetting growth orientation;
- symmetrizing channel incidence.

The Coxeter bridge is more primitive.

Already at the microscopic positional level:

\[
\boxed{
LR2
=
\text{adjacent transposition}.
}
\]

No spectral averaging or orientation-forgetting is needed to obtain the Coxeter/braid relation.

Furthermore every higher block reversal is already a word in those generators.

So the exact algebraic connection:

\[
\boxed{
\text{HC positional reversal algebra}
\longrightarrow
\text{type-A Coxeter/permutation algebra}
}
\]

is considerably stronger than the earlier partial-wave analogy.

---

# 16. No-fit score

## Native Coxeter bridge

\[
\boxed{\textbf{PASS}}
\]

Exact and direct.

## Native braid/constant Yang-Baxter bridge

\[
\boxed{\textbf{PASS}}
\]

Exact for LR2 and the derived embedded braid subchains.

## Spectral-parameter Yang-Baxterization

\[
\boxed{\textbf{CONDITIONAL PASS}}
\]

Exact after adding a continuous additive parameter.

## Standard transfer-matrix integrability

\[
\boxed{\textbf{NOT DERIVED}}
\]

Requires additional representation and tensor-product structure.

## Physical integrable Hamiltonian

\[
\boxed{\textbf{NOT DERIVED}}
\]

No physical energy/time interpretation follows from the current HC foundation.

---

# 17. Main verdict

The strongest safe conclusion is:

\[
\boxed{
\textbf{The HC reversal algebra has an exact native Coxeter/braid backbone.}
}
\]

More specifically:

- LR2 is the native simple-reflection system;
- LR3 is another genuine transposition/reflection system;
- higher LR channels are parabolic longest elements, not new primitive reflections;
- every support contains an exact embedded braid chain;
- GROW-08 recursive polygons are Coxeter longest-element identities;
- standard Yang-Baxterization is algebraically available.

But:

\[
\boxed{
\textbf{HC does not yet derive a spectral parameter, tensor-product quantum chain, commuting transfer matrix, or integrable Hamiltonian.}
}
\]

So the Yang-Baxter **algebra bridge** is real.

The Yang-Baxter **physics bridge** remains open.

---

# 18. Recommended next step

A further probe is justified because the algebraic result is genuinely microscopic.

The appropriate next test is:

## EHF-PHYS-ALG-02 — Baxterization Necessity, Representation, and Transfer-Matrix Audit

It should ask:

1. whether any HC-native observable can serve as the additive spectral parameter;
2. whether a natural representation of the positional algebra exists on an HC-derived local or edge tensor space;
3. whether different embedded LR support braid chains admit one common \(R(u)\) representation or incompatible ones;
4. whether a monodromy/transfer matrix can be defined without arbitrary auxiliary-space choices;
5. whether commuting transfer matrices occur;
6. only then, whether a Hamiltonian can be extracted.

The falsification rule should remain strict:

> If the spectral parameter and tensor-factor representation have to be imported independently, stop at the certified Coxeter/Baxterization theorem rather than calling HC an integrable quantum model.

# EHF-PHYS-ALG-02 — Baxterization Necessity, Representation, and Transfer-Matrix Audit

**Branch:** EHF-PHYS-ALG  
**Inputs:** EHF-HC Foundation v1.0; GROW-01 through GROW-08; PHYS-ALG-01  
**External comparison:** rational permutation \(R\)-matrix, Yangian/RTT construction, periodic transfer matrix, permutation Hamiltonian  
**Status:** HC-native spectral parameter rejected; exact fixed-layer tensor/Hamiltonian embedding established; parity-even transfer descent finite-certified  
**Certification:** \(65/65\) strict expected-outcome checks passed.

---

# 1. Question

PHYS-ALG-01 established a native microscopic Coxeter/braid backbone:

\[
LR2 = \text{adjacent transposition},
\]

higher block reversals are type-\(A\) parabolic longest elements, and every support channel contains an embedded braid chain.

It also established standard Baxterizability:

\[
\check R_i(u)=I+u\,g_i.
\]

PHYS-ALG-02 asks three harder questions:

1. Does HC itself supply the additive spectral parameter \(u\)?
2. Does HC itself supply a representation suitable for a local \(R\)-matrix?
3. Does a commuting transfer family descend to the actual HC state sector, with a Hamiltonian related to an intrinsic HC adjacency?

The answers are:

\[
\boxed{\text{spectral parameter: no HC-native candidate found}}
\]

\[
\boxed{\text{fixed-layer tensor representation: yes}}
\]

\[
\boxed{\text{LR2 Hamiltonian embedding: yes, exactly}}
\]

\[
\boxed{\text{full HC-restricted transfer family: finite-certified, all-}n\text{ proof still open}}
\]

---

# 2. Spectral-parameter necessity test

The rational Yang-Baxter construction uses a scalar evaluation/spectral parameter.

The key distinction is:

> a spectral parameter varies one fixed \(R\)-operator family; it should not simply rename a different generator.

Several HC quantities were tested.

---

# 3. Support \(\ell\) is not the spectral parameter

The support label:

\[
\ell=2,3,\ldots
\]

is intrinsic.

But changing \(\ell\) changes the underlying positional permutation:

\[
R_{i,\ell}.
\]

Thus:

\[
\boxed{
\ell
\text{ changes the operator rather than parametrizing one fixed operator.}
}
\]

The support hierarchy is therefore not itself a Baxter spectral parameter.

---

# 4. Growth layer \(n\) is not the spectral parameter

The layer:

\[
n
\]

is also intrinsic.

But changing \(n\) changes:

- the atom set;
- the HC state space;
- the number of positional sites;
- the representation dimension.

A Baxter/Yangian spectral parameter normally varies within a fixed operator algebra/representation.

Therefore:

\[
\boxed{
n
\text{ is a model-size/growth variable, not the required spectral coordinate.}
}
\]

---

# 5. Cyclic position is a gauge coordinate

A lifted cyclic representative has start positions:

\[
i\in\mathbb Z_n.
\]

Signed differences are additive after choosing an orientation and origin.

However HC states are quotiented by:

\[
D_n.
\]

So:

- the origin is not intrinsic;
- the orientation is not intrinsic;
- reflection flips signed differences.

Moreover changing the start selects a different local generator rather than changing a scalar multiplying the same generator.

Therefore cyclic position does not supply an HC-native spectral parameter.

---

# 6. Unoriented separation and overlap depth fail additivity

The intrinsic cyclic distance:

\[
\min(d,n-d)
\]

is reflection-invariant but not additive.

Likewise the GROW-08 overlap depth:

\[
r
\]

classifies local commutator geometry.

It is not an additive evaluation coordinate.

Thus neither is suitable for the rational difference-property \(R\)-matrix.

---

# 7. Birth rank is the only serious HC-internal candidate

In the optional rank-refined tower:

\[
V_1\subset V_2\subset\cdots,
\]

each atom has a unique first-appearance rank:

\[
\beta(v).
\]

Differences satisfy:

\[
\beta(a)-\beta(b)
+
\beta(b)-\beta(c)
=
\beta(a)-\beta(c).
\]

So birth rank genuinely supplies an additive coordinate.

But it is attached to **atoms**.

The fundamental LR2 permutation \(R\)-matrix acts naturally on **positional tensor factors**.

If tensor factors are instead indexed by atoms, swapping fixed factors exchanges positions assigned to those atoms; that is a label swap, not the state-dependent LR2 operation of swapping whichever atoms currently occupy adjacent positions.

Therefore:

\[
\boxed{
\beta
\text{ is additive but not aligned with the native LR2 local action.}
}
\]

Using it as an inhomogeneity would require a different representation or a dynamical/state-dependent \(R\)-matrix.

That is new structure.

---

# 8. Spectral-parameter verdict

No currently derived HC observable satisfies all of:

1. intrinsic under the HC quotient;
2. additive/difference-compatible;
3. attached to the correct local tensor sites;
4. varies a fixed \(R\)-operator rather than changing the generator.

Hence:

\[
\boxed{
\textbf{HC does not presently derive the Baxter/Yangian spectral parameter.}
}
\]

The formal parameter \(u\) remains an explicit extension.

This does not prevent a mathematical \(R(u)\) construction.

It prevents identifying \(u\) with physical energy, momentum or rapidity without further derivation.

---

# 9. Fixed-layer tensor realization

Now freeze one HC layer \(n\).

Let:

\[
W_n
=
\mathbb C^{V_n}
\]

be the free complex vector space on the atom set.

Consider:

\[
W_n^{\otimes n}.
\]

Tensor factors represent cyclic positions.

The local dimension is:

\[
\dim W_n=n.
\]

This is not a fixed local dimension across the growth tower, but at fixed \(n\) it is a completely natural permutation representation once a linear field is chosen.

---

# 10. One-each weight sector

Inside:

\[
W_n^{\otimes n}
\]

take the subspace:

\[
\mathcal K_n
\]

spanned by pure tensors in which every atom occurs exactly once.

Its dimension is:

\[
\boxed{
\dim\mathcal K_n=n!.
}
\]

A basis element is simply an ordered atom sequence.

The symmetric group on positions acts by permuting tensor factors and preserves:

\[
\mathcal K_n.
\]

So the positional permutation algebra of HC has a literal local tensor realization.

---

# 11. HC as a dihedral invariant sector

The dihedral group:

\[
D_n
\]

acts by rotation and reflection of tensor positions.

Take the invariant subspace:

\[
\boxed{
\mathcal H_n
=
\mathcal K_n^{D_n}.
}
\]

Because each ordered sequence has a free dihedral orbit of size:

\[
2n,
\]

\[
\dim\mathcal H_n
=
\frac{n!}{2n}
=
\boxed{
\frac{(n-1)!}{2}.
}
\]

That is exactly:

\[
|HC_n|.
\]

An orbit-sum basis of:

\[
\mathcal H_n
\]

is canonically indexed by undirected Hamilton cycles.

Therefore:

\[
\boxed{
\textbf{the linearized HC state space is exactly a dihedral-invariant one-each tensor sector.}
}
\]

This is the strongest representation result of PHYS-ALG-02.

---

# 12. LR2 as a local tensor swap

Let:

\[
P_{i,i+1}
\]

swap neighboring tensor factors.

On an ordered sequence, this exchanges the two atoms occupying adjacent cyclic positions.

That is exactly the lifted LR2 positional move.

So:

\[
\boxed{
P_{i,i+1}
\leftrightarrow
\text{one LR2 move at slot }i.
}
\]

Individual \(P_{i,i+1}\) are representative-dependent under the dihedral quotient.

But their cyclic sum is intrinsic.

---

# 13. Exact LR2 Hamiltonian embedding

Define the periodic permutation operator:

\[
\boxed{
H_{\rm perm}
=
\sum_{i\in\mathbb Z_n}
P_{i,i+1}.
}
\]

Conjugating by any dihedral position symmetry merely permutes the summands.

Hence:

\[
H_{\rm perm}
\]

commutes with the dihedral action and preserves:

\[
\mathcal H_n.
\]

For:

\[
n\ge5,
\]

the \(n\) adjacent swaps from any HC state give \(n\) distinct LR2 neighbors.

Therefore:

\[
\boxed{
H_{\rm perm}\big|_{\mathcal H_n}
=
A_{LR2}.
}
\]

At the half-cycle boundary:

\[
n=4,
\]

each distinct LR2 edge is represented by two positional swaps, so:

\[
\boxed{
H_{\rm perm}\big|_{\mathcal H_4}
=
2A_{LR2}.
}
\]

This is an exact all-\(n\) combinatorial theorem.

---

# 14. Relation to the rational permutation \(R\)-matrix

The standard rational fundamental solution is, up to normalization/convention:

\[
R(u)
=
uI+P.
\]

A periodic monodromy is built from:

\[
R_{0i}(u)
\]

acting on an auxiliary factor and each quantum tensor factor.

The transfer matrix:

\[
t_n(u)
=
\operatorname{tr}_0
R_{0,n-1}(u)\cdots R_{0,0}(u)
\]

forms a commuting family in the standard rational/Yangian construction.

At the regular point, its logarithmic derivative gives the periodic nearest-neighbor permutation Hamiltonian.

Therefore:

\[
\boxed{
A_{LR2}
}
\]

is not merely analogous to a standard integrable-chain Hamiltonian.

After the explicit tensor linearization above, it is exactly the restriction of the periodic permutation Hamiltonian to the HC invariant sector.

This is an **integrable ambient embedding**.

---

# 15. Important qualification: the ordinary transfer matrix is oriented

The HC quotient is undirected.

The standard periodic transfer matrix naturally respects cyclic translation, so it acts cleanly on the oriented cyclic sector:

\[
\mathcal K_n^{C_n},
\]

whose dimension is:

\[
(n-1)!.
\]

But the transfer matrix is not generally invariant under spatial reflection term-by-term.

This is familiar in integrable chains: higher conserved charges can have alternating parity.

The explicit audit found:

- direct dihedral preservation at \(n=4,5\);
- failure of direct preservation by the full transfer polynomial at \(n=6\).

Thus one cannot simply claim:

\[
t_n(u)
\]

acts on undirected HC for all \(u\).

The Hamiltonian does, because it is parity even.

---

# 16. Parity-reflected transfer construction

Let:

\[
\Pi
\]

denote spatial reflection on the oriented cyclic tensor sector.

Define the reflected transfer:

\[
\bar t_n(u)
=
\Pi t_n(u)\Pi.
\]

The audit found, for:

\[
n=4,5,6,
\]

that the coefficient algebras of:

\[
t_n(u)
\]

and:

\[
\bar t_n(v)
\]

mutually commute.

Therefore the product:

\[
\boxed{
Q_n(u)
=
t_n(u)\bar t_n(u)
}
\]

has:

1. commuting polynomial coefficients;
2. reflection invariance;
3. rotation invariance.

Hence:

\[
Q_n(u)
\]

preserves the undirected HC sector in the audited layers.

This is a finite-exact result through \(n=6\).

An all-\(n\) HC-specific proof of this descent has not yet been written.

---

# 17. Transfer-Hamiltonian restriction audit

On the undirected HC sector, the finite exact audit gives:

\[
Q_n(0)=I
\]

for:

\[
n=4,5,6.
\]

And:

\[
n=5,6:
\qquad
\boxed{
Q_n'(0)=2A_{LR2}.
}
\]

At the half-cycle boundary:

\[
n=4:
\qquad
\boxed{
Q_4'(0)=4A_{LR2}.
}
\]

This agrees with the exact all-\(n\) permutation-Hamiltonian theorem.

So the LR2 adjacency is recovered from the logarithmic derivative of a parity-even commuting transfer construction in every explicitly audited layer.

---

# 18. What is certified all-\(n\), and what is not

## Exact all-\(n\)

Once the tensor realization is chosen:

\[
\boxed{
\mathcal H_n
=
\mathcal K_n^{D_n}
}
\]

has exactly the HC dimension.

And:

\[
\boxed{
H_{\rm perm}\big|_{\mathcal H_n}
=
A_{LR2}
\qquad(n\ge5).
}
\]

## Standard external integrability

The ambient rational permutation chain has a Yang-Baxter transfer construction and permutation Hamiltonian.

## Finite HC-specific certification

The parity-even product transfer:

\[
Q_n(u)
\]

was explicitly certified on the HC quotient through:

\[
n=6.
\]

## Still open

An HC-specific all-\(n\) proof that the entire parity-even transfer family descends to:

\[
\mathcal H_n
\]

has not yet been supplied.

Therefore the correct claim is **integrable ambient Hamiltonian embedding**, not yet an all-\(n\) proof that HC itself carries the full restricted Bethe algebra.

---

# 19. Higher LR channels are not the higher charges of this family

If LR3, LR4, etc. were simply higher conserved quantities of the same LR2 transfer family, they would need to commute with:

\[
A_{LR2}.
\]

The audit tested the reference-column commutator exactly for:

\[
6\le n\le10.
\]

Every available pair:

\[
A_{LR2},A_{LRk},
\qquad
k>2,
\]

was noncommuting.

Examples:

\[
n=6:
\quad
[A_{LR2},A_{LR3}]\neq0,
\]

\[
n=10:
\quad
[A_{LR2},A_{LR3}]\neq0,
\]

\[
[A_{LR2},A_{LR4}]\neq0,
\]

\[
[A_{LR2},A_{LR5}]\neq0.
\]

Therefore:

\[
\boxed{
\textbf{higher support adjacencies are not simply the higher commuting charges of the LR2 rational transfer family.}
}
\]

They remain distinct composite reversal observables.

---

# 20. Why the tensor embedding does not solve the graded-tower problem

At fixed \(n\):

- chain length:
  \[
  n;
  \]
- local dimension:
  \[
  n.
  \]

At the next HC layer both change.

The native HC counts obey:

\[
\frac{|HC_{n+1}|}{|HC_n|}
=
n.
\]

So there is no single fixed local space \(V\) such that the whole graded tower is:

\[
V^{\otimes n}.
\]

Thus:

\[
\boxed{
\textbf{PHYS-ALG-02 gives a family of fixed-layer integrable embeddings, not one homogeneous quantum chain across HC growth.}
}
\]

This distinction must remain explicit.

---

# 21. Spectral parameter versus Hamiltonian embedding

The most important conceptual outcome is that two questions separate.

## Can HC identify the Hamiltonian operator?

For LR2, after natural fixed-layer linearization:

\[
\boxed{\textbf{yes}.}
\]

It is the periodic permutation Hamiltonian restricted to the HC sector.

## Can HC identify the spectral parameter?

\[
\boxed{\textbf{no, not yet}.}
\]

The rational \(u\) is still a formal/evaluation coordinate introduced by the Yangian/Baxterization extension.

Therefore the integrable embedding does not by itself supply a physical dispersion relation or scattering rapidity.

---

# 22. Overall verdict

PHYS-ALG-02 substantially upgrades the algebra bridge.

The safe hierarchy is now:

### Native HC

\[
\boxed{
\text{LR2 Coxeter/braid algebra}
}
\]

### Canonical fixed-layer linearization

\[
\boxed{
HC_n
\hookrightarrow
(\mathbb C^{V_n})^{\otimes n}
}
\]

as the dihedral-invariant one-each sector.

### Exact Hamiltonian embedding

\[
\boxed{
A_{LR2}
=
H_{\rm perm}\big|_{\mathcal H_n}
\qquad(n\ge5).
}
\]

### External standard Yang-Baxter extension

\[
\boxed{
R(u)=uI+P.
}
\]

### Finite HC-restricted transfer evidence

A parity-even product transfer gives a commuting family on HC for:

\[
n\le6
\]

in the exact audit.

### Not derived

- HC-native spectral parameter;
- one fixed-local-dimension graded chain;
- physical rapidity/momentum/energy;
- higher LR channels as commuting charges;
- all-\(n\) HC-restricted Bethe algebra.

So:

\[
\boxed{
\textbf{LR2 has an exact fixed-layer integrable Hamiltonian embedding.}
}
\]

But:

\[
\boxed{
\textbf{HC as a whole is not yet a derived integrable quantum theory.}
}
\]

---

PHYS-ALG-02 combines:

- parity-even commuting-product checks;
- logarithmic-derivative/LR2 restriction checks;
- cross-support noncommutation checks.

Combined:

\[
\boxed{
65/65
}
\]

expected-outcome checks passed.

\[
n=4,5,6.
\]

\[
n=10.
\]

The Hamiltonian restriction theorem itself is exact for all:

\[
n\ge5.
\]

# 24. Recommended next step

A further algebra probe is justified, but it should now be very specific:

## EHF-PHYS-ALG-03 — HC-Restricted Bethe Algebra and All-\(n\) Transfer Descent Audit

Targets:

   \[
   Q_n(u)=t_n(u)\Pi t_n(u)\Pi
   \]
   preserves the HC sector;

   \[
   \text{one-each weight}
   \cap
   D_n\text{-invariants};
   \]

4. test whether the exact LR2 adjacency spectrum is reproduced by Bethe-ansatz data in small layers;

The falsification criterion should be:

# EHF-PHYS-ALG-03 — HC-Restricted Bethe Algebra and All-\(n\) Transfer Descent Audit

**Branch:** EHF-PHYS-ALG  
**Inputs:** EHF-HC Foundation v1.0; GROW-01 through GROW-08; PHYS-ALG-01/02  
**External framework:** rational permutation \(R\)-matrix, periodic RTT/Bethe algebra, local conserved-charge hierarchy  
**Status:** all-\(n\) parity-even Bethe-algebra descent established; nontrivial restricted higher charges finite-certified; full Yangian symmetry and Bethe completeness not claimed  
**Certification:** \(74/74\) implementation/formula checks passed.

---

# 1. Question

PHYS-ALG-02 established the exact fixed-layer embedding

\[
\mathcal H_n
=
\mathcal K_n^{D_n}
\subset
(\mathbb C^{V_n})^{\otimes n},
\]

where:

- \(\mathcal K_n\) is the one-each weight sector;
- \(\mathcal H_n\) is the dihedral-invariant sector;
- \(\dim\mathcal H_n=(n-1)!/2=|HC_n|\).

It also proved:

\[
H_{\rm perm}|_{\mathcal H_n}
=
A_{LR2}
\qquad(n\ge5),
\]

with the \(n=4\) half-cycle factor \(2\).

The unresolved question was stronger:

> **Does the commuting transfer/Bethe algebra itself descend to the undirected HC sector for every \(n\), or does only the Hamiltonian survive?**

The answer is:

\[
\boxed{
\textbf{a parity-even commuting Bethe subalgebra descends for every }n.
}
\]

And finite exact audits show that, beginning at \(n=6\), the restricted algebra contains information not generated by \(A_{LR2}\) alone.

---

# 2. Ambient rational permutation chain

At fixed \(n\), let

\[
W_n=\mathbb C^{V_n}.
\]

The standard rational permutation \(R\)-matrix is, up to normalization convention,

\[
R_{0i}(u)=uI+P_{0i}.
\]

The periodic monodromy is

\[
T_0(u)
=
R_{0,n-1}(u)\cdots R_{0,0}(u),
\]

and the transfer matrix is

\[
t_n(u)
=
\operatorname{tr}_0 T_0(u).
\]

RTT/Yang-Baxter theory gives:

\[
\boxed{
[t_n(u),t_n(v)]=0
}
\]

for all \(u,v\).

The one-each sector \(\mathcal K_n\) is preserved because every \(P_{0i}\) only permutes basis labels/tensor entries.

---

# 3. Translation and reflection

Let

\[
\tau
\]

be cyclic translation of the \(n\) quantum tensor sites, and let

\[
\Pi
\]

be spatial reflection.

The homogeneous periodic transfer matrix commutes with translation:

\[
\boxed{
[\tau,t_n(u)]=0.
}
\]

At the regular point:

\[
\boxed{
t_n(0)=\tau
}
\]

for the unnormalized rational permutation convention used here.

Reflection reverses the order of the monodromy:

\[
\boxed{
\bar t_n(u)
=
\Pi t_n(u)\Pi.
}
\]

The transfer matrix itself need not be reflection-even term-by-term.

This is why the ordinary \(t_n(u)\) failed to preserve the undirected HC sector directly at \(n=6\) in PHYS-ALG-02.

---

# 4. Local-charge parity

Near the regular point, the periodic transfer matrix generates commuting local charges through its logarithmic expansion.

Use the standard convention:

\[
I_2=H_{\rm perm}.
\]

Then the local charges alternate spatial parity:

\[
\boxed{
\Pi I_r\Pi
=
(-1)^r I_r.
}
\]

Thus:

- \(I_2\) is parity even;
- \(I_3\) is parity odd;
- \(I_4\) is parity even;
- and so on.

All \(I_r\) commute because they are generated by one commuting transfer family.

This gives a parity-stable periodic Bethe algebra.

---

# 5. Reflection does not destroy commutativity

Because reflection maps the transfer-generated charge algebra into itself, the reflected transfer

\[
\bar t_n(v)
=
\Pi t_n(v)\Pi
\]

is built from the same mutually commuting charge family, with the parity-odd charge signs reversed.

Therefore:

\[
\boxed{
[t_n(u),\bar t_n(v)]
=
0
}
\]

for all spectral parameters.

This promotes the finite \(n=4,5,6\) cross-commutation observation from PHYS-ALG-02 to an all-\(n\) transfer-algebra statement.

---

# 6. All-\(n\) parity-even transfer family

Define:

\[
\boxed{
Q_n(u)
=
t_n(u)\bar t_n(u)
=
t_n(u)\Pi t_n(u)\Pi.
}
\]

Since:

\[
[t_n(u),\bar t_n(v)]=0,
\]

the \(Q_n\) family is commuting:

\[
\boxed{
[Q_n(u),Q_n(v)]=0.
}
\]

Under reflection:

\[
\Pi Q_n(u)\Pi
=
\bar t_n(u)t_n(u)
=
Q_n(u).
\]

And translation commutes with both factors.

Therefore:

\[
\boxed{
[Q_n(u),D_n]=0.
}
\]

Hence \(Q_n(u)\) preserves:

\[
\boxed{
\mathcal H_n
=
\mathcal K_n^{D_n}
}
\]

for every \(n\).

This is the central all-\(n\) theorem of PHYS-ALG-03.

---

# 7. Regular-point Hamiltonian

At:

\[
u=0,
\]

\[
t_n(0)=\tau,
\qquad
\bar t_n(0)=\tau^{-1}.
\]

Therefore:

\[
\boxed{
Q_n(0)=I.
}
\]

The standard regular-point transfer identity gives:

\[
t_n'(0)t_n(0)^{-1}
=
H_{\rm perm}
\]

under the present normalization.

Reflection leaves \(H_{\rm perm}\) invariant.

Consequently:

\[
\boxed{
Q_n'(0)
=
2H_{\rm perm}.
}
\]

PHYS-ALG-02 established the exact HC restriction:

\[
H_{\rm perm}|_{\mathcal H_n}
=
A_{LR2}
\qquad(n\ge5).
\]

Therefore:

\[
\boxed{
Q_n'(0)|_{\mathcal H_n}
=
2A_{LR2},
\qquad n\ge5.
}
\]

At the half-cycle boundary:

\[
\boxed{
Q_4'(0)|_{\mathcal H_4}
=
4A_{LR2}.
}
\]

Thus LR2 is the first Hamiltonian of an all-\(n\) HC-restricted commuting transfer algebra, not merely an isolated ambient integrable Hamiltonian.

---

# 8. The parity-even Bethe algebra

Let:

\[
\mathcal B_n
\]

denote the commuting periodic Bethe algebra generated by the transfer coefficients/local charges.

Define its reflection-even part:

\[
\boxed{
\mathcal B_n^+
=
\{B\in\mathcal B_n:\Pi B\Pi=B\}.
}
\]

Since every element of \(\mathcal B_n\) commutes with translation, every element of:

\[
\mathcal B_n^+
\]

commutes with the full dihedral group.

Therefore there is a well-defined restricted commutative algebra:

\[
\boxed{
\mathcal B_{HC,n}
=
\mathcal B_n^+|_{\mathcal H_n}.
}
\]

This exists for every \(n\).

It contains:

\[
A_{LR2}.
\]

---

# 9. Does anything beyond \(A_{LR2}\) survive?

This was the falsification criterion from PHYS-ALG-02.

If every restricted transfer coefficient were merely a polynomial in:

\[
A_{LR2},
\]

then the HC projection would not carry additional independent commuting information.

The explicit parity-even transfer audit gives:

## \(n=4\)

\[
\dim HC_4=3.
\]

Polynomial algebra generated by LR2:

\[
\dim\mathbb C[A_{LR2}]=2.
\]

Restricted \(Q\)-algebra:

\[
2.
\]

No extra charge detected.

## \(n=5\)

\[
\dim HC_5=12.
\]

\[
\dim\mathbb C[A_{LR2}]=4.
\]

Restricted \(Q\)-algebra:

\[
4.
\]

Again no extra charge detected.

## \(n=6\)

\[
\dim HC_6=60.
\]

\[
\dim\mathbb C[A_{LR2}]=7.
\]

The restricted parity-even \(Q\)-algebra has dimension:

\[
\boxed{8}
\]

in the finite closure audit.

Several transfer coefficients are outside:

\[
\mathbb C[A_{LR2}].
\]

So:

\[
\boxed{
\text{the HC Bethe algebra is genuinely larger than functions of the LR2 Hamiltonian at }n=6.
}
\]

## \(n=7\)

\[
\dim HC_7=360.
\]

The LR2 polynomial algebra has dimension:

\[
23
\]

in the exact/numerical matrix-span audit.

The finite generated \(Q\)-algebra reached dimension:

\[
17
\]

within the deliberately capped multiplication closure, and multiple \(Q\)-coefficients lie outside:

\[
\mathbb C[A_{LR2}].
\]

Thus nontrivial higher commuting information also survives at:

\[
\boxed{n=7.}
\]

The capped dimension \(17\) is a lower bound on the restricted \(Q\)-algebra, not a maximality result.

---

# 10. Joint spectral refinement

A second test asks whether the restricted commuting charges split degeneracies left unresolved by the Hamiltonian.

## \(n=4\)

LR2 has:

\[
2
\]

distinct eigenvalues.

A generic \(Q\)-combination also has:

\[
2.
\]

No refinement.

## \(n=5\)

LR2:

\[
4
\]

distinct eigenvalues.

Generic \(Q\)-combination:

\[
4.
\]

No refinement.

## \(n=6\)

LR2:

\[
7
\]

distinct eigenvalues.

Generic restricted \(Q\)-combination:

\[
\boxed{8}.
\]

So the higher commuting algebra resolves at least one LR2 degeneracy.

## \(n=7\)

LR2:

\[
15
\]

distinct numerical eigenvalues.

A generic restricted \(Q\)-combination gives:

\[
\boxed{17}.
\]

Again the transfer algebra refines the Hamiltonian spectrum.

This is direct evidence that the HC projection retains nontrivial Bethe-algebra structure rather than only the Hamiltonian.

---

# 11. LR2 spectra

The directly diagonalized HC LR2 spectra are:

## \(n=4\)

\[
-1^{(2)},
\qquad
2^{(1)}.
\]

## \(n=5\)

\[
-5^{(1)},
\quad
-1^{(5)},
\quad
1^{(5)},
\quad
5^{(1)}.
\]

## \(n=6\)

Numerically:

\[
-4.6055512755^{(5)},
\]

\[
-2^{(15)},
\]

\[
-1.2360679775^{(9)},
\]

\[
1^{(16)},
\]

\[
2.6055512755^{(5)},
\]

\[
3.2360679775^{(9)},
\]

\[
6^{(1)}.
\]

## \(n=7\)

Fifteen distinct numerical eigenvalues occur, with the full multiplicity ledger supplied separately.

Because:

\[
A_{LR2}
=
H_{\rm perm}|_{\mathcal H_n},
\]

these are exactly the energies of the periodic rational permutation Hamiltonian selected by:

1. the one-each weight condition;
2. cyclic invariance;
3. reflection-even projection.

Thus the LR2 spectrum is an exact restricted Bethe-spectrum problem.

No approximate spectral identification is involved.

---

# 12. What “Bethe prediction” means here

There are two levels.

## Operator-level prediction

The periodic rational permutation chain is an algebraic-Bethe-ansatz integrable system.

Since:

\[
A_{LR2}
=
H_{\rm perm}|_{\mathcal H_n},
\]

every HC LR2 eigenvalue is exactly an ambient permutation-chain energy that survives the HC projection.

This is proved by operator restriction.

## Explicit Bethe-root solution

A complete nested-Bethe-root classification of exactly which ambient solutions survive:

\[
\text{one-each}
\cap
C_n\text{-invariant}
\cap
\Pi=+1
\]

has not been solved in this audit.

So we have an exact Bethe-algebra embedding but not yet a closed-form Bethe-root enumeration of all HC eigenstates.

---

# 13. Bethe algebra is not the same as exact Yangian symmetry

This distinction is important.

The transfer/Bethe algebra can exist for a finite periodic chain even when the usual level-one Yangian generator is not an exact symmetry.

Standard Yangian analysis emphasizes that exact Yangian symmetry is sensitive to boundary conditions.

For periodic boundaries, the level-one generator generally closes only up to boundary terms or requires an origin choice.

HC adds the further cyclic/dihedral projection.

Therefore:

\[
\boxed{
\textbf{do not claim full Yangian symmetry of }HC_n.
}
\]

The certified object is:

\[
\boxed{
\textbf{the restricted commuting Bethe algebra.}
}
\]

This is sufficient for transfer-matrix integrability and does not require exact finite-chain Yangian symmetry.

---

# 14. Higher reversal supports are not the Bethe charges

The tempting identification:

\[
LR3,LR4,\ldots
\leftrightarrow
I_3,I_4,\ldots
\]

fails.

PHYS-ALG-02 found:

\[
[A_{LR2},A_{LR3}]\neq0
\]

through every available audited layer.

Likewise:

\[
[A_{LR2},A_{LR4}]\neq0,
\]

and:

\[
[A_{LR2},A_{LR5}]\neq0
\]

where available.

But Bethe conserved charges commute with the Hamiltonian.

Therefore:

\[
\boxed{
\textbf{higher }LR_\ell\textbf{ adjacencies are not the higher charges of the LR2 Bethe hierarchy.}
}
\]

They remain distinct composite reversal operators.

---

# 15. What survives the HC projection?

The qualitative picture is now:

\[
\text{ambient periodic Bethe algebra}
\]

splits into parity-even and parity-odd pieces.

The undirected HC projection keeps the reflection-even portion:

\[
\boxed{
\mathcal B_n
\longrightarrow
\mathcal B_n^+
\longrightarrow
\mathcal B_{HC,n}.
}
\]

Odd local charges do not act within the undirected sector individually.

Even combinations survive.

This resembles the familiar reduction of charge towers under reflection-symmetric boundary/projection constraints, but here the projection is specifically the HC dihedral quotient.

---

# 16. Does this justify “integrable HC sector”?

The answer is now stronger than PHYS-ALG-02 but should remain qualified.

We have proved:

1. an all-\(n\) commuting HC-restricted transfer algebra exists;
2. it contains the LR2 Hamiltonian;
3. at \(n=6,7\), it contains independent information beyond the Hamiltonian;
4. it refines LR2 spectral degeneracies.

That is enough to justify:

\[
\boxed{
\textbf{HC contains a nontrivial integrable LR2 sector at fixed }n
}
\]

in the transfer/Bethe-algebra sense.

What is not proved is that, for every large \(n\), the restricted algebra has a maximal/extensive number of algebraically independent charges after the dihedral projection.

Therefore the strongest wording is:

\[
\boxed{
\textbf{all-\(n\) HC-restricted Bethe algebra with finite-certified nontrivial higher charges.}
}
\]

rather than:

\[
\boxed{
\textbf{complete HC Bethe integrability theorem for all }n.
}
\]

---

# 17. Growth remains separate

Nothing here solves the graded HC growth problem.

For each fixed \(n\), the local tensor space is:

\[
W_n=\mathbb C^{V_n},
\qquad
\dim W_n=n.
\]

At the next layer:

\[
W_{n+1}
\]

has a different dimension.

Thus PHYS-ALG-03 remains a family of fixed-layer integrable sectors:

\[
\mathcal H_3,\mathcal H_4,\mathcal H_5,\ldots
\]

not one homogeneous fixed-local-space quantum chain evolving under HC growth.

The graded-growth operator from GROW-02 remains a separate object.

---

# 18. Main verdict

The principal falsification question was:

> Does only the LR2 Hamiltonian survive the HC dihedral projection?

The answer is:

\[
\boxed{
\textbf{No.}
}
\]

A parity-even commuting Bethe subalgebra descends for every \(n\), and explicit higher commuting information survives at least at:

\[
n=6,7.
\]

Therefore PHYS-ALG-02's “integrable ambient embedding” can be upgraded to:

\[
\boxed{
\textbf{an all-\(n\) HC-restricted Bethe algebra containing the LR2 Hamiltonian.}
}
\]

But:

\[
\boxed{
\textbf{full Yangian symmetry is not certified,}
}
\]

\[
\boxed{
\textbf{higher LR supports are not the Bethe charges,}
}
\]

and:

\[
\boxed{
\textbf{complete Bethe-root characterization/maximality remains open.}
}
\]

---

# 19. Certification

The implementation audit contains:

- exact finite transfer/reflection coefficient checks at \(n=4,5,6,7\);
- restricted commuting-algebra span tests;
- polynomial-in-\(LR2\) rejection tests;
- joint spectral refinement;
- direct LR2 spectra through \(n=7\);
- broad all-\(n\) formula sanity checks for the HC invariant-sector dimension and Hamiltonian scaling.

Combined implementation/formula certification:

\[
\boxed{
74/74
}
\]

checks passed.

The all-\(n\) transfer descent itself is a theorem-level consequence of the standard periodic Bethe algebra plus reflection parity, not an extrapolation from those 74 finite checks.

---

# 20. Recommended next step

The next question should not be another generic Yang-Baxter analogy.

The strongest unresolved issue is now the **projected spectrum**.

A focused follow-up would be:

## EHF-PHYS-ALG-04 — HC Bethe Selection Rules and Restricted Spectrum Audit

Targets:

1. characterize the one-each weight sector in nested Bethe/representation language;
2. impose cyclic momentum zero:
   \[
   \tau=1;
   \]
3. impose reflection parity:
   \[
   \Pi=+1;
   \]
4. determine which Bethe states survive as HC states;

6. determine whether the number of surviving independent even charges grows with \(n\);
7. only after that decide whether a complete fixed-layer HC integrability theorem is justified.

This is now a sharply defined representation/spectral problem, not a search for analogy.

# EHF-PHYS-ALG-04 — HC Bethe Selection Rules and Restricted Spectrum Audit

**Branch:** EHF-PHYS-ALG  
**Inputs:** EHF-HC Foundation v1.0; GROW-01 through GROW-08; PHYS-ALG-01 through 03  
**External framework:** Schur-Weyl duality, nested rational \(GL(n)\)/\(SU(n)\) Bethe ansatz, Specht modules  
**Status:** exact all-\(n\) selection/spectrum formula established; complete \(n=4\)–\(7\) spectra reconstructed representation-theoretically; \(n=8\) prediction moment-validated  
**Certification:** \(\boxed{804/804}\) accumulated finite/symbolic checks passed.

---

# 1. Question

PHYS-ALG-03 established an all-\(n\) HC-restricted commuting Bethe algebra and identified:

\[
A_{LR2}
\]

as its Hamiltonian.

The next problem was:

> **Which ambient Bethe/representation states actually survive the HC quotient, and can the HC LR2 spectrum be recovered from those selection rules rather than by diagonalizing the HC graph?**

The answer is:

\[
\boxed{\textbf{yes, representation-theoretically.}}
\]

The key is to combine:

1. the one-each weight constraint;
2. Schur-Weyl duality;
3. the cyclic/reflection \(D_n\) projection;
4. the cyclic adjacent-transposition Hamiltonian.

This yields an exact all-\(n\) block-spectrum formula.

---

# 2. The one-each sector

At fixed \(n\), let:

\[
W_n=\mathbb C^n.
\]

The ambient chain is:

\[
W_n^{\otimes n}.
\]

The HC tensor realization uses the one-each weight sector:

\[
\mathcal K_n
=
\operatorname{span}
\left\{
e_{v_1}\otimes\cdots\otimes e_{v_n}:
\{v_1,\ldots,v_n\}=V_n
\right\}.
\]

Every basis word is a permutation of the \(n\) atom labels.

Therefore:

\[
\boxed{
\mathcal K_n
\cong
\mathbb C[S_n]
}
\]

as a positional \(S_n\)-module.

Its dimension is:

\[
n!.
\]

The \(GL(n)\) weight is:

\[
\boxed{
(1,1,\ldots,1).
}
\]

For \(SU(n)\) this is zero Cartan weight after removing the common trace component.

---

# 3. Schur-Weyl decomposition

Schur-Weyl duality gives:

\[
W_n^{\otimes n}
\cong
\bigoplus_{\lambda\vdash n}
V_\lambda^{GL(n)}
\otimes
S^\lambda.
\]

Restrict to the one-each weight.

Then:

\[
\boxed{
\mathcal K_n
\cong
\bigoplus_{\lambda\vdash n}
V_\lambda[1^n]
\otimes
S^\lambda.
}
\]

The dimension of the one-each weight space in \(V_\lambda\) is the Kostka number:

\[
K_{\lambda,(1^n)}.
\]

But content \((1^n)\) means every entry is used once, so semistandard tableaux are standard tableaux.

Hence:

\[
\boxed{
K_{\lambda,(1^n)}
=
f^\lambda
=
\dim S^\lambda.
}
\]

Therefore:

\[
\dim\mathcal K_n
=
\sum_{\lambda\vdash n}
(f^\lambda)^2
=
n!,
\]

as required.

---

# 4. Correction to the naïve Bethe root-count rule

A first reading of the one-each weight suggests the nested counts:

\[
M_a=n-a.
\]

That is **not** the correct finite-root description of the whole HC sector.

Standard on-shell nested Bethe vectors are highest-weight/singular vectors.

HC contains zero-weight descendants from many different highest-weight partitions:

\[
\lambda\vdash n.
\]

So we need a partition-resolved rule.

---

# 5. Highest-weight finite-root counts

Pad:

\[
\lambda=(\lambda_1,\ldots,\lambda_n)
\]

with zeros.

For a fundamental length-\(n\) rational \(GL(n)\) chain, the weight of a highest-weight Bethe vector with nesting counts \(M_a\) is:

\[
(n-M_1,\,
M_1-M_2,\,
\ldots,\,
M_{n-2}-M_{n-1},\,
M_{n-1}).
\]

Set this equal to the highest weight \(\lambda\).

Then:

\[
\boxed{
M_a^{HW}
=
n-\sum_{j=1}^{a}\lambda_j,
\qquad
1\le a\le n-1.
}
\]

These are the finite-root nesting numbers of the highest-weight Bethe sector.

---

# 6. Descending to the HC one-each weight

The HC tensor state is not generally the highest-weight vector.

It lies at weight:

\[
(1,1,\ldots,1).
\]

The required cumulative simple-root lowerings are:

\[
\boxed{
L_a^{desc}
=
\sum_{j=1}^{a}\lambda_j-a.
}
\]

Therefore:

\[
\boxed{
M_a^{HW}
+
L_a^{desc}
=
n-a.
}
\]

This explains the original \(n-a\) count correctly.

It is the **total lowering count to zero weight**, not the finite-root count of every highest-weight Bethe solution.

In isotropic Bethe language, symmetry descendants can be represented by roots sent to infinity; however the spectrum reconstruction below does not require that identification.

---

# 7. Example: \(n=6\)

Several highest-weight sectors survive the HC projection.

## \(\lambda=(6)\)

Finite Bethe roots:

\[
M^{HW}=(0,0,0,0,0).
\]

Descendant lowerings:

\[
L^{desc}=(5,4,3,2,1).
\]

## \(\lambda=(4,2)\)

\[
M^{HW}=(2,0,0,0,0),
\]

\[
L^{desc}=(3,4,3,2,1).
\]

## \(\lambda=(3,2,1)\)

\[
M^{HW}=(3,1,0,0,0),
\]

\[
L^{desc}=(2,3,3,2,1).
\]

## \(\lambda=(2,2,2)\)

\[
M^{HW}=(4,2,0,0,0),
\]

\[
L^{desc}=(1,2,3,2,1).
\]

Every case satisfies:

\[
M^{HW}+L^{desc}
=
(5,4,3,2,1).
\]

So one HC weight contains many distinct Bethe highest-weight sectors.

---

# 8. Spatial HC selection

The one-each condition handles the **internal/global \(GL(n)\) weight**.

HC imposes additional spatial conditions.

An oriented cyclic state satisfies:

\[
\boxed{
\tau=1,
}
\]

where \(\tau\) is one-site translation.

In Bethe language this is total momentum:

\[
\boxed{
P=0\pmod{2\pi}.
}
\]

Undirected HC additionally imposes:

\[
\boxed{
\Pi=+1
}
\]

under spatial reflection.

Together:

\[
\boxed{
D_n\text{-fixed}.
}
\]

---

# 9. Exact partition selection rule

On the Specht module \(S^\lambda\), define:

\[
m_\lambda^{D_n}
=
\dim(S^\lambda)^{D_n}.
\]

By the finite-group projection formula:

\[
\boxed{
m_\lambda^{D_n}
=
\frac1{2n}
\sum_{d\in D_n}
\chi_\lambda(d).
}
\]

Therefore:

\[
\boxed{
\lambda
\text{ contributes to HC}
\iff
m_\lambda^{D_n}>0.
}
\]

This is the exact all-\(n\) spatial Bethe/representation selection rule.

---

# 10. HC representation decomposition

Combining weight and spatial selection:

\[
\boxed{
\mathcal H_n^{HC}
\cong
\bigoplus_{\lambda\vdash n}
V_\lambda[1^n]
\otimes
(S^\lambda)^{D_n}.
}
\]

Its dimension is therefore:

\[
\boxed{
|HC_n|
=
\sum_{\lambda\vdash n}
f^\lambda m_\lambda^{D_n}.
}
\]

The audit verified this decomposition through:

\[
n=8.
\]

It reproduces:

\[
\frac{(n-1)!}{2}
\]

exactly.

---

# 11. The LR2 group-algebra element

Define:

\[
\boxed{
X_n
=
\sum_{i\in\mathbb Z_n}
(i,i+1)
}
\]

in the group algebra:

\[
\mathbb C[S_n].
\]

This is the lifted periodic permutation Hamiltonian.

Because cyclic rotation and reflection permute the summands:

\[
[X_n,D_n]=0.
\]

Therefore \(X_n\) preserves every:

\[
(S^\lambda)^{D_n}.
\]

---

# 12. All-\(n\) LR2 spectrum formula

For:

\[
n\ge5,
\]

PHYS-ALG-02 established:

\[
A_{LR2}
=
H_{\rm perm}|_{\mathcal H_n}.
\]

Schur-Weyl says the Hamiltonian acts trivially on the \(GL(n)\) factor and only on the positional Specht factor.

Hence:

\[
\boxed{
A_{LR2}|_\lambda
=
I_{V_\lambda[1^n]}
\otimes
\left[
\rho_\lambda(X_n)
\big|_{(S^\lambda)^{D_n}}
\right].
}
\]

Consequently:

\[
\boxed{
\operatorname{Spec}(A_{LR2})
=
\biguplus_{\lambda\vdash n}
f^\lambda\,
\operatorname{Spec}
\left(
\rho_\lambda(X_n)
|_{(S^\lambda)^{D_n}}
\right).
}
\]

Here multiplication by \(f^\lambda\) means every eigenvalue of the \(D_n\)-fixed Specht block occurs globally with multiplicity multiplied by:

\[
f^\lambda.
\]

At:

\[
n=4,
\]

the half-cycle positional double representation requires the known factor:

\[
A_{LR2}
=
\frac12 X_4|_{HC}.
\]

This is an exact all-\(n\) representation-spectrum theorem.

---

# 13. Why the multiplicities look the way they do

The factor:

\[
f^\lambda
\]

is not accidental.

A Bethe highest-weight eigenvector in spatial sector \(\lambda\) generates a full \(GL(n)\) multiplet.

HC keeps the weight:

\[
(1^n).
\]

That weight occurs with multiplicity:

\[
f^\lambda.
\]

Thus every surviving spatial Bethe energy is repeated precisely by the number of one-each descendants in the corresponding global-symmetry multiplet.

This explains multiplicities such as:

\[
5,\quad9,\quad16,\quad21,\quad35
\]

without fitting them to the HC graph.

---

# 14. Exact \(n=6\) block reconstruction

The surviving \(D_6\)-fixed partitions and exact block polynomials are:

## \((6)\)

\[
x-6.
\]

Multiplicity factor:

\[
1.
\]

## \((4,2)\)

\[
\boxed{
x^2-2x-4.
}
\]

Roots:

\[
1\pm\sqrt5.
\]

Each occurs with multiplicity:

\[
9.
\]

## \((3,2,1)\)

\[
x-1.
\]

Multiplicity:

\[
16.
\]

## \((3,1,1,1)\)

\[
x+2.
\]

Multiplicity:

\[
10.
\]

## \((2,2,2)\)

\[
\boxed{
x^2+2x-12.
}
\]

Roots:

\[
-1\pm\sqrt{13}.
\]

Each occurs with multiplicity:

\[
5.
\]

## \((2,1,1,1,1)\)

\[
x+2.
\]

Additional multiplicity:

\[
5.
\]

Therefore \(-2\) has total multiplicity:

\[
10+5=15.
\]

The complete reconstructed spectrum is exactly the PHYS-ALG-03 direct HC spectrum.

---

# 15. Exact \(n=7\) cubic blocks

At \(n=7\), two genuinely nontrivial \(D_7\)-fixed Specht blocks have cubic characteristic polynomials.

For:

\[
\lambda=(3,2,2),
\qquad
f^\lambda=21,
\]

\[
\boxed{
x^3+x^2-17x-25.
}
\]

Its three roots each carry global multiplicity:

\[
21.
\]

For:

\[
\lambda=(3,1,1,1,1),
\qquad
f^\lambda=15,
\]

\[
\boxed{
x^3+7x^2+7x-7.
}
\]

Its roots each carry multiplicity:

\[
15.
\]

All remaining blocks are linear, quadratic, or completely factored cubics.

Together they reproduce all:

\[
360
\]

HC states and all:

\[
15
\]

distinct LR2 eigenvalues.

---

# 16. Complete \(n=4\)–\(7\) validation

The representation calculation was performed without using the HC adjacency spectrum as input.

Then its output was compared against the frozen PHYS-ALG-03 direct graph spectra.

The match is complete:

\[
\boxed{
n=4:\ 3/3\text{ states},
}
\]

\[
\boxed{
n=5:\ 12/12,
}
\]

\[
\boxed{
n=6:\ 60/60,
}
\]

\[
\boxed{
n=7:\ 360/360.
}
\]

Every eigenvalue and multiplicity agrees.

---

# 17. \(n=8\) out-of-sample prediction

The same representation theorem was applied at:

\[
n=8
\]

without diagonalizing the \(2520\times2520\) HC adjacency matrix.

It predicts:

\[
\boxed{
2520
}
\]

total states and:

\[
\boxed{
41
}
\]

distinct LR2 eigenvalues.

The full predicted spectrum is supplied in the ledger.

---

# 18. \(n=8\) sparse moment validation

As a blind validation, the direct HC LR2 sparse adjacency was used only to compute trace moments.

The representation prediction was then compared against:

\[
\operatorname{tr}(A^k)
\]

for:

\[
0\le k\le8.
\]

All nine moments match exactly after numerical root evaluation and integer rounding.

Examples:

\[
\operatorname{tr}A^2
=
20160,
\]

\[
\operatorname{tr}A^4
=
403200,
\]

\[
\operatorname{tr}A^6
=
11249280,
\]

\[
\operatorname{tr}A^8
=
373484160.
\]

Also:

\[
\operatorname{tr}A^7
=
40320,
\]

so the \(n=8\) LR2 graph has odd closed walks at length seven despite its lower odd moments vanishing.

The \(n=8\) result is therefore independently supported without a full direct spectrum calculation.

---

# 19. What has been solved

The fixed-layer LR2 spectral problem now has an exact representation-theoretic reduction:

\[
\boxed{
\text{factorial HC graph}
\longrightarrow
\text{small }D_n\text{-fixed Specht blocks}.
}
\]

This is a substantial computational and conceptual reduction.

Rather than diagonalizing a matrix of size:

\[
\frac{(n-1)!}{2},
\]

one works partition-by-partition with:

\[
m_\lambda^{D_n}
\]

dimensional compressed blocks inside \(S^\lambda\).

For the audited small layers these fixed dimensions are tiny.

---

# 20. What has not been solved

The audit did **not** explicitly solve the nested Bethe equations root-by-root for every surviving partition.

Thus we have not yet produced, for every HC eigenvalue:

- a complete list of finite rapidities;
- all infinity-root descendant data;
- a root-level parity construction.

However this is no longer necessary to establish the spectrum.

The representation theorem already gives the exact selected Bethe sectors and their Hamiltonian blocks.

A future root-level calculation would be an independent verification and could provide scattering/rapidity interpretation, not the basic spectrum itself.

---

# 21. Bethe meaning of the representation theorem

The clean dictionary is now:

### Highest-weight Bethe label

\[
\lambda\vdash n.
\]

### Finite nesting counts

\[
M_a^{HW}
=
n-\sum_{j\le a}\lambda_j.
\]

### HC internal-weight selection

Take the:

\[
(1^n)
\]

descendant subspace of dimension:

\[
f^\lambda.
\]

### HC spatial selection

Take:

\[
(S^\lambda)^{D_n}.
\]

### HC Hamiltonian

Diagonalize:

\[
\rho_\lambda(X_n)
\]

on that \(D_n\)-fixed space.

This is the exact HC Bethe/representation selection algorithm.

---

# 22. Main verdict

The stated target was to reproduce the HC LR2 spectrum and multiplicities from Bethe/representation data rather than direct graph diagonalization.

That target passes:

\[
\boxed{
\textbf{PASS.}
}
\]

More precisely:

\[
\boxed{
\textbf{HC Bethe selection rules are exactly the one-each weight condition plus }D_n\textbf{-fixed spatial selection.}
}
\]

And:

\[
\boxed{
\textbf{the complete LR2 spectrum is the union of the resulting }D_n\textbf{-fixed Specht block spectra.}
}
\]

The naïve single-sector rule:

\[
M_a=n-a
\]

is rejected as a finite-root description of the entire HC space.

The correct partition-resolved relation is:

\[
\boxed{
M_a^{HW}
=
n-\sum_{j\le a}\lambda_j,
\qquad
L_a^{desc}
=
\sum_{j\le a}\lambda_j-a.
}
\]

---

# 23. Certification

The audit contains:

- Schur/Specht \(D_n\)-selection checks through \(n=8\);
- representation dimensions and spectral reconstruction;
- exact symbolic Specht block characteristic polynomials through \(n=7\);
- partition-resolved nested-weight bookkeeping;
- complete \(n=4\)–\(7\) direct-spectrum reproduction;
- \(n=8\) out-of-sample prediction;
- \(n=8\) trace-moment validation through order eight.

Combined:

\[
\boxed{
804/804
}
\]

checks passed.

---

# 24. Recommended next step

PHYS-ALG-04 has solved the selection-rule problem more cleanly than a brute-force Bethe-root calculation would have.

The next highest-value question is now:

## EHF-PHYS-ALG-05 — Asymptotic HC Bethe/Specht Sector Law

Targets:

1. derive closed formulas/asymptotics for:
   \[
   m_\lambda^{D_n};
   \]
2. determine which partition shapes dominate the HC Hilbert-space dimension at large \(n\);
3. study the asymptotic distribution of LR2 energies from the selected Specht blocks;
4. compare the representation-derived spectral law with direct moment/cycle counts;
5. determine whether a thermodynamic Bethe/root-density description exists despite the local dimension growing with \(n\);
6. ask whether the selected partition ensemble has a natural limit shape.

An alternative, more explicitly Bethe-theoretic probe would solve the nested roots for the small surviving \(n=5,6\) partitions and verify the block energies one-by-one.

But that is now a verification problem, whereas the representation formula already solves the finite-layer spectrum.


---

# EHF-PHYS-ALG-05 — Asymptotic HC Bethe/Specht Sector Law

**Branch:** EHF-PHYS-ALG  
**Inputs:** EHF-HC Foundation v1.0; GROW-01 through GROW-08; PHYS-ALG-01 through 04  
**External asymptotic framework:** Plancherel measure, character orthogonality, Logan–Shepp / Vershik–Kerov limit shape  
**Status:** exact HC-to-Plancherel asymptotic equivalence established; typical multiplicity law established; Gaussian LR2 bulk spectral law established  
**Certification:** \(\boxed{151/151}\) finite/formula checks passed.

---

# 1. Purpose

PHYS-ALG-04 gave the exact fixed-layer decomposition

\[
\mathcal H_n^{HC}
\cong
\bigoplus_{\lambda\vdash n}
V_\lambda[1^n]\otimes(S^\lambda)^{D_n},
\]

with HC dimension contribution

\[
f^\lambda m_\lambda^{D_n},
\]

where

\[
f^\lambda=\dim S^\lambda
\]

and

\[
m_\lambda^{D_n}=\dim(S^\lambda)^{D_n}.
\]

PHYS-ALG-05 asks:

1. Which partition shapes dominate this sum as \(n\to\infty\)?
2. What is the typical size of \(m_\lambda^{D_n}\)?
3. Does the selected ensemble have a limit shape?
4. What is the large-\(n\) LR2 spectral distribution?
5. Does this produce an ordinary thermodynamic Bethe limit?

The first four questions have clean answers.

---

# 2. HC shape measure

Use Hilbert-space contribution as the natural probability measure on partitions:

\[
\boxed{
\mathbb P_{HC,n}(\lambda)
=
\frac{f^\lambda m_\lambda^{D_n}}
{n!/(2n)}.
}
\]

Compare with Plancherel measure:

\[
\boxed{
\mathbb P_{Pl,n}(\lambda)
=
\frac{(f^\lambda)^2}{n!}.
}
\]

From the exact character formula:

\[
m_\lambda^{D_n}
=
\frac1{2n}
\sum_{d\in D_n}\chi_\lambda(d),
\]

we obtain:

\[
\mathbb P_{HC,n}(\lambda)
=
\frac{f^\lambda}{n!}
\sum_{d\in D_n}\chi_\lambda(d).
\]

Separate the identity term:

\[
\chi_\lambda(e)=f^\lambda.
\]

Define:

\[
\boxed{
\delta_n(\lambda)
=
\sum_{\substack{d\in D_n\\d\neq e}}
\frac{\chi_\lambda(d)}{f^\lambda}.
}
\]

Then exactly:

\[
\boxed{
\mathbb P_{HC,n}(\lambda)
=
\mathbb P_{Pl,n}(\lambda)
\left[1+\delta_n(\lambda)\right].
}
\]

So the HC partition ensemble is an explicit dihedral-character tilt of Plancherel measure.

---

# 3. Mean of the tilt

Under Plancherel:

\[
\mathbb E_{Pl}\delta_n
=
\frac1{n!}
\sum_{d\neq e}
\sum_{\lambda}
f^\lambda\chi_\lambda(d).
\]

But:

\[
\sum_\lambda f^\lambda\chi_\lambda
\]

is the character of the regular representation.

It vanishes at every:

\[
d\neq e.
\]

Therefore:

\[
\boxed{
\mathbb E_{Pl}\delta_n=0.
}
\]

This is required because both probability measures normalize to one.

---

# 4. Exact second-moment law

Column orthogonality gives:

\[
\sum_{\lambda\vdash n}
\chi_\lambda(g)\chi_\lambda(h)
=
\begin{cases}
|C_{S_n}(g)|,&g\sim h,\\
0,&g\not\sim h.
\end{cases}
\]

Let:

\[
C
\]

run over the \(S_n\) conjugacy types represented among nonidentity elements of \(D_n\).

Write:

\[
N_C
=
|\{d\in D_n\setminus\{e\}:d\in C\}|
\]

and:

\[
z_C
=
|C_{S_n}(d)|,
\qquad d\in C.
\]

Then:

\[
\boxed{
\mathbb E_{Pl}[\delta_n^2]
=
\frac1{n!}
\sum_C
N_C^2 z_C.
}
\]

This is an exact all-\(n\) formula.

No asymptotic character estimate is needed.

---

# 5. Total-variation theorem

Because:

\[
P_{HC}-P_{Pl}
=
P_{Pl}\delta,
\]

the total-variation distance is exactly:

\[
\boxed{
d_{TV}(P_{HC,n},P_{Pl,n})
=
\frac12
\mathbb E_{Pl}|\delta_n|.
}
\]

Cauchy–Schwarz gives:

\[
\boxed{
d_{TV}
\le
\frac12
\left[
\frac1{n!}
\sum_C N_C^2z_C
\right]^{1/2}.
}
\]

This is the quantitative asymptotic bridge.

---

# 6. Why the bound vanishes

The dihedral group has only:

\[
2n
\]

elements.

Its nonidentity cycle types are very restricted.

## Rotations

A rotation by \(k\) has:

\[
g=\gcd(n,k)
\]

cycles of length:

\[
L=n/g.
\]

Its centralizer is:

\[
\boxed{
z=L^g g!.
}
\]

## Reflections, odd \(n\)

Every reflection has cycle type:

\[
1^1\,2^{(n-1)/2},
\]

so:

\[
\boxed{
z
=
2^{(n-1)/2}
\left(\frac{n-1}{2}\right)!.
}
\]

## Reflections, even \(n\)

There are two types:

\[
2^{n/2}
\]

and:

\[
1^2\,2^{(n-2)/2}.
\]

Their centralizers are factorially smaller than \(n!\).

A crude common bound is:

\[
\boxed{
z_C
\le
2^{\lceil n/2\rceil}
\lceil n/2\rceil!
}
\]

for every nonidentity dihedral cycle type.

Hence:

\[
\mathbb E\delta_n^2
\le
(2n)^2
\frac{
2^{\lceil n/2\rceil}\lceil n/2\rceil!
}{n!}
\to0.
\]

Therefore:

\[
\boxed{
d_{TV}(P_{HC,n},P_{Pl,n})
\longrightarrow0.
}
\]

The exact numerical bound is substantially sharper than this crude estimate.

---

# 7. Numerical size of the bound

The exact character-orthogonality RMS bound gives approximately:

\[
n=10:
\quad
d_{TV}\le0.10446,
\]

\[
n=20:
\quad
d_{TV}\le2.24\times10^{-4},
\]

\[
n=30:
\quad
d_{TV}\le1.05\times10^{-7},
\]

\[
n=50:
\quad
d_{TV}\le1.73\times10^{-15}.
\]

Thus the asymptotic Plancherel regime arrives extremely quickly at the level of this bound.

The small-\(n\) exact HC-vs-Plancherel distances from the PHYS-ALG-04 census are:

\[
n=4:\ 0.79167,
\]

\[
n=5:\ 0.56667,
\]

\[
n=6:\ 0.41111,
\]

\[
n=7:\ 0.25794,
\]

\[
n=8:\ 0.18011.
\]

The finite data already move in the predicted direction.

---

# 8. Typical dihedral multiplicity law

From:

\[
2n\,\frac{m_\lambda^{D_n}}{f^\lambda}
=
1+\delta_n(\lambda),
\]

and:

\[
\delta_n\to0
\]

in Plancherel \(L^2\), we obtain:

\[
\boxed{
m_\lambda^{D_n}
=
\frac{f^\lambda}{2n}
\left[1+o_{\mathbb P}(1)\right]
}
\]

for Plancherel-typical partitions.

Thus the dihedral projection asymptotically does something remarkably simple:

\[
\boxed{
\text{it keeps about a }1/(2n)\text{ fraction of each typical Specht module.}
}
\]

This is not true uniformly over all partitions.

It is a high-probability statement under the dominant partition measure.

---

# 9. Almost all Plancherel mass survives

If:

\[
m_\lambda^{D_n}=0,
\]

then:

\[
1+\delta_n(\lambda)=0,
\]

so:

\[
\delta_n(\lambda)^2=1.
\]

Therefore:

\[
\boxed{
P_{Pl,n}(m_\lambda^{D_n}=0)
\le
\mathbb E_{Pl}\delta_n^2
\to0.
}
\]

Hence the HC projection does not asymptotically select a rare family of exotic Young shapes.

Instead:

\[
\boxed{
\textbf{almost all Plancherel-typical representation mass survives.}
}
\]

The projection mainly reduces multiplicities.

---

# 10. Dominant HC Young shape

Since:

\[
d_{TV}(P_{HC,n},P_{Pl,n})\to0,
\]

every Plancherel high-probability shape theorem transfers immediately to HC.

Therefore HC-dominant Young diagrams have the Logan–Shepp / Vershik–Kerov limit shape.

Under the standard \(\sqrt n\) scaling:

\[
\boxed{
\frac{\lambda_1}{\sqrt n}
\to2
}
\]

in probability, and by conjugation symmetry:

\[
\boxed{
\frac{\lambda_1'}{\sqrt n}
\to2.
}
\]

So dominant HC Bethe/Specht sectors have both row and column scales of order:

\[
\sqrt n,
\]

rather than a finite number of macroscopic rows.

This is the exact answer to the “which partition shapes dominate?” question.

---

# 11. Bethe interpretation of the limit shape

PHYS-ALG-04 associated each partition:

\[
\lambda
\]

with a highest-weight nested-Bethe sector.

Thus the HC thermodynamic representation ensemble is not dominated by:

- the fully symmetric representation;
- the sign representation;
- a fixed rectangular shape;
- a finite-row highest weight.

It is dominated by the same balanced:

\[
\boxed{
\text{Plancherel / VKLS-scale partitions}
}
\]

that dominate the regular representation of \(S_n\).

So the HC dihedral quotient becomes asymptotically negligible at the **shape-distribution** level even though it is essential to the exact finite state definition.

---

# 12. LR2 spectral law: exact trace identity

Now turn to the LR2 Hamiltonian.

Let:

\[
X_n
=
\sum_{i\in\mathbb Z_n}s_i
\]

in the positional group algebra.

The HC space is the \(D_n\)-invariant part of the regular positional representation.

Let:

\[
[d]X_n^k
\]

denote the coefficient of permutation \(d\) in \(X_n^k\).

Then the normalized HC trace is:

\[
\boxed{
\frac1{|HC_n|}
\operatorname{Tr}_{HC}(A_{LR2}^k)
=
\sum_{d\in D_n}
[d]X_n^k.
}
\]

This formula makes the quotient correction explicit.

The identity term:

\[
[e]X_n^k
\]

is the ordinary regular-representation return count.

The other terms are exactly the extra closures caused by identifying states under the dihedral quotient.

---

# 13. Fixed moments forget the quotient

A word of length \(k\) in transpositions moves at most:

\[
2k
\]

points.

Every nonidentity dihedral permutation moves at least:

\[
n-2
\]

points.

Therefore, when:

\[
n>2k+2,
\]

no length-\(k\) transposition word can equal a nonidentity element of \(D_n\).

Hence:

\[
\boxed{
\frac1{|HC_n|}
\operatorname{Tr}(A_{LR2}^k)
=
[e]X_n^k
}
\]

for every fixed \(k\) once \(n\) is sufficiently large.

Thus the finite dihedral quotient has no effect on fixed-order bulk spectral moments asymptotically.

---

# 14. Odd moments

Every \(s_i\) is an odd permutation.

A product of an odd number of transpositions cannot be the identity.

Therefore:

\[
\boxed{
[e]X_n^{2m+1}=0.
}
\]

And consequently the limiting HC spectral law is symmetric.

---

# 15. Leading even return words

Consider a word of length:

\[
2m
\]

representing the identity.

A simple generator occurring exactly once would imply that this simple reflection lies in the standard parabolic subgroup generated by all the other simple reflections, which is impossible.

Therefore every used generator appears at least twice.

So at most:

\[
m
\]

distinct generator labels occur.

The largest power of \(n\) comes from words using exactly:

\[
m
\]

generators.

To leading order those \(m\) generators are pairwise nonadjacent around the cyclic Coxeter graph.

Pairwise nonadjacent generators commute.

Since each occurs exactly twice, every ordering of the paired labels returns to the identity.

The number of pairings of \(2m\) slots is:

\[
(2m-1)!!.
\]

Hence:

\[
\boxed{
[e]X_n^{2m}
=
(2m-1)!!\,n^m
+
O_m(n^{m-1}).
}
\]

Words involving adjacent-generator clusters or fewer distinct generators contribute only lower powers of \(n\).

---

# 16. Gaussian spectral theorem

Define the empirical LR2 spectral probability measure:

\[
\nu_n
=
\frac1{|HC_n|}
\sum_j
\delta_{\lambda_j(A_{LR2})/\sqrt n}.
\]

Its moments satisfy:

\[
\int x^{2m}\,d\nu_n(x)
\to
(2m-1)!!,
\]

and:

\[
\int x^{2m+1}\,d\nu_n(x)
\to0.
\]

These are exactly the moments of:

\[
N(0,1).
\]

The Gaussian moment sequence is determinate.

Therefore:

\[
\boxed{
\nu_n
\Longrightarrow
N(0,1).
}
\]

So the HC LR2 bulk spectrum has a **Gaussian**, not semicircular, large-\(n\) law after:

\[
1/\sqrt n
\]

scaling.

---

# 17. Low-moment laws

The regular cyclic-adjacent return census gives:

\[
\boxed{
\mu_2=n,
}
\]

and:

\[
\boxed{
\mu_4=3n^2-4n.
}
\]

Thus:

\[
\frac{\mu_4}{n^2}
=
3-\frac4n
\to3.
\]

The audit also finite-certified for:

\[
6\le n\le14
\]

the polynomial laws:

\[
\boxed{
\mu_6
=
3n(5n^2-20n+26),
}
\]

and:

\[
\boxed{
\mu_8
=
105n^4-840n^3+2744n^2-3426n.
}
\]

These give:

\[
\frac{\mu_6}{n^3}
\to15,
\]

and:

\[
\frac{\mu_8}{n^4}
\to105.
\]

The general Gaussian theorem does **not** depend on promoting the \(\mu_6,\mu_8\) polynomial fits to all-\(n\) theorems.

Their status remains finite-certified.

---

# 18. Shape law versus spectral law

Two different asymptotic probability distributions are present.

## Representation shape

\[
\boxed{
P_{HC,n}(\lambda)
\to
\text{Plancherel ensemble}
}
\]

in total variation.

This produces the VKLS Young-diagram limit shape.

## Hamiltonian spectrum

\[
\boxed{
A_{LR2}/\sqrt n
\to
N(0,1)
}
\]

in empirical spectral distribution.

These should not be confused.

The first says which highest-weight/Specht sectors carry most HC states.

The second says how LR2 energies are distributed after all selected sectors and their multiplicities are assembled.

---

# 19. Energy scaling

The adjacency convention has:

\[
\mathbb E_{\rm spectral}[A_{LR2}]=0
\]

and:

\[
\operatorname{Var}_{\rm spectral}(A_{LR2})=n.
\]

Thus typical eigenvalues under uniform HC state counting are:

\[
O(\sqrt n).
\]

If one uses the conventional shifted permutation Hamiltonian:

\[
H'
=
nI-A_{LR2},
\]

then energies are centered near:

\[
n
\]

with:

\[
O(\sqrt n)
\]

Gaussian bulk fluctuations.

The isolated extremal eigenvalues of order \(n\) carry negligible empirical mass.

---

# 20. Thermodynamic Bethe caution

Standard thermodynamic Bethe analyses usually send chain length to infinity while holding the underlying local rank/model family fixed.

The HC embedding does something different:

\[
L=n,
\qquad
\dim W_n=n.
\]

The rank/local dimension and chain length grow together.

So PHYS-ALG-05 does **not** justify importing a standard fixed-\(SU(N)\) root-density equation unchanged.

The correct asymptotic problem is a double scaling:

\[
\boxed{
N_{\rm rank}
=
L
=
n
\to\infty.
}
\]

The Plancherel limit shape provides the representation-level answer to this double scaling.

A genuine Bethe-root-density formulation remains a separate problem.

---

# 21. Main verdict

PHYS-ALG-05 gives a strong affirmative answer to its main targets.

## Typical partition law

\[
\boxed{
\textbf{HC representation shapes are asymptotically Plancherel.}
}
\]

## Typical fixed multiplicity

\[
\boxed{
m_\lambda^{D_n}
\sim
\frac{f^\lambda}{2n}.
}
\]

## Dominant shape

\[
\boxed{
\textbf{Logan–Shepp / Vershik–Kerov limit shape.}
}
\]

## LR2 spectral law

\[
\boxed{
\textbf{Gaussian after }A_{LR2}/\sqrt n\textbf{ scaling.}
}
\]

Thus the fixed-layer HC integrable sector now has both:

1. an asymptotic representation law;
2. an asymptotic Hamiltonian spectral law.

---

# 22. Certification

The computational audit includes:

- exact HC-versus-Plancherel small-\(n\) probabilities through \(n=8\);
- exact dihedral cycle-type/centralizer RMS bounds through \(n=100\);
- total-variation bound checks;
- cyclic adjacent-transposition return-word counts;
- moment polynomial certification through order \(8\) for \(n=6,\ldots,14\);
- Gaussian moment convergence diagnostics.

Combined:

\[
\boxed{
151/151
}
\]

finite/formula checks passed.

The central Plancherel-convergence and Gaussian-limit statements are theorem-level arguments, not extrapolations from these finite checks.

---

# 23. Recommended next step

The next probe should distinguish two possible directions.

## Representation-first

### EHF-PHYS-ALG-06A — Dihedral-Plancherel Fluctuation Law

Ask whether the HC character tilt changes the **second-order** Kerov fluctuations even though it leaves the first-order VKLS shape unchanged.

Targets:

- derive the scale of:
  \[
  \delta_n(\lambda);
  \]
- compare HC shape fluctuations with Kerov's Plancherel CLT;
- determine whether the dihedral projection becomes invisible even at fluctuation order.

## Spectral/Bethe-first

### EHF-PHYS-ALG-06B — Gaussian LR2 Spectrum and Bethe Root Double-Scaling Audit

Ask whether the Gaussian energy law can be derived directly from the selected nested Bethe sectors/root data in the simultaneous:

\[
N=L=n\to\infty
\]

limit.

This would be the first genuinely thermodynamic Bethe statement specific to HC rather than inherited from finite-layer representation theory.
