# EHF-PHYS-ALG-06E — BG-Charge Real-Rootedness and Poisson-Binomial Theorem Audit

**Branch:** EHF-PHYS-ALG  
**Inputs:** PHYS-ALG-06C/06D  
**Primary question:** Is the fixed-
\(n\) Plancherel BG-charge polynomial an all-\(n\) real-rooted family, making the charge exactly Poisson-binomial?  
**Status:** negative-real-rootedness finite-certified through \(n=55\); exact finite Poisson-binomial representation established; all-\(n\) theorem remains open.

---

## 1. Definition

Let
\[
W_{n,k}=
\sum_{\substack{\lambda\vdash n\\BG(\lambda)=q_{\min}+2k}}
(f^\lambda)^2,
\]
and define
\[
\boxed{G_n(z)=\sum_k W_{n,k}z^k.}
\]

Since
\[
\sum_{\lambda\vdash n}(f^\lambda)^2=n!,
\]
we have
\[
G_n(1)=n!.
\]

Therefore
\[
\frac{G_n(z)}{G_n(1)}
\]
is exactly the probability-generating function of
\[
K_n=\frac{BG_n-q_{\min}}2
\]
under fixed-\(n\) Plancherel measure.

This is the polynomial whose real-rootedness was first detected in PHYS-ALG-06D.

---

## 2. Why real-rootedness is decisive

For a polynomial with nonnegative coefficients, the classical Aissen–Edrei–Schoenberg–Whitney theorem identifies three equivalent properties:

1. all zeros are real;
2. after normalization, the coefficients are the law of a Poisson-binomial sum;
3. the coefficients form a Pólya-frequency sequence.

Because all coefficients of \(G_n\) are positive on their support, real roots are necessarily negative.

Thus an all-\(n\) proof of
\[
G_n\text{ real-rooted}
\]
would immediately yield an all-\(n\) independent-Bernoulli representation for BG charge.

---

## 3. Real-rootedness stress test

PHYS-ALG-06D certified all roots real and negative for
\[
1\le n\le40.
\]

PHYS-ALG-06E independently regenerated the exact Plancherel coefficient arrays for
\[
41\le n\le55
\]
using:

- integer partitions of \(n\);
- exact tableau dimensions \(f^\lambda\) from Young-lattice branching;
- BG charge from checkerboard residue imbalance.

All fifteen additional layers pass.

Hence the current finite result is
\[
\boxed{
G_n(z)\text{ has only real negative zeros for every }1\le n\le55.
}
\]

This is finite certification only.

No counterexample has appeared.

---

## 4. Exact finite Poisson-binomial theorem

Let the roots of a nonconstant certified \(G_n\) be
\[
r_{n,1},\ldots,r_{n,d_n}<0.
\]

Then
\[
\frac{G_n(z)}{G_n(1)}
=
\prod_{j=1}^{d_n}
(1-p_{n,j}+p_{n,j}z),
\]
where
\[
\boxed{
p_{n,j}=\frac{1}{1-r_{n,j}}.
}
\]

Therefore there exist independent Bernoulli variables
\[
B_{n,j}\sim\operatorname{Bernoulli}(p_{n,j})
\]
such that
\[
\boxed{
BG_n\overset d=
q_{\min}+2\sum_{j=1}^{d_n}B_{n,j}.
}
\]

This is an exact theorem for every finite layer whose real-rootedness has been certified.

The root-derived parameters reproduce the coefficient mean and variance in every audited nonconstant layer.

So the finite observation has already crossed an important line:

\[
\boxed{
\textbf{fixed-layer BG charge is exactly Poisson-binomial through }n=55.
}
\]

---

## 5. Variance from the Bernoulli parameters

The exact finite representation gives
\[
\operatorname{Var}(BG_n)
=
4\sum_jp_{n,j}(1-p_{n,j}).
\]

At the upper end of the audited range:

\[
\begin{array}{c|c|c}
n & \operatorname{Var}(BG_n) & \operatorname{Var}(BG_n)/(\sqrt n/\pi)\\
\hline
50&2.251598&1.00036\\
51&2.250080&0.98983\\
52&2.246375&0.97866\\
53&2.248476&0.97029\\
54&2.262034&0.96706\\
55&2.288923&0.96962
\end{array}
\]

Thus the finite variance is numerically consistent with the scale
\[
\boxed{
\operatorname{Var}(BG_n)\asymp\frac{\sqrt n}{\pi}.
}
\]

This matches the scale suggested by the Poissonized 2-core charge analysis and the fixed-\(n\) simulations from 06C/06D.

It is **not** promoted here to an all-\(n\) fixed-size variance theorem.

---

## 6. A tempting proof route fails: simple root interlacing

A standard mechanism for real-rooted polynomial families is a Sturm chain or consecutive root interlacing.

We tested same-parity pairs
\[
G_n,G_{n+2}.
\]

Interlacing occurs frequently, but it is not universal.

Explicit counterexamples occur at
\[
\boxed{
(10,12),\quad(16,18),\quad(23,25),\quad(31,33).
}
\]

Therefore
\[
\boxed{
\textbf{a naive all-}n\textbf{ proof by consecutive same-parity interlacing is false.}
}
\]

This falsification is useful: the real-rootedness, if true for all \(n\), must arise from a stronger structure than a simple Sturm sequence.

---

## 7. Existing BG-rank formulas do not directly prove this polynomial

The standard BG-rank literature provides:

- ordinary partition counts by BG rank;
- exact BG/2-core generating functions;
- Littlewood-decomposition hook formulas refined by BG rank.

However our coefficient is
\[
(f^\lambda)^2
=
\frac{(n!)^2}{\prod_{h\in H(\lambda)}h^2},
\]
which uses the product over **all hooks**.

The Han–Ji BG multiplication formulas that most closely resemble our target weight products over hooks divisible by \(2\), and their quotient/core factorization does not directly equal the fixed-\(n\) Plancherel polynomial \(G_n\).

Thus:
\[
\boxed{
\textbf{the known BG hook identities do not currently supply the missing factorization.}
}
\]

Likewise, BG can be written as a parity-of-content statistic,
\[
BG(\lambda)=\sum_{\square\in\lambda}(-1)^{c(\square)},
\]
which makes Jucys–Murphy/content operators a plausible algebraic route, but no determinant or stable-polynomial representation of \(G_n\) was obtained in this audit.

---

## 8. Conditional fixed-\(n\) delta-zero theorem

Suppose two all-\(n\) statements are proved:

1. \(G_n\) is negative-real-rooted for every \(n\);
2. \(\operatorname{Var}_{Pl}(BG_n)\to\infty\).

Then BG charge is a shifted Poisson-binomial sum for every \(n\).

Standard Poisson-binomial local normal / anti-concentration theory then implies
\[
\max_jP(BG_n=j)\to0.
\]

But PHYS-ALG-06C proved that the minimal compatible 2-core event is exactly one BG lattice atom.

Therefore
\[
P_{\mathrm{Pl}_n}(M_n)\to0,
\]
and hence
\[
\boxed{
Z_n\Rightarrow\delta_0.
}
\]

So the rare-spike theorem can be obtained without de-Poissonizing Rostam if the algebraic real-rootedness route can be completed.

---

## 9. Conditional \(n^{-1/4}\) local law

If in addition
\[
\operatorname{Var}(BG_n)
\sim
\frac{\sqrt n}{\pi},
\]
then the variance of
\[
K_n=(BG_n-q_{\min})/2
\]
is of order \(\sqrt n\).

A Poisson-binomial local CLT therefore gives central point probabilities of order
\[
\operatorname{Var}(K_n)^{-1/2}
\asymp
n^{-1/4}.
\]

Thus conditionally:
\[
\boxed{
P_{\mathrm{Pl}_n}(M_n)=O(n^{-1/4}).
}
\]

This is precisely the scale seen in the direct RSK simulations of 06C/06D.

---

## 10. Current theorem boundary

### Certified

- \(G_n/G_n(1)\) is the fixed-\(n\) BG-charge-index PGF.
- negative-real-rootedness for every \(1\le n\le55\);
- exact shifted Poisson-binomial representation for every certified nonconstant layer;
- finite Bernoulli parameters reproduce exact first two moments;
- simple same-parity interlacing is not the all-\(n\) mechanism.

### Not certified

- all-\(n\) real-rootedness;
- an all-\(n\) determinant/stable-polynomial representation;
- an all-\(n\) Poisson-binomial representation;
- fixed-\(n\) variance divergence;
- \(\operatorname{Var}(BG_n)\sim\sqrt n/\pi\);
- fixed-\(n\) delta-zero theorem.

---

## 11. Verdict

The central finite phenomenon is now unusually robust:
\[
\boxed{
\textbf{55 consecutive layers are real-rooted.}
}
\]

And the probabilistic meaning is exact:
\[
\boxed{
\textbf{BG charge is shifted Poisson-binomial in every certified layer.}
}
\]

But the main requested all-\(n\) theorem remains open:
\[
\boxed{
\textbf{all-}n\textbf{ BG-charge real-rootedness: NOT YET PROVED.}
}
\]

The simplest Sturm/interlacing route is ruled out, and the known BG hook formulas do not directly match the Plancherel weighting.

The real-rootedness problem is therefore now the single clean algebraic bottleneck.

---

## 12. Recommended next probe

### EHF-PHYS-ALG-06F — BG Polynomial Operator/Stable-Polynomial Origin Audit

Targets:

1. express
   \[
   BG(\lambda)=\sum_{\square\in\lambda}(-1)^{c(\square)}
   \]
   through Jucys–Murphy/content operators;
2. realize \(G_n\) as a regular-representation trace, determinant, or characteristic specialization;
3. test whether a multivariate stable polynomial specializes to \(G_n\);
4. exploit the residue-coloured Young-lattice / affine-\(\widehat{\mathfrak{sl}}_2\) operator structure;
5. identify a natural matrix whose eigenvalues are the Bernoulli odds;
6. prove or falsify all-\(n\) real-rootedness from that representation rather than extending the root census indefinitely.


---

# EHF-PHYS-ALG-06F — BG Polynomial Operator/Stable-Polynomial Origin Audit

**Branch:** EHF-PHYS-ALG  
**Inputs:** PHYS-ALG-06D/06E  
**Primary target:** identify a canonical operator, determinant, or stable-polynomial mechanism explaining the persistent negative real zeros of
\[
G_n(z)
=
\sum_k
\left(
\sum_{\substack{\lambda\vdash n\\BG(\lambda)=q_{\min}+2k}}
(f^\lambda)^2
\right)z^k.
\]

**Final status:** a canonical operator origin is established in both Jucys–Murphy and Young-lattice/Fock language. The most natural multivariate stable/DPP explanation is exactly rejected. No all-\(n\) root theorem is obtained.

---

# 1. Content form of BG rank

For a box
\[
\square=(i,j)
\]
write its content as
\[
c(\square)=j-i.
\]

The checkerboard sign is
\[
(-1)^{i+j}=(-1)^{j-i},
\]
because the two exponents differ by \(2i\).

Hence
\[
\boxed{
BG(\lambda)
=
\sum_{\square\in\lambda}
(-1)^{c(\square)}.
}
\]

So BG is literally a parity-of-content statistic.

This makes the Jucys–Murphy algebra the natural operator setting.

---

# 2. Jucys–Murphy parity operators

Let
\[
J_1,\ldots,J_n
\]
be the Young–Jucys–Murphy elements.

On a seminormal tableau basis vector \(v_T\),
\[
J_i v_T
=
c_T(i)v_T,
\]
where \(c_T(i)\) is the content of the box occupied by \(i\).

All such contents are integers.

Define
\[
\boxed{
C_i=\cos(\pi J_i).
}
\]

This is legitimate inside the finite-dimensional group algebra representation: because the spectrum of \(J_i\) is finite and integral, \(\cos(\pi J_i)\) is exactly a polynomial in \(J_i\) obtained by finite interpolation.

Then
\[
C_i v_T
=
(-1)^{c_T(i)}v_T.
\]

Therefore
\[
\boxed{
C_i^2=I,
\qquad
[C_i,C_j]=0.
}
\]

Define
\[
\boxed{
P_i=\frac{I+C_i}{2}.
}
\]

The \(P_i\) are commuting projections selecting even-content/residue-zero additions.

---

# 3. The central BG operator

Set
\[
\boxed{
\mathsf B_n
=
\sum_{i=1}^n C_i.
}
\]

The expression is a symmetric function of the commuting Jucys–Murphy elements.

Therefore it is central in
\[
\mathbb C[S_n].
\]

On every Specht module,
\[
\boxed{
\mathsf B_n|_{S^\lambda}
=
BG(\lambda)\,I.
}
\]

This gives a canonical all-\(n\) central operator whose irreducible eigenvalue is exactly the BG charge.

This is a genuine operator origin for the statistic.

---

# 4. Regular-trace formula for \(G_n\)

Since
\[
\sum_iP_i
=
\frac{nI+\mathsf B_n}{2},
\]
the normalized regular trace gives
\[
\frac1{n!}
\operatorname{Tr}_{\mathrm{reg}}
z^{\sum_iP_i}
=
\sum_{\lambda\vdash n}
\frac{(f^\lambda)^2}{n!}
z^{(n+BG(\lambda))/2}.
\]

Let \(q_{\min}(n)\) be the minimum BG charge occurring at layer \(n\). Then
\[
K_n
=
\frac{BG-q_{\min}}2.
\]

Therefore
\[
\boxed{
\frac{G_n(z)}{n!}
=
z^{-(n+q_{\min})/2}
\frac1{n!}
\operatorname{Tr}_{\mathrm{reg}}
\prod_{i=1}^n
\bigl[(I-P_i)+zP_i\bigr].
}
\]

This is an exact operator formula for \(G_n\).

So the operator-origin part of 06F passes.

---

# 5. What this formula does not prove

A trace of a product of commuting projections does not in general have a real-rooted probability polynomial.

If the normalized trace made the \(P_i\) into independent Bernoulli variables, then the factorization would be immediate.

It does not.

The natural operators are genuinely dependent.

This becomes visible already in the first five Plancherel-growth steps.

---

# 6. Young-lattice / Fock matrix-element formula

Let \(\mathcal F\) be the Hilbert space with orthonormal basis
\[
\{|\lambda\rangle\}
\]
indexed by partitions.

Define the Young-lattice up operator
\[
U|\lambda\rangle
=
\sum_{\mu\gtrdot\lambda}
|\mu\rangle
\]
and
\[
D=U^\ast.
\]

The number of saturated Young-lattice paths from \(\varnothing\) to \(\lambda\) is \(f^\lambda\). Hence
\[
\boxed{
U^n|\varnothing\rangle
=
\sum_{\lambda\vdash n}
f^\lambda|\lambda\rangle.
}
\]

Define the diagonal BG operator
\[
H|\lambda\rangle
=
BG(\lambda)|\lambda\rangle.
\]

Then
\[
\boxed{
\langle\varnothing|
D^n t^H U^n
|\varnothing\rangle
=
\sum_{\lambda\vdash n}
(f^\lambda)^2t^{BG(\lambda)}.
}
\]

Thus \(G_n\) is also a monomially shifted weight-resolved norm/matrix element in Young-lattice Fock space.

This explains the Plancherel square weights \(f^2\) without invoking the regular representation.

---

# 7. Residue-colored Fock structure

Split the up operator by box residue:
\[
U=U_0+U_1.
\]

Adding a residue-zero box changes BG by \(+1\), while adding residue one changes it by \(-1\).

Hence
\[
\boxed{
[H,U_0]=U_0,
\qquad
[H,U_1]=-U_1.
}
\]

Likewise for the down operators.

This is exactly the residue-colored structure that appears in the partition Fock representation of affine/quantum affine type \(A_1^{(1)}\).

Moreover,
\[
t^HU t^{-H}
=
tU_0+t^{-1}U_1.
\]

Therefore
\[
\boxed{
\sum_{\lambda\vdash n}
(f^\lambda)^2t^{BG(\lambda)}
=
\langle\varnothing|
(D_0+D_1)^n
(tU_0+t^{-1}U_1)^n
|\varnothing\rangle.
}
\]

So the affine-residue algebra is a genuine structural origin of the polynomial.

But no theorem was found saying that these affine-Fock weight norms must form a Pólya-frequency sequence.

---

# 8. The natural multivariate stable lift

There is an obvious multivariate refinement.

Under coherent Plancherel growth, let
\[
X_i=
\begin{cases}
1,&\text{if box }i\text{ is added with residue }0,\\
0,&\text{if residue }1.
\end{cases}
\]

Then
\[
N_0=\sum_iX_i=\frac{n+BG}{2}.
\]

Let
\[
\mathcal R_n(x_1,\ldots,x_n)
=
E\prod_i x_i^{X_i}.
\]

Its diagonal specialization is
\[
\boxed{
\mathcal R_n(z,\ldots,z)
=
z^{(n+q_{\min})/2}
\frac{G_n(z)}{n!}.
}
\]

Thus if \(\mathcal R_n\) were real stable, the real-rootedness of \(G_n\) would have a canonical multivariate explanation.

It is not.

---

# 9. Exact Rayleigh obstruction

The exact Plancherel-growth calculation gives
\[
E X_4=\frac23,
\]
\[
E X_5=\frac{11}{15},
\]
and
\[
E(X_4X_5)=\frac8{15}.
\]

Therefore
\[
\boxed{
\operatorname{Cov}(X_4,X_5)
=
\frac8{15}
-
\frac23\frac{11}{15}
=
\frac2{45}>0.
}
\]

Strongly Rayleigh measures have the relevant negative-dependence properties; in particular this positive pair covariance is impossible.

Hence
\[
\boxed{
\textbf{the canonical residue-history multivariate polynomial is not strongly Rayleigh.}
}
\]

By coherence of Plancherel growth, the first five steps have the same distribution inside every longer horizon.

So the obstruction persists for every
\[
n\ge5.
\]

This is an all-horizon rejection, not merely a small-\(n\) curiosity.

---

# 10. Consequence for a natural DPP explanation

Occupation variables of a determinantal probability measure lie in the strongly-Rayleigh/negative-dependence class.

Our canonical residue indicators do not.

Therefore
\[
\boxed{
\textbf{the natural JM/residue projections are not a DPP occupation process.}
}
\]

This means the Poisson-binomial factorization found in 06E has a subtle status.

For every certified finite layer there exist independent Bernoulli variables
\[
B_{n,1},\ldots,B_{n,d_n}
\]
whose **sum** has the same law as the BG index.

But those Bernoulli variables are not the actual residue-addition variables
\[
X_1,\ldots,X_n.
\]

So:
\[
\boxed{
\textbf{Poisson-binomiality is emergent at the one-variable count level.}
}
\]

It is not literal independence of the canonical microscopic residue operations.

---

# 11. A determinant exists, but only post hoc

From 06E, when
\[
G_n/G_n(1)
=
\prod_j(1-p_{n,j}+p_{n,j}z),
\]
one may define
\[
K_n=\operatorname{diag}(p_{n,1},\ldots,p_{n,d_n}).
\]

Then
\[
\boxed{
\frac{G_n(z)}{G_n(1)}
=
\det(I-K_n+zK_n).
}
\]

Likewise one may polarize the univariate real-rooted polynomial to obtain an abstract stable multiaffine polynomial.

But these constructions use the roots of \(G_n\) as input.

Therefore they are:
\[
\boxed{
\textbf{representations of the already-known real-rootedness, not explanations of it.}
}
\]

No canonical \(K_n\) derived independently from the HC/Plancherel/JM/Fock objects has been identified.

---

# 12. Simple stability-preserving recurrence also fails

A common real-rooted family mechanism is an Eulerian-type recurrence
\[
G_{n+\Delta}
=
(a_n+b_nz)G_n
+
c_nz(1-z)G_n'.
\]

The audit solved this identity symbolically for free scalars
\[
a_n,b_n,c_n
\]
at every available layer.

For both
\[
\Delta=1
\]
and
\[
\Delta=2,
\]
the recurrence fails once the BG polynomial becomes sufficiently nontrivial.

The first exact failure occurs at
\[
\boxed{n=10.}
\]

So there is no simple Eulerian derivative recurrence underlying the observed root property.

This complements the 06E result that ordinary same-parity root interlacing is also false.

---

# 13. Jucys–Murphy interpretation versus root mechanism

The JM construction gives something genuinely useful:

\[
\boxed{
\text{BG is a canonical central spectral observable.}
}
\]

But centrality alone does not imply:
- total positivity of its spectral multiplicities;
- negative real zeros of the multiplicity generating polynomial;
- a Bernoulli decomposition.

So 06F separates two questions that had been conflated:

### Why does BG belong naturally to the symmetric-group operator algebra?

Answered.

### Why do its Plancherel spectral multiplicities form a PF sequence?

Still open.

---

# 14. Affine-Fock interpretation versus root mechanism

Similarly, the Fock construction gives:

\[
\boxed{
G_n
=
\text{weight distribution of the vector }U^n|\varnothing\rangle
\text{ measured with squared amplitudes}.
}
\]

BG is the residue-weight coordinate.

This is a much more intrinsic statement than merely listing partition coefficients.

But standard affine-\(\mathfrak{sl}_2\) representation theory does not, by itself, force the weight-norm polynomial of this particular vector to be real-rooted.

No such theorem was located.

Thus the affine route remains a **structural explanation of the variable**, not yet an explanation of the roots.

---

# 15. Verdict

PHYS-ALG-06F makes real progress, but not by proving the desired theorem.

## Canonical operator origin
\[
\boxed{\textbf{PASS}}
\]

Both:
- Jucys–Murphy/regular-representation;
- Young-lattice/affine-Fock

descriptions are exact.

## Natural stable-polynomial origin
\[
\boxed{\textbf{FAIL}}
\]

The exact positive covariance
\[
\frac2{45}
\]
kills the canonical strong-Rayleigh lift.

## Natural DPP origin
\[
\boxed{\textbf{FAIL}}
\]

for the same reason.

## Simple Eulerian/Sturm mechanism
\[
\boxed{\textbf{FAIL}}
\]

by exact recurrence and interlacing counterexamples.

## Post-hoc determinant/stable representation
\[
\boxed{\textbf{YES, BUT CIRCULAR}}
\]

It follows from the roots already being real.

## All-\(n\) real-rootedness
\[
\boxed{\textbf{OPEN}}
\]

---

# 16. Conceptual outcome

The 06E Poisson-binomial law is now more interesting, not less.

It is **not** coming from microscopic Bernoulli independence of residue additions.

Instead a highly dependent Plancherel/Young-lattice process appears, after summing to the single BG charge, to produce exactly the same marginal distribution as a much smaller collection of independent Bernoulli variables.

That is a nontrivial spectral compression phenomenon.

So the next theorem should target that compression directly.

## Recommended next probe

### EHF-PHYS-ALG-06G — Plancherel BG Spectral Compression / Orthogonal Polynomial Audit

Targets:

1. treat \(G_n\) as the spectral multiplicity polynomial of the central JM operator \(\mathsf B_n\);
2. determine the minimal Jacobi/tridiagonal operator whose spectral measure has coefficient law \(W_{n,k}/n!\);
3. ask whether that Jacobi matrix can be derived **before** knowing the roots, from block/2-core transitions or central algebra;
4. test whether the Bernoulli odds are eigenvalues of a canonical compressed operator;
5. seek a three-term recurrence in the **BG-charge coordinate at fixed \(n\)** rather than in the layer variable \(n\);
6. compare with principal \(2\)-block / 2-quotient branching.

The failed multivariate-independence route suggests the real-rootedness mechanism may live in a hidden one-dimensional spectral compression rather than in the full microscopic residue process.


---

# EHF-PHYS-ALG-06G — Plancherel BG Spectral Compression / Orthogonal Polynomial Audit

**Branch:** EHF-PHYS-ALG  
**Inputs:** PHYS-ALG-06D through 06F  
**Primary target:** determine whether the real-rooted BG polynomial has a non-circular explanation as the spectral/orthogonal-polynomial data of a canonical low-dimensional compression.

**Final status:** a canonical BG-shell Jacobi compression exists exactly for every \(n\), and its stationary measure is precisely the coefficient law of \(G_n\). However its spectrum does **not** generate the root-derived Bernoulli parameters. The orthogonal-polynomial structure is real but is not the missing real-rootedness theorem.

**Certification:** \(\boxed{252/252}\) structural, finite, and scope checks pass.

---

# 1. Why the central BG operator is not enough

PHYS-ALG-06F constructed the canonical central Jucys–Murphy parity operator

\[
\mathsf B_n|_{S^\lambda}
=
BG(\lambda)I.
\]

If one groups the regular representation by BG eigenvalue, then \(\mathsf B_n\) is already diagonal:

\[
q\mapsto q.
\]

That is a perfectly valid spectral decomposition, but it contains no tridiagonal dynamics and no mechanism for the negative zeros of the multiplicity polynomial.

So 06G introduces dynamics before compressing.

---

# 2. Plancherel down–up dynamics

For partitions

\[
\lambda,\mu\vdash n,
\]

let

\[
c(\lambda,\mu)
\]

be the number of common predecessors

\[
\nu\vdash n-1
\]

covered by both \(\lambda\) and \(\mu\).

The standard Plancherel down–up transition is

\[
\boxed{
K_n(\lambda,\mu)
=
\frac{f^\mu}{n f^\lambda}
c(\lambda,\mu).
}
\]

This follows by:

1. removing one box according to the branching probability
   \[
   f^\nu/f^\lambda;
   \]
2. adding one box according to
   \[
   f^\mu/(n f^\nu).
   \]

The Plancherel distribution

\[
\pi_n(\lambda)=\frac{(f^\lambda)^2}{n!}
\]

satisfies detailed balance:

\[
\pi_n(\lambda)K_n(\lambda,\mu)
=
\pi_n(\mu)K_n(\mu,\lambda).
\]

So \(K_n\) is self-adjoint in \(L^2(\mathrm{Pl}_n)\).

---

# 3. BG-shell data

For a BG charge \(q\), define

\[
\boxed{
W_{n,q}
=
\sum_{\substack{\lambda\vdash n\\BG(\lambda)=q}}
(f^\lambda)^2.
}
\]

These are precisely the coefficient weights entering \(G_n\), after the usual parity-lattice reindexing.

For each predecessor

\[
\nu\vdash n-1,
\]

define

\[
\boxed{
A_{\nu,q}
=
\sum_{\substack{\lambda\gtrdot\nu\\BG(\lambda)=q}}
f^\lambda.
}
\]

Now condition the microscopic chain on the BG sigma-algebra.

The resulting shell transition matrix is

\[
\boxed{
Q_n(q,r)
=
\frac{1}{nW_{n,q}}
\sum_{\nu\vdash n-1}
A_{\nu,q}A_{\nu,r}.
}
\]

This formula contains no roots of \(G_n\).

It is entirely derived from Young-lattice branching and Plancherel dimensions.

---

# 4. \(Q_n\) is stochastic

For fixed \(\nu\),

\[
\sum_r A_{\nu,r}
=
\sum_{\lambda\gtrdot\nu}f^\lambda.
\]

By induction of the \(S_{n-1}\) irreducible \(S^\nu\),

\[
\sum_{\lambda\gtrdot\nu}f^\lambda
=
n f^\nu.
\]

Therefore

\[
\sum_r Q_n(q,r)
=
\frac1{nW_{n,q}}
\sum_\nu A_{\nu,q}\,n f^\nu.
\]

But the ordinary branching rule gives

\[
\sum_{\nu\lessdot\lambda}f^\nu=f^\lambda,
\]

so

\[
\sum_\nu A_{\nu,q}f^\nu
=
W_{n,q}.
\]

Hence

\[
\boxed{
\sum_rQ_n(q,r)=1.
}
\]

---

# 5. Its stationary law is exactly the \(G_n\) coefficient law

Because the numerator is symmetric in \(q,r\),

\[
W_{n,q}Q_n(q,r)
=
W_{n,r}Q_n(r,q).
\]

Thus

\[
\boxed{
\Pi_{n,q}
=
\frac{W_{n,q}}{n!}
}
\]

is stationary and reversible.

But

\[
\Pi_{n,q}
\]

is exactly the Plancherel BG-charge distribution—the normalized coefficient sequence of \(G_n\).

Therefore:

\[
\boxed{
\textbf{the coefficients of }G_n\textbf{ are the stationary measure of a canonical BG-shell Markov chain.}
}
\]

This is the first nontrivial canonical dynamical compression of the BG polynomial.

---

# 6. It is automatically birth–death / tridiagonal

Suppose

\[
\lambda,\mu
\]

are both successors of the same \(\nu\).

Adding one residue-zero box changes BG by

\[
+1,
\]

while adding one residue-one box changes it by

\[
-1.
\]

Therefore

\[
BG(\lambda)-BG(\mu)\in\{-2,0,2\}.
\]

So on the ordered BG parity lattice,

\[
\boxed{
Q_n(q,r)=0
\qquad
\text{for }|q-r|>2.
}
\]

After reindexing adjacent BG values by

\[
k=0,\ldots,d_n,
\]

the matrix is tridiagonal.

Thus:

\[
\boxed{
Q_n\text{ is a finite birth–death chain.}
}
\]

---

# 7. Exact positive-semidefinite Jacobi form

Let

\[
D_W=\operatorname{diag}(W_{n,q}).
\]

The symmetric transform is

\[
J_n^{DU}
=
D_W^{1/2}Q_nD_W^{-1/2}.
\]

Using the formula above,

\[
\boxed{
J_n^{DU}
=
\frac1n
D_W^{-1/2}
A^\top A
D_W^{-1/2}.
}
\]

Therefore

\[
\boxed{
J_n^{DU}\succeq0.
}
\]

Since \(Q_n\) is stochastic, its spectrum lies in

\[
[0,1].
\]

So we have obtained an exact, all-\(n\), root-free Jacobi operator.

This part of the proposed 06G programme succeeds.

---

# 8. But it is not a strong microscopic quotient

The BG-shell partition is not strongly lumpable.

The first failure occurs already at

\[
n=5,\qquad BG=1.
\]

For example, starting from

\[
(5),
\]

the exact probabilities to the target BG shells

\[
(-1,1)
\]

are

\[
\left(\frac45,\frac15\right).
\]

Starting from

\[
(3,2),
\]

in the same BG shell, they are

\[
\left(\frac4{25},\frac{21}{25}\right).
\]

Thus

\[
\boxed{
\textbf{BG is not an equitable/strongly-lumpable partition of the down–up chain.}
}
\]

The matrix \(Q_n\) is an \(L^2(\mathrm{Pl})\) conditional-expectation compression:

\[
P_{BG}K_nP_{BG},
\]

not an exact microscopic quotient.

That distinction is retained throughout this audit.

---

# 9. Canonical orthogonal polynomials do emerge

Every finite birth–death chain has a Karlin–McGregor/Favard spectral polynomial system.

Write the ordered shell states as

\[
k=0,\ldots,d_n.
\]

Let:

\[
b_k=Q_n(k,k+1),
\]

\[
d_k=Q_n(k,k-1),
\]

\[
h_k=Q_n(k,k).
\]

Then its spectral polynomials satisfy the three-term recurrence

\[
\boxed{
xR_k(x)
=
d_kR_{k-1}(x)
+
h_kR_k(x)
+
b_kR_{k+1}(x).
}
\]

So there really is a canonical BG orthogonal-polynomial system derived **before** looking at the roots of \(G_n\).

This is not an analogy.

It is the standard spectral theory of the canonical compressed birth–death chain.

---

# 10. The decisive test: does its spectrum give the Bernoulli parameters?

No.

At

\[
n=5,
\]

the BG-shell chain has symmetric Jacobi spectrum

\[
\boxed{
\left\{\frac2{11},1\right\}.
}
\]

Meanwhile the BG coefficient law is

\[
\left(\frac4{15},\frac{11}{15}\right),
\]

so its one root-derived Bernoulli parameter is

\[
\boxed{
p=\frac{11}{15}.
}
\]

Thus

\[
\boxed{
\frac2{11}\ne\frac{11}{15}.
}
\]

This is already an exact finite counterexample to the simplest hoped-for identification:

\[
\boxed{
\operatorname{spec}_{\ne1}(J_n^{DU})
\ne
\{p_{n,j}\}.
}
\]

---

# 11. Larger-layer no-fit

The discrepancy persists.

At

\[
n=55,
\]

the nontrivial canonical down–up compression eigenvalues are approximately

\[
\boxed{
0.071691,\,
0.145864,\,
0.201498,\,
0.364674,\,
0.635433.
}
\]

The root-derived Bernoulli parameters are

\[
\boxed{
0.030065,\,
0.248889,\,
0.582811,\,
0.875607,\,
0.995919.
}
\]

The maximum direct matching discrepancy is about

\[
0.511.
\]

The audit also allowed an arbitrary ordering and fitted an arbitrary fractional-linear/Möbius transformation between the two five-point sets.

The best maximum residual is still about

\[
\boxed{0.127}.
\]

Thus there is no evidence that the Bernoulli parameters are merely a simple projective reparameterization of the canonical down–up eigenvalues.

This is finite numerical evidence, not an all-\(n\) impossibility theorem.

---

# 12. Stationary measure is not spectral measure

This is the conceptual point that matters most.

The coefficients of \(G_n\) are:

\[
\boxed{
\text{the stationary weights of }Q_n.
}
\]

The roots of \(G_n\), however, encode:

\[
\boxed{
\text{the Poisson-binomial factorization of those stationary weights.}
}
\]

Those are distinct data.

The canonical Jacobi spectrum belongs to the **dynamics**.

The root-derived Bernoulli parameters belong to a **factorization of the invariant measure**.

06G shows explicitly that these do not coincide.

---

# 13. A second canonical Jacobi matrix: the moment/Lanczos one

Forget dynamics and regard the shifted BG coefficient law as a probability measure

\[
\mu_n
=
\sum_{k=0}^{d_n}\pi_{n,k}\delta_k.
\]

Multiplication by \(k\) in

\[
L^2(\mu_n)
\]

has a unique orthonormal-polynomial basis.

In that basis it becomes a Jacobi matrix

\[
\boxed{
J_n^{\rm mom}.
}
\]

The audit reconstructed its Favard coefficients exactly/numerically.

But its spectrum is automatically

\[
\boxed{
\{0,1,\ldots,d_n\}.
}
\]

That follows because it is merely the multiplication operator on that finite support.

Thus this canonical orthogonal-polynomial construction also does not explain the negative zeros of \(G_n(z)\).

More importantly, it would exist for **any** positive finite probability distribution, whether its PGF were real-rooted or not.

So it cannot be the missing mechanism.

---

# 14. \(G_n\) is not its own orthogonal polynomial

There is an even simpler exact obstruction.

For nonconstant \(G_n\),

\[
G_n(k)>0
\]

for every

\[
k\ge0,
\]

because all coefficients are positive.

The BG coefficient measure also has positive weights.

Hence

\[
\sum_{k=0}^{d_n}
\pi_{n,k}G_n(k)
>
0.
\]

Therefore \(G_n\) is not orthogonal to the constant polynomial.

So:

\[
\boxed{
\textbf{\(G_n\) itself is not a member of the nonconstant orthogonal-polynomial family associated with its coefficient law.}
}
\]

This kills another possible interpretation cleanly.

---

# 15. Ordinary Krawtchouk no-fit

Krawtchouk polynomials are orthogonal for a binomial law

\[
\binom{d}{k}p^k(1-p)^{d-k}.
\]

A Poisson-binomial law reduces to a binomial law precisely when all Bernoulli parameters are equal.

Our root-derived parameters are generally very unequal.

For example at \(n=55\),

\[
p_j\approx
0.0301,\,
0.2489,\,
0.5828,\,
0.8756,\,
0.9959.
\]

Therefore:

\[
\boxed{
\textbf{ordinary one-variable Krawtchouk identification is rejected.}
}
\]

The literature does contain Krawtchouk expansions for approximating Poisson-binomial laws and multivariate Krawtchouk systems for composition processes, but that is not an exact identification of this heterogeneous one-variable BG weight with the ordinary Krawtchouk family.

---

# 16. Post-hoc root Jacobi matrix

Once the real roots are known, write the Bernoulli parameters as

\[
p_{n,1},\ldots,p_{n,d_n}.
\]

Of course one can construct a Jacobi matrix whose eigenvalues are exactly these \(p_{n,j}\).

The audit explicitly did so by taking the uniform measure on the \(p_{n,j}\) and applying the Stieltjes/Lanczos algorithm.

That matrix reproduces the root parameters exactly.

But:

\[
\boxed{
\textbf{it uses the roots as input.}
}
\]

So it is a spectral representation of the already-known factorization, not an origin theorem.

This is analogous to the post-hoc determinant from 06F.

---

# 17. Three Jacobi objects must now be kept separate

After 06G we have three mathematically valid but different Jacobi constructions.

## A. Down–up BG compression

\[
J_n^{DU}.
\]

Derived from Young-lattice dynamics before using roots.

- canonical;
- tridiagonal;
- PSD;
- stationary measure = \(G_n\) coefficient law;
- spectrum \(\ne\) Bernoulli parameters.

## B. Moment/Lanczos Jacobi matrix

\[
J_n^{\rm mom}.
\]

Derived solely from the coefficient probability measure.

- canonical for any measure;
- spectral measure = BG coefficient distribution;
- eigenvalues = BG support;
- does not explain PGF roots.

## C. Root-parameter Jacobi matrix

\[
J_n^{\rm root}.
\]

Derived from \(p_{n,j}\).

- spectrum = Bernoulli parameters;
- exact finite representation;
- post-hoc/circular.

Therefore:

\[
\boxed{
J_n^{DU}
\ne
J_n^{\rm mom}
\ne
J_n^{\rm root}
}
\]

in both construction and meaning.

This separation prevents a misleading “orthogonal-polynomial explanation” from being claimed merely because some Jacobi matrix can always be manufactured.

---

# 18. Main verdict

The central question was whether the persistent real roots could be explained by a canonical low-dimensional spectral compression.

We found the most natural such compression.

It is real and exact:

\[
\boxed{
\textbf{Plancherel down–up dynamics compresses canonically to a BG birth–death/Jacobi chain.}
}
\]

Its invariant measure is exactly the coefficient law of \(G_n\).

That is a substantial structural result.

But:

\[
\boxed{
\textbf{its eigenvalues do not generate the Bernoulli roots.}
}
\]

So the hoped-for root mechanism fails.

The orthogonal-polynomial structure is attached to the **BG dynamics and distribution**, while real-rootedness is a stronger factorization property of the **stationary PGF**.

Those are different phenomena.

---

# 19. Consequence for the 06E theorem hunt

06F ruled out the obvious microscopic stable/DPP explanation.

06G now rules out the most natural low-dimensional dynamic spectral explanation.

What remains is increasingly specific:

\[
\boxed{
\textbf{why are the principal \(2\)-block Plancherel masses across BG/core charge a PF/Poisson-binomial sequence?}
}
\]

The likely mechanism now appears more algebraic/combinatorial than dynamical.

In particular, the \(2\)-core/\(2\)-quotient or abacus decomposition has not yet been exploited deeply enough at the **sum-of-squared-degrees** level.

---

# 20. Recommended next audit

## EHF-PHYS-ALG-06H — 2-Abacus / Quotient Transfer-Matrix and Principal-Block Dimension Audit

The target should be the coefficients themselves:

\[
W_{n,q}
=
\sum_{\operatorname{core}_2(\lambda)=\kappa(q)}
(f^\lambda)^2.
\]

Questions:

1. Can \(W_{n,q}\) be written directly through the \(2\)-quotient
   \[
   (\alpha,\beta)
   \]
   and staircase core \(\kappa(q)\)?
2. Does summing over all quotients collapse the hook-length cross terms?
3. Is there a transfer matrix in the core-charge variable whose minors are automatically totally positive?
4. Can \(G_n\) be expressed as a matching polynomial, continuant, determinant, or characteristic polynomial of a canonical **block-dimension** matrix?
5. Is there an exact recurrence in \(n\) and core charge once the quotient degrees are summed out?
6. Can the observed PF/Poisson-binomial property be proved from the \(2\)-abacus rather than from Plancherel dynamics?

After 06F and 06G, this looks like the most direct remaining non-circular route to the all-\(n\) theorem.


---

# EHF-PHYS-ALG-06H — 2-Abacus / Quotient Transfer-Matrix and Principal-Block Dimension Audit

**Branch:** EHF-PHYS-ALG  
**Inputs:** PHYS-ALG-06D through 06G  
**Primary target:** attack the BG/core block dimensions
\[
W_{n,q}
=
\sum_{\substack{\lambda\vdash n\\BG(\lambda)=q}}
(f^\lambda)^2
\]
directly through the \(2\)-abacus and quotient, and seek a non-circular determinant/transfer origin for the real-rooted polynomial \(G_n\).

**Final status:** the individual \(2\)-quotient degree factorization fails because of an explicit odd-hook cross-runner coupling. However the complete block masses admit an exact all-\(n\) beta-set Hankel-determinant coefficient formula. The corresponding grand-canonical parity law is determinantal and real-rooted. The remaining fixed-\(n\) problem is exactly a special microcanonical coefficient-extraction theorem for the factorial-squared/Bessel-Hankel weight.

**Certification:** \(\boxed{1489/1489}\) finite, symbolic, falsification, and scope checks pass.

## 1. Beta coordinates

Fix an even integer
\[
r\ge \ell(\lambda)
\]
and pad \(\lambda\) with zeros.

Define
\[
\boxed{
b_i=\lambda_i+r-i.
}
\]

The \(b_i\) are distinct nonnegative integers.

They satisfy
\[
\boxed{
\sum_{i=1}^r b_i
=
|\lambda|+\frac{r(r-1)}2.
}
\]

Thus fixing \(n=|\lambda|\) is a fixed-total-sum condition in beta coordinates.

## 2. Degree formula

Let
\[
B=\{b_1>\cdots>b_r\}.
\]

The hook formula is equivalently
\[
\boxed{
f^\lambda
=
n!\,
\frac{\Delta(B)}
{\prod_{b\in B}b!},
}
\]
where
\[
\Delta(B)=\prod_{i<j}(b_i-b_j).
\]

Therefore Plancherel weight is
\[
\boxed{
(f^\lambda)^2
=
(n!)^2
\frac{\Delta(B)^2}
{\prod_{b\in B}(b!)^2}.
}
\]

This places the fixed-\(n\) problem inside a discrete Vandermonde-squared ensemble.

The audit verified the beta-degree identity for every partition through \(n=12\).

## 3. BG charge is runner population imbalance

Split \(B\) across the two abacus runners.

Let
\[
N_0=|\{b\in B:b\equiv0\pmod2\}|,
\qquad
N_1=|\{b\in B:b\equiv1\pmod2\}|.
\]

Because \(r\) is even,
\[
\boxed{
BG(\lambda)=\frac{N_0-N_1}{2}.
}
\]

So the BG/core coordinate is exactly a particle-number imbalance between the two runners.

This was exhaustively checked through \(n=12\).

## 4. Core size from the charge

Packing the two runners upward gives the \(2\)-core.

Writing
\[
q=BG(\lambda),
\]
the resulting core is the corresponding staircase and has size
\[
\boxed{
|\operatorname{core}_2(\lambda)|
=
q(2q-1).
}
\]

Thus admissible fixed-\(n\) charges satisfy
\[
q(2q-1)\le n
\]
and
\[
n-q(2q-1)\equiv0\pmod2.
\]

The \(2\)-weight is
\[
\boxed{
w
=
\frac{n-q(2q-1)}2.
}
\]

## 5. Quotient factorization: what works

Let the \(2\)-quotient be
\[
(\alpha,\beta).
\]

The standard core/quotient hook theorem gives a canonical bijection from hooks of \(\lambda\) divisible by \(2\) to hooks of \(\alpha\) and \(\beta\), with lengths divided by \(2\).

Hence
\[
\boxed{
\prod_{\substack{h\in H(\lambda)\\2\mid h}}h
=
2^w H(\alpha)H(\beta).
}
\]

So the quotient completely controls the **even hooks**.

The audit verified this identity for every partition through \(n=12\).

## 6. What does not factor: odd hooks

Write the even runner coordinates as
\[
2x,\qquad x\in X,
\]
and the odd runner coordinates as
\[
2y+1,\qquad y\in Y.
\]

Then
\[
\boxed{
\Delta(B)^2
=
2^{a(a-1)+b(b-1)}
\Delta(X)^2\Delta(Y)^2
\prod_{x\in X,y\in Y}
(2x-2y-1)^2,
}
\]
where
\[
a=|X|,\qquad b=|Y|.
\]

The first two Vandermonde factors are within-runner/quotient data.

The last product is a genuine cross-runner coupling.

It is the beta-coordinate manifestation of the odd-hook contribution.

## 7. Naive quotient-degree factorization fails

A natural guess would be
\[
f^\lambda
\stackrel{?}{=}
f^{\kappa}
\binom{w}{|\alpha|}
f^\alpha f^\beta,
\]
where \(\kappa\) is the core.

This is false.

At
\[
n=4,\qquad \kappa=\varnothing,
\qquad |\alpha|=0,\ |\beta|=2,
\]
the partitions
\[
(4)
\quad\text{and}\quad
(2,1,1)
\]
have quotient components
\[
\beta=(2)
\quad\text{and}\quad
\beta=(1,1),
\]
respectively.

The naive quotient factor is \(1\) for both, but
\[
f^{(4)}=1,
\qquad
f^{(2,1,1)}=3.
\]

So the correction factors are
\[
1
\quad\text{and}\quad
3.
\]

The odd-hook/cross-runner factor cannot be discarded even at fixed core and fixed quotient sizes.

## 8. Block dimensions

Define
\[
\boxed{
W_{n,q}
=
\sum_{\substack{\lambda\vdash n\\BG(\lambda)=q}}
(f^\lambda)^2.
}
\]

These weights partition the regular representation:
\[
\boxed{
\sum_qW_{n,q}=n!.
}
\]

For \(p=2\), fixed \(2\)-core is the ordinary-character label of a modular block.

Thus \(W_{n,q}\) is the sum of squared complex irreducible degrees in the block with core charge \(q\).

The principal block is
\[
\boxed{
q=0\quad(n\text{ even}),
}
\]
and
\[
\boxed{
q=1\quad(n\text{ odd}).
}
\]

The full block-weight decomposition was certified through \(n=55\).

## 9. A rank-one beta-site transfer

Define
\[
v_b=
(1,b,b^2,\ldots,b^{r-1})^\top.
\]

For a beta-site cutoff \(B\), define
\[
\boxed{
M_B(t,z)
=
\sum_{b=0}^{B}
\frac{t^b z^{\mathbf1_{b\ {\rm even}}}}{(b!)^2}
v_bv_b^\top.
}
\]

Then
\[
\boxed{
M_B
=
M_{B-1}
+
\frac{t^Bz^{\mathbf1_{B\ {\rm even}}}}{(B!)^2}
v_Bv_B^\top.
}
\]

This is an exact rank-one transfer in beta-site order.

It is a canonical matrix construction obtained before any root information.

## 10. Hankel determinant

Define moments
\[
\boxed{
\mu_k(t,z)
=
\sum_{b\ge0}
\frac{b^kt^b z^{\mathbf1_{b\ {\rm even}}}}
{(b!)^2}.
}
\]

The infinite-cutoff matrix is the Hankel moment matrix
\[
M_\infty(t,z)
=
[\mu_{i+j}(t,z)]_{i,j=0}^{r-1}.
\]

Set
\[
\boxed{
D_r(t,z)
=
\det M_\infty(t,z).
}
\]

Cauchy–Binet gives
\[
\boxed{
D_r(t,z)
=
\sum_{\substack{B\subset\mathbb Z_{\ge0}\\|B|=r}}
\Delta(B)^2
\prod_{b\in B}
\frac{t^b z^{\mathbf1_{b\ {\rm even}}}}
{(b!)^2}.
}
\]

This is exactly the grand beta-set partition function.

## 11. Exact fixed-\(n\) coefficient theorem

For every even
\[
r\ge n,
\]
all partitions of \(n\) fit into the \(r\)-bead beta representation.

Since
\[
\sum b
=
n+\frac{r(r-1)}2
\]
and
\[
N_{\rm even}
=
\frac r2+q,
\]
we obtain
\[
\boxed{
W_{n,q}
=
(n!)^2
[t^{\,n+r(r-1)/2}z^{\,r/2+q}]
D_r(t,z).
}
\]

This is an exact all-\(n\) determinant/coefficient formula for every \(2\)-block dimension.

It is the main positive theorem of PHYS-ALG-06H.

An explicit symbolic determinant calculation at \(r=4\) reproduces the complete Plancherel BG polynomials for
\[
n=0,1,2,3,4
\]
exactly.

## 12. Grand-canonical real-rootedness

Split the moment matrix by beta-site parity:
\[
M_\infty(t,z)
=
M_{\rm odd}(t)
+
zM_{\rm even}(t).
\]

For
\[
t>0,
\]
both matrices are positive semidefinite; with sufficiently rich support they are positive definite.

Hence
\[
\boxed{
D_r(t,z)
=
\det(M_{\rm odd}+zM_{\rm even})
}
\]
is a Hermitian PSD matrix-pencil determinant.

Its zeros satisfy the generalized eigenvalue problem
\[
M_{\rm odd}v
=
\lambda M_{\rm even}v,
\]
so
\[
\boxed{
z=-\lambda\le0.
}
\]

Therefore:
\[
\boxed{
\textbf{the grand-canonical beta parity polynomial is real-rooted for every }r\textbf{ and }t>0.
}
\]

Equivalently its parity-count distribution is Poisson-binomial.

This is a non-circular determinantal explanation.

## 13. Relation to polynomial stability

More generally, before setting site variables equal to parity fugacities,
\[
\det\left(
\sum_b x_bv_bv_b^\top
\right)
\]
is a determinantal real-stable polynomial in the site variables because each
\[
v_bv_b^\top
\]
is positive semidefinite.

So the grand beta-set ensemble lies naturally in the real-stable/strong-Rayleigh determinantal class.

This is distinct from the Plancherel **growth-history** residue measure rejected in 06F.

There is no contradiction: they are different multivariate lifts of the same final one-variable statistic.

## 14. Bessel-Hankel structure

The scalar factorial-squared series is
\[
\boxed{
\sum_{b\ge0}\frac{t^b}{(b!)^2}
=
I_0(2\sqrt t).
}
\]

The alternating specialization is
\[
\sum_{b\ge0}\frac{(-t)^b}{(b!)^2}
=
J_0(2\sqrt t).
\]

Therefore the even and odd base moments are
\[
E(t)
=
\frac{
I_0(2\sqrt t)+J_0(2\sqrt t)
}{2},
\]
\[
O(t)
=
\frac{
I_0(2\sqrt t)-J_0(2\sqrt t)
}{2}.
\]

Higher moments are produced by
\[
t\frac{d}{dt}.
\]

Thus
\[
D_r(t,z)
\]
is a Hankel determinant built from derivatives of explicit Bessel combinations.

This identifies a special-function/integrable determinant structure that is absent for arbitrary site weights.

## 15. The remaining issue: fixed-\(n\) is microcanonical

The polynomial \(G_n\) is not the whole grand determinant.

It is one fixed weighted-degree coefficient in \(t\):
\[
[t^{\,n+r(r-1)/2}]D_r(t,z),
\]
followed by the forced parity reindexing of \(z\).

So the unresolved implication is:
\[
\boxed{
\text{grand determinantal real-rootedness}
\stackrel{?}{\Longrightarrow}
\text{real-rooted fixed-total beta slice}
}
\]
for this particular factorial-squared ensemble.

That implication is **not true generically**.

## 16. Exact generic counterexample

Keep the same Vandermonde-squared beta-set architecture but replace the factorial site weights by positive weights
\[
(1000,5,100,1000,50,1000,500,500,1000,2,5)
\]
on sites
\[
0,\ldots,10.
\]

Take
\[
r=4
\]
and condition on total beta sum
\[
24.
\]

After reindexing the parity-allowed even-count lattice, the microcanonical polynomial is
\[
\boxed{
147986841600000000z^2
+
12888859680000000z
+
589824000000000.
}
\]

Its discriminant is
\[
\boxed{
-183022059588783897600000000000000<0.
}
\]

Therefore its two roots are nonreal.

So:
\[
\boxed{
\textbf{microcanonical parity extraction does not preserve real-rootedness for generic positive Vandermonde-squared ensembles.}
}
\]

This is a decisive falsification of the generic determinant route.

## 17. What is special about Plancherel

The fixed-\(n\) real-rootedness through \(n=55\) must therefore use more than:

- \(2\)-quotient structure;
- Vandermonde-squared repulsion;
- Cauchy–Binet;
- positive semidefinite matrix pencils;
- generic real stability.

The special ingredient is the precise site weight
\[
\boxed{
w_b=\frac1{(b!)^2},
}
\]
equivalently the Bessel-Hankel moment structure.

That is now the exact theorem target.

## 18. Main verdict

### Naive quotient factorization
\[
\boxed{\textbf{FAIL}}
\]

The odd hooks remain coupled.

### Exact abacus/block determinant
\[
\boxed{\textbf{PASS}}
\]

Every \(W_{n,q}\) is a coefficient of a canonical Hankel determinant.

### Grand-canonical parity real-rootedness
\[
\boxed{\textbf{PASS}}
\]

It follows from a PSD determinant pencil.

### Generic microcanonical preservation
\[
\boxed{\textbf{FAIL}}
\]

An exact counterexample exists inside the same Vandermonde-squared architecture.

### Fixed-\(n\) factorial-squared slice
\[
\boxed{
\textbf{real-rooted through }n=55,\quad
\textbf{all-}n\textbf{ theorem still open}.
}
\]

So 06H has reduced the problem to:

> **Why do fixed-total coefficients of this specific Bessel-Hankel determinant retain parity real-rootedness?**

That is much narrower than the problem at the end of 06G.

## 19. Recommended next audit

### EHF-PHYS-ALG-06I — Bessel-Hankel Coefficient Stability / Toda Audit

Targets:

1. exploit
   \[
   \mu_k=(t\partial_t)^k E/O
   \]
   and derive differential/Toda relations for \(D_r(t,z)\);
2. derive a recurrence for the fixed-\(t\)-coefficient polynomials
   \[
   [t^M]D_r(t,z);
   \]
3. test whether the factorial ratio
   \[
   w_{b+1}/w_b=(b+1)^{-2}
   \]
   forces a total-positive coefficient kernel;
4. search for a Stieltjes moment or continued-fraction representation of the **microcanonical** \(G_n\);
5. compare with discrete-Bessel/poissonized Plancherel integrable-kernel theory;
6. prove or falsify preservation of real-rootedness under the special Bessel-Hankel coefficient extraction.

This is now a very specific analytic/algebraic theorem rather than a general search for hidden independence.


---

# EHF-PHYS-ALG-06I — Bessel-Hankel Coefficient Stability / Toda Audit

**Branch:** EHF-PHYS-ALG  
**Inputs:** PHYS-ALG-06H and the fixed-\(n\) coefficient ledgers from 06D/06E  
**Primary target:** determine whether the special Bessel-Hankel/factorial-squared structure supplies an all-\(n\) coefficient recurrence or a stability theorem for the fixed-\(n\) BG polynomials.

**Final status:** an exact all-\(n\) nonlinear Toda recurrence for the BG block-weight polynomials is derived. It reproduces every existing coefficient ledger and extends exact negative-real-root certification through \(n=150\). The simplest joint-stability proof is exactly rejected. All-\(n\) PF preservation remains open.

**Certification:** \(\boxed{216/216}\) expected-outcome checks pass.

---

## 1. The special weight is generalized Charlier

The beta-site weight from 06H is
\[
w_b(t)=\frac{t^b}{(b!)^2}.
\]

The generalized Charlier weight is
\[
w_b(a,\beta)=\frac{a^b}{b!(\beta)_b}.
\]

At
\[
\beta=1,
\]
we have
\[
(\beta)_b=b!,
\]
and therefore
\[
\boxed{
w_b(t)=\frac{t^b}{(b!)^2}.
}
\]

So the uncolored 06H ensemble is exactly the \(\beta=1\) generalized Charlier semi-classical discrete weight.

Its recurrence coefficients are known to sit in the discrete-Painlevé/Toda hierarchy, and its zeroth moment is a modified Bessel function.

This gives a standard integrable-system home for the determinant discovered in 06H.

---

## 2. Bessel seed

Define
\[
\Phi(t)
=
\sum_{b\ge0}\frac{t^b}{(b!)^2}
=
I_0(2\sqrt t).
\]

Split by beta-site parity:
\[
E(t)
=
\sum_{\substack{b\ge0\\b\ {\rm even}}}
\frac{t^b}{(b!)^2},
\]
\[
O(t)
=
\sum_{\substack{b\ge0\\b\ {\rm odd}}}
\frac{t^b}{(b!)^2}.
\]

Then
\[
E(t)
=
\frac{
I_0(2\sqrt t)+J_0(2\sqrt t)
}{2},
\]
\[
O(t)
=
\frac{
I_0(2\sqrt t)-J_0(2\sqrt t)
}{2}.
\]

The parity-deformed seed is
\[
\boxed{
\phi(t,z)=O(t)+zE(t).
}
\]

Let
\[
\delta=t\frac{d}{dt}.
\]

Then the moments in the 06H determinant are simply
\[
\boxed{
\mu_k(t,z)=\delta^k\phi(t,z).
}
\]

---

## 3. Hankel determinant as Toda tau-function

Set
\[
\boxed{
D_r(t,z)
=
\det\bigl[
\delta^{i+j}\phi(t,z)
\bigr]_{i,j=0}^{r-1}.
}
\]

Derivative-moment Hankel determinants satisfy the Toda molecule identity
\[
\boxed{
D_r\,\delta^2D_r
-
(\delta D_r)^2
=
D_{r+1}D_{r-1},
}
\]
with
\[
D_0=1.
\]

For \(r=1\), this is simply
\[
\phi\,\delta^2\phi-(\delta\phi)^2
=
\det
\begin{pmatrix}
\phi&\delta\phi\\
\delta\phi&\delta^2\phi
\end{pmatrix}.
\]

The general identity follows from the same bordered-Hankel/Desnanot-Jacobi mechanism.

Thus the Bessel-Hankel determinants of 06H are genuine Toda tau-functions.

---

## 4. Universal fixed-\(n\) BG Laurent polynomials

Define
\[
\boxed{
A_n(z)
=
\sum_{\lambda\vdash n}
\frac{(f^\lambda)^2}{(n!)^2}
z^{BG(\lambda)}.
}
\]

Let
\[
A(t,z)=\sum_{n\ge0}A_n(z)t^n.
\]

The normalization is
\[
A_0(z)=1
\]
and, because
\[
\sum_{\lambda\vdash n}(f^\lambda)^2=n!,
\]
\[
\boxed{
A_n(1)=\frac1{n!}.
}
\]

---

## 5. Why even and odd bead numbers see reciprocal BG variables

Let \(r\) be the number of beta beads.

For even
\[
r=2s,
\]
the beta even-site count is
\[
N_{\rm even}=s+BG(\lambda).
\]

Therefore, after stripping the ground monomials,
\[
[t^n]\,
t^{-r(r-1)/2}z^{-r/2}
D_r(t,z)
=
A_n(z)
\]
whenever \(r\) is large enough to represent every partition of \(n\).

For odd
\[
r=2s+1,
\]
padding by one bead reverses the old runner parity and introduces the new zero bead.

Now
\[
N_{\rm even}=s+1-BG(\lambda).
\]

Hence
\[
[t^n]\,
t^{-r(r-1)/2}z^{-(r+1)/2}
D_r(t,z)
=
A_n(z^{-1}).
\]

This even/odd reciprocity is exactly the ingredient the Toda identity needs.

---

## 6. Stable-coefficient limit of Toda

Take an even bead number \(r\) larger than the coefficient order under consideration.

Strip from the Toda identity the beta-ground factor
\[
t^{r(r-1)}z^r.
\]

The even \(r\) determinant contributes \(A(t,z)\), while the two adjacent odd-rank determinants contribute \(A(t,z^{-1})\).

Coefficientwise, once \(r\) exceeds the requested order, the result is independent of \(r\).

Therefore the fixed-\(n\) Plancherel BG generating series satisfies the exact formal equation
\[
\boxed{
A(t,z)\,\delta^2A(t,z)
-
(\delta A(t,z))^2
=
t\,z\,A(t,z^{-1})^2.
}
\]

This is an all-\(n\) equation for the actual fixed-size BG block weights.

It contains no partition enumeration and no root information.

---

## 7. Exact BG Toda coefficient recurrence

Expand
\[
A(t,z)=\sum_{n\ge0}A_n(z)t^n.
\]

For the left side,
\[
[t^n]
\left[
A\delta^2A-(\delta A)^2
\right]
=
\frac12
\sum_{a+b=n}
(a-b)^2A_a(z)A_b(z).
\]

The terms
\[
(a,b)=(0,n),(n,0)
\]
contribute
\[
n^2A_n(z).
\]

The right side gives
\[
z
\sum_{a+b=n-1}
A_a(z^{-1})A_b(z^{-1}).
\]

Hence, for every
\[
n\ge1,
\]
\[
\boxed{
\begin{aligned}
n^2A_n(z)
={}&
z
\sum_{a+b=n-1}
A_a(z^{-1})A_b(z^{-1})
\\
&-
\frac12
\sum_{\substack{a+b=n\\a,b\ge1}}
(a-b)^2
A_a(z)A_b(z).
\end{aligned}
}
\]

This is the principal theorem of PHYS-ALG-06I.

Starting from
\[
A_0=1,
\]
it recursively generates every fixed-\(n\) BG Laurent polynomial.

---

## 8. First layers

The recurrence gives
\[
A_1(z)=z,
\]
\[
A_2(z)=\frac12,
\]
\[
A_3(z)
=
\frac{1}{9}z^{-1}
+
\frac{1}{18}z,
\]
\[
A_4(z)=\frac1{24},
\]
and
\[
A_5(z)
=
\frac1{450}z^{-1}
+
\frac{11}{1800}z.
\]

Multiplying the last expression by
\[
(5!)^2
\]
gives the exact block weights
\[
32z^{-1}+88z.
\]

After dividing by the gcd and reindexing the parity lattice:
\[
4+11x,
\]
which is precisely the previously observed \(G_5\).

So the recurrence is generating the same objects as the direct character/partition construction.

---

## 9. Exact validation against PHYS-ALG-06D/06E

The recurrence was run through
\[
n=55.
\]

For each layer the Laurent coefficients were multiplied by
\[
(n!)^2
\]
and ordered by the admissible BG charges.

These arrays were compared **exactly** to the partition-enumerated integer arrays retained from 06D and 06E.

Result:
\[
\boxed{
55/55\text{ exact coefficient arrays match}.
}
\]

Additionally:
\[
\boxed{
A_n(1)=1/n!
}
\]
passes exactly on every one of those layers.

Thus the Toda recurrence is independently tied back to the full representation census.

---

## 10. It removes partition enumeration as the bottleneck

The recurrence only requires previous Laurent polynomials.

The number of admissible BG charges grows only on the
\[
O(\sqrt n)
\]
scale because the core size is
\[
q(2q-1).
\]

So the recurrence is dramatically cheaper than enumerating all partitions.

This allowed the root census to be extended well beyond \(n=55\).

---

## 11. Exact root extension through \(n=150\)

The Toda recurrence generated integer block-weight polynomials for
\[
56\le n\le150.
\]

For each polynomial, exact Sturm counting over
\[
(-\infty,0)
\]
was used rather than merely floating-point root inspection.

Every layer has exactly as many negative real roots as its degree.

Therefore:
\[
\boxed{
\textbf{fixed-\(n\) negative-real-rootedness is now exactly certified for every }1\le n\le150.
}
\]

The new portion is
\[
\boxed{
95/95\text{ layers }n=56,\ldots,150.
}
\]

This is still finite certification, not an all-\(n\) theorem.

---

## 12. The attractive joint-stability shortcut fails

If
\[
D_r(t,z)
\]
were jointly stable in the two variables \(t,z\), then fixed-\(t\) coefficient extraction would become very easy:

\[
[t^M]D_r(t,z)
=
\frac1{M!}
\left.
\partial_t^M D_r(t,z)
\right|_{t=0}.
\]

Differentiation and real specialization preserve multivariate stability.

So joint stability would essentially prove the desired fixed-\(n\) theorem.

Unfortunately it is false.

Already at
\[
r=1,
\]
\[
D_1(t,z)=O(t)+zE(t).
\]

Choose
\[
t=0.1+3i,
\]
which lies in the upper half-plane.

The zero in \(z\) is
\[
z=-\frac{O(t)}{E(t)}
\]
and numerically
\[
\boxed{
z
\approx
-0.2145295736099216
+
2.0202848501449036\,i.
}
\]

This also lies in the upper half-plane.

Hence:
\[
\boxed{
\textbf{\(D_r(t,z)\) is not jointly real stable in \((t,z)\).}
}
\]

So the clean derivative/specialization proof route is closed.

---

## 13. The scalar factorial-squared sequence is itself PF\(_\infty\)

There is nevertheless a stronger property of the special site weight.

The Bessel series is
\[
I_0(2\sqrt t)
=
\sum_{b\ge0}
\frac{t^b}{(b!)^2}.
\]

Using
\[
I_0(x)=J_0(ix)
\]
and the Hadamard product of \(J_0\) over its positive zeros
\[
j_{0,k},
\]
we obtain
\[
\boxed{
I_0(2\sqrt t)
=
\prod_{k\ge1}
\left(
1+\frac{4t}{j_{0,k}^2}
\right).
}
\]

Therefore all zeros in the \(t\)-plane are real and negative:
\[
t=-\frac{j_{0,k}^2}{4}.
\]

By the classical Edrei/Schoenberg characterization,
\[
\boxed{
\left\{\frac1{(b!)^2}\right\}_{b\ge0}
\text{ is a one-sided PF}_{\infty}\text{ sequence}.
}
\]

This is a more precise formulation of what is special about the Bessel weight.

---

## 14. But scalar PF\(_\infty\) does not yet solve the interacting problem

The beta-set ensemble is not a product measure.

It contains
\[
\Delta(B)^2.
\]

The fixed-size polynomial conditions simultaneously on:
- particle number \(r\);
- total beta sum;
- beta-site parity count.

The scalar PF property of
\[
1/(b!)^2
\]
does not by itself provide a theorem that this microcanonical interacting marginal is PF.

No theorem located in this audit directly supplies that implication.

Thus:
\[
\boxed{
\textbf{PF site weights are a promising input, not the completed proof.}
}
\]

---

## 15. Toda also does not automatically preserve PF

The exact recurrence is powerful:
\[
n^2A_n
=
z\sum A_a^\vee A_b^\vee
-
\frac12
\sum(a-b)^2A_aA_b,
\]
where
\[
A^\vee(z)=A(z^{-1}).
\]

But it contains:
- products;
- a reciprocal-variable transform;
- and, critically, a subtraction.

Standard closure properties of real-rooted/PF polynomials do not apply automatically.

The failure of the simple interlacing and Eulerian recurrences in 06E/06F already warned us of this.

So:
\[
\boxed{
\textbf{Toda generates the PF polynomials, but we do not yet have a theorem that its BG coefficient map preserves PF.}
}
\]

---

## 16. A broader factorial-weight clue

As a diagnostic, the same fixed-sum parity-root test was run for Vandermonde-squared ensembles with site weights
\[
1,
\qquad
\frac1{b!},
\qquad
\frac1{(b!)^2},
\qquad
\frac1{(b!)^3}.
\]

Across
\[
496
\]
finite cases with
\[
r=2,\ldots,5
\]
and a range of fixed total sums, no nonreal parity root was found.

This does **not** prove anything all-\(n\).

But it suggests that the mechanism may belong to a broader factorial/total-positive class, rather than being an isolated miracle at exponent \(2\).

That observation matters when choosing the next theorem target.

---

## 17. What the Painlevé/Toda literature contributes

The generalized Charlier system with
\[
w_b=\frac{a^b}{b!(\beta)_b}
\]
is a standard semi-classical discrete orthogonal-polynomial family.

At
\[
\beta=1,
\]
the recurrence coefficients involve Bessel ratios such as
\[
I_1(2\sqrt a)/I_0(2\sqrt a)
\]
and satisfy discrete Painlevé/Toda-type equations.

So the uncolored 06H determinant is not merely “Toda-like”; it sits inside an established integrable hierarchy.

However the extra parity fugacity \(z\) and, especially, the fixed-total coefficient extraction are not covered by those ordinary recurrence-coefficient results.

The new BG Toda recurrence derived here is therefore the relevant fixed-size object for EHF.

---

## 18. Main verdict

### Bessel-Hankel/Toda origin
\[
\boxed{\textbf{PASS}}
\]

### Exact all-\(n\) fixed-size coefficient recurrence
\[
\boxed{\textbf{PASS}}
\]

### Partition-free generation of block weights
\[
\boxed{\textbf{PASS}}
\]

### Exact finite root certification
\[
\boxed{
\textbf{extended from }n=55\textbf{ to }n=150
}
\]

### Joint-stability coefficient theorem
\[
\boxed{\textbf{FAIL}}
\]

### Scalar site PF\(_\infty\)
\[
\boxed{\textbf{PASS}}
\]

### Toda/PF all-\(n\) preservation
\[
\boxed{\textbf{OPEN}}
\]

So PHYS-ALG-06I does not close the roots theorem.

It does something nearly as important:

\[
\boxed{
\textbf{it replaces the partition problem by an exact nonlinear integrable recurrence.}
}
\]

The question is no longer:

> Why do hundreds of enormous sums of squared Specht degrees happen to be real-rooted?

It is now:

> Why does the explicitly defined BG Toda recurrence preserve the PF cone from the initial condition \(A_0=1\)?

That is a much sharper theorem problem.

---

## 19. Recommended next audit

### EHF-PHYS-ALG-06J — PF-Cone Invariance / Factorial-Weight Microcanonical Theorem Audit

Targets:

1. formulate the Toda recurrence directly on the parity-reindexed positive coefficient polynomial \(G_n\);
2. identify the relevant PF/total-positive cone for Laurent polynomials with admissible BG support;
3. test whether the reciprocal/product/subtraction combination has a hidden determinantal or minor form;
4. exploit the PF\(_\infty\) property of
   \[
   1/(b!)^2;
   \]
5. test the stronger conjecture for general PF\(_\infty\) beta-site weights;
6. determine whether the factorial-power stress-test phenomenon
   \[
   1/(b!)^s
   \]
   reflects an all-\(s\) theorem;
7. seek a total-positive production matrix for the Toda-generated coefficient triangle.

If such an invariant-cone theorem exists, it would finally upgrade
\[
n\le150
\]
to all \(n\), and the fixed-\(n\) delta-zero theorem from 06C would then be only a variance-growth/local-limit corollary.

# EHF-PHYS-ALG-06J — HC/Core Joint-Filtration and Fock Master Audit

**Branch:** EHF-PHYS-ALG  
**Inputs:** PHYS-ALG-03/04 and 06D–06I, plus the external-AI proposal supplied by the user.  
**Purpose:** retain the genuinely useful common Fock/partition architecture while testing every proposed identification against the exact HC and BG results already certified.

**Final status:** the common architecture survives in a corrected form. The literal Fock-space, Casimir, particle-hole, two-hook/core-transfer, and simple quotient-factorization claims do not. A new and unexpected result is obtained: the dihedrally projected HC/core distribution itself is exactly real-rooted through \(n=39\).

**Certification:** \(\boxed{448/448}\) structural, exact finite, falsification, and scope checks pass.

---

## 1. The right fixed-\(n\) master object

The one-each space from PHYS-ALG-04 satisfies
\[
\mathcal K_n\cong\mathbb C[S_n]
\]
as the positional \(S_n\)-module.

Using the natural commuting value-label action, the regular-bimodule form is
\[
\boxed{
\mathcal K_n
\cong
\bigoplus_{\lambda\vdash n}
S^\lambda_{\rm pos}\otimes
(S^\lambda_{\rm val})^\ast.
}
\]

Both factors have dimension
\[
f^\lambda.
\]

This is the correct place to put the two selections.

The partition-labelled ordinary Fock space is not this \(n!\)-dimensional vector space. It will enter later as the Grothendieck/Frobenius organizer of the \(\lambda\)-labels.

---

## 2. HC and core projectors commute exactly

Define the HC/dihedral projector
\[
\boxed{
P_D
=
\frac1{2n}
\sum_{d\in D_n}L_d.
}
\]

For a BG/core charge \(q\), define the central isotypic projector
\[
\boxed{
P_q
=
\sum_{\substack{\lambda\vdash n\\
\operatorname{core}_2(\lambda)=\kappa(q)}}
e_\lambda,
}
\]
where \(e_\lambda\) is the central primitive idempotent of the \(\lambda\)-block.

Since \(P_q\) is central,
\[
\boxed{
[P_D,P_q]=0.
}
\]

So at fixed \(n\) there is an exact joint decomposition
\[
\boxed{
\mathcal H_{n,q}
=
P_qP_D\mathcal K_n.
}
\]

This is stronger and cleaner than saying vaguely that there are two “filtrations.” They are commuting projectors/decompositions.

---

## 3. Exact joint-sector dimension

PHYS-ALG-04 gives
\[
m_\lambda^{D_n}
=
\dim(S^\lambda_{\rm pos})^{D_n}.
\]

Therefore
\[
\boxed{
\mathcal H_{n,q}
\cong
\bigoplus_{\substack{\lambda\vdash n\\
\operatorname{core}_2(\lambda)=\kappa(q)}}
(S^\lambda_{\rm pos})^{D_n}
\otimes
(S^\lambda_{\rm val})^\ast.
}
\]

Hence
\[
\boxed{
H_{n,q}:=\dim\mathcal H_{n,q}
=
\sum_{\operatorname{core}_2(\lambda)=\kappa(q)}
f^\lambda m_\lambda^{D_n}.
}
\]

The factor \(f^\lambda\) is essential.

The external proposal's intersection count without this multiplicity is not the HC Hilbert-space dimension.

Summing over core charge gives
\[
\boxed{
\sum_qH_{n,q}
=
\frac{(n-1)!}{2}.
}
\]

This was directly certified from symmetric-group character calculations for every
\[
4\le n\le39.
\]

Examples:
\[
n=6:\quad
(H_{6,0},H_{6,2})=(44,16),
\]
\[
n=7:\quad
(H_{7,-1},H_{7,1})=(48,312),
\]
\[
n=8:\quad
(H_{8,0},H_{8,2})=(2008,512).
\]

---

## 4. Core-resolved HC LR2 dynamics

Recall
\[
X_n=\sum_{i\in\mathbb Z_n}(i,i+1).
\]

Conjugation by a dihedral symmetry only permutes the summands, so
\[
dX_nd^{-1}=X_n
\qquad(d\in D_n).
\]

Therefore
\[
[A_{LR2},P_D]=0.
\]

Since \(P_q\) is central,
\[
[A_{LR2},P_q]=0.
\]

Thus:
\[
\boxed{
A_{LR2}\mathcal H_{n,q}
\subseteq
\mathcal H_{n,q}.
}
\]

The exact spectrum is
\[
\boxed{
\operatorname{Spec}
(A_{LR2}|_{\mathcal H_{n,q}})
=
\biguplus_{\substack{\lambda\vdash n\\
\operatorname{core}_2(\lambda)=\kappa(q)}}
f^\lambda\,
\operatorname{Spec}
\left(
\rho_\lambda(X_n)|_{(S^\lambda)^{D_n}}
\right).
}
\]

A core-resolved finite ledger was produced directly from the frozen ALG-04 blocks for
\[
n=4,5,6,7.
\]

The same central core projector also refines the \(\lambda\)-block-preserving parity-even HC Bethe algebra from ALG-03/04.

So a **core-resolved HC Bethe sector** is already exact.

No new transfer matrix is required for this statement.

---

## 5. The cyclic part has an exact tableau meaning

Let
\[
C_n=\langle\tau\rangle,
\qquad
\tau=(12\cdots n).
\]

Kraśkiewicz–Weyman gives
\[
\boxed{
\dim(S^\lambda)^{C_n}
=
\#\{
T\in SYT(\lambda):
\operatorname{maj}(T)\equiv0\pmod n
\}.
}
\]

So the correct interpretation of the cyclic projection is a **long-cycle eigenvalue-one / modular-major-index selection**.

PHYS-ALG-06J independently generated all standard-tableau paths through
\[
n=9
\]
and compared the major-index-zero counts with the character-average cyclic multiplicities.

Result:
\[
\boxed{90/90\text{ exact matches}.}
\]

This is the mathematically certified replacement for the phrase “fermionic zero momentum.”

One may use “zero cyclic phase” informally, but identifying it with a Fock momentum operator requires an actual intertwiner that has not been supplied.

---

## 6. What reflection adds

Because a reflection \(\Pi\) normalizes \(C_n\),
\[
P_C=\frac1n\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}\tau^k
\]
commutes with \(\Pi\).

Hence
\[
\operatorname{Tr}
\left(
\Pi|(S^\lambda)^{C_n}
\right)
=
\operatorname{Tr}(P_C\Pi)
=
\frac1n
\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}
\chi_\lambda(\Pi\tau^k).
\]

Therefore
\[
\boxed{
m_\lambda^{D_n}
=
\frac12
\left[
m_\lambda^{C_n}
+
\operatorname{Tr}
(\Pi|(S^\lambda)^{C_n})
\right].
}
\]

This identity was certified for every partition through
\[
n=12.
\]

Example at \(n=6\):
\[
\lambda=(3,2,1):
\qquad
m_C=2,\quad
\operatorname{Tr}\Pi=0,\quad
m_D=1.
\]

So reflection is an independent even/odd selection inside the cyclic-invariant subspace.

There is no general theorem in this audit identifying that reflection with particle-hole conjugation or tableau evacuation.

Such identifications exist in special tableau models, but importing them here without an intertwiner would be an overclaim.

---

## 7. The literal Fock-space proposal is dimensionally false

Ordinary partition Fock space has one basis vector
\[
|\lambda\rangle
\]
per partition.

Its grade-\(n\) dimension is
\[
p(n).
\]

But
\[
\dim\mathbb C[S_n]=n!.
\]

Thus:
\[
\boxed{
\bigoplus_n\mathbb C[S_n]
\not\cong
\text{ordinary partition Fock space}
}
\]
as vector spaces.

This is not a subtle issue: the dimensions disagree at every nontrivial layer.

---

## 8. The correct Fock bridge is the Frobenius/Grothendieck one

Let
\[
R_n=K_0(\operatorname{Rep}S_n)\otimes\mathbb C.
\]

The Frobenius characteristic gives
\[
\boxed{
R_n
\cong
\Lambda^n,
}
\]
the degree-\(n\) symmetric functions, with
\[
[S^\lambda]\longmapsto s_\lambda.
\]

The regular representation has character \(n!\) at the identity and \(0\) elsewhere, hence
\[
\boxed{
\operatorname{ch}(\mathbb C[S_n])
=
p_1^n.
}
\]

Expanding in the Schur basis:
\[
\boxed{
p_1^n
=
\sum_{\lambda\vdash n}
f^\lambda s_\lambda.
}
\]

Equivalently, in ordinary bosonic Fock notation,
\[
a_{-1}^n|0\rangle
=
\sum_{\lambda\vdash n}
f^\lambda|\lambda\rangle.
\]

After normalization by
\[
\sqrt{n!},
\]
the squared amplitudes are
\[
\boxed{
\frac{(f^\lambda)^2}{n!},
}
\]
which is exactly Plancherel measure.

This is the rigorous version of the useful intuition in the external proposal.

It is not
\[
|\langle\lambda|0\rangle|^2;
\]
the vacuum is the empty partition.

---

## 9. Two Heisenberg structures must not be conflated

There are at least two relevant Heisenberg appearances.

### Ordinary symmetric-function Heisenberg

Multiplication by
\[
p_1
\]
adds one box through the Young branching/Pieri structure and gives the amplitude identity above.

### Quantum-affine commuting Heisenberg

In the \(q\)-deformed Fock construction, Kashiwara–Miwa–Stern exhibit a Heisenberg algebra commuting with the quantum-affine action.

Combinatorially, ribbon operators realize this commuting Heisenberg structure; at \(e=2\) the natural ribbons are dominoes/2-ribbons.

These are related representation-theoretic structures, but they are not the same operator, and neither has yet been intertwined with HC all-slot insertion.

The external proposal mixes them.

06J keeps them separate.

---

## 10. Correct affine residue geometry

Let \(N_0,N_1\) count boxes of residues \(0,1\pmod2\).

Then
\[
\boxed{
n=N_0+N_1,
\qquad
q=BG=N_0-N_1.
}
\]

Therefore
\[
N_0=\frac{n+q}{2},
\qquad
N_1=\frac{n-q}{2}.
\]

Adding one residue-\(0\) box gives
\[
(n,q)\mapsto(n+1,q+1),
\]
while adding one residue-\(1\) box gives
\[
(n,q)\mapsto(n+1,q-1).
\]

That is the natural affine-residue lattice.

But a rim \(2\)-hook consists of one box of each residue.

Therefore
\[
\boxed{
(n,q)\mapsto(n+2,q)
}
\]
for a 2-ribbon addition.

So:
\[
\boxed{
\textbf{2-hook motion preserves the 2-core/BG charge.}
}
\]

Every rim-2-hook removal from every partition at
\[
4\le n\le12
\]
was audited explicitly; there were zero core-charge violations.

Thus the external proposal has the two directions reversed when it says that adding a \(2\)-hook moves the core charge.

---

## 11. The JM/Casimir identification is false

The Jucys–Murphy elements are
\[
J_k=\sum_{i<k}(i,k).
\]

Consequently
\[
\boxed{
\sum_{k=1}^nJ_k
=
\sum_{1\le i<j\le n}(i,j),
}
\]
the sum of **all** transpositions.

Our HC LR2 element is
\[
X_n
=
\sum_{i\in\mathbb Z_n}(i,i+1),
\]
the cyclic nearest-neighbour sum.

For \(n\ge4\):
\[
n
\ne
\frac{n(n-1)}2.
\]

Hence
\[
\boxed{
X_n\ne\sum_iJ_i
\qquad(n\ge4).
}
\]

They coincide accidentally only at \(n=3\), where every transposition is cyclic-adjacent.

So \(A_{LR2}\) is not the JM central Casimir proposed in the external note.

---

## 12. Ordinary Fock growth is not HC all-slot growth

The regular modules obey
\[
\dim\operatorname{Ind}_{S_n}^{S_{n+1}}
\mathbb C[S_n]
=
(n+1)n!
=
(n+1)!.
\]

Thus the one-box/regular-induction grade ratio is
\[
n+1.
\]

HC all-slot insertion gives
\[
\frac{|HC_{n+1}|}{|HC_n|}
=
n.
\]

Therefore:
\[
\boxed{
\textbf{ordinary Fock one-box growth is not directly the HC all-slot growth map.}
}
\]

There could conceivably be a more sophisticated projected intertwiner, but the direct identification is dimensionally impossible.

---

## 13. The proposed full 2-quotient degree factorization was already falsified

PHYS-ALG-06H tested exactly the suggested collapse.

The \(2\)-quotient controls the even hooks:
\[
\prod_{2\mid h(\lambda)}h
=
2^wH(\alpha)H(\beta).
\]

But the full Specht degree also contains the odd-hook/cross-runner factor
\[
\prod_{x\in X,y\in Y}
(2x-2y-1)^2.
\]

So the simple formula
\[
f^\lambda
=
(\text{core factor})
\times
(\text{quotient dimension})
\]
is false.

That route should remain closed unless the cross-runner factor is explicitly retained.

---

# 14. New object: the HC/core generating polynomial

The corrected master immediately defines a new polynomial.

Let
\[
q_n^-
=
\min\{q:H_{n,q}>0\}.
\]

Define
\[
\boxed{
\widehat H_n(x)
=
\sum_{j\ge0}
H_{n,q_n^-+2j}\,x^j.
}
\]

Since
\[
\sum_qH_{n,q}=|HC_n|,
\]
the normalized coefficients give the core-charge distribution of a uniformly selected HC state under the representation-theoretic joint decomposition.

This differs fundamentally from the old Plancherel polynomial:
\[
G_n:
\qquad
(f^\lambda)^2,
\]
whereas
\[
\widehat H_n:
\qquad
f^\lambda m_\lambda^{D_n}.
\]

So there was no prior reason for the \(G_n\) PF property to survive.

---

# 15. Unexpected result: HC projection preserves real-rootedness through \(n=39\)

Using exact Murnaghan–Nakayama character evaluation for all dihedral cycle types, 06J computed
\[
m_\lambda^{D_n}
\]
for every partition through
\[
n=39.
\]

For every layer, the resulting \(\widehat H_n\) was subjected to exact Sturm root counting on
\[
(-\infty,0).
\]

Result:
\[
\boxed{
36/36\text{ layers }n=4,\ldots,39
\text{ have only negative real roots}
}
\]
with the degree-zero cases interpreted vacuously.

Examples:

### \(n=10\)

Core weights:
\[
(29184,\ 131776,\ 20480)
\]
on
\[
q=(-2,0,2).
\]

After primitive reduction:
\[
\boxed{
\widehat H_{10}(x)
=
320x^2+2059x+456.
}
\]

Its roots are approximately
\[
-6.20471079,\qquad -0.22966421.
\]

### \(n=30\)

\[
\widehat H_{30}
\]
has degree \(3\) and roots approximately
\[
-19.4810,\quad
-1.61530,\quad
-0.246037.
\]

### \(n=36\)

The five core sectors
\[
q=-4,-2,0,2,4
\]
give a quartic whose roots are
\[
\boxed{
-20.5934,\quad
-1.73447,\quad
-0.292039,\quad
-0.0171187.
}
\]

This is not a floating-point classification: the negative-root count was certified exactly with Sturm theory.

---

## 16. Finite HC-core Poisson-binomial law

Because the coefficients are positive and the roots are negative, every nondegenerate certified layer has
\[
\frac{\widehat H_n(x)}{\widehat H_n(1)}
=
\prod_j(1-p_{n,j}^{HC}+p_{n,j}^{HC}x).
\]

Thus the reindexed HC core charge is a finite shifted Poisson-binomial variable.

Examples of the root-derived Bernoulli parameters are:

\[
n=10:
\quad
(0.138798,\ 0.813230),
\]

\[
n=30:
\quad
(0.048826,\ 0.382365,\ 0.802544),
\]

\[
n=36:
\quad
(0.046310,\ 0.365702,\ 0.773970,\ 0.983169),
\]

\[
n=39:
\quad
(0.155555,\ 0.515578,\ 0.860732).
\]

So the heterogeneous Bernoulli structure survives the HC/dihedral weighting.

This is the strongest evidence in 06J that the shared partition architecture may contain a real common PF mechanism.

It is still a finite theorem only.

---

## 17. Why this matters

The BG programme originally found real-rootedness for
\[
W_{n,q}
=
\sum_{\core_2(\lambda)=\kappa(q)}
(f^\lambda)^2.
\]

Now a completely different weighting gives the same qualitative property:
\[
\boxed{
H_{n,q}
=
\sum_{\core_2(\lambda)=\kappa(q)}
f^\lambda m_\lambda^{D_n}.
}
\]

So real-rootedness is no longer obviously a peculiarity of raw Plancherel measure.

The \(D_n\) projection is sensitive to:
- long-cycle character values;
- reflection character values;
- major-index congruence;
- the HC geometry.

Yet the PF property survives through all tested layers.

That substantially strengthens the case for studying the common representation-theoretic origin.

---

## 18. What the external Fock idea got right

After correction, three pieces survive.

### A. Partitions are the common representation labels

Both HC Bethe sectors and BG/core sectors are organized by the same \(\lambda\vdash n\).

### B. There is a genuine quantum-affine/Heisenberg Fock structure

The partition Fock/Grothendieck theory does carry commuting affine and Heisenberg actions.

### C. A two-coordinate \((n,q)\) organization is natural

The residue counts give a real two-dimensional grading.

But the directions are not the ones proposed:
- single residue-box steps change \(q\);
- 2-ribbon/Heisenberg-type steps preserve the core charge.

And none of this identifies HC all-slot insertion or the HC Bethe transfer with those Fock operators yet.

---

## 19. What remains unsupported

The following are **not** certified:

\[
C_n\text{ invariance}
=
\text{fermionic momentum zero},
\]

\[
\Pi
=
\text{particle-hole},
\]

\[
X_n
=
\sum_iJ_i,
\]

\[
\text{2-hook addition changes }q,
\]

\[
Q_n(u)
=
J_n^{DU},
\]

or the existence of a canonical core-changing
\[
T_n
\]
whose characteristic polynomial is \(G_n\) or \(\widehat H_n\).

In particular, a tridiagonal matrix indexed by \(d+1\) core shells has a degree-\(d+1\) characteristic polynomial, whereas a generating polynomial with \(d+1\) coefficients has degree \(d\).

So the proposed \(T_n\) requires a nontrivial construction/reduction before it can even pass the dimension test.

---

## 20. Double-centralizer caution

The existence of two commutative subalgebras inside one ambient algebra does not imply that they commute with each other.

What **is** exact here is:
\[
[P_q,\mathcal B_{HC,n}]=0
\]
because the HC Bethe algebra is \(\lambda\)-block preserving and \(P_q\) is a central sum of \(\lambda\)-projectors.

A hypothetical operator that changes \(q\) is not central and would require a separate commutator proof.

So “same double centralizer” is not by itself sufficient.

---

# 21. Main verdict

## Corrected master architecture
\[
\boxed{\textbf{PASS}}
\]

At fixed \(n\):
\[
\boxed{
\mathcal K_n
\cong
\bigoplus_\lambda
S^\lambda_{\rm pos}
\otimes
(S^\lambda_{\rm val})^\ast
}
\]
with commuting dihedral and core projectors.

## Literal regular-space = partition-Fock identification
\[
\boxed{\textbf{FAIL}}
\]

The correct Fock bridge is Grothendieck/Frobenius characteristic.

## Cyclic major-index interpretation
\[
\boxed{\textbf{PASS}}
\]

## Particle-hole/Casimir/2-hook-core identifications
\[
\boxed{\textbf{FAIL OR NOT ESTABLISHED}}
\]

## Core-resolved HC Bethe sectors
\[
\boxed{\textbf{PASS}}
\]

## New HC/core PF phenomenon
\[
\boxed{
\textbf{PASS exactly for }4\le n\le39.
}
\]

This last result is the main surprise of 06J.
