# EHF-PHYS-ALG-06P — Absolute-Character Ribbon-Tableau PF / Orientation Deformation Audit

**Branch:** EHF-PHYS-ALG  
**Inputs:** PHYS-ALG-06O, the signed-twist programme 06K–06L, and classical ribbon-tableau/Heisenberg theory.

**Final status:** the positive absolute-character family has a canonical spin-weighted ribbon-Heisenberg origin. The fixed-\(d\)-core ribbon graph is \(d\)-differential at \(q=1\). In the audited window, both the ribbon-path and mixed Young×ribbon BG polynomials are negative-real-rooted for the **entire parameter half-line \(q\ge0\)**. By contrast, neither the coarse orientation interpolation nor the natural continuation through negative spin weights stays hyperbolic all the way to the signed endpoint.

**Certification:** \(\boxed{3081/3081}\) expected-outcome, exact finite, falsification, and scope checks pass.

## 1. Positive ribbon-tableau interpretation

For \(n=dm\), \(d\) odd, and empty \(d\)-core,
\[
|\chi^\lambda(d^m)|=\#SRT_d(\lambda).
\]
Hence
\[
A_{n,d}(x)=
\sum_\lambda f^\lambda|\chi^\lambda(d^m)|x^{j(BG(\lambda))}
\]
counts pairs \((P,T)\) of an ordinary standard Young tableau and a standard \(d\)-ribbon tableau of common shape, graded by BG/core charge.

## 2. Canonical spin deformation

Define
\[
U_d(q)|\lambda\rangle
=
\sum_{\mu/\lambda\ d\text{-ribbon}}
q^{h(\mu/\lambda)}|\mu\rangle,
\qquad D_d(q)=U_d(q)^\ast.
\]
Then
\[
R^\lambda_{d,m}(q)
=
\langle\lambda|U_d(q)^m|\varnothing\rangle
=
\sum_{T\in SRT_d(\lambda)}q^{\operatorname{spin}(T)}.
\]
The mixed BG object is
\[
\widetilde A_{n,d}(t;q)
=
\langle\varnothing|D_1^n t^H U_d(q)^m|\varnothing\rangle
=
\sum_\lambda f^\lambda R^\lambda_{d,m}(q)t^{BG(\lambda)}.
\]
At the endpoints,
\[
R^\lambda(1)=|\chi^\lambda(d^m)|,
\qquad
R^\lambda(-1)=\chi^\lambda(d^m).
\]

## 3. Exact ribbon-Heisenberg relation

Ribbon Fock theory gives
\[
\boxed{
D_d(q)U_d(q)-U_d(q)D_d(q)
=
[d]_{q^2}I
=
(1+q^2+\cdots+q^{2(d-1)})I.
}
\]
06P independently verifies this symbolically in
\[
\boxed{177/177}
\]
states for \(d=3,5\).

At \(q=1\),
\[
DU-UD=dI,
\]
so each fixed-\(d\)-core ribbon cover graph is \(d\)-differential in the operator sense.

The usual path-norm consequence is
\[
\sum_\lambda R^\lambda_{d,m}(q)^2
=
m![d]_{q^2}^m.
\]

## 4. BG charge resolution

For odd \(d\), each \(d\)-ribbon changes BG by \(\pm1\). Therefore
\[
U_d(q)=U_+(q)+U_-(q),
\qquad
[H,U_\pm]=\pm U_\pm.
\]
The cross charge commutators vanish as the charge-\(\pm2\) components of the scalar Heisenberg relation.

However, the same-charge pieces do not reduce to functions of layer and BG alone. The finite symbolic audit finds
\[
\boxed{26}
\]
fixed \((d,m,BG,\pm)\) blocks with distinct diagonal coefficients on different partition shapes.

Thus the full Heisenberg relation is exact, but a coarse BG algebra is not.

## 5. Spin endpoint validation

Direct rim-hook recursion replaces the Murnaghan-Nakayama sign by a formal \(q^{\text{height}}\) weight.

Across odd divisors through \(n=24\),
\[
\boxed{2374/2374}
\]
contributing shapes satisfy
\[
R^\lambda(1)=|\chi^\lambda|,
\qquad
R^\lambda(-1)=\chi^\lambda.
\]

## 6. Main finite parameter result: \(q\ge0\)

For each audited \((n,d)\), root type can change only when the \(x\)-discriminant or leading \(x\)-coefficient vanishes as a function of \(q\). 06P isolates all such nonnegative critical values, checks each open cell with exact Sturm theory, and uses closure at the critical boundaries.

Result:
\[
\boxed{
25/25:
\quad
A_{n,d}(x;q)
\text{ has only negative real zeros for every }q\ge0
}
\]
for every audited odd-divisor family through \(n=24\).

This is a finite exact **whole-half-line** result, not a grid sample and not an all-\(n\) theorem.

## 7. The ribbon-path family is PF before the Young factor is inserted

Define
\[
R_{n,d}(x;q)
=
\sum_\lambda
R^\lambda_{d,m}(q)x^{j(BG(\lambda))}.
\]
The same parameter audit gives
\[
\boxed{
25/25:
\quad
R_{n,d}(x;q)
\text{ is negative-real-rooted for every }q\ge0.
}
\]
At \(q=1\), the larger exact census gives
\[
\boxed{
104/104
}
\]
negative-real-rooted ribbon-path BG polynomials through \(n=45\).

So the PF signal is already present in the ribbon differential-poset path distribution.

## 8. Positive BG path hierarchy

The programme now contains the finite PF families

\[
I_n(x)=\sum_\lambda f^\lambda x^{j(BG(\lambda))}
\]
(ordinary Young paths / involution shapes),

\[
R_{n,d}(x)=\sum_\lambda|\chi^\lambda(d^m)|x^{j(BG(\lambda))}
\]
(ribbon paths),

\[
A_{n,d}(x)=\sum_\lambda f^\lambda|\chi^\lambda(d^m)|x^{j(BG(\lambda))}
\]
(mixed Young×ribbon paths),

and

\[
G_n(x)=\sum_\lambda(f^\lambda)^2x^{j(BG(\lambda))}
\]
(Young×Young / Plancherel).

The finite certifications are:
- \(I_n\): \(31/31\), \(0\le n\le30\);
- \(R_{n,d}\): \(104/104\), \(n\le45\);
- \(A_{n,d}\): \(104/104\), \(n\le45\);
- \(G_n\): the established PHYS-ALG-06E/06I window.

This points toward a BG-graded path theorem rather than a peculiarity of absolute characters.

## 9. Exact spin reciprocity

Conjugation preserves \(f^\lambda\) and BG. It sends a \(d\)-ribbon of height \(h\) to one of height \(d-1-h\).

For \(m=n/d\),
\[
\boxed{
A_{n,d}(x;q)
=
q^{m(d-1)}
A_{n,d}(x;q^{-1}).
}
\]
Since \(d\) is odd, \(M=m(d-1)\) is even. Hence
\[
q^{-M/2}A_{n,d}(x;q)
=
\mathscr A_{n,d}(x,y),
\qquad
y=q+q^{-1}.
\]
All \(25/25\) audited bivariate polynomials satisfy this identity exactly.

The positive-spin regime is simply
\[
q>0\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad y\ge2.
\]
The absolute endpoint is \(y=2\), while the signed endpoint is \(y=-2\).

## 10. Negative spin develops a complex-root phase

The natural spin deformation is not hyperbolic throughout the route toward \(q=-1\).

At
\[
n=12,\qquad d=3,\qquad q=-\frac34,
\]
the primitive \(x\)-polynomial is
\[
-2241591552x^2+4418123491x-2194790400,
\]
with negative discriminant and roots
\[
0.9854880759\pm0.0890770193i.
\]

Yet
\[
q=-1:\quad -12x^2+23x-11
\]
is real-rooted, and
\[
q=1:\quad30208x^2+78055x+22528
\]
has negative real roots.

For this example the \(x\)-discriminant has real \(q\)-zeros near
\[
-1.90279,\quad-1.11739,\quad-0.894939,\quad-0.525543.
\]
Thus an open complex-root window occurs approximately between
\[
-0.894939<q<-0.525543.
\]

The signed endpoint is therefore a reentrant real-rooted point, not the continuation of one connected positive hyperbolic phase.

## 11. Coarse orientation interpolation also fails

Split the \(q=1\) absolute weights by Littlewood orientation:
\[
A=A^++A^-,
\qquad
\mathcal T=A^+-A^-.
\]

Only
\[
\boxed{23/62}
\]
nonidentity cases through \(n=45\) satisfy the HKO all-real-linear-combination criterion.

Even the restricted segment
\[
A^++\theta A^-,
\qquad -1\le\theta\le1,
\]
contains a complex-root interval in
\[
\boxed{25/62}
\]
nonidentity layers.

The first failure is \(n=15,d=3\).

So neither ordinary interlacing nor convex orientation interpolation explains why the absolute and signed endpoints are both real-rooted.

## 12. Main verdict

\[
\boxed{\textbf{absolute ribbon-tableau interpretation: PASS}}
\]

\[
\boxed{\textbf{ribbon \(q\)-Heisenberg algebra: PASS}}
\]

\[
\boxed{\textbf{\(q=1\) ribbon graph: \(d\)-DIFFERENTIAL}}
\]

\[
\boxed{\textbf{BG-resolved small algebra: FAIL}}
\]

\[
\boxed{\textbf{mixed positive-spin \(q\ge0\): 25/25 FULL HALF-LINES PASS}}
\]

\[
\boxed{\textbf{ribbon-path positive-spin \(q\ge0\): 25/25 FULL HALF-LINES PASS}}
\]

\[
\boxed{\textbf{spin reciprocity: PASS}}
\]

\[
\boxed{\textbf{negative-spin continuation: NOT HYPERBOLIC THROUGHOUT}}
\]

\[
\boxed{\textbf{coarse orientation interpolation: REJECT}}
\]

\[
\boxed{\textbf{absolute pair \(q=1\): 104/104 PF}}
\]

\[
\boxed{\textbf{ribbon path \(q=1\): 104/104 PF}}
\]

\[
\boxed{\textbf{signed \(q=-1\): 104/104 REAL-ROOTED}}
\]

The all-\(n\) theorem remains open.

## 13. Recommended next audit

### EHF-PHYS-ALG-06Q — Ribbon-Heisenberg BG PF / Positive-Spin Half-Line Theorem Audit

Priority targets:

1. work directly with \(U_d(q),D_d(q),H=BG\) for \(q\ge0\);
2. combine
   \[
   [D_d(q),U_d(q)]=[d]_{q^2}I
   \]
   with
   \[
   [H,U_\pm]=\pm U_\pm;
   \]
3. use \(y=q+q^{-1}\ge2\) to seek an all-\(y\ge2\) recurrence or total-positive representation;
4. attack the ribbon-path polynomial before the mixed \(f^\lambda\)-weighted polynomial;
5. derive a BG-graded ribbon Cauchy identity or a matrix-valued transfer retaining enough partition information to avoid the failed charge-lumping problem;
6. test whether ordinary Young-path pairing is a second PF-preserving functional on the ribbon-path family;
7. do not use continuation through \(q<0\) to prove the signed endpoint.

The positive ribbon-Heisenberg half-line is now the cleanest candidate for the first genuine all-\(n\) BG PF theorem.

# EHF-PHYS-ALG-06Q — Ribbon-Heisenberg BG PF / Positive-Spin Half-Line Theorem Audit

**Branch:** EHF-PHYS-ALG  
**Inputs:** PHYS-ALG-06P and the ribbon-Heisenberg positive-spin programme.

The intended 06Q programme was:

1. work directly with
   \[
   U_d(q),D_d(q),H=BG

   \[
   D_d(q)U_d(q)-U_d(q)D_d(q)=[d]_{q^2}I

   to seek a uniform positive-half-line theorem;

# EHF-PHYS-ALG-06R — Centered Spin \(u\)-Positivity / Hermite-Subdiscriminant PF Theorem Audit

**Branch:** EHF-PHYS-ALG  
**Primary target:** replace parameter-by-parameter root certification by a direct algebraic positivity certificate.

**Final status:** a finite exact theorem certificate is found. Set
\[
u=q+q^{-1}-2=\frac{(q-1)^2}{q}\ge0\qquad(q>0).
\]
After the reciprocal-spin reduction, every audited \(x\)-coefficient polynomial is coefficientwise nonnegative in \(u\), and every audited Hermite subdiscriminant is coefficientwise strictly positive in \(u\).

**Certification:** \(\boxed{278/278}\) checks PASS.

## 1. Centered coordinate

The reciprocal spin parameter is
\[
y=q+q^{-1}.
\]

Center it at the absolute-character point \(q=1\):
\[
\boxed{
u=y-2=q+q^{-1}-2=\frac{(q-1)^2}{q}.
}
\]

Thus the complete positive-spin domain
\[
q>0
\]
maps to
\[
u\ge0.
\]

## 2. Families audited

Ribbon-path:
\[
R_{n,d}(x;q)
=
\sum_\lambda
R^\lambda_{d,m}(q)x^{j(BG(\lambda))}.
\]

Mixed Young×ribbon:
\[
A_{n,d}(x;q)
=
\sum_\lambda
f^\lambda R^\lambda_{d,m}(q)x^{j(BG(\lambda))}.
\]

After removing the harmless reciprocal prefactor, both become polynomials
\[
\boxed{
P_{n,d}(x;u)\in\mathbb Z[x,u].
}
\]

## 3. Hermite criterion

For a real polynomial \(P\), the Hermite root-moment matrix is positive semidefinite exactly when \(P\) is real-rooted.

Its leading principal minors are proportional to the subdiscriminants:
\[
\operatorname{sDisc}_k(P)
=
a_r^{\,2k-2}\det H_k(P).
\]

Therefore strict positivity of all nontrivial subdiscriminants gives simple real roots.

## 4. Coefficient positivity

Across all
\[
\boxed{72}
\]
audited families through
\[
n\le30,
\]
every \(x\)-coefficient is coefficientwise nonnegative in \(u\):
\[
\boxed{
[x^j]P_{n,d}(x;u)\in\mathbb Q_{\ge0}[u].
}
\]

The split is:
\[
\boxed{
36\text{ ribbon-path families}
}
\]
and
\[
\boxed{
36\text{ mixed Young×ribbon families}.
}
\]

## 5. Hermite-subdiscriminant result

The combined audit contains
\[
\boxed{128}
\]
nontrivial subdiscriminants.

Every one satisfies
\[
\boxed{
\operatorname{sDisc}_k(P_{n,d}(x;u))
\in
\mathbb Q_{>0}[u].
}
\]

There are no zero or negative coefficients in any audited subdiscriminant.

Hence for every
\[
u\ge0:
\]

- all nontrivial subdiscriminants are strictly positive;
- the Hermite matrix is positive definite;
- all \(x\)-roots are simple and real;
- the \(x\)-coefficients are positive;
- therefore all roots are strictly negative.

Thus
\[
\boxed{
P_{n,d}(x;q)
\text{ is PF for every real }q>0
}
\]
through the complete finite audited window.

## 6. Stress extension

The
\[
25\le n\le30
\]
extension adds
\[
\boxed{
22/22\text{ new families}
}
\]
and
\[
\boxed{
50/50\text{ new subdiscriminants},
}
\]
all preserving centered-\(u\) coefficient positivity.

Combined:
\[
\boxed{
72/72\text{ families},
\qquad
128/128\text{ subdiscriminants}.
}
\]

## 7. Why this improves the previous finite proof

The earlier positive-half-line route isolated parameter-critical values and Sturm-counted the open cells between them.

In the audited window, 06R replaces that with the single algebraic certificate
\[
\boxed{
\operatorname{sDisc}_k(P)\in\mathbb Q_{>0}[u].
}
\]

No root tracking is required once this certificate is available.

## 8. Theorem ledger

### PA6R-T1 — centered parameter identity

For \(q>0\),
\[
u=q+q^{-1}-2=\frac{(q-1)^2}{q}\ge0,
\]
with
\[
u=0\iff q=1.
\]

**Status:** exact identity.

### PA6R-T2 — reciprocal-spin reduction

Ribbon-spin reciprocity lets each positive-spin BG family be written, up to a harmless power of \(q\), as
\[
P_{n,d}(x;u)\in\mathbb Z[x,u].
\]

**Status:** exact reduction.

### PA6R-T3 — Hermite/subdiscriminant certificate

Hermite-Sylvester theory identifies real-rootedness with PSD of the root-moment Hermite matrix; leading Hermite minors are the subdiscriminants up to positive leading-coefficient scaling.

**Status:** classical exact theorem.

### PA6R-T4 — coefficient positivity

All
\[
72/72
\]
audited centered positive-spin families through \(n=30\) have every \(x\)-coefficient polynomial coefficientwise nonnegative in \(u\).

**Status:** finite exact.

### PA6R-T5 — subdiscriminant positivity

All
\[
128/128
\]
audited nontrivial subdiscriminants through \(n=30\) have strictly positive coefficients in \(u\).

**Status:** finite exact.

### PA6R-T6 — whole-half-line hyperbolicity

Every audited family has positive-definite Hermite matrix for every
\[
u\ge0,
\]
and therefore simple real \(x\)-roots throughout
\[
q>0.
\]

**Status:** finite exact parameter theorem.

### PA6R-T7 — PF conclusion

Positive \(x\)-coefficients force the simple real roots to be strictly negative.

Hence every audited family is PF for the full positive-spin half-line.

**Status:** finite exact parameter theorem.

### PA6R-T8 — stress extension

The \(n=25,\ldots,30\) extension contributes
\[
22/22
\]
passing families and
\[
50/50
\]
passing subdiscriminants.

**Status:** finite exact stress extension.

### PA6R-T9 — all-\(n\) boundary

No all-\(n\) proof of coefficientwise subdiscriminant positivity has been obtained.

A positive Gram/Cauchy-Binet representation, planar-network model, or recurrence in \((n,d,k)\) remains open.

**Status:** open theorem boundary.

## 9. Route ledger

### Centered coordinate
\[
\boxed{\textbf{PASS}}
\]

The coordinate
\[
u=q+q^{-1}-2
\]
exposes the relevant positivity semiring.

### Hermite/subdiscriminant route
\[
\boxed{\textbf{PASS FINITELY}}
\]

\[
128/128
\]
nontrivial subdiscriminants are coefficientwise positive in \(u\).

### Parameter root tracking
\[
\boxed{\textbf{SUPERSEDED IN THE AUDITED WINDOW}}
\]

It is no longer needed once the subdiscriminants are known to be \(u\)-positive.

### Gram/Cauchy-Binet representation
\[
\boxed{\textbf{OPEN}}
\]

This would give a structural explanation for the coefficient positivity.

### Planar-network/LGV model
\[
\boxed{\textbf{OPEN}}
\]

The positive coefficients suggest a possible enumerative origin.

### Recurrence in \((n,d,k)\)
\[
\boxed{\textbf{OPEN}}
\]

This is a possible inductive route to an all-\(n\) theorem.

### Negative \(q\) / signed endpoint
\[
\boxed{\textbf{OUT OF SCOPE FOR 06R}}
\]

PHYS-ALG-06P already showed reentrant complex windows for negative spin; \(q=-1\) is a separate problem.

## 10. Main verdict

\[
\boxed{\textbf{centered \(u\)-coordinate: PASS}}
\]

\[
\boxed{\textbf{\(x\)-coefficient positivity: 72/72}}
\]

\[
\boxed{\textbf{subdiscriminant positivity: 128/128}}
\]

\[
\boxed{\textbf{whole \(q>0\) half-line PF: 72/72}}
\]

\[
\boxed{\textbf{all-\(n\) theorem: OPEN}}
\]

The strongest current finite positive-spin theorem template is therefore:

\[
\boxed{
\textbf{centered-\(u\) Hermite/subdiscriminant positivity.}
}
\]

## 11. Recommended next audit

### EHF-PHYS-ALG-06S — Subdiscriminant Gram / Positive-Cauchy-Binet Representation Audit

Targets:

1. construct the Hermite matrix directly from the ribbon-Heisenberg/Fock object;

2. seek a coefficient-positive Gram factorization
   \[
   H(u)=B(u)B(u)^\top;
   \]

3. otherwise derive principal minors as positive Cauchy-Binet sums;

4. identify coefficients of \(u^r\) combinatorially;

5. exploit conjugation pairing and
   \[
   u=\frac{(q-1)^2}{q};
   \]

6. derive recurrences in
   \[
   (n,d,k)
   \]
   preserving
   \[
   \mathbb R_+[u].
   \]

A successful 06S would be the natural bridge from the finite
\[
128/128
\]
pattern to an all-\(n\) proof.

# EHF-PHYS-ALG-06S — Subdiscriminant Gram / Positive-Cauchy-Binet Representation Audit

**Branch:** EHF-PHYS-ALG  
**Inputs:** PHYS-ALG-06P/06R; centered reciprocal-spin coordinate
\[
u=q+q^{-1}-2=\frac{(q-1)^2}{q}.
\]

**Final status:** the 06R finite positivity theorem is independently reconstructed exactly, but the specific polynomial root-moment Hermite slice-Gram route proposed for 06S is blocked at its entry gate throughout the entire audited family window. Every reconstructed centered polynomial has a nonconstant leading coefficient in \(x\), so monic Newton/Hermite normalization introduces rational functions of \(u\). The audit therefore does **not** reinterpret this as failure of PF positivity; it redirects the structural theorem search toward denominator-free Bezoutian/subresultant matrices.

**Scientific verdict:**

\[
\boxed{
\texttt{polynomial\_hermite\_route\_blocked\_by\_nonconstant\_leading\_coefficient}
}
\]

## 1. Self-contained recovery of the 06R family ledger

The saved 06R centered-family export was not available as a standalone run artifact. Rather than substitute a synthetic template, 06S reconstructs the families directly from the definitions used in 06P/06R.

For every odd divisor \(d\ge3\) of \(n\le30\):

1. partitions of \(n\) are generated exactly;
2. removable \(d\)-rim hooks are implemented by beta-set bead moves;
3. the ribbon spin exponent is the rim-hook height;
4. standard \(d\)-ribbon path weights are generated recursively;
5. mixed Young×ribbon families are weighted by the hook-length dimension \(f^\lambda\);
6. BG charge is aggregated exactly;
7. spin reciprocity is centered in
   \[
   u=q+q^{-1}-2.
   \]

The finite window contains exactly

\[
\boxed{36}
\]

odd-divisor pairs \((n,d)\), giving

\[
\boxed{36\text{ ribbon-path}+36\text{ mixed}=72\text{ centered families}.}
\]

## 2. Independent reproduction of PHYS-ALG-06R

The reconstructed families reproduce the 06R headline ledger exactly:

\[
\boxed{72/72}
\]

families have coefficientwise nonnegative \(x\)-coefficients in \(u\), and the exact Hermite calculation contains

\[
\boxed{128}
\]

nontrivial subdiscriminants, with

\[
\boxed{128/128}
\]

coefficientwise strictly positive in \(u\).

Thus 06S independently confirms that the 06R result was not an artifact of a missing intermediate export.

## 3. The 06S Gram entry gate

The initial 06S mechanism asks whether a determinant-preserving nested change of moment basis can put the root-moment Hermite matrix into the form

\[
T H(u)T^\top=\sum_{r\ge0}u^r H_r,
\qquad
H_r\succeq0.
\]

For this to be a polynomial coefficient-slice problem over \(\mathbb Q[u]\), the Newton root moments must remain polynomial in \(u\). In the specified construction this requires the \(x\)-leading coefficient of \(P(x;u)\) to be independent of \(u\).

Exact result:

\[
\boxed{0/72}
\]

families have a \(u\)-independent \(x\)-leading coefficient.

Equivalently:

\[
\boxed{72/72}
\]

are blocked at this entry gate.

This obstruction is systematic rather than sporadic.

## 4. The leading coefficient is nevertheless positive

Although it varies with \(u\), every audited leading coefficient itself lies in

\[
\boxed{\mathbb Q_{>0}[u]}
\]

coefficientwise:

\[
\boxed{72/72}.
\]

So the obstruction is not a sign failure. It is a **denominator problem** created by monic root normalization.

## 5. Primitive-content normalization does not repair the route

A natural rescue attempt is to divide each polynomial by its common polynomial content in \(x\).

Only

\[
\boxed{1/72}
\]

families then becomes constant-leading.

The sole rescue is therefore exceptional and does not provide a uniform mechanism for the 06R window.

Hence:

\[
\boxed{
\textbf{ordinary polynomial-content normalization does not remove the 06S obstruction.}
}
\]

## 6. Why this is not a negative result for 06R

06R proves, finitely and exactly in the audited window, the positivity of the Hermite subdiscriminants themselves.

06S asks a strictly stronger question: whether that positivity is explained by one particular coefficient-slice Gram representation of a polynomial root-moment Hermite matrix.

Failure to enter that representation class does not affect the determinant identities already certified by 06R.

The correct logical relation is:

\[
\boxed{
\text{06R positivity survives exactly}
\quad\text{but}\quad
\text{the first simple Gram mechanism is unavailable.}
}
\]

It also does not exclude:

- denominator-cleared Gram representations;
- Hermite-Bezout or Bezoutian representations;
- subresultant matrices;
- planar-network/LGV representations;
- positive recurrences;
- a direct ribbon-Heisenberg/Fock construction.

## 7. Finite recurrence screen

The exploratory order-1/order-2 constant-coefficient recurrence scan found

\[
\boxed{2}
\]

finite candidates.

Both occur for \(d=7\), \(k=1\), one in each family, with the same finite coefficient pattern

\[
(c_1,c_2)=(3,-3)
\]

across

\[
n=7,14,21,28.
\]

Because \(k=1\) here tracks the lowest Hermite-minor/degree sequence, this is retained only as a finite bookkeeping pattern, not evidence of the desired all-\(n\) subdiscriminant recurrence.

## 8. Theorem ledger

### PA6S-T1 — exact family reconstruction

The 06P/06R ribbon-spin definitions reconstruct exactly 36 odd-divisor pairs and 72 centered families through \(n\le30\).

**Status:** finite exact.

### PA6S-T2 — parent coefficient positivity reproduced

All

\[
72/72
\]

centered families reproduce coefficientwise nonnegative \(x\)-coefficients in \(u\).

**Status:** finite exact reproduction.

### PA6S-T3 — parent subdiscriminant positivity reproduced

All

\[
128/128
\]

reconstructed nontrivial Hermite subdiscriminants are coefficientwise strictly positive in \(u\).

**Status:** finite exact reproduction.

### PA6S-T4 — nonconstant-leading obstruction

All

\[
72/72
\]

centered families have \(u\)-dependent \(x\)-leading coefficient.

**Status:** finite exact obstruction.

### PA6S-T5 — positive-leading refinement

All

\[
72/72
\]

leading coefficients are coefficientwise strictly positive in \(u\).

**Status:** finite exact.

### PA6S-T6 — primitive-content rescue failure

Only

\[
1/72
\]

families becomes constant-leading after removal of common polynomial \(x\)-content.

**Status:** finite exact obstruction to the simple normalization rescue.

### PA6S-T7 — all-\(n\) boundary

No uniform Gram/Cauchy-Binet or recurrence theorem has yet been proved for arbitrary \((n,d,k)\).

**Status:** open.

## 9. Route ledger

### 06R centered positivity

\[
\boxed{\textbf{REPRODUCED EXACTLY}}
\]

### Polynomial root-moment coefficient-slice Gram route

\[
\boxed{\textbf{BLOCKED AT ENTRY GATE: }0/72\textbf{ CONSTANT-LEADING}}
\]

### Ordinary content normalization

\[
\boxed{\textbf{NOT A UNIFORM RESCUE: }1/72}
\]

### General Gram/Cauchy-Binet mechanism

\[
\boxed{\textbf{OPEN}}
\]

### Bezoutian/subresultant route

\[
\boxed{\textbf{NEXT PRIORITY}}
\]

### All-\(n\) PF theorem

\[
\boxed{\textbf{OPEN}}
\]

## 10. Main verdict

\[
\boxed{\textbf{06R parent positivity: 72/72 and 128/128 REPRODUCED}}
\]

\[
\boxed{\textbf{specified polynomial root-moment Gram route: BLOCKED}}
\]

\[
\boxed{\textbf{general positive representation: STILL OPEN}}
\]

The obstruction is precise: the centered polynomials are not monic over \(\mathbb Q[u]\), and their leading coefficients vary positively with \(u\). The next mechanism should therefore avoid division by that leading coefficient.

## 11. Recommended next audit

### EHF-PHYS-ALG-06T — Polynomial Bezout/Subresultant Gram / Denominator-Free Cauchy-Binet Audit

Priority targets:

1. replace the monic root-moment Hermite matrix by a polynomial Bezoutian, Hermite-Bezout, or subresultant matrix for
   \[
   P(x;u),\qquad \partial_xP(x;u);
   \]
2. identify nested minors equal to the 06R subdiscriminants, or differing only by explicit coefficientwise-positive factors;
3. search determinant-preserving polynomial basis changes whose \(u\)-coefficient slices are PSD;
4. if successful, produce exact rational Gram decompositions and positive Cauchy-Binet expansions;
5. if unsuccessful, emit the first exact negative principal-minor/slice witness;
6. only after a denominator-free finite mechanism is isolated, attempt a uniform ribbon-Heisenberg/Fock proof in \((n,d,k)\).

The falsification rule remains strict:

> If no denominator-free positive matrix representation survives exact finite stress, do not promote the 06R coefficient pattern to an all-\(n\) theorem by analogy alone.

# EHF-PHYS-ALG-06T — Polynomial Bezout/Subresultant Gram / Denominator-Free Cauchy-Binet Audit

**Branch:** EHF-PHYS-ALG  
**Inputs:** PHYS-ALG-06R/06S centered families  
**Primary target:** remove the monic-normalization denominator obstruction found in 06S and test whether a polynomial Bezout/subresultant representation supplies the missing positive matrix mechanism.  
**Final status:** denominator-free Bezout/Sylvester certificate succeeds for every audited family; raw coefficient-slice Gram positivity is not universal.  
**Certification:** all strict implementation and exact-identity gates pass.

## 1. Why 06T

PHYS-ALG-06S reproduced the complete 06R positivity pattern but found that every one of the 72 centered families has a nonconstant x-leading coefficient.

Therefore monic normalization makes the root-moment Hermite matrix rational in
\[
u,
\]
blocking the intended polynomial coefficient-slice Gram audit.

The natural denominator-free replacement is the Bezout matrix of
\[
P(x;u)
\]
and
\[
P_x(x;u).
\]

## 2. Polynomial Bezout matrix

Write
\[
\frac{P(x)P'(y)-P(y)P'(x)}{x-y}
=
\sum_{i,j=0}^{r-1}B_{ij}(u)x^iy^j.
\]

Define
\[
\boxed{B_P(u)=(B_{ij}(u)).}
\]

No division by the leading x-coefficient is required.

For all 72 audited families,
\[
\boxed{B_P(u)\in\operatorname{Sym}_r(\mathbb Z[u]).}
\]

The degree distribution is
\[
32\text{ degree-1},\qquad
24\text{ degree-2},\qquad
16\text{ degree-3}.
\]

## 3. Exact subdiscriminant identity

Let
\[
a_r(u)=[x^r]P(x;u),
\]
and let
\[
\Delta_k(u)
\]
be the determinant of the trailing \(k\times k\) principal block of \(B_P(u)\).

The exact audit verifies
\[
\boxed{
\Delta_k(u)=a_r(u)^2\operatorname{sDisc}_k(P)
}
\]
for every audited minor.

The count is
\[
\boxed{128/128.}
\]

This is the denominator-free bridge that 06S was seeking.

## 4. Positivity of the polynomial Bezout minors

PHYS-ALG-06R/06S gives
\[
\operatorname{sDisc}_k(P)\in\mathbb Q_{>0}[u]
\]
through the audited window.

06S also established
\[
a_r(u)\in\mathbb Q_{>0}[u]
\]
for every family.

Hence
\[
\Delta_k(u)=a_r(u)^2\operatorname{sDisc}_k(P)
\]
has coefficientwise strictly positive coefficients.

The exact 06T result is
\[
\boxed{128/128\text{ trailing Bezout minors coefficientwise positive}.}
\]

Therefore for every
\[
u\ge0,
\]
all nested trailing principal minors are positive.

By the reversed Sylvester criterion,
\[
\boxed{B_P(u)\succ0}
\]
for every audited family throughout the full positive half-line.

Thus:
\[
\boxed{72/72\text{ denominator-free polynomial hyperbolicity certificates}.}
\]

## 5. What this achieves

The monic Hermite matrix from 06S was obstructed by rational denominators.

The Bezout representation removes that obstruction completely:

\[
\boxed{
P,P'
\longmapsto
B_P(u)\in\operatorname{Sym}_r(\mathbb Z[u])
}
\]

with nested polynomial minors equal to the same subdiscriminants up to the explicit positive factor
\[
a_r(u)^2.
\]

So the finite 06R theorem now has an exact denominator-free polynomial Sylvester/subresultant formulation.

## 6. Stronger coefficient-slice Gram test

The stronger proposed mechanism would require
\[
B_P(u)=\sum_{j\ge0}u^jB_j
\]
with
\[
B_j\succeq0
\]
for every coefficient slice.

This is not universal.

Across all 72 families:
\[
\boxed{36/72}
\]
have every coefficient slice PSD.

But 32 of those are the degree-1 cases, where the condition is essentially scalar.

Among the genuinely nontrivial degree-2/3 families:
\[
\boxed{4/40}
\]
have every slice PSD.

The audit records
\[
\boxed{185}
\]
exact indefinite coefficient-slice witnesses.

## 7. First exact obstruction

The first failure occurs for
\[
\boxed{
\text{ribbon\_path}:n=10,d=5.
}
\]

At the coefficient of
\[
u^7,
\]
the Bezout slice is
\[
\boxed{
B_7=
\begin{pmatrix}
68&2\\
2&0
\end{pmatrix}.
}
\]

Its determinant is
\[
\boxed{-4<0.}
\]

So this coefficient slice is indefinite.

## 8. Constant-basis no-rescue theorem

Let
\[
T
\]
be any invertible basis change independent of \(u\).

Then
\[
B_P(u)\mapsto T^TB_P(u)T
\]
and each coefficient slice transforms as
\[
B_j\mapsto T^TB_jT.
\]

By Sylvester inertia invariance, an indefinite coefficient slice remains indefinite under every such congruence.

Therefore:
\[
\boxed{
\textbf{no u-independent unimodular or invertible basis change can rescue the raw coefficient-slice PSD ansatz.}
}
\]

This is stronger than merely failing one monomial basis.

## 9. Subresultant interpretation

The nested Bezout minors are the principal subresultant/subdiscriminant data of
\[
P
\]
and
\[
P'.
\]

Thus the denominator-free route succeeds at the exact finite theorem level:
\[
\boxed{
\textbf{06R Hermite positivity and 06T Bezout positivity are equivalent finite certificates up to }a_r^2.
}
\]

But the coefficientwise positivity of the subdiscriminants is still the unresolved structural phenomenon.

06T repackages it without denominators; it does not yet explain it for all \(n\).

## 10. Theorem ledger

### PA6T-T1 — polynomial Bezout construction

For every audited centered family, \(B(P,P')\) is a symmetric polynomial matrix over \(\mathbb Z[u]\).

**Status:** finite exact.

### PA6T-T2 — trailing-minor/subdiscriminant identity

For all 128 audited nested minors,
\[
\Delta_k=a_r^2\operatorname{sDisc}_k(P).
\]

**Status:** finite exact, 128/128.

### PA6T-T3 — denominator-free positivity

All 128 trailing minors lie in
\[
\mathbb Q_{>0}[u].
\]

**Status:** finite exact, 128/128.

### PA6T-T4 — Bezout positive definiteness

For all 72 families and all
\[
u\ge0,
\]
\[
B_P(u)\succ0.
\]

**Status:** finite exact parameter theorem.

### PA6T-T5 — raw coefficient-slice Gram rejection

Only 36/72 total families, and only 4/40 degree-2/3 families, have every \(u\)-coefficient slice PSD.

**Status:** finite exact obstruction.

### PA6T-T6 — constant-basis invariance

An indefinite coefficient slice cannot become PSD under any \(u\)-independent invertible congruence.

**Status:** exact linear-algebraic theorem.

### PA6T-T7 — all-n boundary

No all-\(n\) proof of coefficientwise subdiscriminant positivity, positive fraction-free Gram factorization, or ribbon-Heisenberg recurrence is obtained.

**Status:** open.

## 11. Route ledger

### Denominator-free Bezout/Sylvester route
\[
\boxed{\textbf{PASS: }72/72}
\]

### Nested Bezout minor identity
\[
\boxed{\textbf{PASS: }128/128}
\]

### Raw coefficient-slice PSD Gram route
\[
\boxed{\textbf{NOT UNIVERSAL}}
\]

### u-independent basis rescue
\[
\boxed{\textbf{IMPOSSIBLE ON THE INDEFINITE-SLICE FAMILIES}}
\]

### u-dependent unimodular / fraction-free semiring Gram
\[
\boxed{\textbf{OPEN}}
\]

### All-n ribbon-Heisenberg proof
\[
\boxed{\textbf{OPEN}}
\]

## 12. Main verdict

The principal target of 06T succeeds:
\[
\boxed{
\textbf{the 06R finite PF theorem admits an exact denominator-free polynomial Bezout/subresultant certificate.}
}
\]

But the stronger hoped-for explanation fails in its simplest form:
\[
\boxed{
\textbf{the Bezout matrix is not coefficientwise PSD in }u\textbf{ for most nontrivial families.}
}
\]

So the positivity mechanism has now been narrowed again.

The next question is not whether a denominator-free matrix exists—it does—but whether its positive nested pivots admit a fraction-free semiring Gram or recurrence structure.

## 13. Recommended next audit

### EHF-PHYS-ALG-06U — Fraction-Free LDL / Stieltjes-Semiring Bezout Gram and Positive-Pivot Recurrence Audit

Targets:

1. perform trailing fraction-free LDL/Bareiss elimination on \(B_P(u)\);
2. express pivots through consecutive positive Bezout minors;
3. test coefficientwise positivity of pivot numerators and denominator-cleared multipliers;
4. seek a representation over the half-line semiring
   \[
   \Sigma\mathbb Q[u]^2+u\Sigma\mathbb Q[u]^2;
   \]
5. identify any uniform recurrence in \((n,d,k)\) for the positive pivot polynomials;
6. attempt a ribbon-Heisenberg/Fock derivation only after the finite algebraic pattern is isolated.

The falsification rule remains:

> Do not infer an all-\(n\) Gram theorem merely from positive principal minors. Require an explicit fraction-free positive representation or a recurrence that preserves the relevant positivity cone.

# EHF-PHYS-ALG-06U — Fraction-Free LDL / Stieltjes-Semiring Bezout Gram and Positive-Pivot Recurrence Audit

**Branch:** EHF-PHYS-ALG  
**Inputs:** PHYS-ALG-06R through 06T; exact 72-family centered-\(u\) ledger; polynomial Bezout matrices from 06T.  
**Primary target:** determine whether the denominator-free Bezout certificate admits a fraction-free positive LDL/Gram structure over the positive coefficient semiring, and whether the positive pivots obey a usable recurrence.

**Final status:** the finite audit finds a substantially stronger structure than the raw coefficient-slice Gram ansatz rejected in 06T. After reversing the Bezout monomial basis, every audited matrix entry and every audited minor is coefficientwise strictly positive in \(u\). The corresponding fraction-free LDL decomposition, ordinary LDL pivot ratios, and Bareiss elimination all remain inside the same positive coefficient semiring throughout the audited window. No simple cross-layer order-1/order-2 constant-coefficient recurrence in \(n\) is found.

**Finite certification window:** the same
\[
\boxed{72}
\]
families through
\[
\boxed{n\le30}
\]
used in 06R–06T, with degree distribution
\[
\boxed{
32\text{ degree-1},\quad
24\text{ degree-2},\quad
16\text{ degree-3}.
}
\]

---

## 1. Why 06U

PHYS-ALG-06T established that the centered-\(u\) PF theorem has an exact denominator-free polynomial Bezout certificate, but it also showed that the strongest naive Gram interpretation fails:

\[
B_P(u)=\sum_j u^jB_j
\]

does not have
\[
B_j\succeq0
\]
for most nontrivial families.

That failure does not prevent the **whole polynomial matrix**
\[
B_P(u)
\]
from possessing a positive elimination structure.

The 06U question is therefore:

> Can the polynomial Bezout matrix be factorized fraction-free so that its minors, pivots, multipliers, and Bareiss updates remain in the positive coefficient cone, even though the individual coefficient slices are not PSD?

The answer is:

\[
\boxed{\textbf{yes, throughout the complete finite audit window.}}
\]

---

## 2. Reverse the Bezout monomial basis

Let
\[
B(P,P')
\]
be the ascending-monomial Bezout matrix used in 06T.

Let \(J\) be the anti-identity permutation matrix reversing the monomial basis, and define

\[
\boxed{
A(u)=J\,B(P,P')\,J.
}
\]

This reversal does not alter ordinary positive definiteness: it is an orthogonal congruence.

It does, however, align the polynomial matrix with the natural leading-principal-minor / fraction-free elimination order.

The resulting \(A(u)\) is the central matrix object of 06U.

---

## 3. Coefficient-semiring positivity of all entries

Across all 72 audited families, every distinct upper-triangular entry of \(A(u)\) is a polynomial with strictly positive coefficients.

The total number of distinct upper-triangular entries is

\[
32(1)+24(3)+16(6)=\boxed{200}.
\]

The exact result is

\[
\boxed{
200/200
}
\]

coefficientwise strictly positive.

Thus every matrix entry belongs to

\[
\boxed{
\mathbb Q_{>0}[u].
}
\]

For every real
\[
u\ge0,
\]
all entries of \(A(u)\) are therefore strictly positive real numbers.

---

## 4. Full minor census

The crucial strengthening is that positivity does not stop at the entries.

For an \(r\times r\) matrix, the total number of nonempty minors is

\[
\sum_{k=1}^{r}\binom{r}{k}^2.
\]

Therefore the complete 72-family audit contains

\[
32(1)+24(5)+16(19)
=
\boxed{456}
\]

minors.

Every one satisfies

\[
\boxed{
\det A[I,J]\in\mathbb Q_{>0}[u].
}
\]

Hence

\[
\boxed{
456/456
}
\]

audited minors are coefficientwise strictly positive.

So every audited reversed Bezout matrix is **strictly totally positive over the coefficient semiring**:

\[
\boxed{
A(u)\text{ is coefficient-semiring strictly totally positive.}
}
\]

In particular, after specializing any

\[
u\ge0,
\]

all 456 audited minors are strictly positive real numbers.

Thus each \(A(u)\) is an ordinary strictly totally positive matrix throughout the entire positive-spin half-line.

This is stronger than the 06T positive-definiteness statement, which required only principal minors.

---

## 5. Leading principal minors and the 06T/06R subdiscriminants

Let

\[
\Delta_k(u)
=
\det A_k(u),
\qquad
\Delta_0=1,
\]

where \(A_k\) is the \(k\times k\) leading principal block of the reversed Bezout matrix.

Because reversing the basis converts the 06T trailing-minor convention into the leading-minor convention,

\[
\boxed{
\Delta_k(u)
=
a_r(u)^2\operatorname{sDisc}_k(P)
}
\]

for every nontrivial audited \(k\).

Thus the 06R Hermite/subdiscriminant sequence is exactly the principal-minor sequence of the reversed polynomial Bezout matrix.

Since

\[
a_r(u)^2\in\mathbb Q_{>0}[u]
\]

and every audited

\[
\operatorname{sDisc}_k(P)\in\mathbb Q_{>0}[u],
\]

all

\[
\boxed{128/128}
\]

nontrivial \(\Delta_k\) are coefficientwise strictly positive.

---

## 6. Fraction-free LDL decomposition

For a symmetric matrix \(A\), define

\[
\Delta_k
=
\det A[0{:}k,0{:}k],
\qquad
\Delta_0=1.
\]

Define the fraction-free lower-triangular matrix \(\bar L\) by

\[
\boxed{
\bar L_{ij}
=
\det
A[
\{0,\ldots,j-1,i\},
\{0,\ldots,j\}
],
\qquad i\ge j.
}
\]

Define

\[
\boxed{
\bar D_j
=
\Delta_j\Delta_{j+1}.
}
\]

Then the exact fraction-free identity is

\[
\boxed{
A
=
\bar L\,
\bar D^{-1}\,
\bar L^{T}.
}
\]

No square roots are required.

No monic normalization is required.

No division by the \(u\)-dependent leading coefficient occurs.

This is precisely the denominator-free algebraic setting that 06S could not access through the root-moment Hermite matrix.

---

## 7. Positivity of the fraction-free \(L\) entries

The lower-triangular matrix \(\bar L\) has

\[
32(1)+24(3)+16(6)
=
\boxed{200}
\]

nonzero structural entries.

Every one is itself a minor of \(A\).

Therefore the full audit gives

\[
\boxed{
200/200
}
\]

\(\bar L\)-entries coefficientwise strictly positive:

\[
\boxed{
\bar L_{ij}\in\mathbb Q_{>0}[u].
}
\]

This is not a post-hoc sign choice.

The canonical bordered-minor construction already lands in the positive coefficient cone.

---

## 8. Total positivity of the fraction-free \(L\) factor

The complete minor census of the 72 lower-triangular \(\bar L\) matrices contains again

\[
456
\]

formal minors.

Because \(\bar L\) is triangular, some vanish identically for structural index reasons.

The audit finds:

\[
\boxed{
336
}
\]

nonzero \(\bar L\) minors and

\[
\boxed{
120
}
\]

structural zero minors.

Every nonzero minor satisfies

\[
\boxed{
\det \bar L[I,J]\in\mathbb Q_{>0}[u].
}
\]

Hence

\[
\boxed{
336/336
}
\]

nonzero \(\bar L\) minors are coefficientwise strictly positive.

So the canonical fraction-free elimination factor itself retains a strong total-positivity signature.

---

## 9. Positivity of the fraction-free diagonal

The diagonal data are

\[
\bar D_j
=
\Delta_j\Delta_{j+1}.
\]

The total number of diagonal entries over the 72 families is

\[
32(1)+24(2)+16(3)
=
\boxed{128}.
\]

Every one satisfies

\[
\boxed{
\bar D_j\in\mathbb Q_{>0}[u].
}
\]

Thus:

\[
\boxed{
128/128
}
\]

fraction-free \(\bar D\)-entries are coefficientwise strictly positive.

---

## 10. A positive weighted Gram representation

The exact identity

\[
A
=
\bar L\bar D^{-1}\bar L^T
\]

can be read as a weighted Gram representation.

Define the positive rational-function semiring

\[
\boxed{
\mathcal S_+(u)
=
\{0\}
\cup
\left\{
\frac{p(u)}{q(u)}:
p,q\in\mathbb Q[u]
\text{ have strictly positive coefficients}
\right\}.
}
\]

For

\[
u\ge0,
\]

every nonzero element of \(\mathcal S_+(u)\) is strictly positive.

Because

\[
\bar L_{ij}\in\mathbb Q_{>0}[u]
\]

and

\[
\bar D_j^{-1}\in\mathcal S_+(u),
\]

the full matrix identity is a Gram-like factorization over \(\mathcal S_+(u)\).

Therefore the 06T certificate can be strengthened from

\[
\boxed{
A(u)\succ0
}
\]

to the finite structural statement

\[
\boxed{
A
=
\bar L\bar D^{-1}\bar L^T
\quad
\text{with all nonzero fraction-free data in }\mathcal S_+(u).
}
\]

This is the successful 06U replacement for the raw coefficient-slice PSD Gram ansatz.

---

## 11. Ordinary LDL pivots

The ordinary LDL pivot sequence is

\[
\boxed{
p_k(u)
=
\frac{\Delta_k(u)}{\Delta_{k-1}(u)},
\qquad
1\le k\le r.
}
\]

Across the full window there are

\[
\boxed{128}
\]

pivots.

For every one, both numerator and denominator have strictly positive coefficients.

Hence

\[
\boxed{
128/128
}
\]

pivots belong to

\[
\mathcal S_+(u).
\]

Thus every pivot is strictly positive for every

\[
u\ge0.
\]

This is an exact positive-pivot certificate for the entire audited half-line.

---

## 12. The first pivot

For a degree-\(r\) polynomial \(P\), the first principal Bezout minor satisfies

\[
\Delta_1
=
r\,a_r(u)^2.
\]

Therefore

\[
\boxed{
p_1(u)
=
r\,a_r(u)^2.
}
\]

The audit verifies this identity exactly for all

\[
\boxed{72/72}
\]

families.

So the first LDL pivot has a particularly simple interpretation: it is just the squared leading coefficient, multiplied by the polynomial degree.

---

## 13. Higher pivots are consecutive subdiscriminant ratios

For

\[
k\ge2,
\]

06T gives

\[
\Delta_k
=
a_r^2\operatorname{sDisc}_k(P)
\]

and

\[
\Delta_{k-1}
=
a_r^2\operatorname{sDisc}_{k-1}(P).
\]

The common leading-coefficient factor cancels:

\[
\boxed{
p_k(u)
=
\frac{
\operatorname{sDisc}_k(P)
}{
\operatorname{sDisc}_{k-1}(P)
}.
}
\]

There are

\[
128-72
=
\boxed{56}
\]

such higher pivots in the audited window.

Every identity passes exactly:

\[
\boxed{
56/56.
}
\]

Thus the positive Hermite/subdiscriminant sequence discovered in 06R is not merely related to the Bezout matrix.

It is **exactly the positive LDL pivot chain** of the reversed Bezout matrix.

That provides a sharper structural interpretation of the 06R theorem.

---

## 14. Unit-LDL multipliers

For the ordinary unit-lower-triangular LDL form, the off-diagonal multipliers can be represented fraction-free using the bordered minors from \(\bar L\) and the corresponding \(\Delta_k\).

The total number of nontrivial off-diagonal multipliers is

\[
24(1)+16(3)
=
\boxed{72}.
\]

For all of them, both numerator and denominator are coefficientwise strictly positive polynomials.

Hence

\[
\boxed{
72/72
}
\]

multipliers belong to

\[
\mathcal S_+(u).
\]

No sign gauge is required.

---

## 15. Positive Bareiss recurrence

The active-block fraction-free Bareiss/Schur recurrence is

\[
\boxed{
R^{(m+1)}_{ij}
=
\frac{
\delta_mR^{(m)}_{i+1,j+1}
-
R^{(m)}_{i+1,0}R^{(m)}_{0,j+1}
}{
\delta_{m-1}
},
}
\]

where

\[
\delta_m=R^{(m)}_{00}
\]

and

\[
\delta_{-1}=1.
\]

For the audited degree-2/3 matrices, this generates

\[
\boxed{88}
\]

independent nontrivial updated polynomial entries.

Every division is exact:

\[
\boxed{
88/88
}
\]

exact polynomial divisions.

Every resulting polynomial has strictly positive coefficients:

\[
\boxed{
88/88
}
\]

positive outputs.

The stage pivots agree exactly with the corresponding leading principal minors:

\[
\boxed{
128/128
}
\]

pivot identities.

Thus the elimination dynamics itself closes inside the positive polynomial cone throughout the finite window.

This is stronger than merely observing positive final determinants.

---

## 16. Why 06T's slice obstruction is no longer fatal

PHYS-ALG-06T found coefficient matrices \(B_j\) with negative determinants, for example an exact indefinite \(u^7\) slice in the \(n=10,d=5\) ribbon-path family.

That result remains true.

06U does **not** contradict it.

The distinction is:

### Rejected in 06T

\[
B(u)
=
\sum_j u^jB_j,
\qquad
B_j\succeq0
\text{ for every }j.
\]

### Established in 06U

\[
A
=
\bar L\bar D^{-1}\bar L^T
\]

with

\[
\bar L,\bar D
\]

built from positive polynomial minors and positive rational semiring data.

Thus positivity lives in the nonlinear determinantal/elimination structure of the whole polynomial matrix, not coefficient-slice by coefficient-slice.

This explains exactly why 06T's counterexample does not obstruct the stronger fraction-free Gram route.

---

(n,d,k).

A conservative exact search was performed for simple order-1 and order-2 constant-coefficient recurrences in \(n\) for the principal minors

\[
\Delta_k(u)
\]

at fixed family type, \(d\), and \(k\).

Result:

\[
\boxed{
0
}
\]

surviving recurrence candidates.

Therefore the finite positive Bareiss recurrence is currently an **intra-matrix** recurrence.

It is not yet an induction from one HC/ribbon layer to the next.

PHYS-ALG-06U substantially strengthens the finite theorem chain.

The 06R positivity pattern is now seen to sit inside a much larger finite determinantal structure:

\[
\boxed{

}

Moreover,

\[

A
=
\bar L\bar D^{-1}\bar L^T

provides an exact fraction-free weighted Gram decomposition over the positive rational-function semiring.

The Hermite/subdiscriminant sequence from 06R is precisely the LDL pivot sequence after the common leading-coefficient factors cancel.

The Bareiss elimination itself preserves polynomial coefficient positivity.

Therefore the finite positive-spin mechanism is no longer merely:

\[
\text{positive subdiscriminants}.
\]

# EHF-PHYS-ALG-06V — Ribbon-Heisenberg Bezout Total-Positivity / Planar-Network Minor Recurrence Audit

**Branch:** EHF-PHYS-ALG  
**Inputs:** PHYS-ALG-06R through 06U; exact centered-\(u\) family ledger and polynomial Bezout matrices.  
**Primary target:** determine whether the 06U total-positivity pattern has a non-circular planar-network/Cauchy-Binet explanation, and separate ordinary pointwise total positivity from the stronger coefficient-semiring positivity in \(u\).

**Final status:** ordinary pointwise total positivity of the reversed Bezout matrix is explained by a general root-quotient Gram/Cauchy-Binet theorem for any polynomial with simple negative roots. A canonical positive planar network therefore exists. However, the observed coefficientwise positivity of every Bezout minor as a polynomial in \(u\) is strictly stronger and remains a special finite ribbon-family phenomenon. An exact cubic counterexample proves that even the full 06R-style coefficient/subdiscriminant hypotheses do not generically force coefficient-semiring total positivity.

**Strict structural gates:** \(\boxed{10/10}\) PASS.

**Finite audited window:** the same
\[
\boxed{72}
\]
families through
\[
\boxed{n\le30},
\]
with degree distribution
\[
\boxed{
32\text{ degree-1},\quad
24\text{ degree-2},\quad
16\text{ degree-3}.
}
\]

---

## 1. Independent semiring recheck

A new exact coefficient-array engine independently rechecked every minor of every reversed Bezout matrix.

The result reproduces 06U exactly:

\[
\boxed{
456/456
}
\]

reversed Bezout minors are coefficientwise strictly positive polynomials in \(u\).

Thus the 06U semiring-total-positivity result survives an independent implementation path.

---

## 2. Universal root-quotient Gram identity

Let

\[
P(x)
=
a\prod_{i=1}^{r}(x+\rho_i),
\qquad
a>0,
\qquad
0<\rho_1<\cdots<\rho_r.
\]

Define

\[
\boxed{
q_i(x)
=
\frac{P(x)}{x+\rho_i}.
}
\]

Since

\[
P'(x)
=
\sum_i q_i(x),
\]

we have the exact identity

\[
\begin{aligned}
P(x)P'(y)-P(y)P'(x)
&=
\sum_i
\left(
P(x)q_i(y)-P(y)q_i(x)
\right)
\\
&=
(x-y)
\sum_i q_i(x)q_i(y).
\end{aligned}
\]

Therefore

\[
\boxed{
\frac{
P(x)P'(y)-P(y)P'(x)
}{
x-y
}
=
\sum_i q_i(x)q_i(y).
}
\]

This is a direct Gram identity for the Bezoutian.

---

## 3. Reversed quotient coefficient matrix

Write the coefficients of each \(q_i(x)\) in descending monomial order and order the rows by decreasing root:

\[
\rho_r,\rho_{r-1},\ldots,\rho_1.
\]

Let the resulting coefficient matrix be \(Q\).

Then the reversed Bezout matrix is

\[
\boxed{
A=Q^{T}Q.
}
\]

The matrix \(Q\) is the positive-sign version of the inverse-Vandermonde/Lagrange coefficient matrix.

A positive Vandermonde matrix on

\[
0<\rho_1<\cdots<\rho_r
\]

is strictly totally positive.

Jacobi's complementary-minor identity implies that the checkerboard-corrected inverse Vandermonde is also strictly totally positive.

The alternating signs from the Lagrange denominators

\[
q_i(-\rho_i)
\]

cancel precisely against that checkerboard sign after the root and monomial reversals above.

Hence:

\[
\boxed{
Q\text{ is strictly totally positive.}
}
\]

This is an all-degree statement.

---

## 4. Cauchy-Binet total-positivity theorem

For any equal-size row and column sets \(I,J\),

\[
\det A[I,J]
=
\det(Q_I^TQ_J).
\]

Cauchy-Binet gives

\[
\boxed{
\det A[I,J]
=
\sum_{S}
\det Q[S,I]\,
\det Q[S,J].
}
\]

Every summand is strictly positive because \(Q\) is strictly totally positive.

Therefore:

\[
\boxed{
\textbf{if \(P\) has simple negative roots, the reversed Bezout matrix is strictly totally positive.}
}
\]

This is not limited to degrees \(1,2,3\).

Thus the 06U observation that all specialized Bezout minors are positive is a general consequence of the PF/simple-negative-root theorem.

---

## 5. Symbolic root-gap certification in the audited degrees

For the actual degrees present in the 06R window, the general theorem was also verified symbolically without using numerical roots.

Parameterize ordered positive root magnitudes by

\[
\rho_1=s,
\]

\[
\rho_2=s+g_1,
\]

\[
\rho_3=s+g_1+g_2,
\]

with

\[
s,g_1,g_2>0.
\]

For degrees \(1,2,3\), every minor of the quotient coefficient matrix \(Q\) expands as a polynomial with strictly positive coefficients in the positive root-gap variables.

Across the three universal templates:

\[
\boxed{
25/25
}
\]

quotient-matrix minors pass.

The three corresponding Gram identities

\[
A=Q^TQ
\]

pass exactly:

\[
\boxed{
3/3.
}
\]

---

## 6. What this does and does not explain

This theorem explains:

\[
\boxed{
\textbf{pointwise total positivity for every fixed \(u\ge0\).}
}
\]

Once 06R has established that the roots are simple and negative, total positivity of the reversed Bezout matrix follows automatically.

But 06U established the stronger statement

\[
\boxed{
\det A[I,J]\in\mathbb Q_{>0}[u].
}
\]

The root-quotient theorem does not imply that the coefficients of the resulting polynomial in \(u\) are positive.

The roots \(\rho_i(u)\) are algebraic functions of \(u\), and positivity after specialization is weaker than positivity coefficient-by-coefficient.

Therefore the special all-\(u\) semiring phenomenon remains a separate theorem target.

---

## 7. Canonical planar-network factorization

For the audited degree range, the unit lower LDL factor admits a canonical reduced bidiagonal factorization.

### Degree 1

Only the positive diagonal pivot is required.

### Degree 2

\[
L
=
E_1(t).
\]

Hence

\[
\boxed{
A
=
E_1(t)
D
E_1(t)^T.
}
\]

### Degree 3

Write the unit lower factor as

\[
L=
\begin{pmatrix}
1&0&0\\
x&1&0\\
y&z&1
\end{pmatrix}.
\]

Then

\[
\boxed{
L
=
E_1(\alpha)
E_2(\beta)
E_1(\gamma),
}
\]

with

\[
\beta=z,
\]

\[
\gamma=\frac{y}{z},
\]

\[
\alpha=x-\frac{y}{z}.
\]

Thus

\[
\boxed{
A=
E_1(\alpha)
E_2(\beta)
E_1(\gamma)
D
E_1(\gamma)^T
E_2(\beta)^T
E_1(\alpha)^T.
}
\]

This is exactly the path matrix of a concatenated planar bidiagonal-chip network.

---

## 8. Positive network weights

Across the complete 72-family audit, the canonical network has

\[
\boxed{
200
}
\]

unique lower-network/pivot weights.

Every one has numerator and denominator with strictly positive \(u\)-coefficients:

\[
\boxed{
200/200
\in
\mathcal S_+(u).
}
\]

Among them:

\[
\boxed{
112
}
\]

are polynomial weights, while

\[
\boxed{
88
}
\]

are genuinely proper rational weights.

Therefore the canonical network is positive over the same Stieltjes-type rational semiring found in 06U.

It is not, in general, a polynomial-edge-weight network.

---

## 9. Universal LGV/Cauchy-Binet templates

The final network minors were computed abstractly in independent edge variables.

Across degrees \(1,2,3\), there are

\[
\boxed{
25
}
\]

universal nonempty minor templates.

Every one is subtraction-free with strictly positive coefficients:

\[
\boxed{
25/25.
}
\]

The chip-by-chip multiplication was also audited.

At every intermediate stage, every nonzero minor remains a polynomial with nonnegative coefficients in the network edge variables.

Thus the planar-network minor recurrence is exactly a subtraction-free Cauchy-Binet recurrence.

This provides a clean finite LGV explanation of the final total positivity once the positive edge weights are known.

---

## 10. Circularity boundary

The canonical network weights are derived from Neville/LDL minor ratios.

Therefore:

\[
\boxed{
\textbf{their positivity is a repackaging of already certified total positivity.}
}
\]

Likewise, the root-quotient Gram network assumes the simple negative roots that 06R had already certified.

So neither network currently gives an independent all-\(n\) ribbon-Heisenberg proof of PF.

They explain the structure **after** the PF/total-positivity theorem is known.

The missing bridge is still from ribbon/Fock coefficients directly to coefficient-semiring minor positivity.

---

## 11. Exact counterexample to generic semiring positivity

The distinction above is real, not merely logical.

Consider

\[
\boxed{
P_*(x;u)
=
x^3
+
(6u^2+7u+13)x^2
+
(9u^2+11u+12)x
+
(u^2+5u+2).
}
\]

Every \(x\)-coefficient polynomial has strictly positive \(u\)-coefficients.

Its nontrivial cubic Hermite/subdiscriminant data are

\[
\boxed{
\operatorname{sDisc}_2
=
72u^4
+
168u^3
+
356u^2
+
298u
+
266
}
\]

and

\[
\boxed{
\begin{aligned}
\operatorname{Disc}
={}&
2052u^8
+
6588u^7
+
17433u^6
+
23390u^5
\\
&+
29273u^4
+
17368u^3
+
14184u^2
+
1156u
+
5356.
\end{aligned}
}
\]

Both are coefficientwise strictly positive.

Hence for every

\[
u\ge0
\]

the polynomial has three simple real roots; because every \(x\)-coefficient is positive, all three roots are negative.

So this polynomial satisfies the same type of coefficient/Hermite positivity used by 06R.

Nevertheless one \(2\times2\) minor of its reversed Bezout matrix is

\[
\boxed{
342u^6
+
699u^5
+
1700u^4
+
1185u^3
+
1392u^2
-
150u
+
592.
}
\]

This polynomial is positive for every

\[
u\ge0,
\]

but has a negative coefficient.

Therefore:

\[
\boxed{
\textbf{06R-style PF/subdiscriminant positivity does not generically imply coefficient-semiring total positivity of every Bezout minor.}
}
\]

The 456/456 semiring result is genuinely additional structure of the audited ribbon families.

---

## 12. Theorem ledger

### PA6V-T1 — root-quotient Bezout Gram identity

For

\[
P(x)=a\prod_i(x+\rho_i),
\]

\[
\frac{P(x)P'(y)-P(y)P'(x)}{x-y}
=
\sum_i q_i(x)q_i(y).
\]

**Status:** exact all-degree algebraic identity.

### PA6V-T2 — quotient coefficient total positivity

For

\[
0<\rho_1<\cdots<\rho_r,
\]

after reversing root and monomial order, the quotient coefficient matrix \(Q\) is strictly totally positive.

**Status:** theorem-level consequence of positive Vandermonde total positivity plus the checkerboard inverse/complementary-minor identity.

### PA6V-T3 — PF implies reversed-Bezout total positivity

If \(P\) has simple negative roots, then

\[
A=Q^TQ
\]

is strictly totally positive.

**Status:** exact all-degree theorem by Cauchy-Binet.

### PA6V-T4 — finite semiring recheck

All

\[
456/456
\]

audited reversed-Bezout minors lie in

\[
\mathbb Q_{>0}[u].
\]

**Status:** finite exact independent recheck.

### PA6V-T5 — canonical positive network

All

\[
72/72
\]

audited matrices admit the degree-appropriate bidiagonal LDL planar-network factorization.

**Status:** finite exact.

### PA6V-T6 — network-weight positivity

All

\[
200/200
\]

canonical unique weights belong to

\[
\mathcal S_+(u).
\]

**Status:** finite exact.

### PA6V-T7 — polynomial-weight boundary

Only

\[
112/200
\]

canonical weights are polynomial in \(u\); the remaining

\[

\]

are proper rational positive-semiring weights.

**Status:** finite exact.

### PA6V-T8 — subtraction-free minor templates

All

\[
25/25
\]

universal final-network minor templates are subtraction-free and strictly positive in the edge variables.

**Status:** exact symbolic theorem for degrees \(1,2,3\).

### PA6V-T9 — generic semiring implication

The cubic counterexample above satisfies 06R-style coefficient/subdiscriminant positivity but has a reversed-Bezout minor with a negative \(u\)-coefficient.

Therefore full coefficient-semiring total positivity is not a generic consequence of PF.

**Status:** exact counterexample.

### PA6V-T10 — all-\(n\) ribbon boundary

No direct ribbon-Heisenberg/Fock recurrence proving the coefficientwise positivity of all Bezout minors for arbitrary \(n\) has yet been obtained.

**Status:** open.

---

## 13. Main verdict

The 06U pattern splits into two mathematically different statements.

### Ordinary total positivity

\[
\boxed{
\textbf{explained generally by PF/simple negative roots.}
}
\]

The root-quotient Gram identity plus total positivity of the quotient coefficient matrix gives a direct all-degree Cauchy-Binet theorem.

### Coefficient-semiring total positivity

\[
\boxed{
\textbf{still special and only finite-certified.}
}
\]

The exact cubic counterexample proves this stronger property is not automatic even under the same kind of coefficient/subdiscriminant positivity used in 06R.

Thus the remaining theorem target has become substantially sharper:

\[
\boxed{
\textbf{why do the ribbon families force every universal cubic Bezout inequality into }\mathbb Q_{>0}[u]\textbf{?}
}
\]

---

Targets:

   \[
   bc-3ad,
   \]
   \[
   c^2-3bd,
   \]
   \[
   bc-9ad,
   \]
   \[
   3acd-4b^2d+bc^2,
   \]
   and the remaining \(2\times2\) determinant templates;
4. test those expressions directly against the ribbon-Heisenberg/Fock coefficient formulas;
5. seek coefficientwise-positive recurrences in \((n,d)\);
6. determine whether the mixed Young×ribbon family inherits the same inequalities from one common kernel;
7. promote to all-\(n\) only if the relevant coefficient cone is preserved directly, without invoking already-known root negativity.

# EHF-PHYS-ALG-06W — Ribbon/Fock Coefficient-Semiring Minor Positivity / Cubic Bezout Inequality Recurrence Audit

**Branch:** EHF-PHYS-ALG  
**Inputs:** PHYS-ALG-06R through 06V; ribbon/Fock reconstruction engine from 06S.  
**Primary target:** reduce the degree-\(\le3\) coefficient-semiring Bezout positivity problem to a minimal set of coefficient inequalities and test whether exact recurrence/divisibility structure is visible directly in the centered ribbon coefficients.

**Final status:** the complete degree-\(\le3\) ribbon/Fock regime through \(n\le35\) is finite-certified. The original 72-family \(n\le30\) window reproduces exactly and extends to 88 families. All 162 Hermite/subdiscriminant gates and all 624 reversed-Bezout minors are coefficientwise positive. For cubics, full semiring total positivity reduces exactly to four additional normalized gates \(H_1,H_2,K_3,K_4\). All 96/96 gate instances pass. Two exact positive-quotient recurrences and two exact \(K_3\) dominance identities are found. Simple scalar growth recurrence is rejected. The first quartic families enter at \(n=36,d=3\).

**Strict structural checks:** \(\boxed{12/12}\) PASS.

## 1. Full cubic-regime stress extension

The centered families were reconstructed directly from the ribbon/Fock definitions through

\[
n\le35.
\]

There are

\[
\boxed{44}
\]

admissible \((n,d)\) pairs and

\[
\boxed{88}
\]

families.

Degree distribution:

\[
\boxed{
38\text{ degree-1},\quad
26\text{ degree-2},\quad
24\text{ degree-3}.
}
\]

The frozen \(n\le30\) data reproduce exactly:

\[
\boxed{72/72}.
\]

The enlarged centered coefficient audit gives:

\[
\boxed{88/88}.
\]

The Hermite/subdiscriminant audit gives:

\[
\boxed{162/162}.
\]

The complete reversed-Bezout minor census gives:

\[
\boxed{624/624}.
\]

Thus the coefficient-semiring pattern survives every family before the first quartic layer appears.

## 2. Quadratic reduction

For

\[
P(x)=Ax^2+Bx+C,
\]

the reversed Bezout matrix is

\[
\begin{pmatrix}
2A^2 & AB\\
AB & B^2-2AC
\end{pmatrix}.
\]

Its determinant is

\[
A^2(B^2-4AC).
\]

And

\[
B^2-2AC
=
(B^2-4AC)+2AC.
\]

Therefore positive coefficient polynomials together with the 06R discriminant gate already force every quadratic Bezout minor to be coefficientwise positive.

So degree two introduces no new semiring inequality beyond the 06R theorem.

## 3. Cubic normalization

Write

\[
\boxed{
P(x)=Ax^3+3Bx^2+3Cx+D.
}
\]

Define the normalized Hankel minors

\[
\boxed{
H_0=B^2-AC,
}
\]

\[
\boxed{
H_1=BC-AD,
}
\]

\[
\boxed{
H_2=C^2-BD.
}
\]

The cubic discriminant has the exact form

\[
\boxed{
\Delta
=
4H_0H_2-H_1^2
=
\frac{\operatorname{Disc}(P)}{27}.
}
\]

The 06R principal-minor theorem supplies coefficientwise positivity of

\[
H_0
\]

and

\[
\Delta.
\]

## 4. Exact four-gate reduction

Symbolically reducing all 19 cubic reversed-Bezout minors shows that only four additional expressions are needed:

\[
\boxed{
H_1,\quad H_2,\quad K_3,\quad K_4,
}
\]

where

\[
\boxed{
K_3
=
ACD-4B^2D+3BC^2
}
\]

and

\[
\boxed{
K_4
=
-A^2D^2+10ABCD-6AC^3-12B^3D+9B^2C^2.
}
\]

All other cubic minors are positive monomial multiples or positive sums of:

- \(A,B,C,D\);
- \(H_0\);
- \(\Delta\);
- \(H_1,H_2,K_3,K_4\).

Thus, relative to 06R, full cubic semiring total positivity is equivalent to these four extra gates.

Across the 24 cubic families through \(n\le35\):

\[
\boxed{
96/96
}
\]

extra gate instances are coefficientwise strictly positive.

## 5. Plücker/Hankel identity

The normalized Hankel minors obey:

\[
\boxed{
BH_2-CH_1+DH_0=0.
}
\]

This is the first exact recurrence relation in the cubic gate system.

## 6. Positive quotient recurrence for \(H_2\)

The discriminant identity can be rewritten as

\[
\boxed{
4H_0H_2
=
H_1^2+\Delta.
}
\]

Therefore

\[
\boxed{
H_2
=
\frac{H_1^2+\Delta}{4H_0}.
}
\]

For every one of the 24 cubic ribbon/mixed families the division is exact and the quotient is coefficientwise strictly positive.

This does not by itself prove all-\(n\) semiring closure, because an exact quotient of positive-coefficient polynomials need not generically have positive coefficients.

But it converts \(H_2\) from an independent mystery into a precise semiring-divisibility problem.

## 7. \(K_3\) dominance recurrence

The mixed \(2\times2\) Bezout minor satisfies both:

\[
\boxed{
K_3=3BH_2-DH_0
}
\]

and

\[
\boxed{
K_3=3CH_1-4DH_0.
}
\]

Thus \(K_3\)-positivity is a direct coefficientwise domination condition.

All 24 audited instances pass.

## 8. Positive quotient recurrence for \(K_4\)

Desnanot-Jacobi condensation gives:

\[
\boxed{
H_0K_4
=
2A^2H_2^2
+
(2B^2+H_0)\Delta.
}
\]

Hence

\[
\boxed{
K_4
=
\frac{
2A^2H_2^2+(2B^2+H_0)\Delta
}{
H_0
}.
}
\]

Again, all 24 divisions are exact and all quotients are coefficientwise strictly positive.

Thus \(K_4\) is a second semiring exact-division problem rather than an unrelated inequality.

## 9. Exact recurrence audit

Across 24 cubic families there are five universal identities per family:

- one Plücker identity;
- one \(H_2\) quotient identity;
- two \(K_3\) dominance identities;
- one \(K_4\) quotient identity.

Combined:

\[
\boxed{
120/120
}
\]

exact identities pass.

So the four-gate cone is controlled by a compact recurrence ladder.

## 10. Finite growth monotonicity

For every available consecutive cubic

\[
m=n/d
\]

step at

\[
d=3,5,7,
\]

and for both family types, the differences of

\[
H_1,H_2,K_3,K_4
\]

are coefficientwise strictly positive.

Combined:

\[
\boxed{
48/48.
}
\]

Likewise, at every cubic \((n,d)\) pair,

\[
G_{\rm mixed}-G_{\rm ribbon}
\]

is coefficientwise strictly positive for every

\[
G\in\{H_1,H_2,K_3,K_4\}.
\]

Combined:

\[
\boxed{
48/48.
}
\]

These are finite structural signals only.

## 11. Simple growth recurrence no-fit

A constant scalar multiplicative recurrence was tested on every available three-step consecutive cubic window.

Result:

\[
\boxed{
0/24.
}
\]

Thus the gate growth is not merely geometric scaling.

## 12. Quartic boundary

At

\[
\boxed{
n=36,\qquad d=3,
}
\]

both family types acquire

\[
\boxed{
\deg_x P=4.
}
\]

Therefore \(n\le35\) is the complete natural finite window for the universal cubic analysis.

## 13. Main verdict

The complete degree-\(\le3\) ribbon/Fock sector now has the finite exact theorem:

\[
\boxed{
624/624
\text{ reversed-Bezout minors lie in }
\mathbb Q_{>0}[u].
}
\]

For cubics, all extra content beyond the 06R principal-minor theorem reduces exactly to

\[
\boxed{
H_1,\ H_2,\ K_3,\ K_4.
}
\]

And the recurrence ladder reduces the conceptual bottleneck further:

\[
\boxed{
\textbf{two direct dominance gates }(H_1,K_3)
\textbf{ plus two positive exact-division closures }(H_2,K_4).
}
\]

This is a major narrowing of the all-\(n\) problem, but not yet an all-\(n\) proof.

## 14. Recommended next audit

### EHF-PHYS-ALG-06X — Quartic Ribbon Bezout Semiring Cone / Higher Hankel-Minor Recurrence Audit

The next audit should begin exactly where the cubic theorem ceases to apply:

\[
n=36,\ d=3.
\]

It should derive the binomially normalized quartic coefficient cone, classify all quartic Bezout minors, determine which are already forced by Hermite subdiscriminants, and test whether the cubic Plücker/positive-division ladder survives in the first degree-4 sector.

# EHF-PHYS-ALG-06X — Quartic Ribbon Bezout Semiring Cone / Higher Hankel-Minor Recurrence Audit

**Branch:** EHF-PHYS-ALG  
**Inputs:** PHYS-ALG-06R through 06W  
**Status:** first quartic stress window finite-certified; 1656/1656 reversed-Bezout minors coefficientwise positive; exact Plücker and Desnanot–Jacobi recurrence structure found; naive higher-Hankel sign hypothesis rejected.

## 1. First quartic window

The audited `36 <= n <= 50` range contains 12 quartic `(n,d)` pairs and 24 ribbon/mixed families.

Use

\[
P(x)=Ax^4+4Bx^3+6Cx^2+4Dx+E.
\]

## 2. Complete quartic Bezout census

Each reversed 4x4 Bezout matrix has 69 nonempty minors.

Combined:

\[
\boxed{1656/1656}
\]

minor instances are coefficientwise strictly positive in \(u\).

The 69 raw templates reduce to 36 distinct symbolic expressions.

## 3. Quartic Hankel front cone

Define

\[
H_{01}=B^2-AC,\quad H_{02}=BC-AD,\quad H_{03}=BD-AE,
\]

\[
H_{12}=C^2-BD,\quad H_{13}=CD-BE,\quad H_{23}=D^2-CE,
\]

and

\[
J_3=H_{01}H_{03}+9H_{01}H_{12}-3H_{02}^2.
\]

Together with the normalized quartic discriminant, these form an eight-gate semiring front cone.

Result:

\[
\boxed{192/192}
\]

coefficientwise positive.

Also

\[
\boxed{\Delta_3=192A^2J_3}.
\]

## 4. Plücker relation

\[
\boxed{H_{01}H_{23}-H_{02}H_{13}+H_{03}H_{12}=0}.
\]

Exact in all 24 families.

## 5. Higher-Hankel no-fit

The natural 3x3 quantity

\[
T_3=AH_{23}-BH_{13}+CH_{12}
\]

is not sign-uniform:

\[
\boxed{18/24\text{ positive},\qquad 6/24\text{ mixed-sign}.}
\]

Therefore universal higher coefficient-Hankel determinant positivity is not the quartic theorem mechanism.

## 6. Desnanot–Jacobi positive-division ladder

There are 14 nontrivial solid-minor recurrences per family.

Combined:

\[
\boxed{336/336}
\]

are exact, with coefficientwise positive outputs, denominators, and subtraction margins.

## 7. Growth and inheritance

For the seven sign-stable Hankel gates:

\[
\boxed{84/84}
\]

available consecutive growth differences are coefficientwise positive, and

\[
\boxed{84/84}
\]

mixed-minus-ribbon differences are coefficientwise positive.

A simple scalar multiplicative law gives

\[
\boxed{0/28}
\]

hits.

## 8. Main verdict

The first quartic window preserves full coefficient-semiring Bezout total positivity, but the higher-Hankel sign failure shows that the correct all-n object is a smaller chamber/solid-minor cone closed by exact positive divisions.

The all-n theorem remains open.

## 9. Recommended next audit

### EHF-PHYS-ALG-06Y — Quartic Chamber-Minor Positive-Division / Ribbon-Heisenberg Induction Audit

Reduce the 36 symmetry-distinct quartic templates to a minimal chamber/solid-minor generating set and seek a direct ribbon/Fock semiring-preservation recurrence.

# EHF-PHYS-ALG-06Y — Quartic Chamber-Minor Positive-Division / Ribbon-Heisenberg Induction Audit

**Branch:** EHF-PHYS-ALG  
**Inputs:** PHYS-ALG-06R through 06X  
**Status:** exact ten-chamber network reduction established; quartic chamber coefficient positivity finite-certified; naive chamber-growth induction and BG-only ribbon-Heisenberg closure rejected.

## 1. Ten chamber coordinates

For the symmetric \(4\times4\) planar-network factorization

\[
M=L(t_1,\ldots,t_6)
\operatorname{diag}(d_1,\ldots,d_4)
L^T
\]

with reduced word

\[
s_1s_2s_3s_1s_2s_1,
\]

choose the ten matrix minors

\[
\boxed{
\Delta_1,\Delta_2,\Delta_3,\Delta_4,
\Gamma_3,\Gamma_{23},\Gamma_{123},
\Gamma_{35},\Gamma_{2345},\Gamma_{356}.
}
\]

They are exact monomials in the ten network parameters.

All:

\[
\boxed{10/10}
\]

monomial identities pass.

The inverse chamber formulas recover all six edge weights and four diagonal pivots exactly:

\[
\boxed{10/10}.
\]

## 2. Universal network minors

Every one of the 69 nonempty minors of the generic network matrix is subtraction-free in the ten positive network parameters.

Result:

\[
\boxed{69/69}.
\]

Thus, at matrix level,

\[
\boxed{
69\text{ minors}
\longrightarrow
10\text{ chamber coordinates}
\longrightarrow
10\text{ positive network parameters}.
}
\]

This is an exact generic symmetric-network theorem.

## 3. Ribbon chamber positivity

Across the 24 quartic ribbon/mixed families from 06X:

\[
\boxed{240/240}
\]

selected chamber-minor instances are coefficientwise strictly positive in \(u\).

The exact chamber-to-network formulas therefore place all

\[
\boxed{240/240}
\]

network-parameter instances in the positive rational-function semiring.

This compactly explains the finite pointwise total-positivity structure.

The stronger statement that every exact polynomial minor has positive \(u\)-coefficients remains the finite 06X semiring theorem rather than a generic consequence of the rational network formulas.

## 4. Mixed-family inheritance

Every mixed-minus-ribbon chamber difference is coefficientwise strictly positive:

\[
\boxed{120/120}.
\]

So the mixed Young×ribbon family dominates the ribbon-path family at the chamber-coordinate level throughout the first quartic window.

## 5. Chamber monotonic induction fails

There are 120 available consecutive-\(m\) chamber differences.

Only

\[
\boxed{113/120}
\]

are coefficientwise strictly positive.

Seven exact failures occur.

Therefore the all-\(n\) result cannot follow from the naive induction

\[
\Gamma_{m+1}-\Gamma_m\in\mathbb Q_+[u]
\]

for every chamber coordinate.

A constant scalar multiplicative recurrence also fails completely:

\[
\boxed{0/40}.
\]

## 6. BG-only ribbon-Heisenberg transfer fails

The ribbon-path coefficients arise from repeated action of the \(d\)-ribbon up operator \(U_d(q)\) on partition Fock space.

If BG rank were sufficient for an exact reduced induction, partitions in the same BG sector would have identical target-BG \(q\)-polynomial transition profiles.

The first nontrivial transfer

\[
m=1\to2
\]

already violates this condition for every tested odd support:

\[
\boxed{d=3,5,7,9,11}.
\]

Combined:

\[
\boxed{5/5\text{ BG-lumpability tests fail}.}
\]

So BG rank alone is not an exact quotient/intertwiner of the ribbon-Heisenberg up operator.

## 7. Main verdict

06Y solves the quartic matrix-compression part but rejects the simplest induction mechanisms.

The exact structure is:

\[
\boxed{
\textbf{ten positive chamber coordinates generate the symmetric TP network,}
}
\]

while the unresolved all-\(n\) step is:

\[
\boxed{
\textbf{derive those chamber coordinates from a richer shape-resolved ribbon-Heisenberg transfer.}
}
\]

Any successful induction must retain more information than BG rank alone.

## 8. Recommended next audit

### EHF-PHYS-ALG-06Z — Shape-Resolved Ribbon-Heisenberg Transfer / Refined Chamber-Cone Intertwining Audit

Targets:

1. test candidate refinements of BG rank such as \(d\)-abacus/quotient data, residue profiles, and removable-ribbon signatures;
2. identify the smallest state descriptor that closes \(U_d(q)\);
3. build the resulting positive transfer kernel;
4. connect its minors/moments to the ten chamber coordinates;
5. test whether mixed Young×ribbon weighting shares the same kernel structure;
6. require exact cone-preservation before any all-\(n\) promotion.

# EHF-PHYS-ALG-06Z — Shape-Resolved Ribbon-Heisenberg Transfer / Refined Chamber-Cone Intertwining Audit

**Branch:** EHF-PHYS-ALG  
**Inputs:** PHYS-ALG-06R through 06Y  
**Status:** full ordered \(d\)-quotient gives exact transfer coordinates; natural coarse refinements fail; mixed finite-horizon exact quotient becomes shape-resolved; first quartic shape-transfer/chamber intertwining certified.

## 1. Ordered \(d\)-quotient transfer

The audit uses exact reachable ribbon graphs for

\[
d=3,5,7,9,11
\]

with finite horizons chosen to keep the complete partition census exact.

Across all audited transfers:

\[
\boxed{7407/7407}
\]

reachable ribbon edges become a one-box addition on exactly one component of the ordered \(d\)-quotient.

Thus the empty-core ribbon sector has the canonical coordinate

\[
\boxed{
\lambda
\leftrightarrow
(\lambda^{(0)},\ldots,\lambda^{(d-1)}).
}
\]

The full ordered quotient is injective on every audited level and is strongly lumpable on all

\[
\boxed{15/15}
\]

tested transfers.

This is exact but not a compression: it is a shape coordinate.

## 2. Natural coarse refinements fail

BG rank alone is not an exact transfer coordinate.

Adding the ordered quotient-size vector improves the first transfer but fails at deeper layers.

Even the richer runner descriptor

\[
(BG;\ |\alpha_i|,\ell(\alpha_i),\alpha_{i,1})_{i=0}^{d-1}
\]

eventually fails, first in the audited \(d=3\) horizon.

Thus the \(q\)-weighted ribbon transfer depends on finer quotient-shape information than runner occupation counts or simple boundary statistics.

## 3. Coarsest finite-horizon exact refinement

Backward equitable refinement from terminal observables was used to compute the minimal exact quotient in each finite horizon.

For the ribbon terminal observable \(BG\), the quotient rapidly approaches full shape resolution several layers before the horizon.

For the mixed terminal observable

\[
(BG(\lambda),f^\lambda),
\]

the result is stronger:

\[
\boxed{
20/20\text{ audited preterminal layers are fully shape-resolved.}
}
\]

In other words, within every tested horizon, no nontrivial preterminal quotient preserves the mixed observable exactly.

Representative class counts are:

### \(d=3\), horizon \(m=8\)

Ribbon refinement:

m=0: 1/1, m=1: 3/3, m=2: 9/9, m=3: 22/22, m=4: 51/51, m=5: 108/108, m=6: 217/221, m=7: 182/429, m=8: 3/810

Mixed refinement:

m=0: 1/1, m=1: 3/3, m=2: 9/9, m=3: 22/22, m=4: 51/51, m=5: 108/108, m=6: 221/221, m=7: 429/429, m=8: 365/810

### \(d=5\), horizon \(m=5\)

Ribbon refinement:

m=0: 1/1, m=1: 5/5, m=2: 20/20, m=3: 65/65, m=4: 150/190, m=5: 4/506

Mixed refinement:

m=0: 1/1, m=1: 5/5, m=2: 20/20, m=3: 65/65, m=4: 190/190, m=5: 244/506

The mixed refinement is injective at every preterminal layer in both cases.

## 4. First quartic shape-transfer reconstruction

For

\[
n=36,\qquad d=3,\qquad m=12,
\]

the exact shape-resolved ribbon recursion has

\[
\boxed{7868}
\]

reachable endpoint partitions.

Aggregating their exact ribbon-spin polynomials by BG rank and applying the centered variable

\[
u=q+q^{-1}-2
\]

reproduces the frozen 06X quartic coefficient vectors for both family types.

Combined:

\[
\boxed{10/10}
\]

centered coefficient slots match exactly.

## 5. Chamber-cone intertwining

The reconstructed quartic polynomials reproduce all ten 06Y chamber coordinates for both family types:

\[
\boxed{20/20}.
\]

So there is an exact finite intertwining chain:

\[
\boxed{
\text{shape-resolved ribbon/Fock transfer}
\longrightarrow
\text{BG-centered quartic polynomial}
\longrightarrow
\text{positive chamber cone}.
}
\]

The obstruction is therefore not reconstruction of the chamber cone.

It is the lack of a nontrivial compressed state space on which the exact transfer closes.

## 6. Main verdict

The natural exact state variable is the full ordered \(d\)-quotient.

The branch should therefore move from “find a better scalar statistic than BG” to:

\[
\boxed{
\textbf{factor the exact transfer on the \(d\)-quotient runners.}
}
\]

This is compatible with the ribbon-Heisenberg/Fock interpretation and preserves all shape information required by the mixed family.

The all-\(n\) chamber theorem remains open.

## 7. Recommended next audit

### EHF-PHYS-ALG-07A — \(d\)-Quotient Fock Factorization / Runner-Resolved Positive Kernel and Chamber Functional Audit

Targets:

1. derive the exact one-box runner move and its \(q\)-height directly in quotient coordinates;
2. factor the transfer into runner-local creation operators and a positive interaction/ordering factor;
3. express BG-centered coefficients and the quartic chamber coordinates as positive functionals/minors of the runner-resolved kernel;
4. treat mixed Young×ribbon weighting as a terminal functional on the same exact transfer;
5. seek all-\(n\) semiring preservation in the full quotient/Fock coordinates.
