# EHF-PHYS-REV-14 — Reversal-Sector Theorem Synthesis / Minimal Axiom and Structural Dependency Audit

**Side branch:** EHF-PHYS-REV  
**Inputs:** REV-01 through REV-13.  
**Status:** PASS. The reversal theorem chain separates into microscopic anchors, a five-hypothesis reusable spectral core, an exact-certifiability hypothesis, and five kernel-specific certificate modules.

## 1. Microscopic anchors

Two facts connect the packet effective theory back to actual local reversals.

### M1 — Local reversal conjugacy

\[
\sigma_RR_{s,L}\sigma_R^{-1}
=
R_{-s-L+1,L}.
\]

### M2 — Packet descent and locked finite-\(n\) family

\[
\beta(\sigma_Rc)=\operatorname{rev}\beta(c),
\]

with

\[
H_n
=
H_9+(n-9)7\,\operatorname{diag}(L_b).
\]

These are OPW anchoring facts.

They are not required if the packet pencil itself is taken as primitive.

## 2. Five-hypothesis reusable structural core

REV-14 identifies the following minimal sufficient structural core relative to the generic spectral theorem package.

### A1 — Finite reversible Laplacian pencil

\[
L_n=B_n^{-1}A,
\]

with fixed symmetric graph Laplacian \(A\) and positive diagonal \(B_n\).

### A2 — Reflection covariance

\[
R^2=I,
\qquad
[A,R]=[B_n,R]=0,
\]

with the observed scalar \(s\in V^-\).

### A3 — Odd-sector gap

\[
A|_{V^-}\succ0.
\]

### A4 — Strict metric growth and continuum limit

\[
B_{n+2}\succeq c_nB_n,
\qquad
c_n>1,
\qquad
B_n\to B_\infty.
\]

### A5 — Cyclic odd scalar

The scalar \(s\) is cyclic in the odd sector.

For the concrete OPW family the odd dimension is six, but the generic theorem uses an arbitrary finite cyclic dimension \(d\).

## 3. Generic consequences of A1–A5

The following are reusable theorem schemas rather than OPW-specific numerical facts:

- invariant parity sectors;
- reversible finite relaxation / Dirichlet formulation;
- strictly positive odd relaxation spectrum;
- positive \(d\)-atom scalar Stieltjes measure;
- exact depth-\(d\) Lanczos continued fraction;
- strict response-memory and truncation interlacing;
- exact finite Schur/Feshbach and memory reductions;
- individual complete monotonicity;
- strict monotone pole convergence under A4.

Thus most of the structural content of REV-07, REV-08 and REV-10 belongs to a broader finite reversible spectral class.

## 4. A6 — Rational parameterization

The exact proof machinery additionally uses

\[
A,B(t),s\in\mathbb Q(t),
\qquad
t=\frac1n.
\]

This gives rational Lanczos laws and algebraic residue laws.

A6 is a **certifiability hypothesis**, not a qualitative spectral axiom.

The generic spectral theorems above do not require rational coefficients.

## 5. Kernel-specific certificate modules

The stronger late-REV results require additional concrete sign information.

### C1 — Concrete channel/Fourier certificate

Needed for the exact L2/L3, Boolean-mask and Fourier/operator diagnostics.

### C2 — Residue turning certificate

Needed for the exact \(n=11,13\) residue turning laws of REV-11.

### C3 — Stieltjes derivative sign certificate

The REV-12 all-\(n\) Stieltjes order uses the concrete \(143/143\) negative transformed coefficients.

### C4 — Raw power-order certificate

REV-13 uses:

\[
k\le34,\qquad K_{\rm tail}=35,
\]

plus exact endpoint closure to prove every positive raw moment decreases.

### C5 — Complementary power-order certificate

REV-13 uses

\[
M=\frac{25}{4},
\qquad
K_{\rm comp}=2,
\]

plus endpoint closure to prove every complementary moment increases and hence the all-\(\tau\) Laplace order.

The **propagation lemmas** are generic.

The seed values and sign certificates are concrete.

## 6. Exact minimality counterexamples

REV-14 certifies several non-implications.

### Pole contraction does not imply moment contraction

Both support points can move left while residue transfer raises the first moment.

### All raw moments do not imply Laplace order

An exact three-atom counterexample is

\[
\mu_{\rm old}
=
\frac1{20}\delta_1
+
\frac9{20}\delta_3
+
\frac12\delta_5,
\]

\[
\mu_{\rm new}
=
\frac1{20}\delta_1
+
\frac1{20}\delta_2
+
\frac9{10}\delta_4.
\]

For every \(k\ge1\),

\[
m_k(\mu_{\rm old})>m_k(\mu_{\rm new}).
\]

For \(k=1,2,3\) this is checked exactly.

For \(k\ge4\),

\[
m_k(\mu_{\rm old})
\ge\frac12\,5^k
>
4^k
\ge m_k(\mu_{\rm new}).
\]

Yet at

\[
\tau=\log2,
\]

\[
C_{\rm old}(\log2)=\frac{31}{320},
\]

while

\[
C_{\rm new}(\log2)=\frac3{32}=\frac{30}{320}.
\]

Therefore

\[
\boxed{
C_{\rm new}(\log2)<C_{\rm old}(\log2).
}
\]

Hence the complementary-order information used in REV-13 is genuinely additional.

This is the sharpest minimality result of REV-14.

## 7. Abstract theorem schemas extracted from REV-01–13

### SYN-1 — Reversible reflection-sector theorem

A1+A2+A3+A5 give the positive finite scalar spectral/Jacobi/interlacing/complete-monotonicity package.

### SYN-2 — Monotone metric spectral-flow theorem

A1+A2+A3+A4 give strict pole contraction and continuum convergence.

### SYN-3 — Algebraic-certifiability theorem

A6 plus cyclic finite data give rational recurrence laws and algebraic residue laws.

### SYN-4 — Raw-tail propagation lemma

A finite branchwise raw-power seed plus strict pole contraction propagates to every larger raw power.

### SYN-5 — Complementary-tail Laplace lemma

A common spectral ceiling and ordered complementary moments give all-\(\tau\) Laplace-response order by a positive exponential generating series.

## 8. Abstraction boundary

The following concrete values are **not** promoted to general EHF axioms:

\[
16,\quad6,\quad \frac{25}{4},\quad35,\quad2,\quad11,\quad13.
\]

Nor are the concrete mask counts, Fourier coefficients or critical-resultant signs.

They remain properties of the locked OPW packet family.

## 9. Main synthesis verdict

The reversal branch has produced more than one isolated computation, but less than a universal EHF theorem.

Its reusable backbone is:

\[
\boxed{
\text{finite reversible pencil}
+
\text{reflection covariance}
+
\text{odd gap}
+
\text{strict metric growth}
+
\text{cyclic odd scalar}.
}
\]

The strong residue/transform/moment/Laplace orders require extra certificate modules.

That is the appropriate theorem boundary after REV-13.

## 10. Recommended next audit

### EHF-PHYS-REV-15 — Abstract Reversal-Spectral Family / Generic Theorem Formalization and Non-OPW Example Census

The next step should leave the concrete OPW kernel temporarily and test the abstract core itself:

1. formalize SYN-1 and SYN-2 as standalone theorems;
2. formalize the raw/complement propagation lemmas;
3. construct non-OPW examples satisfying A1–A5;
4. determine which examples satisfy or violate C3/C4/C5-type stronger orders.

That would establish whether the reversal program has genuinely discovered a reusable theorem family.

# EHF-PHYS-REV-15 — Abstract Reversal-Spectral Family / Generic Theorem Formalization and Non-OPW Example Census

**Side branch:** EHF-PHYS-REV  
**Inputs:** REV-14 structural synthesis, with theorem mechanisms inherited from REV-10 and REV-13.  
**Status:** PASS. The five-hypothesis structural core is instantiated outside OPW in five exact weighted-path families, while the stronger C3/C4/C5 order properties split and fail independently.

## 1. Generic theorem formalization

### SYN-1 — Finite reversible reflection-sector theorem

Assume:

\[
A=A^T,
\qquad
B>0,
\]

an involution

\[
R^2=I
\]

with

\[
[A,R]=[B,R]=0,
\]

positive definiteness of \(A\) on the \(R\)-odd sector, and a cyclic odd scalar \(s\).

Then, if the odd cyclic dimension is \(d\):

\[
\boxed{
\mu_s
=
\sum_{j=0}^{d-1}
w_j\delta_{\kappa_j}
}
\]

has exactly \(d\) simple positive atoms with

\[
w_j>0.
\]

The scalar Lanczos chain has exact depth \(d\), the Jacobi matrix is irreducible, response/memory and consecutive-truncation poles strictly interlace, and

\[
C(\tau)
=
\sum_jw_je^{-\kappa_j\tau}
\]

is completely monotone.

This is a generic finite-dimensional theorem.

### SYN-2 — Monotone metric spectral-flow theorem

For a fixed positive odd-sector form \(A\), suppose

\[
B_{n+2}\succeq c_nB_n,
\qquad
c_n>1.
\]

Then the generalized Rayleigh quotient gives

\[
\boxed{
\kappa_j(n+2)
\le
\frac{\kappa_j(n)}{c_n}
<
\kappa_j(n).
}
\]

If

\[
B_n\to B_\infty,
\]

then

\[
\boxed{
\kappa_j(n)\downarrow\kappa_j(\infty).
}
\]

This is the reusable theorem behind the REV-10 pole-flow result.

### SYN-3 — Algebraic certifiability

If the finite data lie in

\[
\mathbb Q(t),
\]

then every nonterminated Lanczos coefficient is rational over \(\mathbb Q(t)\), and residues are algebraic through

\[
w_j(t)
=
\frac{N(\kappa_j(t),t)}
{P_x(\kappa_j(t),t)}.
\]

This is a certifiability theorem, not an order theorem.

### SYN-4 / SYN-5 — Tail propagation

The REV-13 propagation mechanisms are generic:

- a raw branch-power seed plus pole contraction propagates to all larger raw powers;
- a complementary branch-power seed below a common ceiling \(M\) propagates to all larger complementary powers;
- ordered complementary moments imply all-\(\tau\) Laplace order by a positive exponential generating series.

The existence of the seed inequalities remains model-specific.

## 2. Five non-OPW example families

REV-15 constructs:

\[
\boxed{
\begin{aligned}
&E1:\ P_4,\ \text{edge weights }(1,3,1),\\
&E2:\ P_4,\ \text{edge weights }(5,3,5),\\
&E3:\ P_4,\ \text{edge weights }(2,3,2),\\
&E4:\ P_4,\ \text{edge weights }(1,2,1),\\
&E5:\ P_6,\ \text{edge weights }(3,1,2,1,3).
\end{aligned}
}
\]

Each uses a reflection-symmetric metric

\[
B_n=B_\infty-\frac{C}{n},
\]

with \(C>0\), and a reflection-odd scalar.

The first four have odd dimension \(2\); the fifth has odd dimension \(3\).

No OPW packet kernel is used.

## 3. Exact A1–A5 certification

For every example:

### A1

The graph is a connected positive weighted path, hence its Laplacian is symmetric and has only the constant zero mode.

### A2

The edge weights and metrics are reflection symmetric and the distinguished scalar is reflection odd.

### A3

The graph-kernel constant mode is reflection even, so the odd restriction of the Laplacian is positive definite.

### A4

Because

\[
B_n=B_\infty-\frac{C}{n},
\]

\[
\boxed{
B_{n+2}-B_n
=
C\left(\frac1n-\frac1{n+2}\right)>0.
}
\]

Thus a strict finite uniform factor exists and

\[
B_n\to B_\infty.
\]

### A5

Cyclicity was certified symbolically.

The exact Krylov determinants are

\[
E1:
\quad
\frac{n(393n-461)}
{5(n-1)(6n-7)},
\]

\[
E2:
\quad
\frac{n(73n-82)}
{(n-4)(2n-1)},
\]

\[
E3:
\quad
\frac{n(2n-7)}
{(2n-3)(2n-1)},
\]

\[
E4:
\quad
\frac{4n}{3n-7},
\]

and

\[
E5:
\quad
-\frac{
n^3(5058n^3-70173n^2+80696n-24652)
}{
32(n-2)^2(2n-1)^2(3n-2)^2
}.
\]

For \(E1\)–\(E4\), all numerator zeros are explicitly outside the allowed odd domain.

For \(E5\), the nontrivial cubic has no root modulo \(13\), hence no integer root.

Therefore all five examples satisfy A1–A5 for every odd integer

\[
\boxed{n\ge9}.
\]

## 4. Generic-core census

The five families were evaluated at every odd

\[
9\le n\le1001.
\]

That is

\[
\boxed{497}
\]

sizes per family.

Across all five examples:

\[
\boxed{
\text{positive spectral-measure failures}=0,
}
\]

\[
\boxed{
\text{strict interlacing failures}=0,
}
\]

\[
\boxed{
\text{strict pole-contraction failures}=0.
}
\]

This is a falsification census, not the proof of SYN-1/SYN-2; those are already generic theorems.

Its value is that the abstraction actually survives on concrete non-OPW families.

## 5. Exact failure of raw-moment universality

The stronger C4 property fails inside the A1–A5 class.

For \(E3\),

\[
\boxed{
m_6(9)-m_6(11)
=
-\frac{
2537693017246761896387
}{
1076887531766032060725000
}
<0.
}
\]

So even with:

- reflection covariance;
- a positive odd gap;
- cyclicity;
- strict pole contraction;

the sixth raw moment moves in the wrong direction.

Therefore

\[
\boxed{
A1\text{--}A5
\not\Rightarrow
\text{all-positive-moment order}.
}
\]

C4-type information is genuinely additional.

## 6. Exact failure of Stieltjes-order universality

The stronger C3 property also fails inside A1–A5.

For \(E4\),

\[
\boxed{
\widehat C_{11}(0)
-
\widehat C_9(0)
=
-\frac{1402}{69003}
<0.
}
\]

Thus

\[
\boxed{
A1\text{--}A5
\not\Rightarrow
\text{REV-12 Stieltjes-transform order}.
}
\]

The OPW \(143/143\) derivative-sign certificate was not merely a convenient proof of something automatic.

It encoded real additional structure.

## 7. Laplace order also separates

Three core-valid families show finite response-grid violations:

\[
\boxed{
E2,\ E4,\ E5.
}
\]

For example, \(E2\) first shows

\[
C_{11}(\tau)-C_9(\tau)<0
\]

on the census grid near

\[
\tau\approx0.2283.
\]

These Laplace witnesses are numerical census results rather than symbolic all-\(\tau\) counterexamples.

The exact REV-14 three-atom example already proves logical independence of all-positive-moment and Laplace order.

## 8. The stronger certificate classes split

The five-family census does not produce one yes/no block.

Instead:

- \(E1\): C3/C4/C5 finite grids all pass;
- \(E2\): Stieltjes and first-20 raw-moment grids pass, Laplace grid fails;
- \(E3\): Stieltjes/Laplace grids pass, but raw moment order fails exactly at \(k=6\);
- \(E4\): raw grid passes, Stieltjes order fails exactly and Laplace grid fails;
- \(E5\): three-dimensional odd sector; Stieltjes grid passes while raw/Laplace grids eventually fail.

Therefore

\[
\boxed{
C3,\ C4,\ C5
}
\]

should be treated as separate order-certificate properties.

They are not hidden consequences of one universal A1–A5 order.

## 9. Near-miss census

REV-15 also records examples dropping one core assumption:

- degenerate \(K_4\) odd sector: A5 fails and scalar atom count collapses;
- disconnected reflected graph: A3 fails and an odd zero mode survives;
- asymmetric metric: A2 fails and parity is not invariant;
- shrinking metric: A4 fails and poles increase.

These agree with the REV-14 minimality counterexamples.

## 10. Main verdict

REV-14's abstraction survives contact with non-OPW examples.

The reusable class is genuinely larger than the original packet model:

\[
\boxed{
A1+A2+A3+A4+A5
}
\]

supports the finite reversible reflection-spectral backbone.

But the stronger order theorems from REV-11–13 are **not** structural consequences of that core.

The correct architecture is therefore:

\[
\boxed{
\text{generic structural core}
+
\text{separate stronger-order certificate modules}.
}
\]

That is now supported both by theorem formalization and by explicit non-OPW examples.

## 11. Claim boundary

SYN-1 and SYN-2 are exact finite-dimensional theorems.

The five weighted-path families satisfy A1–A5 exactly for every allowed odd \(n\).

The stronger-order census contains:

- exact algebraic counterexamples for raw moments and Stieltjes order;
- finite numerical classifications for the remaining response-grid behavior.

The census does not classify every abstract reversible reflection family.

## 12. Recommended next audit

### EHF-PHYS-REV-16 — Abstract Certificate Taxonomy / Necessary-and-Sufficient Order Criteria and Random Reversible-Family Counterexample Search

The next task should characterize the extra order certificates rather than enlarge the example list blindly.

Targets:

1. necessary/sufficient conditions for Stieltjes-transform order;
2. usable criteria for all-positive-moment order;
3. usable criteria for complementary-moment/Laplace order;
4. randomized rational reversible-family searches to test independence and locate minimal counterexamples.

# EHF-PHYS-REV-16 — Abstract Certificate Taxonomy / Necessary-and-Sufficient Order Criteria and Random Reversible-Family Counterexample Search

**Side branch:** EHF-PHYS-REV  
**Inputs:** REV-12 through REV-15.  
**Status:** PASS. The stronger-order layer is reorganized into exact finite-measure criteria plus independent model-specific certificate classes.

## 1. C3 — Exact Stieltjes criterion

For two finite positive atomic measures define

\[
\Delta \widehat C(z)
=
\widehat C_{\rm new}(z)-\widehat C_{\rm old}(z).
\]

After placing the terms over a common denominator,

\[
\Delta\widehat C(z)
=
\frac{N(z)}{D(z)}.
\]

Because every relaxation atom is positive,

\[
D(z)>0
\qquad
(z\ge0).
\]

Therefore

\[
\boxed{
\Delta\widehat C(z)\ge0\ \forall z\ge0
\iff
N(z)\ge0\ \forall z\ge0.
}
\]

Thus C3 is exactly a polynomial nonnegativity problem on the positive half-line.

For rational finite data it is decidable by exact root isolation/Sturm methods.

The REV-12 \(143/143\) coefficient-sign result is therefore a strong sufficient certificate for the exact C3 property, not the definition of C3.

## 2. C4 — Exact all-positive-moment criterion

Write the signed raw-moment difference as

\[
\Delta m_k
=
m_k({\rm old})-m_k({\rm new})
=
\sum_{i=1}^{s}a_ir_i^k,
\]

with distinct

\[
0<r_1<\cdots<r_s.
\]

If the coefficient at the largest nonzero support satisfies

\[
a_s<0,
\]

then

\[
\Delta m_k<0
\]

eventually, so all-positive-moment order is impossible.

If

\[
a_s>0,
\]

choose \(K\) such that

\[
a_sr_s^K
>
\sum_{a_i<0}|a_i|r_i^K.
\]

Then the same inequality persists for every \(k\ge K\), because

\[
\frac{r_i}{r_s}<1.
\]

Hence

\[
\boxed{
\Delta m_k>0\ \forall k\ge1
}
\]

if and only if:

1. the top signed support coefficient is positive; and
2. the finitely many exact inequalities for \(1\le k<K\) pass.

Thus C4 is an exact finite-tail decision problem for finite positive support.

REV-13's \(K=35\) proof is one concrete realization of this general criterion.

## 3. C5 — Exact complementary-moment criterion

Choose a common ceiling

\[
M\ge\max \operatorname{supp}\mu.
\]

Define

\[
y=M-\lambda.
\]

Then complementary moments

\[
q_k=\int y^k\,d\mu
\]

are ordinary positive-power moments in the transformed support.

Therefore C5 has exactly the same finite-tail necessary-and-sufficient criterion as C4.

This tells us precisely what REV-13 proved for the OPW packet family.

## 4. C5 is not necessary for Laplace order

Take

\[
\mu_{\rm old}
=
\frac7{10}\delta_4+\frac3{10}\delta_2
\]

and

\[
\mu_{\rm new}
=
\frac14\delta_5+\frac7{10}\delta_3+\frac1{20}\delta_1.
\]

Let

\[
y=e^{-\tau}.
\]

Then

\[
\boxed{
C_{\rm new}(\tau)-C_{\rm old}(\tau)
=
\frac{
y(1-y)^2(5y^2-4y+1)
}{20}.
}
\]

For

\[
\tau>0,
\qquad
0<y<1.
\]

The quadratic factor is strictly positive because

\[
\operatorname{disc}(5y^2-4y+1)
=
-4.
\]

Therefore

\[
\boxed{
C_{\rm new}(\tau)>C_{\rm old}(\tau)
\qquad
\forall\tau>0.
}
\]

But with

\[
M=5,
\]

\[
\boxed{
q_4({\rm new})-q_4({\rm old})=-1.
}
\]

Hence

\[
\boxed{
\text{C5 is sufficient for Laplace order but not necessary.}
}
\]

This is complementary to REV-14's exact result that even all raw positive moments do not imply Laplace order.

## 5. Exact Laplace criterion for commensurate spectra

If

\[
\lambda_i=\lambda_0+hm_i,
\qquad
m_i\in\mathbb Z_{\ge0},
\]

then

\[
\Delta C(\tau)
=
e^{-\lambda_0\tau}
P(e^{-h\tau}).
\]

Set

\[
y=e^{-h\tau}.
\]

Then \(y\in(0,1]\), so

\[
\boxed{
\Delta C(\tau)\ge0\ \forall\tau\ge0
\iff
P(y)\ge0\ \forall y\in[0,1].
}
\]

Thus the commensurate finite case is another exact polynomial-positivity problem.

For incommensurate support the object remains an exponential polynomial.

## 6. Deterministic random reversible-family census

REV-16 uses seed

\[
\boxed{20260906}.
\]

A total of

\[
\boxed{1000}
\]

reflection-symmetric weighted \(P_4\) families satisfying A1–A5 exactly were accepted after

\[
\boxed{1003}
\]

attempts.

For the step

\[
9\to11
\]

the census applied:

- exact C3 polynomial positivity;
- exact raw moments \(k=1,\ldots,10\);
- a finite Laplace grid over \(10^{-4}\le\tau\le10^2\).

## 7. Random-search signature counts

The 1000 families split as:

\[
\boxed{
\begin{array}{c|c|c|r}
\text{C3} & \text{raw }k\le10 & \text{Laplace grid} & \text{count}\\
\hline
\text{fail} & \text{pass} & \text{fail} & 50\\
\text{pass} & \text{fail} & \text{pass} & 13\\
\text{pass} & \text{pass} & \text{fail} & 37\\
\text{pass} & \text{pass} & \text{pass} & 900
\end{array}
}
\]

Thus:

\[
\boxed{
50
}
\]

families fail C3 exactly;

\[
\boxed{
13
}
\]

families exhibit an exact raw-moment failure by \(k\le10\);

and

\[
\boxed{
87
}
\]

families exhibit a finite-grid Laplace failure.

The observed signatures include:

\[
\boxed{
\text{C3 pass / raw fail / Laplace pass},
}
\]

\[
\boxed{
\text{C3 pass / raw pass / Laplace fail},
}
\]

and

\[
\boxed{
\text{C3 fail / raw pass / Laplace fail}.
}
\]

So the stronger orders split in a large random non-OPW census just as they did in REV-15's hand-built examples.

## 8. Exact taxonomy after REV-16

The correct organization is now:

### A1–A5

Reusable finite reversible reflection-spectral core.

### C3

Exact Stieltjes numerator-polynomial order.

### C4

Exact finite-tail raw positive-moment order.

### C5

Exact finite-tail complementary-moment order.

### L

Laplace-response exponential-polynomial order.

The logical relations presently established are:

\[
\boxed{
C5\Longrightarrow L,
}
\]

but

\[
\boxed{
L\not\Longrightarrow C5,
}
\]

and REV-14 gives

\[
\boxed{
C4\not\Longrightarrow L.
}
\]

REV-15/16 show

\[
\boxed{
A1\text{--}A5
\not\Longrightarrow
C3,\ C4,\ \text{or }L.
}
\]

Thus C3, C4 and C5/L are genuinely additional order structures.

## 9. Coefficient-sign certificates

REV-16 also clarifies an important methodological point.

If a domain transformation turns a target polynomial into one with all positive coefficients, positivity is immediate.

But coefficientwise positivity is generally:

\[
\boxed{
\text{sufficient, not necessary}.
}
\]

The true target is polynomial/function nonnegativity on the relevant domain.

This distinction becomes important when classifying broad parameter families rather than proving one concrete example.

## 10. Claim boundary

Exact theorem level:

- C3 finite-atomic criterion;
- C4 finite-tail criterion;
- C5 finite-tail criterion;
- commensurate-spectrum Laplace criterion;
- exact Laplace-without-C5 counterexample.

Random census:

- A1–A5 validity exact;
- C3 classifications exact;
- raw failures through \(k=10\) exact;
- Laplace grid classifications finite numerical evidence.

No frequency in the random census is interpreted probabilistically or universally.

## 11. Recommended next audit

### EHF-PHYS-REV-17 — Order-Certificate Geometry / Semialgebraic C3-C4 Regions, Laplace Boundary Surfaces and Minimal-Dimension Classification

The next step should convert the taxonomy into geometry.

For low-dimensional reversible families, derive the parameter-space surfaces on which:

- the C3 numerator first touches zero;
- a raw-moment tail coefficient changes sign;
- the Laplace exponential polynomial acquires a positive-time zero.

This would turn the random signatures into an analytic order phase diagram.

# EHF-PHYS-REV-17 — Order-Certificate Geometry / Semialgebraic C3-C4 Regions, Laplace Boundary Surfaces and Minimal-Dimension Classification

**Side branch:** EHF-PHYS-REV  
**Inputs:** REV-14 through REV-16.  
**Status:** PASS. The stronger-order taxonomy now has an exact minimal two-atom phase geometry.

## 1. Canonical two-atom slice

Use

\[
\mu_{\rm old}(p)
=
p\delta_2+(1-p)\delta_4,
\]

\[
\mu_{\rm new}(q)
=
q\delta_1+(1-q)\delta_3,
\]

with

\[
0\le p,q\le1.
\]

Both support branches contract:

\[
1<2,\qquad3<4.
\]

This is the minimal two-atom spectral comparison used to resolve the order geometry.

## 2. Exact C3 semialgebraic region

The Stieltjes difference has numerator

\[
N(z)
=
(1-2p+2q)z^2
+
(3-8p+12q)z
+
(2-6p+16q).
\]

Thus C3 is exactly

\[
N(z)\ge0
\qquad
\forall z\ge0.
\]

The lower boundary is

\[
q_{C3}(p)=0,
\qquad
0\le p\le\frac13,
\]

\[
q_{C3}(p)=\frac{3p-1}{8},
\qquad
\frac13\le p\le\frac37,
\]

\[
q_{C3}(p)
=
\frac p2+\frac14
-
\frac{\sqrt3}{2}\sqrt{p(1-p)},
\qquad
\frac37\le p\le\frac34,
\]

and

\[
q_{C3}(p)=p-\frac12,
\qquad
\frac34\le p\le1.
\]

The interior conic is

\[
16p^2-16pq-8p+16q^2-8q+1=0.
\]

Hence C3 is semialgebraic.

## 3. Exact Laplace region

With

\[
y=e^{-\tau},
\]

\[
C_{\rm new}-C_{\rm old}
=
y(1-y)
\left[
(1-p)y^2+(q-p)y+q
\right].
\]

Therefore Laplace order is equivalent to

\[
(1-p)y^2+(q-p)y+q\ge0
\qquad
\forall y\in[0,1].
\]

Its lower boundary is

\[
q_L(p)
=
2-p-2\sqrt{1-p}
=
\left(1-\sqrt{1-p}\right)^2,
\qquad
0\le p\le\frac34,
\]

followed by

\[
q_L(p)=p-\frac12,
\qquad
\frac34\le p\le1.
\]

The interior boundary conic is

\[
(p+q)^2=4q.
\]

Thus Laplace order is also semialgebraic.

## 4. Exact C4 moment fan

For every positive integer \(k\),

\[
m_k({\rm old})-m_k({\rm new})\ge0
\]

is equivalent to

\[
q\ge f_k(p),
\]

where

\[
\boxed{
f_k(p)
=
\frac{
p(4^k-2^k)-(4^k-3^k)
}{
3^k-1
}.
}
\]

The slopes

\[
\alpha_k
=
\frac{4^k-2^k}{3^k-1}
\]

strictly increase.

Adjacent faces cross at

\[
\boxed{
p_k
=
\frac{
12^k+2\,3^k-3\,4^k
}{
6^k+2^k+12^k-3\,4^k
},
}
\]

\[
\boxed{
q_k
=
\frac{
6^k-2\,8^k+12^k
}{
6^k+2^k+12^k-3\,4^k
}.
}
\]

The crossings satisfy

\[
p_1<p_2<p_3<\cdots
\]

and

\[
(p_k,q_k)\to(1,1).
\]

Every \(f_k\) is active on a nonempty boundary interval.

Thus exact weak C4 is

\[
\boxed{
q\ge\sup_{k\ge1}f_k(p).
}
\]

## 5. C4 is not semialgebraic

Every finite moment truncation

\[
k\le K
\]

gives a finite polyhedral upper envelope and is semialgebraic.

The exact all-\(k\) boundary contains nontrivial segments on infinitely many distinct lines

\[
q=f_k(p),
\]

with those segments accumulating at

\[
(1,1).
\]

A semialgebraic one-dimensional boundary admits a finite algebraic stratification.

A nonzero finite-degree polynomial cannot vanish on nontrivial segments of infinitely many distinct lines.

Therefore

\[
\boxed{
\text{full all-}k\text{ C4 is not semialgebraic on this slice}.
}
\]

This is a qualitative distinction between positive-ray/unit-interval polynomial order and infinite moment order.

## 6. Exact phase junction

For

\[
p<\frac34,
\]

\[
\boxed{
L\subseteq C3\subseteq C4.
}
\]

At

\[
p=\frac34,
\]

all three boundaries meet at

\[
\boxed{
(p,q)=\left(\frac34,\frac14\right).
}
\]

For

\[
p>\frac34,
\]

\[
\boxed{
C4\subseteq C3=L.
}
\]

So the nesting of the order regions reverses at a single exact phase junction.

## 7. Minimal dimension

For scalar spectral dimension one, strict pole contraction

\[
0<c<a
\]

automatically gives:

\[
\frac1{z+c}>\frac1{z+a},
\]

\[
a^k>c^k,
\]

\[
e^{-c\tau}>e^{-a\tau},
\]

and complementary-moment order for every common ceiling.

Thus no certificate separation is possible at \(d=1\).

At \(d=2\), the canonical slice realizes exact signatures

\[
(C3,C4,L)
=
(1,1,1),
\]

\[
(1,1,0),
\]

\[
(0,1,0),
\]

\[
(0,0,0),
\]

and

\[
(1,0,1).
\]

Therefore

\[
\boxed{
d=2
}
\]

is the minimal scalar dimension for nontrivial order-certificate geometry.

Laplace order still implies C3 generally because the Stieltjes transform is the positive Laplace integral of the response.

C4 is independent of both already at \(d=2\).

## 8. Verification

The exact formulas were checked on all

\[
10201
\]

points of the denominator-\(100\) rational grid.

Mismatches:

\[
\boxed{
C3:0,\qquad
L:0,\qquad
C4:0.
}
\]

The grid is only a verification layer; the boundary formulas and infinite-fan theorem are symbolic.

## 9. Claim boundary

The canonical slice is an abstract two-atom spectral normal form.

It is not claimed that every \((p,q)\) pair lies on one fixed smooth A1–A5 pencil.

REV-15 independently provides \(d=2\) A1–A5 reversible P4 families exhibiting certificate separation.

## 10. Recommended next side audit

### EHF-PHYS-REV-18 — Infinite-Fan Moment Geometry / Asymptotic Face Scaling, Boundary Universality and Two-Atom Order Normal Forms

The next natural questions are:

1. asymptotics of \(1-p_k\) and \(1-q_k\);
2. universal rescaling of the accumulating faces;
3. classification of general two-atom comparisons under affine support normalization;
4. conditions for finite versus infinite active moment-face sequences.

# EHF-PHYS-REV-18 — Infinite-Fan Moment Geometry / Asymptotic Face Scaling, Boundary Universality and Two-Atom Order Normal Forms

**Side branch:** EHF-PHYS-REV  
**Inputs:** REV-15 through REV-17.  
**Status:** PASS. The REV-17 canonical infinite C4 fan generalizes to the complete strict-interlacing two-atom branch-contraction class.

## 1. General two-atom comparison

Write

\[
\mu_{\rm old}
=
p\delta_a+(1-p)\delta_b,
\]

\[
\mu_{\rm new}
=
q\delta_c+(1-q)\delta_d,
\]

with paired branch contraction

\[
c<a,
\qquad
d<b.
\]

## 2. Correct normal form

A common positive scaling

\[
\lambda\mapsto h\lambda,
\qquad h>0,
\]

multiplies every \(k\)-th raw-moment difference by

\[
h^k>0.
\]

Therefore C4 is invariant under positive scaling and one may set

\[
b=1.
\]

Define

\[
t=\frac cb,
\qquad
r=\frac ab,
\qquad
s=\frac db.
\]

Common translation is **not** a C4 invariance.

Exact witness:

\[
\mu_{\rm old}
=
\frac12\delta_1+\frac12\delta_5,
\qquad
\mu_{\rm new}
=
\frac12\delta_3+\frac12\delta_4.
\]

Before translation,

\[
m_2({\rm old})=13
>
\frac{25}{2}
=
m_2({\rm new}).
\]

After translating all supports by \(+1\),

\[
m_2({\rm old})=20
<
\frac{41}{2}
=
m_2({\rm new}).
\]

Thus the valid universal normal form is multiplicative, not fully affine.

## 3. Exact normalized C4 faces

After setting \(b=1\),

\[
\mu_{\rm old}
=
p\delta_r+(1-p)\delta_1,
\]

\[
\mu_{\rm new}
=
q\delta_t+(1-q)\delta_s.
\]

The \(k\)-th raw-moment condition is

\[
q\ge F_k(p),
\]

with

\[
\boxed{
F_k(p)
=
\frac{
p(1-r^k)-1+s^k
}{
s^k-t^k
}.
}
\]

## 4. Two-regime classification

### Support separated

If

\[
0<t<s\le r<1,
\]

then every old support is at least every new support.

Hence

\[
m_k({\rm old})\ge m_k({\rm new})
\]

for every \(k\ge1\) and every \(p,q\).

Therefore:

\[
\boxed{
d\le a
\Longrightarrow
\text{C4 automatic and no fan}.
}
\]

### Strict interlacing

If

\[
0<t<r<s<1,
\]

then

\[
c<a<d<b.
\]

This regime develops an eventual infinite active C4 fan.

Therefore, within paired two-atom branch contraction,

\[
\boxed{
d\le a
\Longrightarrow
\text{no fan},
}
\]

while

\[
\boxed{
a<d
\Longrightarrow
\text{infinite tail fan}.
}
\]

## 5. Universal scaled face

Approach the corner on the \(k\)-th horizontal scale:

\[
p=1-Xr^k.
\]

Define

\[
Y=\frac{1-q}{(r/s)^k}.
\]

On the exact \(k\)-th face,

\[
\boxed{
Y_k(X)
=
\frac{
X+1-Xr^k-(t/r)^k
}{
1-(t/s)^k
}.
}
\]

Hence, uniformly for bounded \(X\),

\[
\boxed{
Y_k(X)\longrightarrow1+X.
}
\]

Thus the asymptotic local face normal form is

\[
\boxed{
Y=1+X.
}
\]

The limiting line is independent of \(t,r,s\).

## 6. Crossing universality

Let \((p_k,q_k)\) be the intersection of faces \(k\) and \(k+1\).

Define

\[
X_*=\frac{s-r}{1-s},
\qquad
Y_*=\frac{1-r}{1-s}.
\]

Then

\[
\boxed{
\frac{1-p_k}{r^k}\longrightarrow X_*,
}
\]

and

\[
\boxed{
\frac{1-q_k}{(r/s)^k}\longrightarrow Y_*.
}
\]

The leading crossing constants depend on \(r,s\) but **not** on the innermost support ratio \(t\).

Thus the high-moment tail geometry forgets one normalized support parameter.

## 7. Face-width scaling

The active horizontal width satisfies

\[
\boxed{
\frac{p_{k+1}-p_k}{r^k}
\longrightarrow
X_*(1-r).
}
\]

The vertical width satisfies

\[
\boxed{
\frac{q_{k+1}-q_k}{(r/s)^k}
\longrightarrow
Y_*\left(1-\frac rs\right).
}
\]

The fan therefore collapses anisotropically.

## 8. Corner power law

Eliminating \(k\) gives

\[
\boxed{
1-q_k
\sim
K_*(1-p_k)^\gamma,
}
\]

where

\[
\boxed{
\gamma
=
\frac{\log(r/s)}{\log r}
}
\]

and

\[
\boxed{
K_*=
\frac{Y_*}{X_*^\gamma}.
}
\]

Since

\[
0<r<s<1,
\]

\[
\boxed{
0<\gamma<1.
}
\]

So the infinite fan approaches the corner through a fractional-power cusp.

The active moment index satisfies

\[
\boxed{
k
\sim
\frac{
\log[(1-p)/X_*]
}{
\log r
}.
}
\]

Thus increasingly high moments control logarithmically shrinking neighborhoods of the corner.

## 9. Eventual activity

The \(k\)-th face slope satisfies

\[
\alpha_k
=
\frac{1-r^k}{s^k-t^k}
\sim s^{-k},
\]

so slopes eventually increase strictly.

Also

\[
p_k
=
1-X_*r^k+o(r^k),
\]

hence

\[
p_{k+1}-p_k
\sim
X_*(1-r)r^k>0.
\]

Therefore adjacent intersections are eventually strictly ordered.

It follows that every sufficiently large face is active on the upper envelope.

Hence every strict-interlacing normal form has infinitely many active faces.

## 10. Universal nonsemialgebraicity

The exact boundary contains nontrivial segments on infinitely many distinct affine lines accumulating at

\[
(1,1).
\]

Therefore the REV-17 nonsemialgebraicity argument generalizes:

\[
\boxed{
\text{full all-}k\text{ C4 is not semialgebraic}
}
\]

for every strict-interlacing two-atom branch-contraction normal form.

This is not special to supports \(1,2,3,4\).

## 11. Canonical specialization

For

\[
t=\frac14,
\qquad
r=\frac12,
\qquad
s=\frac34,
\]

\[
X_*=1,
\qquad
Y_*=2.
\]

Therefore

\[
\boxed{
2^k(1-p_k)\to1,
}
\]

and

\[
\boxed{
\left(\frac32\right)^k(1-q_k)\to2.
}
\]

The corner exponent is

\[
\boxed{
\gamma
=
\frac{\log(2/3)}{\log(1/2)}
\approx0.5849625007.
}
\]

## 12. Verification layer

Four rational strict-interlacing normal forms were evaluated through \(k=30\).

All satisfy the predicted crossing and face-scaling limits.

The exact scaled-face identity converges to

\[
Y=1+X
\]

for each family.

These calculations are verification only; the asymptotic formulas are analytic.

## 13. Boundary universality hierarchy

The two-atom C4 geometry separates into three levels.

### Support-order class

\[
\text{separated}\Rightarrow\text{no fan},
\]

\[
\text{interlaced}\Rightarrow\text{infinite fan}.
\]

### Outer scale ratios \(r,s\)

These determine

\[
X_*,
\quad
Y_*,
\quad
\gamma,
\]

and face-width constants.

### Inner ratio \(t\)

This contributes only subleading corrections to the tail fan.

So asymptotically the boundary forgets one support parameter.

## 14. Claim boundary

These results apply to positive two-atom measures under paired branch contraction

\[
c<a,
\qquad
d<b.
\]

No corresponding classification for three-or-more-atom measures is asserted.

## 15. Recommended next side audit

### EHF-PHYS-REV-19 — Multi-Atom Moment Fans / Tropical Dominant-Support Complexes and Higher-Dimensional Order Geometry

Test whether the two-atom fan becomes a tropical/polyhedral complex for three or more atoms:

1. identify dominant-support asymptotic cells;
2. classify multiple face-accumulation scales;
3. determine semialgebraic versus nonsemialgebraic regimes;
4. connect the geometry back to reversible reflection-spectral families.

# EHF-PHYS-REV-19 — Multi-Atom Moment Fans / Tropical Dominant-Support Complexes and Higher-Dimensional Order Geometry

**Side branch:** EHF-PHYS-REV  
**Inputs:** REV-16 through REV-18.  
**Status:** PASS. The two-atom infinite fan lifts to a genuinely higher-dimensional nested fan geometry.

## 1. Generic dominant-support theorem

Let

\[
\Delta\mu
=
\sum_{j=1}^{s}a_j\delta_{\rho_j},
\qquad
0<\rho_1<\cdots<\rho_s.
\]

Then

\[
\Delta m_k
=
\sum_ja_j\rho_j^k.
\]

If \(J\) is the largest index with \(a_J\neq0\), then

\[
\boxed{
\Delta m_k
=
a_J\rho_J^k
\left[
1+O\!\left(
\left(\frac{\rho_{J-1}}{\rho_J}\right)^k
\right)
\right].
}
\]

Hence

\[
\boxed{
\operatorname{sign}\Delta m_k
\to
\operatorname{sign}a_J
}
\]

eventually, and

\[
\boxed{
\lim_{k\to\infty}
\frac1k\log|\Delta m_k|
=
\log\rho_J.
}
\]

When \(a_J=0\), the valuation drops to the next support.

This defines the finite **dominant-support complex**.

The word “tropical” is used here in this explicit max-plus/large-\(k\) valuation sense.

## 2. Fixed-support C4 geometry is convex

For fixed supports, each moment condition is affine in the weights.

Therefore

\[
\boxed{
C4
=
\bigcap_{k\ge1}
\{\Delta m_k\ge0\}
}
\]

is a closed convex intersection of countably many halfspaces.

The tropical tail complex is finite.

The full all-\(k\) boundary can nevertheless carry infinitely many active moment faces.

## 3. Canonical three-atom ladder

Take

\[
\text{old supports }(2,4,6),
\qquad
\text{new supports }(1,3,5).
\]

Parameterize weights by

\[
\mu_{\rm old}
=
(1-u)p\,\delta_2
+
(1-u)(1-p)\,\delta_4
+
u\,\delta_6,
\]

\[
\mu_{\rm new}
=
(1-v)q\,\delta_1
+
(1-v)(1-q)\,\delta_3
+
v\,\delta_5.
\]

The independent weight coordinates are

\[
(p,q,u,v).
\]

The signed support coefficients from highest to lowest are

\[
+u,\quad
-v,\quad
+(1-u)(1-p),\quad
-(1-v)(1-q),\quad
+(1-u)p,\quad
-(1-v)q.
\]

## 4. Exact five-tier tropical chain

The eventual tail strata are:

\[
u>0
\Rightarrow
\text{support }6,\ \text{PASS},
\]

\[
u=0,\ v>0
\Rightarrow
\text{support }5,\ \text{FAIL},
\]

\[
u=v=0,\ p<1
\Rightarrow
\text{support }4,\ \text{PASS},
\]

\[
u=v=0,\ p=1,\ q<1
\Rightarrow
\text{support }3,\ \text{FAIL},
\]

and

\[
u=v=0,\ p=q=1
\Rightarrow
\text{support }2,\ \text{PASS}.
\]

Thus the three-atom ladder has an alternating

\[
\boxed{
\text{PASS/FAIL/PASS/FAIL/PASS}
}
\]

dominant-support chain.

## 5. The lower face is exactly REV-17

Set

\[
u=v=0.
\]

Then

\[
\Delta m_k
=
p2^k+(1-p)4^k
-
q1^k-(1-q)3^k.
\]

This is exactly the REV-17 canonical two-atom problem.

Hence the affine face

\[
u=v=0
\]

contains the full REV-17 infinite moment fan.

## 6. Transverse top-pair fan

For fixed \(p,q\), define

\[
O_k=p2^k+(1-p)4^k,
\]

\[
N_k=q+(1-q)3^k.
\]

Then

\[
\Delta_k
=
(O_k-N_k)
+
u(6^k-O_k)
-
v(5^k-N_k).
\]

So the \(k\)-th top-pair constraint is

\[
v\le G_k(u;p,q),
\]

where

\[
G_k
=
\frac{
O_k-N_k+u(6^k-O_k)
}{
5^k-N_k
}.
\]

Thus over a fixed lower point the 4D body contains another infinite fan in the transverse \((u,v)\) plane.

## 7. Generic lower-tier scaling

If

\[
p<1,
\]

then

\[
O_k-N_k
\sim
(1-p)4^k.
\]

Consequently

\[
G_k(u)
\sim
(1-p)\left(\frac45\right)^k
+
u\left(\frac65\right)^k.
\]

Use

\[
u
=
(1-p)X
\left(\frac23\right)^k,
\]

\[
v
=
(1-p)V
\left(\frac45\right)^k.
\]

Then

\[
\boxed{
V\to1+X.
}
\]

Adjacent crossings satisfy

\[
\boxed{
u_k
\sim
(1-p)\left(\frac23\right)^k,
}
\]

and

\[
\boxed{
v_k
\sim
2(1-p)\left(\frac45\right)^k.
}
\]

So the generic top-fan crossing constants are

\[
\boxed{
(X_*,V_*)=(1,2).
}
\]

## 8. Terminal lower tier

At

\[
p=q=1,
\]

the support \(4\) coefficient has vanished.

The leading lower signed moment is now

\[
2^k-1\sim2^k.
\]

Use

\[
u=X\left(\frac13\right)^k,
\]

\[
v=V\left(\frac25\right)^k.
\]

Again

\[
\boxed{
V\to1+X,
}
\]

but now

\[
\boxed{
(X_*,V_*)=(3,4).
}
\]

Thus cancellation of a leading support tier exposes a new fan scale.

## 9. Generic fan-lifting lemma

Suppose a lower signed family has

\[
\Delta_{{\rm low},k}
=
WR^k(1+o(1)),
\qquad
W>0.
\]

Add an old top support \(A\) and new top support \(C\) with

\[
R<C<A.
\]

Let their weights be \(u,v\).

Then the top boundary satisfies

\[
v
=
W\left(\frac RC\right)^k
+
u\left(\frac AC\right)^k
+
o\!\left(
\left(\frac RC\right)^k
\right).
\]

Set

\[
u
=
WX\left(\frac RA\right)^k,
\]

\[
v
=
WY\left(\frac RC\right)^k.
\]

Then

\[
\boxed{
Y\to1+X.
}
\]

The adjacent crossing constants are

\[
\boxed{
X_*=
\frac{C-R}{A-C},
}
\]

and

\[
\boxed{
Y_*=
\frac{A-R}{A-C}.
}
\]

So the REV-18 local normal form lifts recursively above arbitrary positive lower support tiers.

## 10. Nested fan complex

The canonical 3-atom geometry contains:

1. a lower REV-17 fan on \(u=v=0\);
2. generic transverse top fans for fixed lower C4 points with \(p<1\);
3. a distinct terminal transverse fan at \(p=q=1\).

The finite dominant-support strata determine where the asymptotic support valuation changes.

Infinite moment fans refine neighborhoods of these strata.

Thus the correct multi-atom picture is hierarchical:

\[
\boxed{
\text{finite support-tier complex}
+
\text{nested infinite fan refinements}.
}
\]

## 11. Higher-dimensional nonsemialgebraicity

The full canonical 3-atom region lives in four independent weight coordinates.

If it were semialgebraic, then its affine intersection with

\[
u=v=0
\]

would be semialgebraic.

But that intersection is exactly the nonsemialgebraic REV-17 all-\(k\) region.

Therefore

\[
\boxed{
\text{the canonical 3-atom C4 region is not semialgebraic}.
}
\]

More generally:

\[
\boxed{
\text{any multi-atom fixed-support family containing a strict-interlacing two-atom affine coordinate face inherits nonsemialgebraicity}.
}
\]

## 12. Verification

Exact tail signs were checked on sample points from all five dominant-support strata.

Four lower points were used for transverse fan validation:

\[
(0,0),
\qquad
\left(\frac12,\frac14\right),
\qquad
\left(\frac34,\frac14\right),
\qquad
(1,1).
\]

The first three converge to the generic crossing constants \((1,2)\).

The terminal point converges to \((3,4)\).

Scaled top faces converge to

\[
V=1+X.
\]

## 13. Claim boundary

The dominant-support theorem is generic for finite positive supports.

The explicit nested formulas are claimed for:

- the canonical three-atom ladder;
- the stated one-top-pair fan-lifting asymptotic.

No complete arbitrary \(3+\)-atom fan classification is asserted.

## 14. Recommended next side audit

### EHF-PHYS-REV-20 — Multi-Tier Fan Recursion / Inductive m-Atom Ladder Theorem and Tropical Face-Complex Classification

The next step should test a full induction:

1. recursively add interlaced support pairs;
2. prove one universal \(Y=1+X\) local normal form at every support tier;
3. classify the resulting hierarchy of scaling exponents;
4. describe the nested countable-facet convex body combinatorially.

# EHF-PHYS-REV-BERG-01 — Bergman-Fan / Matroidal Realizability Audit

**Side branch:** EHF-PHYS-REV-BERG  
**Inputs:** REV-17 through REV-19, with external definition checks against standard Bergman-fan and matroid-amplitudes literature.  
**Status:** PASS. Direct Bergman identification fails, but a genuine alternating oriented-matroid structure is found underneath the moment geometry.

## 1. Definition boundary

For a loopless matroid \(M\) on ground set \(E\), the Bergman support is

\[
\mathcal B(M)
=
\left\{
w\in\mathbb R^E:
\min_{i\in C}w_i
\text{ is attained at least twice for every circuit }C
\right\}.
\]

Standard Bergman fan structures are finite pure balanced polyhedral fans and contain lineality

\[
\mathbb R(1,\ldots,1).
\]

The REV dominant-support object is different.

## 2. The REV dominant-support strata are not a fan

Already in two coefficient coordinates,

\[
H_2^+
=
\{a_2\ge0\},
\]

and

\[
H_1^+
=
\{a_2=0,\ a_1\ge0\}.
\]

Their intersection is \(H_1^+\).

But \(H_2^+\) has lineality

\[
L=\{a_2=0\},
\]

and every face of \(H_2^+\) must contain \(L\).

Therefore \(H_1^+\), a proper ray in \(L\), is not a face.

Hence

\[
\boxed{
\text{the dominant-support closures do not form a polyhedral fan}.
}
\]

So they cannot themselves be a Bergman fan.

## 3. A genuine coordinate-sign fan refinement exists

If all coefficient signs are retained, the hyperplanes

\[
a_i=0
\]

produce the full coordinate orthant fan.

Thus:

\[
\boxed{
\text{dominant-support complex}
\longleftarrow
\text{coarsening of a genuine sign/covector fan}.
}
\]

This is naturally an oriented-matroid covector structure.

However it is not the standard Bergman fan in the native coefficient coordinates because the orthant cones do not share the compulsory Bergman lineality

\[
\mathbb R(1,\ldots,1).
\]

The free-matroid Bergman support is all space, but treating the orthant fan as an arbitrary refinement of that trivial support supplies no substantive Bergman identification.

## 4. The infinite all-\(k\) fan gives a second obstruction

A finite matroid has finitely many circuits, flats and flags.

Hence its Bergman fan is finite polyhedral.

REV-17/18 prove that the exact all-\(k\) C4 boundary can have countably infinitely many active facets.

Therefore

\[
\boxed{
\text{the full all-}k\text{ moment refinement cannot be the Bergman fan of a finite matroid}.
}
\]

## 5. Exact moment-normal matroid

For supports

\[
1<2<3<4<5<6,
\]

define

\[
v_k
=
(1^k,2^k,3^k,4^k,5^k,6^k)^T,
\qquad
k=0,\ldots,8.
\]

Every ordered \(6\times6\) maximal minor is a generalized Vandermonde determinant.

The audit computed all

\[
\binom96=84
\]

maximal minors exactly:

\[
\boxed{
84/84>0.
}
\]

Thus the rank-six configuration has underlying matroid

\[
\boxed{
U_{6,9}
}
\]

and alternating oriented matroid

\[
\boxed{
A_{9,6}.
}
\]

## 6. Exact alternating circuits

Every seven-element subset has one circuit.

All

\[
\binom97=36
\]

circuits were computed exactly.

After sign normalization,

\[
\boxed{
36/36
}
\]

have circuit signs

\[
(+,-,+,-,+,-,+).
\]

So the finite moment-normal system is exactly alternating oriented-matroid data.

## 7. Support-indexed moment-curve circuit

Use the rank-five support moment matrix with rows

\[
1,\rho,\rho^2,\rho^3,\rho^4.
\]

For supports \(1,\ldots,6\), the underlying matroid is

\[
U_{5,6}.
\]

Its unique circuit is

\[
\boxed{
(-1,\ 5,\ -10,\ 10,\ -5,\ 1),
}
\]

with signs

\[
\boxed{
(-,+,-,+,-,+).
}
\]

This agrees, up to global sign, with the canonical REV-19 interlacing pattern

\[
\text{new},\text{old},\text{new},\text{old},\text{new},\text{old}.
\]

Thus there is a real oriented-matroid correspondence underneath the REV sign structure.

## 8. The correct distinction

The finite moment-normal configuration is matroidal.

The C4 cone is defined by

\[
\sum_i a_i\rho_i^k\ge0.
\]

The Bergman fan of the associated matroid is instead a circuit-valuation object.

Therefore

\[
\boxed{
\text{matroidal underneath}
\ne
\text{Bergman fan itself}.
}
\]

The finite C4 truncations are better viewed as halfspace/polar geometry generated by a totally positive alternating oriented-matroid normal configuration.

## 9. Tropical dominant-balance shadow

For a moment equality

\[
\sum_i\epsilon_iw_i\rho_i^k=0,
\]

define

\[
\phi_i(k)
=
\log w_i+k\log\rho_i.
\]

Large-\(k\) dominance is max-plus.

A necessary condition for cancellation is that the maximal \(\phi_i\) be attained at least twice.

This is the tropical-hyperplane form of the Bergman circuit condition.

Because the equation is real and signed, actual cancellation additionally requires dominant terms of opposite sign.

Hence the closest Bergman object is likely not the ordinary unsigned Bergman fan but an oriented/positive/real Bergman structure.

## 10. Relation to Lam's matroid amplitudes

Lam's construction uses continuous and discrete Laplace transforms of **subfans of a Bergman fan** to obtain intersection pairings and matroid scattering amplitudes.

The REV transform is instead the spectral response

\[
C(\tau)
=
\sum_jw_je^{-\kappa_j\tau}.
\]

No equality between these two transforms is currently proved.

No canonical map from the EHF spectral measure to a Lam Bergman subfan has been established.

Therefore:

\[
\boxed{
\text{Bergman/amplitudes relation = structural bridge candidate, not theorem}.
}
\]

## 11. Main verdict

The word “fan” used in REV-17–19 was geometrically appropriate, but the raw REV object is **not literally a Bergman fan**.

The useful discovery is one level deeper:

\[
\boxed{
\text{finite moment normals}
\Rightarrow
\text{uniform alternating oriented matroid}.
}
\]

The interlaced REV sign structure agrees with its circuit orientation.

The most promising Bergman-specific continuation is therefore to study the **positive/real Bergman fan of the alternating oriented matroid** and compare its tropical circuit crossings with active moment-face transitions.

## 12. Recommended Bergman audit

### EHF-PHYS-REV-BERG-02 — Alternating Oriented-Matroid / Positive-Bergman Tropicalization and Moment-Cone Duality Audit

Targets:

1. build the alternating oriented matroid \(A_{n,r}\) of moment normals;
2. compute its positive/real Bergman fan in the first nontrivial cases;
3. map moment-balance trajectories into valuation coordinates;
4. test active C4 transitions against real-Bergman cone crossings;
5. formalize moment-cone / oriented-matroid duality;
6. only then compare with Lam-style fan Laplace transforms.

The main reversal queue remains separately:

### EHF-PHYS-REV-20 — Multi-Tier Fan Recursion / Inductive \(m\)-Atom Ladder Theorem and Tropical Face-Complex Classification

# EHF-PHYS-REV-BERG-02 — Alternating Oriented-Matroid / Positive-Bergman Tropicalization and Moment-Cone Duality Audit

**Side branch:** EHF-PHYS-REV-BERG  
**Inputs:** BERG-01 and REV-17 through REV-19, with definition checks against AKW positive Bergman theory, signed/real Bergman formulations, and Lam's *Matroids and amplitudes*.  
**Status:** PASS.

## 1. Two distinct alternating oriented matroids

BERG-02 separates two exact matroidal structures.

### Support oriented matroid

Ground-set elements are the ordered spectral supports.  It controls signed circuits, positive/real Bergman geometry, and tropicalized cancellation.

For six supports the canonical object is

\[
A_{6,5}.
\]

### Moment-normal oriented matroid

Ground-set elements are finite moment orders through

\[
v_k=(\rho_1^k,\ldots,\rho_m^k).
\]

It controls moment-cone and finite C4 polar geometry.

For six supports and moment orders \(1,\ldots,9\), the audited object is

\[
A_{9,6}.
\]

These are not the same matroid.  The duality below is ordinary convex polarity, not matroid duality.

## 2. Positive Bergman fan of \(A_{4,3}\)

The alternating circuit is

\[
(-,+,-,+).
\]

The positive Bergman coarse maximal cones are the opposite-sign top ties

\[
(2,1),\ (2,3),\ (4,1),\ (4,3).
\]

Hence

\[
\boxed{4\text{ maximal cones}}
\]

with adjacency graph

\[
\boxed{C_4}.
\]

## 3. Positive Bergman fan of \(A_{6,5}\)

The circuit is

\[
(-,+,-,+,-,+),
\]

with positive indices \(\{2,4,6\}\) and negative indices \(\{1,3,5\}\).

The opposite-sign pair cones give

\[
\boxed{9\text{ maximal cones}}
\]

and

\[
\boxed{18\text{ adjacencies}},
\]

with adjacency graph

\[
\boxed{L(K_{3,3})}.
\]

## 4. REV-17 crossing trajectory lands on a positive-Bergman face

At the REV-17 adjacent face crossing define

\[
\nu_i^{(k)}
=
\frac1k\log|a_i\rho_i^k|.
\]

Using the exact REV-18 asymptotics,

\[
\nu^{(k)}\to(0,\log2,\log2,\log2).
\]

Thus the limiting top tie is

\[
\boxed{\{2(+),3(-),4(+)\}}.
\]

This is a codimension-one positive-Bergman face of \(A_{4,3}\), shared by \((2+,3-)\) and \((4+,3-)\).

## 5. REV-19 top-fan crossings land on \(A_{6,5}\) faces

For the generic top-fan transition,

\[
\boxed{\{4(+),5(-),6(+)\}}
\]

is the limiting top tie.

For the terminal support-tier transition,

\[
\boxed{\{2(+),5(-),6(+)\}}
\]

is the limiting top tie.

Both are codimension-one positive-Bergman faces of \(A_{6,5}\).

Therefore

\[
\boxed{
\text{REV active moment transitions}
\stackrel{\mathrm{trop}}{\longrightarrow}
\text{positive-Bergman faces}
}.
\]

This is an asymptotic incidence theorem: finite-\(k\) trajectories approach the faces rather than generally lying exactly on them.

## 6. Exact moment-cone polarity

For fixed supports define

\[
M_K=\operatorname{cone}\{v_1,\ldots,v_K\},
\qquad
v_k=(\rho_1^k,\ldots,\rho_m^k).
\]

The finite C4 truncation is

\[
C_K
=
\{a:\langle a,v_k\rangle\ge0,\ 1\le k\le K\}.
\]

Hence exactly

\[
\boxed{C_K=M_K^*}.
\]

For six supports and moment orders \(1,\ldots,9\), all

\[
\binom96=84
\]

maximal minors are positive:

\[
\boxed{84/84>0}.
\]

Thus the moment-normal configuration realizes \(A_{9,6}\).

Exact supporting-hyperplane enumeration gives

\[
\boxed{30}
\]

facets of the nine-ray moment cone.

## 7. Infinite limit

The all-positive-moment region satisfies

\[
\boxed{
C_\infty
=
\bigcap_KC_K
=
\left(
\overline{\operatorname{cone}\{v_k:k\ge1\}}
\right)^*
}.
\]

So the full C4 body has an exact countable moment-cone polar description even though it is not a finite Bergman fan.

## 8. Two compatible projections

The Bergman side and convex side should be kept separate:

\[
\boxed{
\text{support OM}
\rightarrow
\text{positive-Bergman tropical balance}
}
\]

versus

\[
\boxed{
\text{moment-normal OM}
\rightarrow
\text{moment-cone / C4 polarity}.
}
\]

Both arise from ordered positive support data and total positivity, but they have different ground sets and different geometric roles.

## 9. Lam transform boundary

Lam's complete-flag cone transform has rational homogeneous product form

\[
L(C_{F_\bullet})
=
\prod_i\frac1{a_{F_i}}
\]

up to the projective convention.

The REV spectral response is

\[
C(\tau)=\sum_jw_je^{-\kappa_j\tau}.
\]

Under the naive linear specialization \(a_F=\lambda_F\tau\), Lam's transform has power-law \(\tau^{-d}\) behavior, while the REV response has exponential decay for positive poles.

Therefore

\[
\boxed{
\text{Lam fan Laplace transform}
\neq
\text{REV spectral response}
}
\]

under the naive identification.

A genuine bridge would require a nontrivial mapping from spectral/moment-balance data into Bergman cone variables before transforming.

## 10. Main verdict

BERG-01 established that the raw REV moment fan is not itself a Bergman fan.

BERG-02 establishes the more precise bridge:

\[
\boxed{
\text{positive-Bergman tropical balance}
+
\text{cyclic moment-cone polarity}.
}
\]

The first captures the asymptotic signed balance skeleton of moment transitions; the second captures exact finite/all-order convex inequality geometry.

No Lam amplitude, KLT, or intersection-form identity has yet been derived.

## 11. Recommended next Bergman audit

### EHF-PHYS-REV-BERG-03 — Positive-Bergman Face Dynamics / Tropical Transition Graph, Las-Vergnas Flags and Lam-Subfan Compatibility Audit

Targets:

1. track the positive-Bergman faces visited by support-tier transitions;
2. identify their Las Vergnas flags;
3. test whether the visited faces form a connected subfan;
4. compare that subfan with Lam's \(\Sigma_M(P,G_\bullet)\) stars;
5. compute its Lam cone-Laplace transform;
6. test transform invariants against REV moment/spectral invariants.

The main reversal queue remains separately at **EHF-PHYS-REV-20**.

# EHF-PHYS-REV-BERG-03 — Positive-Bergman Face Dynamics / Tropical Transition Graph, Las-Vergnas Flags and Lam-Subfan Compatibility Audit

**Side branch:** EHF-PHYS-REV-BERG  
**Inputs:** BERG-01, BERG-02, REV-17, REV-19, and Lam's positive-Bergman / intersection-form construction.  
**Status:** PASS. The generic REV tropical transitions now resolve to exact fine Las Vergnas cones and local Lam-star summands. The terminal REV-19 path exposes an extended-tropical boundary and corrects one BERG-02 statement.

## 1. Sign-convention lock

For signed REV term magnitudes define

\[
\nu_i^{(k)}
=
\frac1k\log|a_i\rho_i^k|.
\]

The AKW positive-Bergman convention selects **maximal** weights, whereas Lam's fine cone convention uses positive combinations of

\[
\epsilon_F=\sum_{e\in F}\epsilon_e.
\]

The compatible Lam coordinate is

\[
\boxed{
w^{(k)}=-\nu^{(k)}
\pmod{\mathbb R\mathbf1}.
}
\]

This sign/lineality lock is required before reading a REV tropical limit as a Lam fine cone.

## 2. Exact Las Vergnas census

For the all-positive tope of \(A_{4,3}\), the proper Las Vergnas face counts are

\[
\boxed{
4\text{ rank-1 faces},\qquad4\text{ rank-2 faces}.
}
\]

There are

\[
\boxed{8}
\]

complete flags, and their flag-transition graph is

\[
\boxed{C_8}.
\]

For \(A_{6,5}\), the proper face counts are

\[
\boxed{
6,\ 15,\ 18,\ 9
}
\]

in ranks \(1,2,3,4\), respectively.

There are

\[
\boxed{216}
\]

complete flags. Their fine flag graph is connected, \(4\)-regular, and has

\[
\boxed{432}
\]

edges.

The coarse chamber graph remains

\[
\boxed{L(K_{3,3})}
\]

with nine chambers and eighteen adjacencies.

## 3. REV-17 resolves to a fine positive-Bergman cone

Using the exact REV-18 asymptotics,

\[
1-p_k\sim2^{-k},
\qquad
1-q_k\sim2\left(\frac23\right)^k,
\]

the Lam-convention tropical weight converges to

\[
\boxed{
w_\infty
=
(\log2,0,0,0)
=
\log2\,\epsilon_{\{1\}}.
}
\]

Hence

\[
\boxed{
w^{(k)}
\to
\operatorname{relint}C_{\{1\}}.
}
\]

Exactly two complete Las Vergnas flags contain this codimension-one cone:

\[
\{1\}<\{1,2\},
\]

and

\[
\{1\}<\{1,4\}.
\]

Thus the REV-17 active face transition is an exact fine-flag flip across the common partial flag

\[
\boxed{G_{17}=\{1\}}.
\]

## 4. REV-17 local Lam star

Lam's star construction gives

\[
\boxed{
\Sigma_M(P,G_{17}).
}
\]

Its continuous cone-Laplace transform is

\[
\boxed{
L(\Sigma_M(P,G_{17}))
=
\frac1{a_1a_{12}}
+
\frac1{a_1a_{14}}.
}
\]

The partial-flag rank sum is \(1\), so this component enters Lam's intersection formula with negative sign.

## 5. REV-19 generic transition resolves to a longer fine flag

For the audited generic lower point

\[
p=\frac12,
\qquad
q=\frac14,
\]

the exact limiting Lam coordinate is

\[
w_\infty
=
(\log4,\log2,\log(4/3),0,0,0).
\]

It has the positive decomposition

\[
\boxed{
w_\infty
=
\log2\,\epsilon_{\{1\}}
+
\log(3/2)\,\epsilon_{\{1,2\}}
+
\log(4/3)\,\epsilon_{\{1,2,3\}}.
}
\]

Therefore

\[
\boxed{
w_\infty
\in
\operatorname{relint}
C_{\{1\}<\{1,2\}<\{1,2,3\}}.
}
\]

Exactly two complete flags extend this partial flag:

\[
\{1\}<12<123<1234,
\]

and

\[
\{1\}<12<123<1236.
\]

Hence the generic REV-19 transition is an exact rank-\(4\) fine-flag flip.

## 6. REV-19 generic Lam star

For

\[
G_{19}:
\quad
\{1\}<12<123,
\]

the local Lam star is

\[
\Sigma_M(P,G_{19}),
\]

with transform

\[
\boxed{
L(\Sigma_M(P,G_{19}))
=
\frac1{a_1a_{12}a_{123}a_{1234}}
+
\frac1{a_1a_{12}a_{123}a_{1236}}.
}
\]

Its rank sum is

\[
1+2+3=6,
\]

so it enters the Lam intersection formula with positive sign.

## 7. Local transition stars are not whole Lam intersections

For REV-17,

\[
Q=(-,+,+,+),
\]

and exact Las Vergnas enumeration gives

\[
\boxed{|G(P,Q)|=3}.
\]

The three component stars contain four maximal fine cones in total.

For generic REV-19,

\[
Q=(-,+,-,+,+,+),
\]

and

\[
\boxed{|G(P,Q)|=31}.
\]

Their component distribution is

\[
\boxed{
\begin{array}{c|c|c}
\text{flag length}&\text{star size}&\text{component count}\\
\hline
1&12&1\\
2&4&6\\
3&2&12\\
4&1&12
\end{array}
}
\]

for a total of

\[
\boxed{72}
\]

maximal fine cones.

Thus the selected REV transition star is an exact **local Lam summand**, not generally the entire positive-Bergman intersection.

## 8. REV-17 global pole cancellation

The complete REV-17 Lam-star sum is

\[
\frac1{a_1a_{12}}
+
\frac1{a_1a_{14}}
+
\frac1{a_2a_{12}}
+
\frac1{a_4a_{14}}.
\]

Using

\[
a_{12}=a_1+a_2,
\qquad
a_{14}=a_1+a_4,
\]

this simplifies exactly to

\[
\boxed{
\frac1{a_1a_2}
+
\frac1{a_1a_4}.
}
\]

So composite-flat denominators present in the selected local star cancel only after companion \(G(P,Q)\) components are included.

With the Lam sign,

\[
\boxed{
\langle\Omega_P,\Omega_Q\rangle_{dR}
=
-
\left(
\frac1{a_1a_2}
+
\frac1{a_1a_4}
\right).
}
\]

## 9. Minimal-building-set regrouping

The exact \(G(P,Q)\) components in each audited case share one minimal-building-set decomposition.

For REV-17,

\[
\boxed{
T_{17}=\{\{1\}\}.
}
\]

For generic REV-19,

\[
\boxed{
T_{19}=\{\{1\},\{3\}\}.
}
\]

Thus the fine-star decomposition is compatible with Lam's nested-building-set regrouping.

The same tope intersection can be organized both as individual fine stars and as a nested-fan contribution indexed by \(T\).

## 10. Tropical transition graph

For \(A_{4,3}\), the REV transition is the fine-graph edge

\[
\{1\}<12
\longleftrightarrow
\{1\}<14.
\]

For \(A_{6,5}\), the generic REV transition is

\[
\{1\}<12<123<1234
\longleftrightarrow
\{1\}<12<123<1236.
\]

At coarse codimension-one level, \(A_{6,5}\) has

\[
\boxed{18}
\]

mixed-sign triple-tie transition faces and

\[
\boxed{54}
\]

adjacencies; the graph is \(6\)-regular.

The generic and terminal BERG-02 labels are combinatorially adjacent through the coarse chamber with top tie \(\{5,6\}\).

## 11. Correction to BERG-02 terminal incidence

At the exact REV-19 terminal point

\[
p=q=1,
\]

the coefficients on supports \(3\) and \(4\) vanish identically.

Hence

\[
\nu_3=\nu_4=-\infty,
\]

so

\[
w_3=w_4=+\infty
\]

in the Lam convention.

Therefore the exact terminal REV path does **not** define a finite vector in Lam's \(\mathbb R^E\) Bergman fan.

The coarse candidate

\[
\{2(+),5(-),6(+)\}
\]

is retained only as an

\[
\boxed{
\text{extended-tropical / regularized candidate}.
}
\]

Accordingly, BERG-02's wording that the exact terminal path itself lands on a finite positive-Bergman face is withdrawn.

## 12. Main verdict

The Bergman bridge is now local and theorem-level for the generic paths:

\[
\boxed{
\text{REV moment transition}
\longrightarrow
\text{fine Las Vergnas cone}
\longrightarrow
\text{Lam star summand}.
}
\]

The full Lam intersection is larger than the local star and supplies global cancellations invisible locally.

The terminal path identifies a genuine boundary of the finite-fan formulation and motivates an extended-tropical regularization problem.

## 13. Claim boundary

Exact claims cover:

- Las Vergnas face and flag enumeration;
- generic REV tropical limits;
- fine-cone incidence;
- local Lam-star transforms;
- \(G(P,Q)\) decomposition;
- REV-17 global pole cancellation;
- minimal-building-set regrouping.

No equality between the REV spectral response and Lam's fan Laplace transform is claimed.

The terminal path is **not** finite-Lam certified.

## 14. Recommended next Bergman audit

### EHF-PHYS-REV-BERG-04 — Lam-Intersection Summand Closure / Connected-Flat Pole Cancellation, Extended-Tropical Terminal Regularization and Tope-Pair Classification Audit

Targets:

1. prove the full connected-flat pole cancellation for the \(A_{6,5}\) REV-19 tope pair without brute-force rational explosion;
2. classify the 31 \(G(P,Q)\) components structurally;
3. construct an admissible terminal regularization;
4. determine whether its extended-tropical limit is canonical;
5. classify which REV support-tier transitions correspond to whole Lam intersections versus local summands.

The main reversal queue remains separately at:

### EHF-PHYS-REV-20 — Multi-Tier Fan Recursion / Inductive \(m\)-Atom Ladder Theorem and Tropical Face-Complex Classification

# EHF-PHYS-REV-BERG-04 — Lam-Intersection Summand Closure / Connected-Flat Pole Cancellation, Extended-Tropical Terminal Regularization and Tope-Pair Classification Audit

**Side branch:** EHF-PHYS-REV-BERG  
**Inputs:** BERG-01 through BERG-03, REV-17 through REV-19, and Lam's Theorem 10.17 / Theorem 10.24 / Corollary 10.25.  
**Status:** PASS. The \(A_{6,5}\) REV19 Lam intersection is closed exactly, all disconnected-flat poles cancel structurally, all 62 tope pairs receive a closed formula, and the terminal regularization problem is classified.

## 1. Connected flats of \(U_{5,6}\)

The proper flats of \(U_{5,6}\) have sizes \(1,2,3,4\).

Their restrictions are free matroids

\[
U_{s,s}.
\]

Thus a proper flat is connected if and only if

\[
s=1.
\]

Therefore the minimal building set contains only singleton proper flats.

Lam Corollary 10.25 predicts that completed deRham intersections can have poles only at singleton variables \(a_i\).

## 2. Exact closure of the 31 REV19 components

For the generic REV19 difference set

\[
D=\{1,3\},
\]

there are exactly

\[
\boxed{31}
\]

fine Lam-star components.

Their rank-sequence census is

\[
\boxed{
\begin{array}{c|c|c}
\text{rank sequence}&\text{components}&\text{star size}\\
\hline
(2)&1&12\\
(1,3)&3&4\\
(2,4)&3&4\\
(1,2,3)&6&2\\
(1,3,4)&6&2\\
(1,2,3,4)&12&1
\end{array}
}
\]

for a total of

\[
\boxed{72}
\]

maximal fine cones.

Every component has the same minimal decomposition

\[
\boxed{
T=\{\{1\},\{3\}\}.
}
\]

Hence Lam Theorem 10.24 closes the 31 summands into one nested subfan.

## 3. Structural pole cancellation

The three positive minimal-nested maximal cones are

\[
\{1,2,3,4\},
\qquad
\{1,2,3,6\},
\qquad
\{1,3,4,6\}.
\]

Each is triangulated by \(4!=24\) fine complete flags.

The exact identity

\[
\sum_{\pi\in S_m}
\frac1{
a_{\pi_1}
(a_{\pi_1}+a_{\pi_2})
\cdots
(a_{\pi_1}+\cdots+a_{\pi_m})
}
=
\frac1{\prod_i a_i}
\]

collapses every 24-term triangulation block.

Therefore the 72-term fine transform closes to

\[
\boxed{
\frac1{a_1a_2a_3a_4}
+
\frac1{a_1a_2a_3a_6}
+
\frac1{a_1a_3a_4a_6}.
}
\]

Equivalently,

\[
\boxed{
\frac{
a_5(a_2+a_4+a_6)
}{
a_1a_2a_3a_4a_5a_6
}.
}
\]

All disconnected/composite-flat poles cancel without a global common-denominator expansion.

## 4. Closed formula for all 62 \(A_{6,5}\) topes

Let

\[
P_+=\{2,4,6\},
\qquad
P_-=\{1,3,5\},
\]

and let \(D\) be the coordinates where \(Q\) differs from the all-positive reference tope.

Define

\[
A_+^{\rm out}
=
\sum_{i\in P_+\setminus D}a_i,
\qquad
A_-^{\rm out}
=
\sum_{j\in P_-\setminus D}a_j,
\]

\[
A_+^{\rm in}
=
\sum_{i\in P_+\cap D}a_i,
\qquad
A_-^{\rm in}
=
\sum_{j\in P_-\cap D}a_j.
\]

Then

\[
\boxed{
\langle\Omega_P,\Omega_Q\rangle^{dR}
=
(-1)^{|D|}
\frac{
A_+^{\rm out}A_-^{\rm out}
-
A_+^{\rm in}A_-^{\rm in}
}{
\prod_{h=1}^{6}a_h
}.
}
\]

This was independently checked against the direct fine-star formula for all 62 topes at two exact rational specializations:

\[
\boxed{124/124}
\]

comparisons passed.

## 5. Fourteen tope-pair classes

Write

\[
o=|D\cap\{1,3,5\}|,
\qquad
e=|D\cap\{2,4,6\}|.
\]

The 62 topes split into

\[
\boxed{14}
\]

nonempty \((o,e)\) classes.

The number of minimal-nested maximal cones on the \(Q\) branch is

\[
\boxed{
N_D=(3-e)(3-o),
}
\]

and on the \(-Q\) branch

\[
\boxed{
N_{D^c}=eo.
}
\]

The total number of fine maximal cones is

\[
\boxed{
24[(3-e)(3-o)+eo].
}
\]

## 6. Local star versus whole intersection

A single \(G^\pm(P,Q)\) component equals the entire intersection only for

\[
\boxed{Q=P}
\]

and

\[
\boxed{Q=-P}.
\]

Every nontrivial transition is necessarily a proper local Lam summand.

In particular:

- REV17 is a proper local summand;
- generic REV19 is a proper local summand;
- any finite terminal regularization preserving the REV19 tope pair is also a proper local summand.

## 7. Terminal exponential regularization

Let

\[
1-p_k\sim C_pe^{-\alpha k},
\qquad
1-q_k\sim C_qe^{-\beta k}.
\]

The terminal top-pair scaling remains

\[
u_k\sim3(1/3)^k,
\qquad
v_k\sim4(2/5)^k.
\]

The Lam-convention tropical limit is

\[
\boxed{
w_\infty
=
\left(
\log2,\,
0,\,
\beta-\log(3/2),\,
\alpha-\log2,\,
0,\,
0
\right).
}
\]

The prefactors \(C_p,C_q\) disappear from the tropical limit.

## 8. Exact terminal stability region

The terminal tier \(\{2,5,6\}\) remains dominant if and only if

\[
\boxed{
\alpha\ge\log2,
\qquad
\beta\ge\log(3/2).
}
\]

Strict inequalities give finite positive-Bergman points.

If either inequality fails, support \(4\) or support \(3\) overtakes the terminal tier.

## 9. Fine terminal noncanonicity

With

\[
L=\log2,
\qquad
A=\alpha-\log2,
\qquad
B=\beta-\log(3/2),
\]

the strict terminal region has

\[
L,A,B>0.
\]

The six possible strict orderings of \(L,A,B\) give six distinct fine Las Vergnas sectors ending at the common face \(\{1,3,4\}\).

Thus finite terminal regularization is not fine-cone canonical.

## 10. Exactly two sectors preserve \(D=\{1,3\}\)

The generic REV19 tope difference is

\[
D=\{1,3\}.
\]

Exactly two strict terminal sectors preserve it:

\[
\boxed{
L>A>B>0,
}
\]

with fine flag

\[
\boxed{
\{1\}<\{1,4\}<\{1,3,4\},
}
\]

and

\[
\boxed{
B>A>L>0,
}
\]

with fine flag

\[
\boxed{
\{3\}<\{3,4\}<\{1,3,4\}.
}
\]

Therefore a finite tope-preserving terminal regularization exists but is not unique at fine-fan level.

## 11. Nested terminal canonicity

Both \(D=\{1,3\}\)-preserving fine sectors decompose to

\[
\boxed{
T=\{\{1\},\{3\}\}.
}
\]

This is the same nested index as the generic REV19 transition.

Hence

\[
\boxed{
\text{fine regularization noncanonical}
}
\]

but

\[
\boxed{
\text{tope-preserving nested regularization canonical}.
}
\]

## 12. One global Lam closure contains multiple REV regimes

The generic REV19 transition and the two finite tope-preserving terminal sectors are different elements of the same \(G(P,Q)\) family.

All close to

\[
\boxed{
\Sigma_M(P,\{\{1\},\{3\}\}).
}
\]

Thus different REV support-tier regimes can expose different local fine Lam summands while sharing one global intersection invariant.

## 13. REV17-inherited threshold is incompatible with tope preservation

If

\[
\alpha=\log2,
\qquad
\beta=\log(3/2),
\]

then

\[
w_\infty=\log2\,\epsilon_{\{1\}}.
\]

This recovers the REV17 fine limit but changes the tope difference from

\[
\{1,3\}
\]

to

\[
\{1\}.
\]

Therefore “inherit the lower REV17 asymptotics” and “preserve the REV19 Lam tope pair” are distinct regularization principles.

## 14. Main verdict

BERG-04 completes the first nontrivial Lam intersection closure:

\[
\boxed{
31\text{ fine components}
\to
72\text{ fine cones}
\to
3\text{ singleton nested cones}.
}
\]

This produces exact connected-flat pole cancellation, a closed all-tope formula, and a precise regularization boundary.

The terminal path has no unique fine regularization, but its tope-preserving nested closure is canonical.

## 15. Claim boundary

All tope-pair formulas are exact for \(A_{6,5}\).

Terminal statements concern exponential tropical regularization.

No unique EHF-intrinsic regularization has been found.

No equality with the REV spectral response or a physical amplitude is claimed.

## 16. Recommended next Bergman audit

### EHF-PHYS-REV-BERG-05 — Corank-One Alternating Lam Formula / General \(A_{2m,2m-1}\) Tope-Pair Theorem, Nested-Fan Closure and KLT-Relevance Audit

The next target is the evident all-\(m\) generalization.

The main reversal sequence remains separately parked at EHF-PHYS-REV-20.

# EHF-PHYS-REV-BERG-05 — Corank-One Alternating Lam Formula / General \(A_{2m,2m-1}\) Tope-Pair Theorem, Nested-Fan Closure and KLT-Relevance Audit

**Side branch:** EHF-PHYS-REV-BERG  
**Inputs:** BERG-01 through BERG-04, REV-17 through REV-19, Lam Theorem 6.6 / Theorem 10.24 / Corollary 10.25 / Section 19.3.  
**Status:** PASS. The BERG-04 \(A_{6,5}\) result extends to a complete all-\(m\) corank-one theorem, with an explicit KLT-relevance boundary.

## 1. Family and topes

Let

\[
M_m=A_{2m,2m-1}
\]

with underlying matroid

\[
U_{2m-1,2m}.
\]

Use alternating circuit orientation

\[
(-,+,-,+,\ldots,-,+).
\]

Set

\[
P_+=\{2,4,\ldots,2m\},
\qquad
P_-=\{1,3,\ldots,2m-1\}.
\]

Relative to the all-positive reference tope \(P\), let

\[
D=\operatorname{sep}(P,Q).
\]

The only non-topes are the circuit and its negative:

\[
D=P_-,
\qquad
D=P_+.
\]

Hence

\[
\boxed{
|\mathcal T|=2^{2m}-2.
}
\]

## 2. Connected-flat theorem

Every proper flat \(F\) of \(U_{2m-1,2m}\) has

\[
|F|\le2m-2.
\]

The restriction to \(F\) is the free matroid

\[
U_{|F|,|F|}.
\]

Therefore

\[
\boxed{
F\text{ proper connected}
\iff
|F|=1.
}
\]

Thus the minimal building set contains singleton proper flats only, and completed Lam intersections can have only singleton poles.

## 3. Nested branches

Write

\[
o=|D\cap P_-|,
\qquad
e=|D\cap P_+|.
\]

The \(Q\)-branch has

\[
\boxed{
N_D=(m-e)(m-o)
}
\]

minimal-nested maximal cones.

Its transform is

\[
\boxed{
L_D
=
\frac{
A_+^{\rm out}A_-^{\rm out}
}{
\prod_h a_h
}.
}
\]

The opposite branch has

\[
\boxed{
N_{D^c}=eo
}
\]

maximal cones and

\[
\boxed{
L_{D^c}
=
\frac{
A_+^{\rm in}A_-^{\rm in}
}{
\prod_h a_h
}.
}
\]

## 4. General tope-pair formula

Since the rank \(2m-1\) is odd,

\[
\boxed{
\langle\Omega_P,\Omega_Q\rangle^{dR}
=
(-1)^{|D|}
\frac{
A_+^{\rm out}A_-^{\rm out}
-
A_+^{\rm in}A_-^{\rm in}
}{
\prod_{h=1}^{2m}a_h
}.
}
\]

This is the exact all-\(m\) generalization of BERG-04.

## 5. Constructive pole cancellation

Each minimal-nested maximal cone has dimension

\[
2m-2
\]

and is triangulated by

\[
(2m-2)!
\]

fine flag cones.

The identity

\[
\sum_{\pi\in S_d}
\frac1{
x_{\pi_1}
(x_{\pi_1}+x_{\pi_2})
\cdots
(x_{\pi_1}+\cdots+x_{\pi_d})
}
=
\frac1{x_1\cdots x_d}
\]

collapses every full permutation triangulation.

Therefore all composite-flat denominators cancel facetwise, leaving singleton poles only.

## 6. Symmetry classes

The pair depends combinatorially only on

\[
(o,e).
\]

There are

\[
\boxed{
(m+1)^2-2
}
\]

valid symmetry classes.

Class multiplicity is

\[
\boxed{
\binom mo\binom me.
}
\]

The number of fine maximal cones is

\[
\boxed{
(2m-2)!
\left[
(m-e)(m-o)+eo
\right].
}
\]

## 7. Exact Las Vergnas component formula

Let

\[
d=o+e,\qquad
\bar o=m-o,\qquad
\bar e=m-e.
\]

For flag length \(t\), define

\[
r=\left\lceil\frac t2\right\rceil,
\qquad
s=\left\lfloor\frac t2\right\rfloor.
\]

The \(D\)-elements contribute

\[
r!\,S(d,r).
\]

The outside elements contribute

\[
H_s(\bar o,\bar e)
=
\sum_{\substack{r_o\ge1\\r_e\ge1}}
\binom{\bar o}{r_o}
\binom{\bar e}{r_e}
s!\,
S(
\bar o+\bar e-r_o-r_e,
s
).
\]

Hence, for \(D\neq\varnothing\),

\[
\boxed{
|G(P,Q)|
=
\sum_{t\ge1}
r!\,S(d,r)\,
H_s(\bar o,\bar e).
}
\]

For \(D=\varnothing\),

\[
|G(P,P)|=1.
\]

This formula was directly verified for every \(D\) at \(m=2,3\).

## 8. Local versus global

The exact count formula implies that the only one-component whole intersections are

\[
\boxed{
Q=P,\qquad Q=-P.
}
\]

Every nontrivial tope transition is a proper local Lam summand.

The result was exhaustively checked from the formula through \(m=8\).

## 9. Independent validation

The general formula was checked independently against direct fine-star expansions:

- \(m=2\): all \(14\) topes, exact rational arithmetic;
- \(m=3\): all \(62\) topes, exact rational arithmetic;
- \(m=4\): all \(254\) topes at two exact finite-field specializations.

Thus the \(m=4\) layer supplies

\[
\boxed{508}
\]

exact modular tope checks.

No failures occurred.

## 10. Matroid invariants

For a uniform matroid,

\[
\mu^+(U_{r,n})
=
\binom{n-1}{r-1},
\]

\[
\beta(U_{r,n})
=
\binom{n-2}{r-1}.
\]

Therefore

\[
\boxed{
\mu^+(U_{2m-1,2m})=2m-1,
}
\]

and

\[
\boxed{
\beta(U_{2m-1,2m})=1.
}
\]

Lam's bounded-tope counts give

\[
\boxed{
|T^\star|=2m-1,
}
\]

for a general extension, while

\[
\boxed{
|T^0|=1.
}
\]

## 11. KLT relevance

Lam Theorem 6.6 puts this family inside the exact inverse-pairing architecture:

\[
\text{deRham cohomology intersection}
\longleftrightarrow
\text{scaled deRham homology inverse}.
\]

Thus, after selecting a \(T^\star\) basis, the corank-one family yields a legitimate matroidal inverse-pairing problem of size

\[
2m-1.
\]

This is the positive KLT-type connection.

## 12. KLT no-go boundary

Lam's actual field-theory KLT recovery occurs for the complete graphic matroid

\[
M(K_n),
\]

not for \(U_{2m-1,2m}\).

The differences are structural.

### Basis scaling

The general-extension basis here grows only as

\[
2m-1.
\]

The projective bounded sector has

\[
\beta=1.
\]

### Physical channel structure

All proper connected flats here are singletons.

The complete graphic matroid has nontrivial connected flats that encode multiparticle factorization channels.

### Object type

The BERG-05 formula is an amplitude-side deRham cohomology intersection entry.

A KLT-like kernel arises only after inversion on a bounded-tope basis.

Therefore

\[
\boxed{
\text{BERG-05 formula itself is not a KLT kernel}.
}
\]

## 13. Scientific verdict

The entire alternating corank-one family is now solved at the Lam-intersection level:

\[
\boxed{
A_{2m,2m-1}
\Rightarrow
\text{closed tope-pair formula}
+
\text{singleton nested closure}
+
\text{exact component combinatorics}.
}
\]

The KLT connection is real but limited:

\[
\boxed{
\text{matroidal inverse-pairing test family}
\neq
\text{physical complete-graphic KLT family}.
}
\]

## 14. Recommended next Bergman audit

### EHF-PHYS-REV-BERG-06 — Beyond Corank One / First Rich-Connected-Flat Alternating Matroid, Nontrivial Beta Sector and KLT-Matrix Emergence Audit

The next useful step is to leave corank one and demand:

1. non-singleton connected proper flats;
2. \(\beta>1\);
3. a genuinely multidimensional projective bounded-tope basis;
4. nontrivial factorization channels.

That is the first regime in which a serious KLT-matrix comparison can begin.

The main REV sequence remains parked at EHF-PHYS-REV-20.
