# EHF-PHYS-XKLT-01 — OPW/AMP Momentum-Kernel vs Lam/Bergman Intersection Cross-Identification Audit

**Cross branch:** EHF-PHYS-XKLT  
**Inputs:** AMP-08/AMP23, OPW3-XBR, BERG-01 through BERG-05, Lam complete-graphic amplitude theorem.  
**Status:** PASS. Direct matrix equality is ruled out, but an exact Hamilton-cycle matroid-restriction junction is found.

## 1. Historical source lock

The AMP program identifies canonical Hamilton cycles on \(K_n\), modulo cyclicity and reflection, with the gauge-amplitude trace basis.

At BCJ level,

\[
V=\operatorname{col}m(\alpha|\beta),
\]

with

\[
\dim V=(n-3)!,
\]

and \(m(\alpha|\beta)\) is the inverse field-theory KLT momentum kernel on a BCJ basis.

The OPW3-XBR program gives an exact amplitude-relation transport bridge over a 72-chart, 5184-arrow groupoid, including KK-row covariance, deletion, transmitted images and Krylov closure.

The REV-BERG branch produces Lam intersection forms for alternating corank-one oriented matroids \(A_{2m,2m-1}\).

## 2. Direct matrix identity is impossible

For \(n\ge5\),

\[
(n-3)!
\]

is even.

For the corank-one Lam family,

\[
\mu^+=2m-1
\]

is odd.

Therefore

\[
\boxed{
(n-3)!\ne2m-1
}
\]

for every nontrivial amplitude multiplicity.

The projective corank-one sector is even smaller:

\[
\boxed{\beta=1}.
\]

Hence neither the general-extension nor projective corank-one Lam matrix can equal the AMP BCJ/KLT matrix for \(n\ge5\).

## 3. Ambient state spaces also differ

For odd \(n\ge5\),

\[
|\Omega_n|=\frac{(n-1)!}{2}\equiv0\pmod4,
\]

while

\[
|\mathcal T(A_{n,n-1})|
=
2^n-2
\equiv2\pmod4.
\]

Thus the OPW trace states are not the corank-one Lam topes.

## 4. AMP-08 sees a column space, not a unique KLT matrix

For every invertible \(R\),

\[
\operatorname{col}(mR)=\operatorname{col}(m),
\]

and every chamber-restricted rank is unchanged:

\[
\operatorname{rank}((mR)|_C)
=
\operatorname{rank}(m|_C).
\]

An exact finite witness checked all 63 nonempty row subsets with zero mismatches.

Therefore the AMP-08 chamber law is exact BCJ/KLT contact but does not reconstruct \(m\) or \(m^{-1}\).

## 5. XBR is relation/subspace-level

The source-certified XBR bridge is built from KK rows, amplitude relation images, deletion, middle-block growth and OPW adjacency/Krylov closure.

No XBR theorem currently identifies the entries of \(m(\alpha|\beta)\), its bilinear form, or its KLT inverse with an OPW operator.

Thus

\[
\boxed{
\text{exact OPW/amplitude bridge}
\ne
\text{exact KLT-matrix intertwiner}.
}
\]

## 6. Pole obstruction

At five points a standard field-theory KLT kernel convention gives

\[
S_5
=
\begin{pmatrix}
s_{12}(s_{13}+s_{23})&s_{12}s_{13}\\
s_{12}s_{13}&s_{13}(s_{12}+s_{23})
\end{pmatrix},
\]

with

\[
\det S_5
=
s_{12}s_{13}s_{23}(s_{12}+s_{13}+s_{23}).
\]

Thus \(m_5=S_5^{-1}\) has nontrivial channel poles.

The REV corank-one Lam family has only singleton connected-flat poles.

So direct channel-preserving identification is impossible.

## 7. Exact positive cross-route theorem

Let \(\alpha\) be a Hamilton-cycle ordering in \(K_n\).

Its edge set satisfies

\[
E(C_\alpha)\subset E(K_n).
\]

The complete graphic matroid restriction is

\[
\boxed{
M(K_n)|_{E(C_\alpha)}
=
M(C_\alpha)
\cong
U_{n-1,n}.
}
\]

A cycle has \(n\) edges, rank \(n-1\), and exactly one circuit containing all edges.

This is the precise corank-one graphic type appearing in BERG.

## 8. Oriented realization

For even \(n=2m\), orient the cycle edges alternately relative to one traversal.

The unique signed circuit is

\[
(-,+,-,+,\ldots,-,+).
\]

Hence

\[
\boxed{
M^{\rm or}(C_{2m})
\cong
A_{2m,2m-1}.
}
\]

So the REV alternating family is an oriented Hamilton-cycle restriction type of a complete graphic matroid.

## 9. Why this is an OPW bridge

The AMP trace basis is itself the Hamilton-cycle basis.

Therefore every AMP/OPW state \(\alpha\) selects an embedded corank-one graphic restriction

\[
M(K_n)|_{E(C_\alpha)}.
\]

Lam's complete-graphic specialization is the matroid setting where biadjoint scalar amplitudes are recovered.

Thus the two EHF routes meet at

\[
\boxed{
\text{Hamilton-cycle restrictions inside complete-graphic matroid geometry}.
}
\]

This is the central positive result of XKLT-01.

## 10. Different levels of one architecture

The old AMP/KLT route lives at the complete-graphic, pair-of-orderings level:

\[
m(\alpha|\beta).
\]

The REV/Bergman route lives at the single-cycle restriction level:

\[
U_{n-1,n}
\subset
M(K_n).
\]

Therefore the routes are related but not identical.

The missing operation must combine two Hamilton-cycle restrictions.

## 11. Pairwise structure is the missing theorem

Biadjoint double-partial amplitudes are indexed by two cyclic orderings and involve cubic trees simultaneously planar in both.

Thus the natural unresolved object is

\[
(C_\alpha,C_\beta)
\longmapsto
\text{common compatible flat/split/tree structure}.
\]

No EHF theorem yet proves that this gluing reconstructs \(m(\alpha|\beta)\).

## 12. The 72 coincidence is rejected

XBR has a pair groupoid with

\[
72
\]

objects and

\[
5184
\]

arrows, with trivial isotropy.

The signed-circuit automorphism group of \(A_{6,5}\), allowing global circuit sign reversal, has order

\[
3!\,3!\,2=72.
\]

But, viewed as a groupoid, it has one object and isotropy order 72.

Hence these are categorically different structures.

The shared number 72 is not the cross-route bridge.

## 13. Verdict

Direct matrix identity:

\[
\boxed{\text{NO}}.
\]

Exact Hamilton-cycle matroid restriction junction:

\[
\boxed{\text{YES}}.
\]

Existing EHF theorem reconstructing the double-partial/KLT matrix from that restriction junction:

\[
\boxed{\text{NOT YET}}.
\]

The previous idea that EHF had independently reached “the same KLT matrix” from two directions is therefore replaced by the more precise statement:

\[
\boxed{
\text{the two routes reach different levels of the same complete-graphic matroid-amplitude architecture}.
}
\]

## 14. Recommended next cross audit

### EHF-PHYS-XKLT-02 — Hamilton-Cycle Pair Restriction / Common-Flat Compatibility, Simultaneous Planarity and Double-Partial \(m(\alpha|\beta)\) Reconstruction Audit

Targets:

1. take pairs \(C_\alpha,C_\beta\);
2. construct both cycle-matroid restrictions inside \(M(K_n)\);
3. enumerate common compatible flats/splits;
4. compare with simultaneously planar cubic trees;
5. reconstruct \(m(\alpha|\beta)\) at small \(n\);
6. test whether Lam's complete-graphic intersection is a gluing/closure of the two corank-one cycle sectors.

The other queues remain:

- BERG-06;
- REV-20;
- ALG-07C.

# EHF-PHYS-XKLT-02 — Hamilton-Cycle Pair Restriction / Common-Flat Compatibility, Simultaneous Planarity and Double-Partial \(m(\alpha|\beta)\) Reconstruction Audit

**Cross branch:** EHF-PHYS-XKLT  
**Inputs:** AMP-08/AMP23, XKLT-01, BERG-01 through BERG-05, standard double-partial tree formula, Frost dual-associahedron formulation, Lam complete-graphic amplitude theorem.  
**Status:** PASS. The missing pair-of-cycle operation is found: ambient complete-graphic channel-flat compatibility reconstructs the double-partial combinatorics and small-\(n\) KLT inverse pairings.

## 1. Literal cycle restrictions are too coarse

For every Hamilton-cycle ordering \(\alpha\),

\[
M(K_n)|_{E(C_\alpha)}
\cong
U_{n-1,n}.
\]

Thus the abstract restricted matroid type forgets \(\alpha\).

A pair of abstract copies

\[
U_{n-1,n},U_{n-1,n}
\]

cannot reconstruct relative cyclic order or \(m(\alpha|\beta)\).

## 2. Literal common edge data also fail

At \(n=6\), take

\[
\alpha=(1,2,3,4,5,6),
\]

\[
\beta_A=(1,2,5,6,3,4),
\]

\[
\beta_B=(1,2,4,3,6,5).
\]

Both pairs have identical common cycle-edge set

\[
\{12,34,56\}.
\]

But

\[
|\mathcal T(\alpha)\cap\mathcal T(\beta_A)|=1,
\]

while

\[
|\mathcal T(\alpha)\cap\mathcal T(\beta_B)|=2.
\]

So literal common restricted-flat/edge overlap is insufficient.

Collision counts increase:

\[
n=6:5,
\qquad
n=7:35,
\qquad
n=8:122.
\]

## 3. Correct ambient channel-flat object

A factorization channel is an unordered split

\[
I|I^c,
\qquad
2\le |I|\le n-2.
\]

It is planar in \(\alpha\) exactly when \(I\) is a cyclic interval in \(\alpha\).

Inside the ambient complete graphic matroid associate

\[
\boxed{
F_I=E(K_I).
}
\]

This connected flat generally is not contained in the cycle-edge restriction.

For example, for the planar channel \(I=\{1,2,3\}\) in the standard six-cycle,

\[
F_I=\{12,13,23\},
\]

but edge \(13\) is not a Hamilton-cycle edge.

Therefore the amplitude channels live in the ambient \(M(K_n)\), selected by cyclic-order data.

## 4. Common channel-flat theorem

Define

\[
\mathcal C_\alpha
=
\{
[I|I^c]:
I\text{ is a cyclic interval in }\alpha
\}.
\]

For a pair,

\[
\boxed{
\mathcal C_{\alpha\beta}
=
\mathcal C_\alpha\cap\mathcal C_\beta.
}
\]

These are exactly the factorization channels planar in both orderings.

Two splits are compatible when one of the four intersections

\[
A\cap B,\quad
A\cap B^c,\quad
A^c\cap B,\quad
A^c\cap B^c
\]

is empty.

Then

\[
\boxed{
T\text{ cubic and planar in both }\alpha,\beta
}
\]

if and only if its \(n-3\) channels form a pairwise compatible \(n-3\)-subset of

\[
\mathcal C_{\alpha\beta}.
\]

Thus the required gluing operation is

\[
\boxed{
\text{common ambient interval-flat selection}
+
\text{split compatibility}.
}
\]

## 5. Complete finite pair census

Two independent routes were compared:

1. enumerate all \(\alpha\)-planar triangulations and filter by \(\beta\);
2. build common ambient channels first and enumerate compatible size-\(n-3\) systems.

Complete relative-pair counts:

\[
n=5:12,
\]

\[
n=6:60,
\]

\[
n=7:360,
\]

\[
n=8:2520.
\]

Total:

\[
\boxed{2952}.
\]

Mismatch count:

\[
\boxed{0/2952}.
\]

## 6. Catalan diagonal

For \(\alpha=\beta\), the reconstruction gives

\[
C_{n-2}
\]

planar cubic trees:

\[
5,\ 14,\ 42,\ 132
\]

at \(n=5,6,7,8\).

## 7. Exact row-support distributions

For fixed standard \(\alpha\), the simultaneous-tree distributions are:

\[
n=5:
\quad
0:1,\ 1:5,\ 2:5,\ 5:1,
\]

\[
n=6:
\quad
0:15,\ 1:14,\ 2:21,\ 4:3,\ 5:6,\ 14:1,
\]

\[
n=7:
\quad
0:163,\ 1:42,\ 2:84,\ 4:28,\ 5:28,\ 10:7,\ 14:7,\ 42:1,
\]

\[
n=8:
\quad
0:1617,\ 1:132,\ 2:330,\ 4:180,\ 5:120,\ 8:12,\ 10:72,\ 14:36,\ 25:4,\ 28:8,\ 42:8,\ 132:1.
\]

These are the exact monomial-support multiplicities of a relative row before propagator weights and signs.

## 8. Channel count alone eventually fails

At \(n=8\), equal numbers of common channels need not yield equal numbers of common trees.

Examples:

\[
11\text{ common channels}
\Rightarrow
14\text{ or }25\text{ trees},
\]

and common counts \(6\) and \(8\) also split.

Thus the compatibility complex itself is essential.

## 9. Double-partial reconstruction

With common endpoints, the standard sign convention gives

\[
\boxed{
m(\alpha|\beta)
=
(-1)^{\operatorname{flip}(\alpha|\beta)}
\sum_{T\in\mathcal T(\alpha)\cap\mathcal T(\beta)}
\prod_{I\in T}\frac1{s_I}.
}
\]

The audit implements this directly from the common ambient channel-flat complex.

Hence the pair construction reconstructs every propagator monomial of \(m(\alpha|\beta)\).

## 10. Independent BCJ-rank reproduction

On the fixed-endpoint KK basis

\[
(1,\sigma(2,\ldots,n-1),n),
\]

the reconstructed signed matrices have:

\[
n=5:
\quad
6\times6,\quad
\operatorname{rank}=2,
\]

\[
n=6:
\quad
24\times24,\quad
\operatorname{rank}=6,
\]

in exact rational arithmetic.

At \(n=7\), the \(120\times120\) reconstruction gives

\[
\boxed{\operatorname{rank}=24}
\]

over both

\[
\mathbb F_{1000003}
\]

and

\[
\mathbb F_{1000033}.
\]

Thus

\[
\boxed{
\operatorname{rank}m=(n-3)!
}
\]

is independently recovered through the pair/common-flat pipeline for \(n=5,6,7\).

## 11. Small-\(n\) inverse pairing

Select

\[
\mathcal B_n
=
\{
(1,\sigma(2,\ldots,n-2),n-1,n)
\}.
\]

Then

\[
|\mathcal B_n|=(n-3)!.
\]

The reconstructed submatrix is invertible exactly for \(n=5,6\).

Its exact inverse satisfies

\[
\boxed{
m_{\mathcal B}K_{\mathcal B}=I.
}
\]

At \(n=7\), the \(24\times24\) submatrix is full rank over both exact finite fields.

Thus the cycle-pair reconstruction reaches a basis-specific KLT inverse pairing at small \(n\).

No claim is made that this same-endpoint basis is identical to every conventional presentation of the momentum kernel.

## 12. OPW chamber boundary

At \(n=7\), the 360 \(\beta\)-states split into the 19 OPW \(\Pi\)-chambers.

Relative to standard \(\alpha\),

\[
\boxed{18/19}
\]

chambers contain multiple simultaneous-tree counts.

Only one chamber is constant, and it is uniformly zero.

Therefore

\[
\boxed{
\Pi(\beta)\text{ does not determine }m(\alpha|\beta)\text{ entrywise}.
}
\]

The AMP-08 chamber law is therefore a collective restricted-rank/relation-space effect, not an entrywise block rule.

## 13. Cross-route closure

XKLT-01 gave

\[
\alpha
\longrightarrow
C_\alpha
\longrightarrow
U_{n-1,n}\subset M(K_n).
\]

XKLT-02 now gives

\[
\boxed{
(C_\alpha,C_\beta)
\longrightarrow
\mathcal C_{\alpha\beta}
\longrightarrow
\text{compatible ambient channel-flat systems}
\longrightarrow
m(\alpha|\beta).
}
\]

Thus the old AMP/KLT route and the REV/Bergman route genuinely meet inside the complete-graphic matroid architecture.

## 14. Remaining Lam-specific gap

What is **not** proved is

\[
\text{complete-graphic Lam intersection}
=
\text{algebraic gluing of two completed corank-one Lam intersections}.
\]

The reason is structural.

Completed corank-one Lam forms have singleton connected-flat poles.

The reconstructed double-partial object requires multiparticle ambient flats

\[
E(K_I).
\]

Therefore any Lam gluing must occur before corank-one completion, or through a restriction/induction map retaining ambient flat data.

## 15. Claim boundary

The signed common-tree formula for \(m(\alpha|\beta)\) is standard amplitude theory.

The EHF theorem here is the exact cross-identification of that formula with the Hamilton-cycle restriction / ambient connected-flat structure reached independently by the REV-Bergman branch.

This is not a derivation of biadjoint field theory from OPW dynamics.

## 16. Recommended next cross audit

### EHF-PHYS-XKLT-03 — Complete-Graphic Flat Gluing / Associahedral Shared-Facet Complex, Lam Intersection Restriction-Induction and KLT Kernel Basis-Map Audit

Targets:

1. identify the common channel-flat complex with the shared dual-associahedron facets explicitly;
2. construct restriction/induction maps from the cycle sectors into the complete graphic Lam object;
3. test pre-completion Lam gluing;
4. give an explicit basis transformation to a standard momentum-kernel convention;
5. compare the resulting map with XBR's exact relation-space transport.

Other queued branches remain BERG-06, REV-20 and ALG-07C.

# EHF-PHYS-XKLT-03 — Complete-Graphic Flat Gluing / Associahedral Shared-Facet Complex, Lam Intersection Restriction-Induction and KLT Kernel Basis-Map Audit

**Cross branch:** EHF-PHYS-XKLT  
**Status:** PASS.  
**Supersession:** physical Lam indexing in XKLT-01/02 is corrected from M(K_N) to M(K_{N-1}); their common-tree reconstruction remains valid and is sharpened.

**Status:** PASS.

## 1. A necessary indexing correction

Lam's complete graphic matroid \(M(K_r)\) realizes the configuration space
\(M_{0,r+1}\).

Therefore an \(N\)-point amplitude is aligned with

\[
\boxed{
M(K_{N-1}),
}
\]

not \(M(K_N)\).

This corrects the physical indexing used in parts of XKLT-01/02.

The mathematical identity

\[
M(K_N)|E(C_N)=U_{N-1,N}
\]

remains true, but \(M(K_N)\) is Lam's complete-graphic model for an
\((N+1)\)-point amplitude.

The corrected alignment turns out to be stronger.

## 2. Trace/Hamilton-cycle states are Lam positive topes

Choose one external leg \(d\) as the distinguished/root point.

A cyclic ordering of \(N\) labels modulo cyclicity and reflection becomes,
after deleting \(d\), a linear ordering of the remaining \(N-1\) labels
modulo reversal.

Lam's positive topes of \(M(K_{N-1})\) are indexed by exactly such
permutations after one orientation is selected.

Thus

\[
\boxed{
\{\text{N-point trace/Hamilton-cycle states}\}
\cong
\mathcal T^+(M(K_{N-1})).
}
\]

Both sides have

\[
\frac{(N-1)!}{2}
\]

elements.

The audit constructs the bijection explicitly and verifies it completely
for

\[
N=5,6,7,8,9.
\]

This is a stronger state-level identification than XKLT-01.

## 3. Nine points: the delete-active sector is Lam's \(T^\star\)

Use the reference chart with deleted/root leg \(9\) and anchor \(1\).

The nine-point tail-filtration theorem has exactly

\[
5040
\]

delete-active source states in this chart.

For each active state, removing root \(9\) gives a permutation of
\(\{1,\ldots,8\}\) beginning with \(1\).

Lam's standard general extension of \(M(K_8)\) has bounded topes

\[
\mathcal T^\star
=
\{
\sigma:\sigma(1)=1
\},
\]

also of size

\[
7!=5040.
\]

The audit verifies the exact equality of the two finite sets under the
state-to-tope map:

\[
\boxed{
\text{reference delete-9 active source sector}
\cong
\mathcal T^\star(M(K_8)).
}
\]

This gives the nine-point deletion theorem a direct Lam state-space
placement.

## 4. The deletion target is the \(A_{8,7}\) BERG type

Deleting \(9\) from an active source cycle leaves an eight-label Hamilton
cycle.

Inside the **source** complete graphic matroid

\[
M(K_8),
\]

the target edge set has restriction

\[
\boxed{
M(K_8)|E(C_8)
=
U_{7,8}.
}
\]

Alternating the edge orientation gives the oriented matroid

\[
\boxed{
A_{8,7}.
}
\]

This is exactly the \(m=4\) member of the BERG family

\[
A_{2m,2m-1}.
\]

So at nine points the correct junction is:

\[
\boxed{
\mathcal T^\star(M(K_8))
\overset{\text{delete }9}{\longrightarrow}
\text{8-cycle sectors of type }A_{8,7}
\subset M(K_8).
}
\]

The 5040 active source states map two-to-one onto

\[
2520
\]

canonical eight-point target cycles, matching the established deletion
census.

## 5. What this means for the tail filtration

Nothing in the six-space theorem changes.

The established relation-image law remains

\[
V_0,V_2,V_8,V_9,V_{14},V_{15}.
\]

Its new placement is:

\[
\boxed{
\text{KK-relation transmission over a Lam }T^\star(M(K_8))
\text{ sector onto }A_{8,7}\text{-type target cycle restrictions}.
}
\]

The six \(V_d\) are still **not** KLT blocks.

They are exact transmitted relation subspaces.

But their ambient Lam/matroid position is now much sharper than before.

## 6. Lam positive flats are rooted planar channels

For \(M(K_{N-1})\), Lam's positive-flat lattice for a tope \(P_\sigma\)
consists of set partitions whose blocks are intervals in the linear order
\(\sigma\).

A proper flat is connected exactly when it has one non-singleton block.

Therefore the connected positive flats of \(P_\sigma\) are exactly

\[
\boxed{
F(S)=E(K_S)
}
\]

where \(S\) is a proper non-singleton interval in \(\sigma\).

After the root external leg has been removed, those intervals are exactly
the usual planar factorization channels.

The audit reconstructs the positive-flat lattice directly and verifies
this equality for every positive tope at \(N=5,6,7,8\).

## 7. Pair gluing is now literally a Lam flat intersection

For two rooted orderings \(\alpha,\beta\),

\[
F(S)
\]

is selected by both topes if and only if \(S\) is an interval in both
linear orders.

Hence

\[
\boxed{
\text{common planar channels}
=
\text{common connected positive flats of }
P_\alpha,P_\beta.
}
\]

This upgrades XKLT-02's "ambient complete-graphic flat" statement to a
precise Lam positive-flat statement.

## 8. Minimal nested sets are the shared associahedral complex

For the complete graph, two connected flats \(F(S),F(T)\) in the minimal
building set are nested-compatible exactly when the rooted subsets \(S,T\)
are:

- nested, or
- disjoint.

That is the rooted form of ordinary noncrossing split compatibility.

Therefore the minimal nested-set complex generated by the common connected
positive flats is exactly the common planar split complex.

Frost's dual-associahedron description identifies the same complex as the
shared boundary/facet complex of the two dual associahedra.

Thus:

\[
\boxed{
\text{Lam common connected-flat nested complex}
=
\text{shared dual-associahedron complex}.
}
\]

Its maximal faces have \(N-3\) channels and are exactly the cubic trees
planar in both orderings.

Two independent constructions were compared for all

\[
12+60+360+2520
=
\boxed{2952}
\]

relative pairs at \(N=5,6,7,8\).

Mismatch count:

\[
\boxed{0}.
\]

## 9. This is the exact pre-completion gluing operation

The gluing sought in XKLT-02 does exist, but at the correct level:

\[
\boxed{
P_\alpha,P_\beta
\longrightarrow
L(P_\alpha)\cap L(P_\beta)
\longrightarrow
\text{common connected flats}
\longrightarrow
\text{minimal nested complex}.
}
\]

Applying the amplitude valuation to its maximal nested faces gives the
double-partial tree sum.

So the pairwise gluing is an ambient positive-flat/nested-fan operation
**before** the corank-one sectors are separately completed.

## 10. Why Lam Proposition 5.10 does not glue the completed BERG forms

Lam proves that the deRham intersection form is compatible with restriction
to a **flat** \(F\).

But a Hamilton-cycle edge set in \(K_r\) is spanning and connected.

Its closure in the graphic matroid is therefore

\[
\operatorname{cl}(E(C_r))
=
E(K_r).
\]

For

\[
r\ge4,
\]

\[
E(C_r)\subsetneq E(K_r),
\]

so the Hamilton-cycle edge set is not a flat.

The audit checks this exactly for

\[
K_4,\ldots,K_8.
\]

Therefore:

\[
\boxed{
\text{Lam Proposition 5.10 does not directly identify the complete-graphic
intersection form with the BERG cycle-restriction form.}
}
\]

This is an applicability obstruction, not a proof that no more elaborate
induction/restriction construction can exist.

## 11. Correct KLT basis indexing

For an \(N\)-point amplitude, put

\[
r=N-1.
\]

The standard BCJ permutation labels are

\[
\alpha,\beta\in S_{\{2,\ldots,N-2\}}.
\]

The corresponding Lam bounded-topes labels in \(M(K_r)\) are

\[
\boxed{
P_{1\,\alpha\,r},
\qquad
P_{1\,\beta\,r}.
}
\]

There are

\[
(N-3)!
\]

of them.

The standard double-partial inverse pairing can be represented by the dual
Parke--Taylor bases

\[
(1,\alpha,r,N),
\qquad
(1,\beta,N,r).
\]

This separates the permutation label used by the Lam tope from the
orientation convention of the two Parke--Taylor factors.

## 12. Lam Theorem 19.9 reduces to the usual momentum kernel

Let

\[
W_{\alpha\beta}^{\rm Lam}
\]

be Lam's deRham homology product formula, covariantly relabelled from the
identity row.

For endpoint-fixed permutations, the final factor in Lam's product is

\[
\sum_{k=1}^{N-2}s_{k,N-1}.
\]

Momentum conservation gives

\[
\sum_{k=1}^{N-2}s_{k,N-1}
=
-s_{N-1,N}.
\]

The remaining factors are exactly the standard momentum-kernel factors.

Therefore

\[
\boxed{
W_{\alpha\beta}^{\rm Lam}
=
-s_{N-1,N}\,
S[\alpha|\beta]_1.
}
\]

This is the explicit basis/normalization map that XKLT-02 left open.

## 13. Exact inverse check against reconstructed \(m\)

Let

\[
M_{\alpha\beta}^{\rm cross}
=
m(1,\alpha,N-1,N\mid1,\beta,N,N-1)
\]

with the signed common-tree convention used in XKLT-02.

Then the audit finds

\[
\boxed{
M^{\rm cross}
\left(
\frac{W^{\rm Lam}}{s_{N-1,N}}
\right)
=
I.
}
\]

Because

\[
\frac{W^{\rm Lam}}{s_{N-1,N}}
=
-S_{\rm standard}
\]

in the audited orientation convention, the extra minus sign is a global
Parke--Taylor/KLT convention sign.

Verification:

### \(N=5\)

Exact rational arithmetic:

\[
\boxed{\text{PASS}}.
\]

### \(N=6\)

Exact rational arithmetic:

\[
\boxed{\text{PASS}}.
\]

### \(N=7\)

Exact finite fields

\[
\mathbb F_{1000003},
\qquad
\mathbb F_{1000033}:
\]

\[
\boxed{\text{PASS in both}}.
\]

So the reconstructed cycle-pair double-partial matrix and Lam's KLT product
are now explicitly cross-identified on a conventional BCJ permutation
basis.

## 14. What survives from XKLT-01 and XKLT-02

### Retained

The double-partial common-tree reconstruction of XKLT-02 is unchanged.

The statement that a Hamilton cycle gives a corank-one graphic restriction
is mathematically unchanged.

### Superseded

The physically aligned complete-graphic matroid for an \(N\)-point
amplitude is \(M(K_{N-1})\), not \(M(K_N)\).

So the correct corank-one object is the **gap-closed cycle after the
distinguished root leg is removed**.

For nine points this is \(A_{8,7}\), not a same-label-count \(A_{9,8}\)
comparison.

### Sharpened

The common channels are not merely ambient graphic flats.

They are exactly the common **positive connected flats** in Lam's two tope
lattices.

## 15. Relation to the OPW/XBR atlas

The OPW/AMP state set now has a direct complete-graphic interpretation:

\[
\boxed{
\Omega_N
\cong
\mathcal T^+(M(K_{N-1}))
}
\]

after a root chart is chosen.

At nine points, the delete-active reference sector is

\[
\boxed{
\mathcal T^\star(M(K_8)).
}
\]

The existing 72-chart XBR atlas then transports the OPW dynamics and AMP
relation data over chartwise relabellings of this rooted complete-graphic
picture.

No claim is made that OPW LR3 adjacency equals the braid-arrangement
chamber adjacency of Lam's topes.

The state spaces coincide under the rooted identification; the graph
structures are separate.

## 16. Main verdict

The cross-route architecture is now:

\[
\boxed{
\begin{array}{c}
\text{N-point OPW/AMP trace state}\\
\updownarrow\\
\text{Lam positive tope of }M(K_{N-1})
\end{array}
}
\]

and, for a pair,

\[
\boxed{
(P_\alpha,P_\beta)
\to
\text{common positive connected flats}
\to
\text{shared nested/associahedral complex}
\to
m(\alpha|\beta)
\to
K_{\rm KLT}.
}
\]

At odd-to-even deletion, the target Hamilton cycle is simultaneously a
corank-one BERG restriction inside the source complete-graphic matroid.

This is a substantially stronger and more precise bridge than the
same-label-count comparison in XKLT-01.

## 17. Claim boundary

Exact in this audit:

- state-to-tope bijection;
- nine-point active \(T^\star\) identification;
- \(A_{8,7}\) target restriction;
- common positive-flat/channel equality;
- nested/shared-associahedron complex equality;
- non-flat restriction obstruction;
- Lam/KLT endpoint-factor formula;
- small-\(N\) inverse checks.

External theorem inputs:

- Lam's complete-graphic realization and Theorem 19.9;
- Frost's shared dual-associahedron interpretation of
  \(m(\alpha|\beta)\).

Not claimed:

- OPW dynamics derives Lam's intersection theory;
- LR3 adjacency is Lam tope adjacency;
- the six tail-filtration spaces are KLT blocks;
- completed BERG corank-one forms algebraically induce the complete-graphic
  pairing.

## 18. Recommended next audit

### EHF-PHYS-XKLT-04 — Nine-Point Tail Filtration vs Complete-Graphic KLT Restriction / \(V_d\)-Resolved BCJ Rank, Common-Channel Support and Deletion-Factorization Compatibility Audit

This is now the highest-value next test.

At nine points we can ask directly whether the six exact transmitted
relation spaces

\[
V_0,V_2,V_8,V_9,V_{14},V_{15}
\]

control any invariant of the complete-graphic amplitude/KLT object:

1. BCJ rank after restricting to each deletion fibre;
2. common connected-flat support;
3. simultaneous-tree support;
4. residues/factorization channels surviving deletion;

# EHF-PHYS-XKLT-04 — Nine-Point Tail Filtration vs Complete-Graphic KLT Restriction / \(V_d\)-Resolved BCJ Rank, Common-Channel Support and Deletion-Factorization Compatibility Audit

**Cross branch:** EHF-PHYS-XKLT  
**Input theorem:** Exact Odd-to-Even Tail Filtration of Parke–Taylor Fibre Relations at Nine Points, draft v1.5.  
**Other inputs:** AMP-08 double-partial/BCJ construction and completed XKLT-02/03 pairwise complete-graphic bridge.  
**Status:** PASS.

## 1. Imported tail-filtration input

For

\[
A=\{a<b<c\}\subset\{2,\ldots,8\},
\]

the completed nine-to-eight theorem assigns one of

\[
V_0,\ V_2,\ V_8,\ V_9,\ V_{14},\ V_{15}
\]

with dimensions

\[
0,\ 2,\ 8,\ 9,\ 14,\ 15
\]

and fibre multiplicities

\[
4,\ 3,\ 3,\ 6,\ 6,\ 13.
\]

Every target fibre contains

\[
72
\]

canonical eight-point orderings and has a \(15\)-dimensional local KK relation space.

The tail theorem is prior input in XKLT-04 and is not re-proved.

## 2. Exact generic BCJ rank of every target fibre

At eight points the global BCJ dimension is

\[
(8-3)!=120.
\]

For each of the \(35\) target fibres, the \(72\) corresponding rows of the signed double-partial matrix were evaluated against a \(120\)-column BCJ basis.

Two independent exact finite-field witnesses were used:

\[
\mathbb F_{1000003},
\qquad
\mathbb F_{1000033}.
\]

At both primes:

\[
\operatorname{rank}M_{\mathrm{global}}=120,
\]

and every one of the \(35\) fibre restrictions has

\[
\boxed{
\operatorname{rank}M_A^{\mathrm{BCJ}}=57.
}
\]

The exact \(15\)-dimensional KK relation space already supplies

\[
\operatorname{rank}M_A^{\mathrm{BCJ}}
\le72-15=57.
\]

The finite-field nonzero-minor witnesses supply the matching generic lower bound, hence the generic rank is exactly \(57\).

Therefore

\[
\boxed{
\dim\ker M_A^{\mathrm{BCJ}}=15.
}
\]

Since

\[
R_A^{(8)}
\subseteq
\ker M_A^{\mathrm{BCJ}}
\]

and both spaces have dimension \(15\),

\[
\boxed{
\ker M_A^{\mathrm{BCJ}}
=
R_A^{(8)}.
}
\]

After canonical transport,

\[
\boxed{
\ker M_A^{\mathrm{BCJ}}
\cong V_{15}.
}
\]

Thus there are no extra fibre-local BCJ relations beyond the fifteen local KK directions.

## 3. The exact \(V_d\)-resolved BCJ law

Because

\[
V_d\subseteq V_{15}=\ker M_A^{\mathrm{BCJ}},
\]

quotienting the fibre coefficient space by \(V_d\) leaves the non-null BCJ rank unchanged:

\[
\boxed{
\operatorname{rank}_{\mathrm{BCJ}}(F_A^{(8)}/V_d)=57.
}
\]

The remaining nullity is

\[
\boxed{
15-d.
}
\]

The six levels are therefore:

\[
\begin{array}{c|c|c|c}
V_d&\text{fibre multiplicity}&
\text{transmitted null directions}&
\text{residual nullity}\\
\hline
V_0&4&0&15\\
V_2&3&2&13\\
V_8&3&8&7\\
V_9&6&9&6\\
V_{14}&6&14&1\\
V_{15}&13&15&0
\end{array}
\]

Hence the precise amplitude-side interpretation is

\[
\boxed{
V_d
=
\text{a \(d\)-dimensional deletion-transmitted subspace of the complete local BCJ/KLT nullspace}.
}
\]

The six spaces are not six different KLT blocks.

## 4. Global nullspace accounting

The \(35\) target fibres carry

\[
35\cdot15=525
\]

local BCJ-null directions when counted fibre by fibre.

The nine-to-eight map transmits

\[
\sum_A d_A=363.
\]

Therefore

\[
\boxed{
525-363=162
}
\]

local BCJ-null directions are not supplied by this deletion channel.

The old tail-filtration deficit is thus exactly reinterpreted as

\[
\boxed{
363\text{ transmitted},
\qquad
162\text{ untransmitted}
}
\]

directions of the already-existing local BCJ nullspaces.

## 5. Common connected-flat/channel support is blind to \(V_d\)

For every target fibre, all

\[
72^2=5184
\]

ordered pairs were classified by the number of common planar channels.

Every one of the \(35\) fibres has exactly the same histogram:

\[
\begin{array}{c|rrrrrrr}
\#\text{ common channels}&0&2&3&6&8&12&20\\
\hline
\#\text{ ordered pairs}&72&576&1152&1296&1440&576&72
\end{array}
\]

Therefore

\[
\boxed{
\text{common connected-flat/channel support does not distinguish }V_d.
}
\]

## 6. Simultaneous cubic-tree support is also blind to \(V_d\)

The complete common-tree distribution is likewise identical in all target fibres:

\[
\begin{array}{c|rrrrrr}
\#\text{ common cubic trees}&0&2&5&8&28&132\\
\hline
\#\text{ ordered pairs}&1800&1296&576&864&576&72
\end{array}
\]

Thus

\[
3384
\]

of the \(5184\) ordered pairs in every target fibre have nonzero common-tree support.

Hence

\[
\boxed{
V_d
\text{ is not an associahedral/common-tree support sector}.
}
\]

## 7. Exact delete-9 factorization tree lift

Every delete-active nine-point source state has the deleted label \(9\) adjacent to anchor \(1\).

For a target eight-point split

\[
I|I^c,
\]

lift it by placing label \(9\) on the side containing \(1\).

Then adjoin the universal factorization split

\[
\boxed{
\{1,9\}\mid\{2,\ldots,8\}.
}
\]

For every target cubic tree \(T_8\),

\[
\boxed{
L(T_8)
=
\{\operatorname{lift}(I):I\in T_8\}
\cup
\{\{1,9\}\}.
}
\]

An eight-point polygon has

\[
C_6=132
\]

planar cubic trees.

A nine-point polygon has exactly

\[
132
\]

planar cubic trees containing the fixed \(\{1,9\}\) cherry.

The lift was checked on every delete-active source state:

\[
\boxed{
5040/5040
}
\]

with zero failures.

Thus

\[
\boxed{
\mathcal T_8(Dc)
\cong
\{T\in\mathcal T_9(c):\{1,9\}\in T\}.
}
\]

## 8. Pairwise factorization compatibility

Because the lift is defined splitwise and independently of the second ordering, it commutes with intersection.

For active sources \(c,c'\),

\[
\boxed{
\mathcal T_8(Dc)\cap\mathcal T_8(Dc')
\cong
\{
T\in\mathcal T_9(c)\cap\mathcal T_9(c'):
\{1,9\}\in T
\}.
}
\]

Each target ordering has exactly two active source preimages.

Therefore every ordered target pair has four active ordered source-pair lifts.

Across the \(35\) fibres this gives

\[
35\cdot144^2
=
\boxed{725760}
\]

active ordered source-pair incidences.

The \(s_{19}\)-factorization support is exactly the fourfold incidence pullback of the target common-tree support.

## 9. Mechanism exclusion

The deletion tree lift is identical at all six tail levels.

The target common-channel histogram is identical at all six levels.

The target common-tree histogram is identical at all six levels.

The BCJ rank is identical at all six levels.

Therefore the six-level law is not caused by:

- differing non-null BCJ rank;
- differing complete-graphic channel availability;
- differing simultaneous-planarity support;
- differing delete-9 factorization support.

The surviving distinction is exactly the rank of the linear relation-transmission map

\[
D_A|_{\ker Q_A}.
\]

## 10. Main theorem-level interpretation

XKLT-04 sharpens the status of the tail spaces to

\[
\boxed{
V_0,V_2,V_8,V_9,V_{14},V_{15}
=
\text{a deletion-transmitted filtration of one fixed 15-dimensional local BCJ/KLT nullspace}.
}
\]

This is stronger than merely saying the filtration sits upstream of KLT.

It is directly visible inside the local nullspace of the complete-graphic double-partial matrix.

But it still does not decompose the non-null KLT matrix into six amplitude blocks.

## 11. Claim boundary

Imported exact theorem:

- the six \(V_d\);
- their dimensions, multiplicities and lattice;
- the 15-dimensional target-local KK relation space.

New exact/generic results:

- generic fibre BCJ rank \(57\);
- equality of local KK and BCJ nullspaces;
- residual-nullity law \(15-d\);
- complete common-channel support no-go;
- complete simultaneous-tree support no-go;
- 5040-state deletion tree-lift theorem;
- pairwise factorization-support compatibility.

The generic rank statement is certified by exact finite-field nonzero-minor witnesses together with the exact KK upper bound.

The propagator-weighted residue identity is standard field-theory factorization and is not re-derived as a new theorem here.

## 12. Recommended next audit

### EHF-PHYS-XKLT-05 — Tail-Filtration Mechanism / Source-KK Kernel Stratification, Deletion Incidence Quotients and Why the Fixed BCJ Nullspace Receives Exactly \(0,2,8,9,14,15\) Directions

The obvious KLT/channel mechanisms have now been excluded.

The next target is internal to

\[
D_A:\ker Q_A\to V_{15}.
\]

Required questions:

1. source-KK kernel dimensions by fibre \(A\);
2. \(\ker(D_A|_{\ker Q_A})\);
3. exact surviving quotient directions;
4. a closed block-rank formula for the ordered-tail law;
5. switch-on of the \(C_2^3\) character layers as \(b,c\) move right;
6. possible Schubert/incidence-style rank interpretation.

BERG-06, REV-20 and PHYS-ALG-07C remain queued separately.

# EHF-PHYS-XKLT-05 — Tail-Filtration Mechanism / Source-KK Kernel Stratification, Deletion Incidence Quotients and Why the Fixed BCJ Nullspace Receives Exactly \(0,2,8,9,14,15\) Directions

**Cross branch:** EHF-PHYS-XKLT  
**Inputs:** exact nine-point tail-filtration theorem, XKLT-04 local-BCJ-nullspace identification.  
**Status:** PASS. The six rank values are now explained by an exact source-side KK leakage/cancellation mechanism and a five-layer threshold formula.

## 1. Active/inactive source block form

In every nine-point source fibre, exactly \(144\) states are delete-active.

For an active ordering

\[
c=(1,\sigma,9),
\]

the KK expansion has no right tail after \(9\), so its KK column is a single basis coordinate.

After ordering source columns and KK rows appropriately,

\[
\boxed{
Q_A=
\begin{pmatrix}
I_{144}&A_A\\
0&B_A
\end{pmatrix}.
}
\]

The inactive columns split by their final selected label

\[
a,\qquad b,\qquad c.
\]

## 2. Exact source-KK kernel reduction

Write inactive coefficients as \(z\).

Every source relation is uniquely

\[
(-A_Az,z)
\]

with

\[
B_Az=0.
\]

Therefore

\[
\boxed{
R_A^{(9)}
\cong
\ker B_A.
}
\]

This reduces the complete \(5040\)-row KK problem to the exact kernel of the off-active leakage block.

## 3. Deletion incidence quotient

Let

\[
P_A:\mathbb Q^{144}\to\mathbb Q^{72}
\]

be the delete-incidence map on active coefficients.

Then

\[
D_A(-A_Az,z)
=
-P_AA_Az.
\]

Hence

\[
\boxed{
I_A
=
\operatorname{im}
\left(
P_AA_A|_{\ker B_A}
\right).
}
\]

And exactly

\[
\boxed{
R_A^{(9)}
/
\left(
R_A^{(9)}\cap\ker D_A
\right)
\cong
I_A.
}
\]

After canonical target transport,

\[
I_A=V_d.
\]

So \(d\) is the dimension of an exact incidence quotient of the source KK kernel.

## 4. Boundary capacities

For

\[
A=\{a<b<c\},
\]

define

\[
h_x
=
\#\{u\in U\setminus A:u<x\}.
\]

Then

\[
\boxed{
h_a=a-2,\qquad
h_b=b-3,\qquad
h_c=c-4.
}
\]

The inactive sector ending in \(x\) has exactly

\[
\boxed{
36h_x
}
\]

source states.

Thus

\[
\boxed{
|F_A^{(9)}|
=
144+36(h_a+h_b+h_c).
}
\]

## 5. Universal one-sector stratification

For every \(a\)-, \(b\)- or \(c\)-ending inactive sector, the exact rational kernel/image law depends only on the capacity \(h\):

\[
\boxed{
\begin{array}{c|ccccc}
h&0&1&2&3&4\\
\hline
\text{sector columns}&0&36&72&108&144\\
\dim\ker B_x&0&0&2&10&29\\
\dim I_x&0&0&2&9&9\\
\dim(\ker B_x\cap\ker D)&0&0&0&1&20
\end{array}
}
\]

Thus:

\[
h\le1\Rightarrow0,
\]

\[
h=2\Rightarrow2,
\]

\[
h\ge3\Rightarrow9.
\]

The source kernel continues to grow after \(h=3\), but the transmitted image saturates at dimension \(9\).

## 6. Exact rightmost-two sufficiency

For every one of the \(35\) source fibres,

\[
\boxed{
I_{a,b,c}=I_{b,c}.
}
\]

Moreover, the image obtainable from the \(a\)-sector alone is always contained in \(I_{b,c}\).

Therefore the smallest selected label is exactly target-redundant.

It may enlarge the source KK kernel and deletion-blind redundancy, but it cannot activate a new target direction.

This is the mechanism behind the two-rightmost-label law.

## 7. Universal branch precursors

After canonical target transport, the rightmost \(c\)-branch is

\[
h_c\le1\to0,
\]

\[
h_c=2\to
\boxed{C_2=V_2},
\]

\[
h_c\ge3\to
\boxed{C_9=V_9}.
\]

The second-rightmost \(b\)-branch is

\[
h_b\le1\to0,
\]

\[
h_b=2\to B_2,\qquad\dim B_2=2,
\]

\[
h_b\ge3\to B_9,\qquad\dim B_9=9.
\]

Exactly,

\[
\boxed{
B_2\subset B_9.
}
\]

The \(B\)-branch precursor spaces are not invariant under the full \(C_2^3\) tail symmetry and therefore are not themselves nodes of the final tail lattice.

## 8. Cooperative two-sector closure

The full \(b,c\) image is not generally the ordinary sum of the two independently generated branch images.

The exact class table is

\[
\boxed{
\begin{array}{c|ccccc}
&\dim B&\dim C&\dim(B+C)&
\text{cooperative quotient}&\dim I_{bc}\\
\hline
V_0&0&0&0&0&0\\
V_2&0&2&2&0&2\\
V_8&2&2&4&4&8\\
V_9&0&9&9&0&9\\
V_{14}&2&9&11&3&14\\
V_{15}&9&9&12&3&15
\end{array}
}
\]

The additional dimensions arise from cross-sector combinations that cancel off-active KK leakage only when the two inactive sectors are used together.

## 9. \(V_8\) is intrinsically cooperative

For

\[
b=5,\qquad c=6,
\]

the two independent branch images each have dimension \(2\).

Their ordinary span has dimension

\[
4.
\]

Mixed KK cancellation produces four further directions:

\[
\boxed{
4+4=8.
}
\]

So \(V_8\) cannot be obtained by merely adding two independently transmitted two-dimensional images.

## 10. \(V_{14}\) cooperative closure

For

\[
b=5,\qquad c\ge7,
\]

the independent branch ranks are

\[
2,\qquad9.
\]

Their span has dimension

\[
11.
\]

Cross-sector cancellation adds

\[
3
\]

new directions:

\[
\boxed{
11+3=14.
}
\]

The only missing direction is the already-established final quotient

\[
V_{15}/V_{14}.
\]

## 11. \(V_{15}\) high-tail closure

For

\[
b\ge6,
\]

both branches independently have dimension \(9\).

Their intersection has dimension

\[
\boxed{6},
\]

so their ordinary sum has dimension

\[
12.
\]

Joint KK cancellation contributes another

\[
3,
\]

giving

\[
\boxed{
12+3=15.
}
\]

Thus \(V_{15}\) is the cooperative closure of two saturated nine-dimensional boundary branches.

## 12. Exact five-layer switch theorem

The prior tail theorem decomposed \(V_{15}\) into five filtration quotient layers

\[
L_2,\ L_3,\ L_8,\ L_9,\ L_{15}
\]

with dimensions

\[
2,\ 1,\ 5,\ 6,\ 1.
\]

Their exact activation thresholds are

\[
\boxed{
L_2\iff c\ge6,
}
\]

\[
\boxed{
L_3\iff b\ge5\ \lor\ c\ge7,
}
\]

\[
\boxed{
L_8\iff b\ge5,
}
\]

\[
\boxed{
L_9\iff c\ge7,
}
\]

\[
\boxed{
L_{15}\iff b\ge6.
}
\]

Therefore

\[
\boxed{
r_A
=
2\,\mathbf1_{c\ge6}
+
\mathbf1_{b\ge5\lor c\ge7}
+
5\,\mathbf1_{b\ge5}
+
6\,\mathbf1_{c\ge7}
+
\mathbf1_{b\ge6}.
}
\]

Equivalently, with

\[
u=b-3,\qquad v=c-4,
\]

\[
\boxed{
r_A
=
2\,\mathbf1_{v\ge2}
+
\mathbf1_{u\ge2\lor v\ge3}
+
5\,\mathbf1_{u\ge2}
+
6\,\mathbf1_{v\ge3}
+
\mathbf1_{u\ge3}.
}
\]

This exact weighted threshold formula reproduces all \(35\) fibres.

## 13. The six values are activated-layer sums

The six cases are

\[
\boxed{
\begin{array}{c|c|c}
\text{condition}&\text{active layers}&r_A\\
\hline
b\le4,\ c\le5&\varnothing&0\\
b\le4,\ c=6&L_2&2\\
b=5,\ c=6&L_2+L_3+L_8&8\\
b\le4,\ c\ge7&L_2+L_3+L_9&9\\
b=5,\ c\ge7&L_2+L_3+L_8+L_9&14\\
b\ge6&L_2+L_3+L_8+L_9+L_{15}&15
\end{array}
}
\]

So

\[
0,2,8,9,14,15
\]

are the dimensions of five fixed target-null quotient layers switched on by two ordered boundary capacities.

## 14. Character-theoretic final switch

The exact prior character theorem gives

\[
L_{15}=V_{15}/V_{14}
\]

as the unique

\[
\boxed{(-+-)}
\]

character direction.

Therefore

\[
\boxed{
b\ge6
}
\]

is precisely the source-tail threshold at which that final BCJ-null direction becomes deletion-accessible.

## 15. Source redundancy saturation

At fixed \(b,c\), increasing \(a\) changes source-side redundancy but not the target image.

For example,

\[
A=\{a,6,7\},
\qquad
a=2,3,4,5,
\]

has exact source relation dimensions

\[
\boxed{
40,\ 40,\ 59,\ 90
}
\]

but every fibre transmits

\[
V_{15}.
\]

More generally, at fixed \(b,c\), the exact source KK kernel and deletion-blind kernel are nondecreasing as \(a\) moves right, while \(V_d\) remains fixed.

## 16. Mechanistic verdict

The six-level theorem now has the exact finite mechanism

\[
\boxed{
\text{boundary capacity}
\to
\text{off-active KK leakage kernel}
\to
\text{rightmost-two reduction}
\to
\text{cooperative \(b/c\) cancellation}
\to
\text{five quotient-layer switches}
\to
V_d.
}
\]

The ranks are therefore not unexplained numerical strata.

They are the dimensions of incidence-activated layers inside the fixed 15-dimensional target BCJ nullspace.

## 17. Schubert-like boundary

The weighted indicator law is genuinely flag-like:

- ordered boundary positions;
- threshold activation;
- nested quotient layers;
- rank determined by which thresholds are crossed.

The present result justifies the term **Schubert-like** in this finite structural sense.

It does not assert a general Schubert-variety or Schubert-calculus theorem.

## 18. Recommended next audit

### EHF-PHYS-XKLT-06 — Universal Tail-Capacity Law / Eleven-to-Ten Prediction, Two-Boundary Sector Extrapolation and Failure-or-Extension Audit

The next test should determine whether the mechanism just proved is specific to \(9\to8\) or persists at the next odd-to-even transition.

Targets:

1. identify the active identity KK block at \(11\to10\);
2. define terminal boundary capacities;
3. test whether the smallest selected labels are again target-redundant;
4. measure one-sector threshold saturation;
5. measure multi-sector cooperative cancellation;
6. use the nine-point mechanism to make predictions before the complete \(11\to10\) computation.

BERG-06, REV-20 and PHYS-ALG-07C remain queued separately.

# EHF-PHYS-XKLT-06 — Universal Tail-Capacity Law / Eleven-to-Ten Prediction, Two-Boundary Sector Extrapolation and Failure-or-Extension Audit

**Status:** PASS.

## 1. Question

XKLT-05 explained the \(9\to8\) tail filtration through an active KK identity block, terminal boundary capacities and cooperative cancellation. XKLT-06 asks which parts survive at \(11\to10\).

The answer is:

\[
\boxed{\text{the mechanism extends, but the nine-point numerical law does not.}}
\]

## 2. Exact structural extension

For

\[
A=\{a<b<c<d\}\subset\{2,\ldots,10\},
\]

every ten-point target fibre has

\[
\boxed{1440}
\]

states.

Every eleven-point source fibre has exactly

\[
\boxed{2880}
\]

delete-active states with \(11\) at the final position. Their KK columns are coordinate basis vectors, giving

\[
\boxed{
Q_A=
\begin{pmatrix}
I_{2880}&A_A\\
0&B_A
\end{pmatrix}.
}
\]

Each target state has exactly two active source preimages.

## 3. Exact capacity law

Define

\[
h_a=a-2,\quad h_b=b-3,\quad h_c=c-4,\quad h_d=d-5.
\]

For a terminal sector ending in \(x_i\), exactly \(h_i\) complement labels may occupy slot 2, and for each such choice the remaining labels have

\[
4!\,4!=576
\]

arrangements.

Hence

\[
\boxed{\#\text{sector}(x_i)=576h_i.}
\]

Therefore

\[
\boxed{
|F_A^{(11)}|
=
2880+576(h_a+h_b+h_c+h_d).
}
\]

This is the exact \(11\to10\) analogue of the \(9\to8\) \(36h\) law.

## 4. The target relation module grows from \(15\) to \(296\)

An exact rational KK calculation on a reference ten-point target fibre gives

\[
\operatorname{rank}_{\mathbb Q}Q^{(10)}_A=1144.
\]

Since the fibre has \(1440\) states,

\[
\boxed{
\dim R_A^{(10)}=1440-1144=296.
}
\]

All target fibres are related by label relabelling, so the dimension is universal.

Thus the target relation flag itself grows dramatically:

\[
15\longrightarrow296.
\]

## 5. The literal \(0/2/9\) law fails

At \(9\to8\), a single terminal sector had transmitted ranks

\[
0,0,2,9,9.
\]

At \(11\to10\), exact rational calculations give

\[
\boxed{
h=0,1,2,3
\quad\longrightarrow\quad
0,0,6,36.
}
\]

The numerical universality therefore fails already at the first two nonzero thresholds.

## 6. Minimal two-boundary test

Take

\[
\boxed{A=(2,5,6,7)}.
\]

Its capacities are

\[
\boxed{(0,2,2,2)}.
\]

The \(a=2\) sector is empty, so all nontrivial source information lies in the three rightmost terminal sectors.

Keeping only the two rightmost sectors \(\{6,7\}\), the transmitted image has exact rational rank

\[
\boxed{30}.
\]

Each \(h=2\) branch separately has rank \(6\), so the ordinary independent sum would have rank at most \(12\). Their cooperative KK closure therefore contributes

\[
\boxed{30-12=18}
\]

additional directions.

## 7. Adding the third boundary sector

Now add the terminal sector ending in \(5\).

Over the exact field

\[
\mathbb F_{1000003},
\]

the three-sector transmitted rank is

\[
\boxed{87}.
\]

For integer matrices,

\[
\operatorname{rank}_{\mathbb F_p}
\le
\operatorname{rank}_{\mathbb Q}.
\]

Hence

\[
\boxed{
\operatorname{rank}_{\mathbb Q}I_A\ge87.
}
\]

Since the two-rightmost image has exact rational rank \(30\),

\[
87>30
\]

proves

\[
\boxed{\text{two-boundary sufficiency fails over }\mathbb Q.}
\]

The same modular calculation gives off-active kernel dimension \(123\). Reduction modulo \(p\) can only enlarge the kernel, so

\[
\boxed{
87\le\operatorname{rank}_{\mathbb Q}I_A\le123.
}
\]

The exact three-sector rank is deliberately left open.

## 8. What is forced

At nine points, two rightmost selected-label sectors sufficed for every fibre.

At eleven points, the witness above proves:

\[
\boxed{\text{at least three terminal sectors may be necessary.}}
\]

XKLT-06 does not yet prove that three suffice for every one of the \(126\) fibres.

## 9. What remains universal

The following structural ingredients survive:

\[
\boxed{
\text{active KK identity block}
+
\text{boundary capacities}
+
\text{cooperative cancellation}.
}
\]

These are the plausible universal parts of the odd-to-even mechanism.

## 10. What was nine-point-specific

The following do not survive literally:

- the one-sector \(0/2/9\) dimensions;
- the 15-dimensional target relation flag;
- rightmost-two sufficiency;
- the exact five layer dimensions \(2,1,5,6,1\).

Thus the nine-point theorem is best understood as the smallest nontrivial member of a growing hierarchy, not a fixed numerical template.

## 11. Verdict

The universal tail-capacity law survives in **form**, not in fixed numbers.

At \(11\to10\),

\[
\boxed{
\text{capacity-controlled source sectors still feed a fixed target relation module through cooperative KK cancellation,}
}
\]

but both the size of that target module and the number of boundary sectors needed increase.

## 12. Claim boundary

Exact over \(\mathbb Q\):

- \(I_{2880}\) active block;
- \(576h\) sector count;
- target KK rank \(1144\) and nullity \(296\);
- one-sector ranks \(0,0,6,36\) through \(h=3\);
- two-boundary witness rank \(30\).

Exact over \(\mathbb F_{1000003}\):

- three-sector witness rank \(87\);
- three-sector off-active kernel dimension \(123\).

The finite-field rank is used only as a rigorous lower bound over \(\mathbb Q\), enough to prove the exact failure of two-boundary sufficiency.

No full \(126\)-fibre rank atlas is claimed.

## 13. Recommended next audit

### EHF-PHYS-XKLT-07 — Eleven-to-Ten Three-Boundary Cooperative Closure / Full Capacity-Cell Rank Atlas, \(296\)D Target-Relation Filtration and Smallest-Sector Redundancy Audit

The next audit should determine:

1. whether the three rightmost terminal sectors suffice globally;
2. the exact rank on every capacity cell;
3. whether the smallest selected-label sector is again target-redundant;
4. the high-capacity one-sector exact ranks;
5. the nested subspaces inside the \(296\)-dimensional target relation module;
6. the analogue of the nine-point quotient/character filtration.

# EHF-PHYS-XKLT-07A — Boundary-Corrected Three-Tail Reconciliation / 126-Cell Multi-Prime Rank Atlas Import and Smallest-Sector Exception Audit

## Status

```text
EHF_PHYS_XKLT_07A_BOUNDARY_CORRECTED_THREE_TAIL_RECONCILIATION_PASS
```

This is a **finite-field atlas/reconciliation theorem**, not yet the full characteristic-zero XKLT-07 theorem.

## 1. Recovered parent computation

The earlier AMP-FOUND-04B run had already completed the expensive `11 -> 10` census:

- 126/126 channels over GF(3) and GF(5);
- exact agreement between those two odd primes on every channel;
- target relation dimension 296 in GF(2), GF(3), and GF(5);
- boundary-corrected three-tail law passes;
- complete target recovery iff `b>=7`;
- exactly 21 complete-recovery cells;
- 34/126 channel ranks independently certified over Q in that parent audit.

This prevents XKLT-07 from redundantly rerunning a computation already present in the framework.

## 2. Correct rank law in XKLT capacity variables

For

\[
A=(a<b<c<d),
\]

let `R(b,c,d)` denote the three-rightmost base rank. Then the complete GF(3)=GF(5) atlas obeys

\[
\boxed{
r(a,b,c,d)
=R(b,c,d)
+\mathbf 1_{\{d=10,\ a\ge4,\ b\in\{5,6\}\}}.
}
\]

Equivalently, with

\[
(h_a,h_b,h_c,h_d)
=(a-2,b-3,c-4,d-5),
\]

\[
\boxed{
\epsilon=1
\iff
h_d=5,
\quad h_a\ge2,
\quad h_b\in\{2,3\}.
}
\]

Thus **pure three-rightmost sufficiency fails at eleven points** at the modular rank-law level.

## 3. Seven and only seven split suffixes

The split suffix classes are

\[
(5,6,10),
(5,7,10),
(5,8,10),
(5,9,10),
(6,7,10),
(6,8,10),
(6,9,10).
\]

Their rank pairs are

\[
182/183,
244/245,
269/270,
269/270,
266/267,
290/291,
290/291.
\]

Every split is one-dimensional and every split lies on the maximal deleted-tail boundary `d=10`.

## 4. High-capacity one-sector continuation

The one-sector ladder is now

\[
\boxed{0,0,6,36,105,105.}
\]

Status by capacity:

- `h=0,1,2,3`: exact over Q from XKLT-06;
- `h=4,5`: 105 over GF(3), GF(5), and independently GF(1000003); exact-Q equality remains open.

Therefore the eleven-point one-sector law exhibits a high-capacity plateau at 105 in every tested odd characteristic, but this plateau is not yet elevated to a rational theorem.

## 5. Exact low-capacity fourth-sector inertness

For the cell

\[
A=(3,4,5,6),
\qquad h=(1,1,1,1),
\]

the complete off-active KK block has 2304 columns and rank 2304 modulo 1000003. Hence it has full column rank over Q and zero off-active kernel over Q. Therefore

\[
\boxed{r_{\mathbb Q}(3,4,5,6)=0.}
\]

All four `h=1` sectors are exactly inert cooperatively in this cell.

For

\[
A=(3,5,6,7),
\qquad h=(1,2,2,2),
\]

adding the `h_a=1` smallest sector leaves the GF(1000003) image rank unchanged at 87. This is consistent with the global correction threshold `h_a>=2`.

## 6. Updated smallest-sector theorem boundary

The exact nine-point theorem said that the smallest selected label changed source-side redundancy but did not change the transported target image.

At eleven points the correct current statement is:

\[
\boxed{
\text{the smallest sector is generically target-redundant but has a one-dimensional maximal-tail correction locus.}
}
\]

This is exact as a GF(3)/GF(5) rank-law statement. The corresponding characteristic-zero correction line is not yet certified.

## 7. Full rank multiplicity census

Across the 126 cells:

\[
\begin{array}{c|r@{\qquad}c|r}
\text{rank}&\text{count}&\text{rank}&\text{count}\\\hline
0&5&182&5\\
6&4&183&1\\
30&3&185&3\\
36&4&198&6\\
60&3&209&4\\
87&3&225&9\\
105&8&244&5\\
116&3&245&1\\
129&6&266&6\\
138&3&267&2\\
269&7&270&2\\
290&8&291&4\\
296&21&&
\end{array}
\]

The 21 rank-296 cells are exact over Q by target saturation.

## 8. Exact-Q split attempt

The economical candidate pair

\[
(3,5,6,10):182,
\qquad
(4,5,6,10):183
\]

was selected for a direct exact-upper-bound route. The current generic sparse DomainMatrix implementation did not finish the 5184-column `{5,6,10}` three-sector rank within the execution window. Therefore no Q upper certificate is claimed from this attempt.

The correct response is not to brute-force all 126 cells with that backend; it is to exploit canonical target-space transport and quotient structure.

## 9. Strongest justified statement after XKLT-07A

> At `11 -> 10`, the complete GF(3)=GF(5) 126-cell rank atlas is governed by three rightmost selected labels except for a sharply localized one-dimensional correction from the smallest selected-label sector. The correction occurs only at maximal deleted-tail capacity `h_d=5`, only after smallest-sector capacity reaches `h_a>=2`, and only in the `h_b=2,3` bands, producing seven split suffix classes. Complete target recovery occurs in exactly 21 cells and is exact over Q. The one-sector odd-prime ladder is `0,0,6,36,105,105`, with the first four terms exact over Q. The literal nine-point smallest-label invisibility theorem therefore does not persist, but it deforms into a boundary-corrected redundancy law. The 296-dimensional transported subspace lattice and the exact-Q identity of the one-dimensional correction remain open.

## 10. Interpretation boundary

This is a finite theorem/candidate structure for exact and modular linear relation spaces associated with the EHF Hamilton-cycle/KLT construction. No physical scattering law, spacetime dynamics, particle ontology, or empirical claim follows from the rank atlas alone.

# EHF-PHYS-XKLT-07B — 296D Canonical Target-Transport / Seven Boundary-Correction Lines, Three-Tail Subspace Atlas and Character-Orbit Audit

## Status

```text
EHF_PHYS_XKLT_07B_COMMON_BOUNDARY_LINE_PASS
```

**Scope:** three-prime transported-subspace theorem candidate plus an exact characteristic-zero strict-inclusion certificate in the minimal alternating sector.

The seven exceptional suffixes have base/upper dimensions
\[
182/183,\ 244/245,\ 269/270,\ 269/270,\ 266/267,\ 290/291,\ 290/291
\]
over GF(3), GF(5), and GF(1000003). A single primitive integer vector \(\ell_\partial\in\mathbb Z^{296}\), with 15 nonzero coordinates and coefficients in \(\{-2,-1,1,2\}\), lies in every upper space and in none of the corresponding base spaces. Hence every exceptional upper image is \(W_i+\langle\ell_\partial\rangle\) in all three tested characteristics.

Under \(S_4\times S_5\), the projective orbit of \(L_\partial\) has size 480. Its stabilizer is \(S_{\{8,9,10\}}\cong S_3\), acting by the sign character.

For the minimal split family, exact rational alternating-sector dimensions are
\[
\dim_\mathbb Q W_{225}^{\rm sgn}=59,\qquad \dim_\mathbb Q I_{225}^{\rm sgn}=60,
\]
and \(\ell_\partial\) lies in the upper image but not the lower. Therefore
\[
\boxed{W_{225}(\mathbb Q)\subsetneq I_{225}(\mathbb Q)}.
\]
This proves characteristic-zero smallest-sector necessity, but does not yet certify complete rational ranks exactly \(182/183\).

The 56 nondecreasing three-tail capacity triples collapse to exactly 20 distinct base spaces with dimensions
\[
0,6,30,36,60,87,105,116,129,138,182,185,198,209,225,244,266,269,290,296.
\]

# EHF-PHYS-XKLT-07C — Three-Tail Intersection Completion / 61-Node Distributive Relation Lattice and 17-Block Birkhoff Decomposition Audit

## Status

```text
EHF_PHYS_XKLT_07C_THREE_PRIME_61_NODE_DISTRIBUTIVE_COMPLETION_PASS
```

**Scope:** three-prime finite-field subspace-lattice theorem candidate; complete characteristic-zero lift remains open.

The 20 base spaces have 35 incomparable pairs. Three intersect at their ordinary base meet; 32 have positive meet defect and produce 32 distinct new subspaces. The first completion therefore has 52 nodes. A second closure pass adds exactly nine triple-intersection spaces of dimensions
\[
9,33,38,62,77,79,79,81,143,
\]
and the next pass adds none. Hence the complete closure has exactly 61 nodes, and every generated intersection is representable using at most three original base spaces.

Over GF(3), GF(5), and GF(1000003), the 61-node family has identical dimensions, 113 Hasse covers, meet table, join table, and 17 join-irreducibles. It is closed under actual subspace sums and intersections. All \(61^3=226981\) distributivity tests pass.

The 17 join-irreducible block dimensions are
\[
1,1,1,2,2,2,6,6,7,7,21,24,24,29,44,57,62,
\]
summing to 296. At each tested prime, explicit complement blocks reconstruct all 61 nodes as direct sums indexed by order ideals of the 17-element join-irreducible poset.

The unique coatom has dimension 290, leaving a terminal six-dimensional primitive block. The XKLT-07B boundary line reduces into this block as \(-e_{294}-e_{295}\), and it lies in no proper node of the completed 61-node three-tail lattice.
